# BrainCode Syntax Changelog

Append-only. One entry per attempt, written by the Documenter role. Each entry records: what set
of changes was proposed and by which model, the Critic's decision and its benefit/harm judgment
per pre-KPI, the sampled dataset items it simulated (with the BrainCode expressions it produced),
whether independent cross-provider translations were available, the required changes it asked
for, and the final decision. This is the audit trail for "what did this specific syntax change
actually contribute" — the raw records also land in `../runs/kpi_history.jsonl`.

No decision recorded here is gated on a statistical test. The Critic's critical judgment is the
decision; the scripted doc-hygiene check (every change has a gloss + worked example) is the only
override.

*(Empty at seed. Sprint 0, when it runs, appears first.)*

## Baseline — imported v9.0.0 from `C:\projects\BrainCode\syntax-loop-favourite-products\v1` — 2026-09-30

- **Continues from sprint:** 9
- Spec and glossary imported as-is; earlier sprint history is not carried into this changelog.

## Sprint 10 (attempt 1/3) — 2026-09-30

- **Language version:** 9.0.0 → 9.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N1 [hard] Closed vocabulary: every symbol used in a BrainCode expression is defined in the glossary.
- **Shaper proposal (model: openai/gpt-5.6-terra):** This change set replaces all source-level free text and unconstrained identifiers with glossary-defined structural symbols, typed literal forms, opaque local handles, and ontology categories. It also makes action, utterance, and generation payloads symbolic propositions whose attributes and values are constrained by glossary categories.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — Every expression token is now either a listed structural symbol, a member of a glossary vocabulary category, an opaque handle generated by the glossary-defined HANDLE rule, or an atomic literal generated by a glossary-defined literal rule. STRING literals, free-text content, open operation identifiers, unconstrained attributes, and English-derived binding names are removed from the grammar.
- **Changes:** `Types & Lexicon` (revise, MAJOR), `structural-symbol` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `literal-form` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `operation-symbol` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `entity-symbol` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `role-symbol` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `intent-symbol` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `attribute-symbol` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `attribute-value-symbol` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `Utterance` (revise, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `Task` (revise, MAJOR), `Bind` (revise, MAJOR), `Conversation` (revise, MAJOR), `Flow-If` (revise, MAJOR), `Iterate` (revise, MAJOR), `Check` (revise, MAJOR)
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — The central idea—replace free text with typed literals, glossary categories, and recursive propositions—is a positive direction and removes the prior direct prose violations. It cannot be accepted because N1 remains hard-violated by an incomplete structural vocabulary, and the Task revision makes all UTTER-containing conversations grammatically invalid. The proposal also leaves deterministic handle allocation and canonical plan selection unresolved and lacks a symbolic code representation needed for sampled closed SWE-bench tasks.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — The ontology-category approach can express many closed operational tasks assuming N6's complete glossary, including household placement, shopping constraints, and structured communication. However, closed SWE-bench requests containing source code, exact expected/actual program output, configuration fragments, and reproduction procedures have no literal or symbolic code-data category, so their actionable core cannot be fully encoded.
  - expressivity: mixed — Recursive PROPOSITION values materially improve representation of intent, quantities, recipients, tone, corrections, and nested constraints without prose. But code-bearing items fail, and the revised Task grammar accidentally removes utterance from step even though Conversation requires UTTER in a Turn body; consequently the supplied Conversation worked examples are not valid expressions.
  - determinism: harm — The change removes free prose and gives canonical attribute ordering only in unspecified future glossary entries, which is directionally useful. Nevertheless HANDLE spellings remain arbitrary ($book_flight, $main, $t1, $request), operation/intention selection is open-ended, and independent translations visibly diverge between distinct plans such as repeated pick/place versus find-and-loop and TASK/GENERATE versus CONVO/UTTER; no canonical strategy or handle-allocation rule resolves those alternatives.
  - interpretability: mixed — Typed propositions and explicit ontology categories are easier to inspect than quoted content. But the claimed closed structural vocabulary is incomplete: expressions use or grammar requires SPEAKER, USER, AGENT, REPLY_TO, REVISES, DECREASES, ANY, ALL, SATISFIES, comparison operators, arrows, assignment, braces, brackets, commas, and delimiters that are not individually listed or generated by a precise glossary membership rule; a reader cannot look them all up in the supplied glossary.
  - improvement: mixed — Structured operation signatures, pure checks, typed bindings, and symbolic message propositions would help a weaker model plan ordinary operational tasks. The invalid conversation grammar and inability to encode source-code evidence are severe practical gaps, and unconstrained opaque-handle naming adds ceremony without preserving useful information.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `dev-embodied-2` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Put both pillows from the sofa onto the armchair, one at a time." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
  ACTION act_find(target=entity_pillow, source=entity_sofa, quantity=2) -> $v2 : LIST[REF[NAME]]
  FOR EACH $v3 IN $v2 {
    ACTION act_place(target=$v3, destination=entity_armchair)
  }
}` — operation-symbol, entity-symbol, attribute-symbol, and typed handles enable the operational core; act_find, source, quantity, entity_pillow, entity_sofa, and entity_armchair are assumed members of the complete existing categories under N6.
  - `dev-email-2` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "If my manager hasn't replied by Friday, send a polite follow-up; otherwise do nothing." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
  ACTION act_check_replied(target=role_manager, deadline=@d2026-10-02) -> $v2 : BOOL
  IF NOT $v2 THEN {
    ACTION act_send_email(recipient=role_manager, content=intent_follow_up(tone=value_polite))
  }
}` — Symbolic content and DATE_LITERAL avoid prose. The date necessarily depends on an unstated translation anchor after removal of CONTEXT, so the proposed language has no way to audit which Friday was intended.
  - `e344b89e-767a-4618-ba82-4b81cdcba280` (Mind2Web (osunlp/Mind2Web, train, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Full] NL: "add a motherboard under $200 and a compatible processor at any price to the shopping cart." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
  ACTION act_search_product(target=entity_motherboard, filter=intent_price(limit=200, relation=value_less_than)) -> $v2 : LIST[REF[PROPOSITION]]
  ACTION act_select_compatible(target=$v2, related=entity_processor) -> $v3 : LIST[REF[PROPOSITION]]
  ACTION act_add_to_cart(target=[$v2, $v3])
}` — The proposed category architecture can cover the constraints under N6, but it needs exact operation signatures for selection, compatibility, list combination, and cart addition.
  - `f118238f-ef8f-4b63-9159-a81e981ef46e` (Mind2Web (osunlp/Mind2Web, train, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Full] NL: "Add decorative LED Candles to the cart." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
  ACTION act_search_product(target=entity_decorative_led_candles) -> $v2 : LIST[REF[PROPOSITION]]
  ACTION act_add_to_cart(target=$v2)
}` — Uses assumed complete operation and entity entries; no prose fallback is needed.
  - `trial_T20190906_214148_552057#1` (ALFRED (json_2.1.0, train)) [Full] NL: "get a cup ready for coffee" → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
  ACTION act_pick_up(target=entity_cup, source=entity_fridge) -> $v2 : REF[NAME]
  ACTION act_heat(target=$v2, destination=entity_microwave) -> $v3 : REF[NAME]
  ACTION act_place(target=$v3, destination=entity_coffee_maker)
}` — The trajectory disambiguates 'ready for coffee' as retrieving, heating, and positioning the cup. Current listed operations do not include act_pick_up, act_heat, or these entities, but N6 permits members of the established categories.
  - `trial_T20190908_102045_139402#1` (ALFRED (json_2.1.0, train)) [Full] NL: "To put the toilet paper away." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
  ACTION act_pick_up(target=entity_toilet_paper, source=entity_toilet) -> $v2 : REF[NAME]
  ACTION act_place(target=$v2, destination=entity_wall_holder)

  - `sympy__sympy-15345` (SWE-bench_Verified (princeton-nlp/SWE-bench_Verified, test, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Fail] NL: "mathematica_code gives wrong output with Max

PATH and URL atoms can preserve only individual paths/URLs, not code or configuration structure.

TASK $v1 : PROPOSITION {

'A very experienced + very charming + very competent American Psychololinguist who explains complicated topics in eloquent simple casual conversational American style and tone' + 2. A very experienced + very charming + very competent American Harvard Educated Logical Reasoning professor who explains complicated topics in eloquent simple practical casual conversational American style and tone' + 4. 'A very experienced + very charming + very competent American Harvard Educated Large Language Models, "Transformers" and Generative AI top-notch Expert and Practitioner, who explain complicated topics in eloquent simple practical casual conversational American style and tone' to then while acting exactly as the collective persona Mark, explain to me in depth how linear algebra is exactly applied to transfer text into equations and numbers a model like ChatGPT-4 can understand and then how the model reapplies linear algebra to transform the equations and figures into answers to a user prompt. Please also illustrate using concrete examples" → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 : PROPOSITION {
  GENERATE(target=role_user, specification=intent_explain(topic=intent_transform_text_to_numeric_representation(model=entity_chatgpt4), continuation=intent_transform_numeric_representation_to_answer(model=entity_chatgpt4), depth=value_in_depth, examples=value_concrete_numerical, persona=intent_collective_persona(name=@nMark, roles=[role_psycholinguist, role_linear_algebra_professor, role_logic_professor, role_llm_expert], traits=[value_experienced, value_charming, value_competent, value_american, value_harvard_educated], tone=[value_eloquent, value_simple, value_casual, value_conversational, value_practical], notation_style=value_18th_century_math))) -> $v2 : PROPOSITION
  RETURN $v2
}` — The recursive proposition mechanism can retain the specified persona, subject, depth, style, and examples if all shown ontology entries/schemas exist. The exact attribute schemas are not supplied, so this relies on N6; the independent translations differ sharply in whether to use GENERATE or CONVO.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-148973` (PATHs (microsoft/prototypical-hai-collaborations, wildchat1m_en3u-task_utterance_wintent_anns, model=gpt-4-0125-preview)) [Full] NL: "Write a hilarious script where the Scooby gang and Scrappy-Doo have a funny meeting behind the scenes and react to my badly translated sentences, laughing and hilariously question and hilariously roast errors and inconsistencies that make no sense in the translation and the names of stuff with occasional funny quotes mocking a situation, in my prompt in a hilarious comedy 17+ way: (The gang constanlty imagine and make silly quotes to mock the translation)

Ok gang get this… * I start reading the whole thing to the gang * 

Velma and Shaggy compete on Jeopardy, hosted by Alex Trebek, but things go awry when a robot competitor begins attacking anyone who gets more points than him.
" → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 : PROPOSITION {
  GENERATE(target=role_user, specification=intent_generate_script(characters=[entity_scooby_gang, entity_scrappy_doo], setting=value_behind_the_scenes_meeting, genre=value_comedy, audience_age=value_17_plus, action=intent_react_to_bad_translation(source=intent_game_show_competition(participants=[entity_velma, entity_shaggy], host=entity_alex_trebek, game=entity_jeopardy, antagonist=entity_robot_competitor, event=intent_attack(target=value_higher_scoring_participant))), requirements=[value_hilarious, value_roast_inconsistencies, value_mocking_quotes, value_silly_imagining])) -> $v2 : PROPOSITION
  RETURN $v2
}` — All substantive requested elements are structurally captured only by assuming many entity, intent, and value entries under N6. It demonstrates that the language can become expressive, but also that intent categories need carefully defined schemas rather than merely permissive open naming.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-142592` (PATHs (microsoft/prototypical-hai-collaborations, wildchat1m_en3u-task_utterance_wintent_anns, model=gpt-4-0125-preview)) [Full] NL: "Please write steps for homiletics and engaging Christian sermons" → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 : PROPOSITION {
  GENERATE(target=role_user, specification=intent_provide_steps(topic=entity_homiletics, goal=intent_create_engaging_christian_sermon())) -> $v2 : PROPOSITION
  RETURN $v2
}` — The request is symbolically representable with assumed ontology categories; a later request to expand every step and provide examples needs explicit detail and example constraints.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-22638` (PATHs (microsoft/prototical-hai-collaborations, wildchat1m_en3u-task_utterance_wintent_anns, model=gpt-3.5-turbo-0301)) [Full] NL: "write an email introducing yourself and job position to other party asking for collaborations" → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 : PROPOSITION {
  GENERATE(target=role_user, specification=intent_compose_email(sender=role_user, recipient=role_other_party, content=intent_request_collaboration(include=[intent_self_introduction(), intent_state_job_position()]))) -> $v2 : PROPOSITION
  RETURN $v2
}` — The request's message type, recipient, introduction, role statement, and collaboration request are symbolic. The actual name, position, organization, and recipient identity require NAME_LITERAL or defined entity/role values.
  - `1775955172098` (ThoughtTrace (SCAI-JHU/ThoughtTrace, Parquet column-projected sample, JSON-decoded messages)) [Full] NL: "I need help planning a trip to Italy" → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 : PROPOSITION {
  GENERATE(target=role_user, specification=intent_plan_trip(destination=entity_italy)) -> $v2 : PROPOSITION
  RETURN $v2
}` — The initial broad request is representable. The later one-week, architecture-focused, Chile-origin, one-million-CLP itinerary requires explicit categories for duration, origin, interest, currency, budget, itinerary, and cost estimate; these fit the established categories under N6.
  - `1775612391896` (ThoughtTrace (SCAI-JHU/ThoughtTrace, Parquet column-projected sample, JSON-decoded messages)) [Fail] NL: "hello" → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
CONVO $v1 {
  TURN $v2 SPEAKER=USER {
    UTTER intent_greet()
  }
}` — This is the natural expression for the greeting, but it is invalid under the proposed grammar because Conversation's turn body is step+ while Task's revised step production omits utterance. SPEAKER and USER are also absent from the listed structural-symbol members.
  - `1775448065619` (ThoughtTrace (SCAI-JHU/ThoughtTrace, Parquet column-projected sample, JSON-decoded messages)) [Full] NL: ""I want to start a healthy diet. Can you help me plan a simple meal prep for 3 days that includes breakfast, lunch, and dinner? I prefer high-protein options."" → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 : PROPOSITION {
  GENERATE(target=role_user, specification=intent_plan_meal_prep(duration=3, unit=value_day, meals=[value_breakfast, value_lunch, value_dinner], diet=value_healthy, nutrition=value_high_protein, complexity=value_simple)) -> $v2 : PROPOSITION
  RETURN $v2
}` — The meal-prep request's duration, meal ordering, dietary goal, protein preference, and simplicity are symbolic. The later time-saving refinement would need a controlled value such as value_time_saving rather than prose.
  - `1775760283577` (ThoughtTrace (SCAI-JHU/ThoughtTrace, Parquet column-projected sample, JSON-decoded messages)) [Full] NL: "Hi there, I am curious to learn a little about end-stage heart failure. Talk to me like you would a 5-year-old." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 : PROPOSITION {
  GENERATE(target=role_user, specification=intent_explain(topic=entity_end_stage_heart_failure, audience_age=5, depth=value_brief, register=value_child_friendly)) -> $v2 : PROPOSITION
  RETURN $v2
}` — The medical topic, requested depth, and child-directed register are structurally represented. The later prognosis question would require a prognosis intent, a heart-function percentage attribute, and a treatment condition excluding transplant.
- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=low — Independent translations disagree on substantive plan shape for dev-embodied-2 (two individual pick/place operations versus find-then-loop), on the ALFRED cup task (rinse versus pick-up), and on whether open requests are TASK/GENERATE or CONVO/UTTER. The SymPy translation also ranges from an explicit gap to a detailed invented symbolic ontology. These are not merely surface differences, and the proposal supplies no canonical planning, construct-selection, or handle-naming rule to resolve them.
- **Required changes:**
  - `structural-symbol`: Make structural-symbol genuinely complete: individually list, or give an exact finite lexical membership rule covering, every keyword, operator, delimiter, and type token usable in source. At minimum cover SPEAKER, USER, AGENT, REPLY_TO, REVISES, DECREASES, ANY, ALL, SATISFIES, ==, !=, <, <=, >, >=, ->, =, :, parentheses, braces, brackets, commas, and the lexical delimiters/prefixes used by literals. Add their plain-language meanings and a worked example that contains the full set of source-level token classes.
  - `Task`: Restore utterance to step (or define distinct task_step and turn_step productions) so a CONVO TURN can contain UTTER as required by Utterance and Conversation. Revalidate and correct every Conversation and Utterance worked example after this change.
  - `Types & Lexicon`: Define one canonical opaque-handle allocation rule, such as preorder introduction order $v1, $v2, ..., and require it for entrypoints, tasks, turns, parameters, results, and loop variables. The current HANDLE regex accepts arbitrary semantically suggestive spellings and prevents deterministic encoding.
  - `operation-symbol and intent-symbol`: Add a canonical construct-selection/planning rule that determines when a request is encoded as TASK/GENERATE versus CONVO/UTTER and, for common closed task patterns, determines canonical decomposition rather than allowing equivalent find-and-loop and repeated-action plans.
  - `literal-form`: Add a non-prose, recursively structured CODE/PROGRAM category sufficient to encode source-language tokens, calls, assignment, lists, expected/actual outputs, configuration mappings, commands, and assertions. Define typed symbolic syntax-tree constructs and vocabulary categories rather than a quoted code string; this is required to cover SWE-bench without violating N4.
  - `Types & Lexicon`: Define valid literal lexical forms completely: specify calendar validity for DATE_LITERAL, ranges for TIME_LITERAL, URL grammar/boundaries, PATH normalization, NAME normalization/casing, and whether lexical productions are mutually exclusive. Also define the static type of ontology symbols and constrain ontology_symbol so an operation symbol cannot be passed wherever an arbitrary value is accepted.
  - `Task`: Replace the removal of CONTEXT with a glossary-defined, typed translation-anchor mechanism for resolving relative dates, or require relative dates to be rejected unless a DATE/TIME anchor is supplied. The current proposal cannot deterministically translate 'Friday' while preserving auditable source meaning.
- **Logic issues:** N1 is not realized: structural-symbol is declared closed but omits numerous source tokens used by grammar and examples, and its null membership_rule does not define the omitted tokens.; Conversation is broken because revised Task.step excludes utterance, while turn ::= ... { step+ } and Utterance requires legal placement inside a CONVO TURN body.; The Conversation worked examples use SPEAKER and USER, neither of which is a listed structural-symbol member.; The claim that every ontology symbol matches exactly one category is not enforced by the grammar: ontology_symbol alternatives and attribute-symbol's unrestricted lowercase lexical form can overlap without a global prefix/reserved-word disjointness rule.; HANDLE suffixes are claimed opaque but remain arbitrary source tokens; absent a canonical allocation rule, equivalent expressions can differ in every binding name.; The proposal asserts canonical attribute order but supplies no actual schemas for the shown operation and intent members; the examples depend on unshown signatures.; The Types & Lexicon worked example uses recipient, duration, unit, and tone but does not establish their allowed parent/schema rules in the supplied entries.; No representational category exists for program/configuration syntax and exact multi-token outputs, producing a genuine closed-domain coverage failure for SWE-bench.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N1: violated — The proposal removes STRING and free identifiers, but its own grammar and examples use structural source tokens not defined as structural-symbol members or produced by a precise glossary rule. In particular SPEAKER, USER, AGENT, REPLY_TO, REVISES, DECREASES, ANY, ALL, SATISFIES, punctuation delimiters, arrows, and comparison operators are not traceable through the supplied closed category.
  - N2: violated — The proposal makes substantial progress by adding descriptive vocabulary categories, membership rules, glosses, and examples. Yet the structural category is incomplete, operation and intent signatures are deferred rather than shown for the listed entries, and several word kinds used in examples have no complete glossary entry.
  - N3: partial — The semantic rule correctly requires attributes to be authorized by their parent signature and values to belong to defined categories or allowed types. However, the supplied glossary does not give the required per-intent and per-operation schemas/value sets for the displayed entries, so the rule cannot yet be checked from the glossary.
  - N4: upheld — The revised grammar removes STRING and quoted payloads, and the simulated non-code expressions use only symbolic propositions, typed literals, ontology symbols, and handles. The missing code representation is a coverage failure, not a license to use prose.
  - N5: partial — Atomic DATE, TIME, URL, PATH, NAME, NUMBER, and HANDLE forms are a sound self-contained direction, and the proposal prohibits phrase syntax. But several literal-form boundaries and normalization rules remain underspecified, and incomplete structural-token definition means a reader cannot fully resolve source vocabulary using only the glossary.
  - N6: satisfied — The grammar is largely category-based and the simulations use assumed members of existing entity, role, operation, intent, attribute, and attribute-value categories rather than reshaping syntax around absent individual entries. The missing CODE category is correctly treated as a genuine category gap rather than a missing member.
  - N7: violated — Canonical attribute order is promised but not fully supplied, arbitrary handle spellings remain legal, and independent translations choose materially different plans and top-level constructs for equivalent requests. The broken turn grammar additionally prevents a unique valid parse/encoding for ordinary dialogue.
  - N8: partial — Recursive propositions preserve considerably more structure than prose payloads, including nested persona, tone, quantity, recipient, and ordering constraints. Richness is nevertheless lost for code-bearing tasks, and schemas must be concretely defined to avoid collapsing nuanced requests into broad uninspectable intent symbols.
- **Decision:** needs-rework
- **Documenter summary:** Steering note N1 (closed vocabulary) prompted a comply-position overhaul replacing STRING/free-identifier grammar with typed literals, opaque HANDLEs, and closed ontology categories (structural-symbol, literal-form, operation/entity/role/intent/attribute/attribute-value-symbol) across Types & Lexicon, Action, Utterance, Generate, Task, Bind, Conversation, Flow-If, Iterate, and Check. The Critic ruled needs-rework despite doc hygiene passing: the revised Task grammar's step production silently dropped utterance, making every Conversation/Utterance worked example (e.g. the 'hello' ThoughtTrace item) grammatically invalid, and structural-symbol was declared closed yet omitted tokens like SPEAKER, USER, REVISES, and comparison operators actually used in examples—directly re-violating N1 it was meant to satisfy. The cross-check showing low agreement (divergent plan shapes, arbitrary HANDLE spellings, TASK/GENERATE vs CONVO/UTTER ambiguity) and the SWE-bench code-representation failures further underscored that determinism and coverage were not yet secured, so the proposal was sent back for rework rather than accepted.
- **Cost this sprint:** $1.3613

## Sprint 10 (attempt 2/3) — 2026-09-30

- **Language version:** 9.0.0 → 9.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N1 [hard] Closed vocabulary: every symbol used in a BrainCode expression is defined in the glossary.
- **Shaper proposal (model: openai/gpt-5.6-terra):** This revision closes the remaining N1 gaps by making structural tokens, handle allocation, literal validity, ontology namespaces, task/conversation placement, translation anchors, and code-bearing requests fully glossary-traceable. It also restores valid symbolic conversations and defines canonical construct and plan selection.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — Every source token is now either a member of structural-symbol, admitted by an exact glossary-defined lexical rule, or a member of a disjoint ontology category with a defined schema. Quoted prose and unrestricted identifiers are prohibited; message content, operations, attributes, code evidence, dates, bindings, and control syntax are represented by glossary-defined symbolic forms.
- **Changes:** `Types & Lexicon` (revise, MAJOR), `structural-symbol` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `operation-symbol` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `intent-symbol` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `code-form` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `Task` (revise, MAJOR), `Conversation` (revise, MAJOR), `Utterance` (revise, MAJOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR)
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — This is a promising attempt to replace free strings with typed symbolic categories, and it addresses several prior rework requests conceptually. It cannot be accepted because doc hygiene failed and the resulting glossary does not actually contain the vocabulary that N1 requires; additionally, undefined lexical forms and hexadecimal prose-bearing code text independently violate hard N1, N4, and N5. The Shaper should repair the concrete glossary and grammar gaps, then demonstrate full symbolic SWE-bench encodings without `@q` prose smuggling.
- **Doc hygiene:** FAIL — Doc hygiene FAILED — `structural-symbol` missing an existing entry to revise (not in the glossary's Vocabulary section — use op: add); `operation-symbol` missing an existing entry to revise (not in the glossary's Vocabulary section — use op: add); `intent-symbol` missing an existing entry to revise (not in the glossary's Vocabulary section — use op: add)
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — The proposed namespace categories, proposition form, atomic literals, and CODE trees substantially broaden symbolic coverage for household, web, conversation, and code tasks. However, the documented schemas cover only a few operations and intents, no glossary entries were successfully added for the claimed ontology categories, and the sampled SWE-bench reports still require opaque encoded prose/code payloads or unlisted schemas to preserve their full meaning.
  - expressivity: mixed — Structured requests such as finding, placing, extensions, greetings, and simple generation can now preserve recipients, quantities, and ordering symbolically. The proposal still cannot demonstrably preserve the detailed causal claims, evidence, legal provisions, creative constraints, and exact source-code behavior in several samples without `@q`-encoded text or assumed undocumented ontology entries; that is a material gap under N4.
  - determinism: benefit — Canonical handle allocation, ordered schemas, namespaces, date anchors, and construct-selection guidance improve convergence. Nonetheless, independent translations still disagree on operation decomposition and attribute selection (for example `attr_source` versus `attr_location`, separate sorting versus a sort attribute, TASK/GENERATE versus live find), while the proposed rule only chooses a least sequence when operation postconditions and schemas are already fully defined.
  - interpretability: mixed — Prefixes such as `op_`, `int_`, `ent_`, and `attr_` make token roles easier to inspect, and typed propositions are clearer than prose payloads. Interpretability is harmed by the nonexistent glossary entries, undefined lexical metavariables, external RFC references, and hexadecimal `@q`/`@i` encodings: a reader with only the supplied glossary cannot look up or meaningfully recover many source tokens.
  - improvement: mixed — The symbolic task structures would help a weaker executor plan the simple closed tasks and retain conversation correction structure. For complex reports, stories, contracts, and technical issues, the language either collapses important content into coarse invented intent labels or hides it in hexadecimal code text, which provides little actionable decomposition beyond the raw request.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `dev-embodied-2` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Put both pillows from the sofa onto the armchair, one at a time." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v2 {
ACTION op_find(attr_target=ent_pillow,attr_source=ent_sofa,attr_quantity=2)->$v3:LIST[REF[ENTITY]]
FOR EACH $v4 IN $v3 { ACTION op_place(attr_target=$v4,attr_destination=ent_armchair) }
}` — operation-symbol and the canonical plural-find-then-loop rule help. The required `op_find` and `op_place` schemas are represented by the proposed category, although only the former is concretely listed.
  - `dev-embodied-1` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Rinse the mug in the sink, then put it in the coffee maker." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v2 {
ACTION op_rinse(attr_target=ent_mug,attr_destination=ent_sink)->$v3:REF[ENTITY]
ACTION op_place(attr_target=$v3,attr_destination=ent_coffee_maker)
}` — The handles preserve coreference and ordering. `op_rinse` must be an assumed complete-glossary member because it is not among the actual proposed operation members.
  - `86afd67c-1bff-455c-baa7-e18dcb64b0f3` (Mind2Web) [Full] NL: "Find comedy tv shows on netflix sorted by audience score." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v2:LIST[REF[ENTITY]] {

ACTION op_sort(attr_target=$v3,attr_ordering=val_audience_score_desc)->$v4:LIST[REF[ENTITY]]
RETURN $v4
}` — The task is structurally expressible, but the proposed canonical planning rule does not decide whether sorting is a separate `op_sort` or an `op_find` attribute; independent translations exhibit both readings.
  - `bf469f30-6628-4017-b963-672645d7feab` (Mind2Web) [Full] NL: "Find the highest rated dog collar under 10 dollar." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1

RETURN $v3

- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=low — The providers agree on the broad find-then-place pattern for pillows, but Gemini uses `attr_location` where Anthropic uses `attr_source`. For Netflix, Anthropic uses find then sort whereas Gemini embeds sorting in find; for travel recommendations, Anthropic chooses GENERATE while Gemini chooses ACTION op_find; and the two PATH translations choose different intent decompositions and target categories. These are substantive encoding alternatives, not merely surface spelling differences.
- **Required changes:**
  - `doc hygiene (scripted — blocks acceptance)`: Doc hygiene FAILED — `structural-symbol` missing an existing entry to revise (not in the glossary's Vocabulary section — use op: add); `operation-symbol` missing an existing entry to revise (not in the glossary's Vocabulary section — use op: add); `intent-symbol` missing an existing entry to revise (not in the glossary's Vocabulary section — use op: add)
  - `basis`: Fix the doc-hygiene failure before resubmission: change `structural-symbol`, `operation-symbol`, and `intent-symbol` from `op: revise` to `op: add`, place them in the glossary Vocabulary section, and ensure their rendered glossary entries contain their membership rule, members, gloss, and example.
  - `Types & Lexicon`: Close every lexical hole with glossary-defined rules: define POSITIVE_INTEGER, YYYY, MM, DD, HH, SS, URL_BODY, PATH_BODY, hexadecimal scalar sequences, whitespace/comments, newline handling, and token separation. Do not rely on an unincorporated RFC or an English-language external standard as the definition of an accepted source token.
  - `structural-symbol`: List or rule-define every token that code-form and all grammar productions use, including `callee`, `args`, `target`, `value`, `items`, `entries`, `tokens`, `expected`, and `actual`, or change code-form to use namespace-valid attribute symbols such as `attr_callee` and give each a complete schema.
  - `code-form`: Replace `@q` and unrestricted `code_text` with a constrained code-token/category grammar that distinguishes identifiers, operators, delimiters, numeric literals, string literals, comments, and language-specific syntax. Prohibit using hexadecimal encoding to carry natural-language reports or arbitrary prose, and reconcile the stated `code_node` grammar with examples such as `code_text(@q...)` and `code_id(@i...)`.
  - `ontology vocabulary`: Actually add glossary categories for entity-symbol, role-symbol, value-symbol, and attribute-symbol, with member/schema rules, examples, disjointness, synonym normalization, and each attribute's admissible value types or value set. Generic `VALUE` is insufficient for N3; define the permissible values for attributes such as tone, unit, ordering, currency, state, goal, and format.
  - `operation-symbol and intent-symbol`: Supply canonical schemas and selection rules for the sampled patterns that currently have multiple valid encodings: source/location/platform, filters versus finder attributes, sort-plus-extract versus ordered find, acquisition/slicing paths, live search versus generation, and revision of a generated document. The rule must choose one canonical plan, not merely prefer a shortest plan under unspecified postconditions.
  - `Task and Conversation`: Correct the document-level handle example and semantics so ENTRYPOINT unambiguously references exactly one declared task/conversation handle, and state whether declarations named by handles are introductions under the allocation preorder. Then revalidate all examples against that rule.
  - `code-form and SWE-bench coverage`: Add symbolic bug-report, reproduction, expected-behavior, actual-behavior, regression-test, and code-change schemas sufficient to represent the factual content of SWE-bench issues without opaque text. Demonstrate this with full symbolic encodings of both sampled SWE-bench items.
- **Logic issues:** Doc hygiene failed because three claimed vocabulary revisions target glossary entries that do not exist; therefore the categories that are supposed to establish N1 are not added at all.; The proposed grammar references undefined lexical nonterminals including POSITIVE_INTEGER, YYYY, MM, DD, HH, SS, URL_BODY, and PATH_BODY, so it does not define every token it admits.; The `code-form` grammar uses raw argument labels such as `callee`, `args`, `target`, `value`, `items`, `entries`, `tokens`, `expected`, and `actual`; none are structural symbols or namespace-valid attribute symbols.; The code-form membership rule says `code_id` and `code_text` are code nodes but the worked examples call them with parentheses, while the grammar gives neither call syntax nor a production for those forms.; `@q` can encode an arbitrary natural-language sentence exactly, only hidden as hexadecimal code points. Calling decoded text data does not prevent it from being an N4-prohibited prose payload.; The proposal relies on a hypothetical complete glossary for most symbols used in its own examples (`int_plan_trip`, `attr_destination`, `ent_italy`, and many more), but does not add the referenced entity, role, value, or attribute categories to the actual glossary.; The canonical-operation rule is underdetermined because operation postconditions, identity behavior, and schemas are absent for the many operations required by the samples; independent provider disagreement confirms this is not a cosmetic issue.; The worked Types & Lexicon example says 'On 10 June 2025' but emits ANCHOR `@d2025-06-03`; this changes stated source information and the anchor has no explained relation to the date in the request.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N1: violated — The hard closed-vocabulary requirement is not met: the vocabulary additions failed hygiene and therefore do not exist in the glossary, multiple lexical productions use undefined metavariables, and code-form introduces unglossed raw labels. A reader cannot trace every token in the simulations to the supplied glossary or a fully defined glossary rule.
  - N2: violated — The actual glossary remains one-entry-per-construct and lacks the proposed descriptive Vocabulary section. Entity, role, value, and attribute categories are asserted in grammar but are neither added nor described with members, rules, and worked examples.
  - N3: violated — Schemas broadly accept `VALUE`, but individual attributes do not list their allowed value sets or an exact derivation rule. This permits undefined values and does not make attribute-value choice traceable.
  - N4: violated — `code_text(@q...)` permits arbitrary decoded natural-language text to be embedded in an expression, and the proposal supplies no symbolic schema capable of replacing detailed bug reports and other prose substance. Hexadecimal wrapping does not make a sentence non-prose for this constraint.
  - N5: violated — The URL rule delegates acceptance to RFC-3986 rather than defining it in the language/glossary, and `@q` admits arbitrary codepoint sequences whose interpretation can depend on external language/code syntax. The language is therefore not self-contained in the required sense.
  - N6: partial — The revised grammar is mostly phrased over categories and the simulations use category-compatible assumed symbols. However, the missing actual ontology categories and missing schemas mean this is an assertion rather than a usable complete-glossary design.
  - N7: partial — Preorder handles, schema ordering, and some construct-selection rules are progress. Provider disagreement on source/location, sorting, finder versus generation, and intent decomposition shows that one canonical encoding per meaning has not been realized.
  - N8: partial — Recursive propositions and code trees aim to preserve richness, but the present schemas are too sparse for detailed software, legal, and creative requests. Conversely, `@q` preserves richness only by violating the closed symbolic-content constraints.
- **Decision:** needs-rework
- **Documenter summary:** For steering note N1 (closed vocabulary), the Shaper proposed complying by adding namespaced ontology categories, canonical handle allocation, atomic literal rules, a recursive CODE-form for code evidence, and construct-selection rules for TASK/CONVO/GENERATE/UTTER; the Critic found the direction promising but ruled needs-rework, decisively because doc hygiene failed (structural-symbol, operation-symbol, and intent-symbol were marked 'revise' for glossary entries that don't yet exist, so the vocabulary N1 requires was never actually added) and because undefined lexical metavariables plus hexadecimal `@q`/`@i` code-text encoding let arbitrary prose slip through, independently violating hard constraints N1, N4, and N5. The Critic's cross-provider simulations showing low agreement on operation/attribute decomposition (e.g., attr_source vs attr_location, sort-as-operation vs sort-as-attribute) further underscored that canonical vocabulary and planning rules remain underspecified.
- **Cost this sprint:** $1.3670

## Sprint 11 (attempt 1/3) — 2026-09-30

- **Language version:** 9.0.0 → 9.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N1 [hard] Closed vocabulary: every symbol used in a BrainCode expression is defined in the glossary.
- **Shaper proposal (model: gemini/gemini-3.1-pro-preview):** Replaces unrestricted identifiers and strings with a complete lexical system, typed ontology categories, and atomic literals. Defines every token and structural symbol, eliminating prose payloads and hexadecimal encoding in favor of structured code nodes and exact schemas.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — By defining every lexical nonterminal, adding all required ontology categories to the glossary with explicit schemas, and replacing code text with structured token trees, BrainCode achieves a genuinely closed, prose-free vocabulary.
- **Changes:** `Types & Lexicon` (revise, MAJOR), `structural-symbol` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `literal-form` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `code-form` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `entity-symbol` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `role-symbol` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `value-symbol` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `attribute-symbol` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `operation-symbol` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `intent-symbol` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `Utterance` (revise, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `Task` (revise, MAJOR), `Bind` (revise, MAJOR), `Conversation` (revise, MAJOR), `Flow-If` (revise, MAJOR), `Iterate` (revise, MAJOR), `Check` (revise, MAJOR)
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — The attempt addresses several prior structural requests and passes doc hygiene, but it cannot be accepted because hard notes N1 through N5 and N7 remain violated. In particular, prefix-only namespace rules and unrestricted comments prevent a genuinely closed, prose-free vocabulary, while generic VALUE and incomplete schemas leave encoding non-canonical. The core direction is sound, so the appropriate decision is needs-rework rather than rejection.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — Typed entities, operations, intents, code nodes, and atomic literals improve coverage for simple placement, follow-up, and trip-planning tasks. However, many sampled actionable cores still have no schema: cart addition, acquisition/pick-up, rich code syntax and regressions, recipes, medical explanations, CRUD design advice, meal-prep plans, and detailed generated writing. Under N4, these cannot fall back to prose, so most open requests and several closed tasks fail outright.
  - expressivity: mixed — The proposal preserves some formerly opaque details, including quantities, dates, roles, structured calls, and a simple conversational correction. But it loses or cannot encode substantive requirements in the SWE-bench reports, compatibility constraints, high-review filtering, response duration, tone/persona composition, comedy-script constraints, sermon structure, and code output notation. `VALUE` is too broad and attributes do not enumerate allowed value sets, so the claimed schemas do not preserve all distinctions deterministically.
  - determinism: harm — Opaque canonical handles are an improvement, but operation and intent schemas are mostly absent or incomplete. Independent translations already disagree on whether to include the stated two-pillow quantity, on when to add cart items, on whether bug reports are Tasks or Conversations, and on whether a recipe request is generation or dialogue. `op_find` has an unspecified REF-versus-LIST result selection, generic VALUE admits arbitrary category members, and no canonical attribute order is supplied for most intent schemas.
  - interpretability: mixed — Namespace prefixes and typed literals make simple expressions easier to inspect than unrestricted English identifiers and strings. Conversely, the proposal makes validity depend on an asserted but unavailable 'complete glossary,' uses external RFC-3986 as part of URL validity, permits arbitrary comment text, and leaves many category membership rules as prefix tests rather than glossary lookup. A reader cannot recover the intended value set or operation/intent schema from the supplied glossary entries.
  - improvement: mixed — For simple procedural requests such as locating pillows and placing them sequentially, the structure would help a weaker model execute the plan. For the majority of sampled tasks, the language either has no valid expression or reduces the request to a coarse intent, so it cannot reliably improve planning over the original request.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `dev-embodied-2` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Put both pillows from the sofa onto the armchair, one at a time." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
  ACTION op_find(attr_target=ent_pillow, attr_source=ent_sofa, attr_quantity=2) -> $v2:LIST[REF[ENTITY]]
  FOR EACH $v3 IN $v2 {
    ACTION op_place(attr_target=$v3, attr_destination=ent_armchair)
  }
}` — The entity, quantity, find, place, and sequential iteration vocabulary helps. `op_find` still does not specify why this result must be LIST rather than REF, so the expression is not canonically determined.
  - `dev-email-2` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "If my manager hasn't replied by Friday, send a polite follow-up; otherwise do nothing." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 ANCHOR @d2026-09-30 {
  ACTION op_check_replied(attr_target=role_manager, attr_deadline=@d2026-10-02) -> $v2:BOOL
  IF NOT $v2 THEN {
    ACTION op_send_email(attr_recipient=role_manager, attr_content=int_follow_up(attr_tone=val_polite))
  }
}` — The new symbolic content and anchor avoid prose. This assumes Friday relative to the supplied current date; the global translator anchor policy needs a normative source for the translation date.
  - `e344b89e-767a-4618-ba82-4b81cdcba280` (Mind2Web) [Fail] NL: "add a motherboard under $200 and a compatible processor at any price to the shopping cart." → `<no valid BrainCode expression>` — No `op_add_to_cart` exists. `attr_requirement` only accepts VALUE, so it cannot express compatibility with the selected motherboard reference; no compatibility relation schema exists.
  - `f118238f-ef8f-4b63-9159-a81e981ef46e` (Mind2Web) [Fail] NL: "Add decorative LED Candles to the cart." → `<no valid BrainCode expression>` — Even assuming `ent_decorative_led_candles` belongs to the complete entity glossary, no cart-add operation or cart destination relation is defined.
  - `trial_T20190906_214148_552057#1` (ALFRED) [Fail] NL: "get a cup ready for coffee" → `<no valid BrainCode expression>` — The trajectory requires retrieving a cup from the fridge, heating it in a microwave, removing it, and placing it under the coffee-maker spout. The proposal has no acquire/pick-up operation, no remove operation, and no schema defining the compound state 'ready for coffee.'
  - `trial_T20190908_102045_139402#1` (ALFRED) [Full] NL: "To put the toilet paper away." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
  ACTION op_find(attr_target=ent_toilet_paper, attr_source=ent_toilet) -> $v2:REF[ENTITY]
  ACTION op_place(attr_target=$v2, attr_destination=ent_wall_holder)
}` — Assumes the complete entity category includes `ent_toilet_paper`, `ent_toilet`, and `ent_wall_holder`, as N6 directs. The source location and destination are represented symbolically.
  - `sympy__sympy-15345` (SWE-bench_Verified) [Fail] NL: "mathematica_code gives wrong output with Max: symbols('x'); mathematica_code(Max(x,2)) should produce Max[x,2] but produces Max(2, x)." → `<no valid BrainCode expression>` — `code_call` represents both expected `Max[x,2]` and actual `Max(2,x)` only as generic calls; it has no bracket-call versus parenthesis-call syntax, ordered source-file change model, or test/regression schema. Therefore the defining defect is lost.
  - `django__django-15098` (SWE-bench_Verified) [Fail] NL: "Django i18n_patterns fails for language locales containing both script and region, such as en-Latn-US; modify django/utils/translation/trans_real.py so these locale paths return 200 rather than 404." → `<no valid BrainCode expression>` — The issue needs imports, assignments, list literals, string literals containing locale tags and URLs, HTTP-status assertions, multiple reproduction steps, expected/actual responses, and a code patch/test. The seven generic code nodes and `attr_reproduction: CODE` cannot encode this report.
  - `c5957` (PRISM) [Fail] NL: "Please provide a recipe for high protein flatbread; change it to one with high reviews; how much protein is in these recipes; how long will this take me to make?" → `<no valid BrainCode expression>` — This is a multi-turn revision and follow-up. There is no recipe intent, ingredient/recipe schema, review-score attribute, nutrition/protein measurement schema, preparation-time schema, or conversational reference to prior generated recipes.
  - `c436` (PRISM) [Fail] NL: "What places to visit in Bilbao? Is Guggenheim Museum open everyday? What can I do if it rains? Suggest picturesque towns nearby." → `<no valid BrainCode expression>` — `int_recommend_places(attr_location=ent_bilbao)` could encode only an initial coarse recommendation. It cannot encode museum opening status, rain contingency, nearby relation, picturesque criterion, or the linked multi-turn sequence.
  - `c996` (PRISM) [Fail] NL: "What is your take on love? Do you think you will fall in love?" → `<no valid BrainCode expression>` — No intent/schema represents an opinion request, an AI-self-capability question, or the follow-up relationship. N4 disallows retaining the substantive questions as prose.
  - `c4939` (PRISM) [Fail] NL: "Is marriage important? How did marriage come to the world?" → `<no valid BrainCode expression>` — No opinion, historical-origin, or social-institution explanation intent/schema exists. Encoding this merely as `int_learn_about(attr_topic=ent_marriage)` would lose both distinct questions.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-129421` (PATHs) [Fail] NL: "Explain in depth, through a composite persona Mark, how linear algebra lets ChatGPT-4 transform text into numbers and numbers into answers, with concrete numerical examples and a casual conversational American style plus 18th-century mathematical illustration." → `<no valid BrainCode expression>` — There are no categories/schemas for a composite persona, expertise roles, style composition, depth, the bidirectional transformer explanation, equations, or numerical examples. The provided `int_learn_about` is far too coarse.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-148973` (PATHs) [Fail] NL: "Write a hilarious 17+ comedy script in which the Scooby gang and Scrappy-Doo react to badly translated sentences, roast errors, and use occasional funny quotes." → `<no valid BrainCode expression>` — No script-generation intent, character/cast relation, comedy register, age-rating constraint, translation-error subject, or dialogue-scene schema is defined. N4 prevents preserving the requested script substance as a text payload.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-142592` (PATHs) [Fail] NL: "Please write steps for homiletics and engaging Christian sermons." → `<no valid BrainCode expression>` — Neither a sermon/homiletics artifact schema nor ordered instructional-step, audience-engagement, religious-context, and example relations are present.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-22638` (PATHs) [Fail] NL: "Write an email introducing yourself and your job position to another party and asking for collaborations; then provide the recipient's next response." → `<no valid BrainCode expression>` — `op_send_email` describes an external sending action, not generation of a two-party email exchange. There is no self/job-position/organization/collaboration schema or response-turn generation intent.
  - `1775955172098` (ThoughtTrace) [Full] NL: "I need help planning a trip to Italy." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1:PROPOSITION {
  GENERATE(attr_target=val_trip_plan, attr_specification=int_plan_trip(attr_destination=ent_italy)) -> $v2:PROPOSITION
  RETURN $v2
}` — The new planning intent and destination attribute cover the initial request, assuming `val_trip_plan` is an entry in the complete value glossary under N6. Its exact intent schema and required/optional attribute order are not actually supplied.
  - `1775612391896` (ThoughtTrace) [Fail] NL: "For a React and Node.js CRUD job-hunt tracker, should Update use a new page or modal? I want a balance of easy implementation and daily-dashboard UX." → `<no valid BrainCode expression>` — No technical-design-advice, CRUD, React, Node.js, modal/page alternative, comparison criterion, or recommendation schema exists.
  - `1775448065619` (ThoughtTrace) [Fail] NL: "Plan a simple three-day high-protein meal prep with breakfast, lunch, and dinner, optimized to save time and eat better." → `<no valid BrainCode expression>` — No meal-plan, meal/course, dietary nutrient, duration, optimization, shopping-list, preparation-step, or ingredient quantity schema exists.
  - `1775760283577` (ThoughtTrace) [Fail] NL: "Explain end-stage heart failure like to a five-year-old, including how long someone with 20% heart function may live without a transplant." → `<no valid BrainCode expression>` — No medical-explanation, age-appropriate register, prognosis, probability/range, heart-function percentage, or transplant-condition schema exists. `int_learn_about` would lose the essential question and audience constraint.
- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=low — Anthropic and Gemini disagree on including quantity for the pillows, on cart-addition order, on whether the ALFRED cup task is representable, on Task/GENERATE versus CONVO/UTTER for recipe requests, and materially on the SWE-bench encoding. The supplied SWE expressions are themselves invalid against the proposed grammar in places: `attr_reproduction` receives a list despite its declared CODE type, and Gemini uses malformed spaced NAME literals. These disagreements confirm missing canonical schemas rather than harmless surface variation.
- **Required changes:**
  - `Types & Lexicon`: Remove COMMENT from BrainCode source or constrain comments so they cannot contain arbitrary natural-language text; N4 forbids the current `COMMENT ::= "--" [^\n]*` prose channel. Replace the URL semantic dependency on RFC-3986 with a complete in-spec lexical and validity grammar, and define all character-set/complement and newline semantics used by lexical productions.
  - `role-symbol, value-symbol, attribute-symbol, operation-symbol, intent-symbol`: Change every open-category membership rule from prefix-only acceptance to: valid exactly when a corresponding member entry exists in the complete glossary. A spelling such as `role_unlisted` or `int_unlisted` must not be a legal expression token merely because it matches a regex.
  - `attribute-symbol and value-symbol`: Implement N3: for every attribute, list its allowed value symbols/categories and any compositional rule, not merely the broad type VALUE. In particular define closed/permitted sets for tone, unit, ordering, currency, state, quality, goal, format, requirements, and failure values; reject values from an unrelated VALUE subcategory.
  - `operation-symbol and intent-symbol`: Give every operation and intent an actual complete ordered schema: required and optional attributes, their precise types/category restrictions, result type selection, duplicate/default behavior, and canonical selection/decomposition rules. Add a rule resolving `op_find`'s REF versus LIST result and define canonical ordering for all intent attributes.
  - `operation-symbol`: Add schemas required by the closed samples: `op_add_to_cart`; acquisition/pick-up and removal/retrieval operations; compatible-with/reference constraints for product selection; and the web/household navigation or state transitions needed to make those actions executable. Specify when a source-location find is sufficient versus acquisition is required.
  - `code-form and SWE-bench coverage`: Replace the seven-node generic code subset with a typed, closed code AST sufficient for sampled issue facts: ordered statement sequences, imports, member/attribute access, indexing/bracket calls versus parenthesized calls, lists/tuples/dictionaries, typed string atoms, URLs/status assertions, file edits, tests, expected/actual behavior, and regression criteria. Give `int_bug_report` a complete schema whose reproduction field accepts the appropriate typed code sequence. Demonstrate full valid symbolic encodings for both sampled SWE-bench reports.
  - `intent-symbol and descriptive vocabulary`: Add compositional symbolic schemas/categories for the missing open-task content rather than treating it as opaque prose: recipes and nutrient/review/time constraints; recommendations with proximity/conditions; opinions and historical explanations; technical explanation and persona/register composition; scripts, cast, scene, humor and rating; sermons and ordered instructional guidance; email-generation and generated reply relations; CRUD design alternatives; meal plans; and medical explanations/prognosis with audience-age constraints.
  - `Conversation and Generate`: Define a canonical representation for conversational requests versus requested generated artifacts, plus references to previously generated artifacts and follow-up questions. It must encode c5957's revision, nutrition question, and time question without prose, and remove the current Task-versus-CONVO ambiguity shown by the independent translations.
  - `basis`: Revalidate every worked example against the revised grammar and schemas. In particular, ensure generated target symbols have a declared value entry, operation result types match their schemas, and no example relies on an intent attribute (`attr_tone` on `int_follow_up`, `attr_destination` on `int_plan_trip`) that its intent schema has not defined.
- **Logic issues:** N1 is still violated because role, value, attribute, operation, and intent membership rules accept any namespace-shaped token without requiring a corresponding glossary entry.; N4 is violated by arbitrary COMMENT text, which is a direct natural-language channel inside a source expression.; N3 is violated because attributes typed VALUE accept the undifferentiated open value category; the proposal does not list admissible values or a derivation rule per attribute.; The URL semantic requirement delegates validity to RFC-3986, contradicting N5's requirement that reading BrainCode not depend on an external natural-language standard.; `op_find` promises `LIST[REF[ENTITY]] or REF[ENTITY]` without a type-selection rule, leaving valid encodings and subsequent typechecking indeterminate.; Most intent entries name a meaning but provide no schema at all, despite the grammar allowing arbitrary listed attributes. This creates both under-expression and multiple competing encodings.; The code-form example uses `int_bug_report` as a code-change payload, but bug-report schemas do not define that as a patch/change representation; generic code_call also cannot distinguish the critical Mathematica syntactic forms.; The proposed lexical grammar says ontology categories are namespace-disjoint, but the structural glossary aggregates multiple punctuation tokens as single members and does not define a per-token lookup rule for those grouped entries.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N1: violated — The proposal makes substantial progress by adding namespaces, literal forms, and structural symbols, but prefix-only membership rules for role/value/attribute/operation/intent symbols permit tokens absent from the glossary. A direct arbitrary-prose COMMENT production is also legal. Therefore a reader cannot trace every source token to a glossary member or glossary-defined closed production.
  - N2: violated — Descriptive categories were added, which is progress, but they do not provide a complete lexicon in the required sense: broad prefix rules stand in for actual members, many necessary categories are absent, and most operation/intent entries lack complete schemas and examples of their descriptive relations.
  - N3: violated — The attribute entries state coarse static types such as VALUE, but they do not give each attribute its permissible value set or a precise derivation rule. Consequently `val_comedy` is formally admissible as a tone, unit, or currency despite not being meaningful.
  - N4: violated — The language removes STRING and quoted text, but unrestricted comment text remains legal in source. In addition, most substantive sampled requests cannot be symbolically encoded, demonstrating that the replacement ontology is not yet sufficient for the no-prose requirement.
  - N5: violated — URL validity is defined by reference to RFC-3986 rather than fully by BrainCode's own specification. Prefix-only ontology admission also leaves symbol meaning dependent on an alleged external complete glossary entry that need not exist.
  - N6: partial — The syntax is now mostly written over categories and simulations appropriately assume missing entities such as toilet paper can belong to a complete entity glossary. However, many failures are missing categories/schemas, not merely missing members, and the categories are not closed by actual glossary membership.
  - N7: violated — The handle rule is helpful, but canonical encoding is not achieved: `op_find` result cardinality is unspecified, intent schemas and argument order are absent, and independent translations diverge substantially on plan shape and construct selection.
  - N8: partial — Structured literals and code nodes attempt to retain richness, but the current ontology is too coarse for the sampled open requests and too weak for both SWE reports. The necessary response is richer compositional symbolic schemas, not restoration of prose payloads.
- **Decision:** needs-rework
- **Documenter summary:** Steering sprint on N1 (closed vocabulary): the Shaper proposed a major overhaul replacing free identifiers/strings with typed ontology categories (entity/role/value/attribute/operation/intent symbols), atomic literals, and structured code nodes, fully complying with the note. The Critic found the direction sound but ruled needs-rework because membership rules only checked namespace prefixes rather than actual glossary entries, VALUE was too broad to preserve N3 distinctions, arbitrary-text COMMENT still violated N4, URL validity relied on external RFC-3986 (violating N5), and op_find's result type and most operation/intent schemas remained underspecified, causing most sampled real-world tasks (cart actions, SWE-bench reports, recipes, meal plans, medical/persona requests) to have no valid encoding. The decisive evidence was the batch of failed simulations (e.g. shopping-cart, SWE-bench django/sympy, PRISM/PATHs open requests) showing the closed vocabulary is still too sparse and its membership/schema rules too loose to satisfy N1-N5 and N7 together.
- **Cost this sprint:** $1.4718

## Sprint 12 (attempt 1/3) — 2026-09-30

- **Shaper failed to produce a usable proposal:** the proposal was cut off at the output limit, so its trailing changes were lost — resubmit a smaller, complete set (at most 12 changes, concise fields)
- **Decision:** needs-rework (no proposal to assess)

## Sprint 13 (attempt 1/3) — 2026-09-30

- **Language version:** 9.0.0 → 9.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N1 [hard] Closed vocabulary: every symbol used in a BrainCode expression is defined in the glossary.
- **Shaper proposal (model: openai/gpt-5.6-terra):** This revision replaces free identifiers, strings, comments, and open prefix acceptance with glossary-governed handles, atomic literals, ontology entries, and typed symbolic code trees. It also gives Actions, Utterances, Generation, Tasks, and Conversations one canonical symbolic representation for executable work, generated artifacts, revisions, and follow-up questions.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — All expression tokens are either structural symbols defined by Types & Lexicon, canonical handles, exact atomic literals, or symbols whose corresponding complete glossary entry defines their category, schema, type, and canonical attribute order. No quoted prose, unrestricted identifier, comment channel, or prefix-only namespace rule remains.
- **Changes:** `Types & Lexicon` (revise, MAJOR), `structural-symbol` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `ontology-symbol` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `Utterance` (revise, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `Task` (revise, MAJOR), `Bind` (revise, MAJOR), `Iterate` (revise, MAJOR), `Check` (revise, MAJOR), `Conversation` (revise, MAJOR), `Flow-If` (revise, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR)
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — This is a sound and substantial move toward the closed symbolic vocabulary required by N1: it eliminates the largest free-text and free-identifier channels while adding typed literals, handles, exact-membership rules, and structured values. It cannot be accepted because two incompatible Generate revisions leave the grammar ambiguous, multiple worked examples violate their own schemas, and most claimed closed vocabulary is only promised rather than supplied in usable category schemas. Fixing those concrete issues would preserve the strong N1 progress while materially improving determinism and interpretability.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — The complete-glossary contract, typed propositions, symbolic code trees, and new household/cart operations materially improve coverage without prose for the sampled domains. However, the explicitly supplied operation and intent schemas remain far narrower than the current Action vocabulary, and several required domains are only asserted to exist in an unspecified future complete glossary; the revised grammar does not yet provide a complete executable basis for them.
  - expressivity: benefit — The proposal can structurally encode quantities, ordering, compatibility, date constraints, revisions, follow-up questions, code-tree facts, generated artifacts, and many conversational constraints that were formerly hidden in strings. It still loses or leaves unspecified important distinctions such as exact artifact content, persona composition, source-code literal values, medical qualification, and many detailed travel, meal, and sermon constraints because the corresponding schemas are not actually defined.
  - determinism: mixed — Opaque canonical handles, canonical field order, exact glossary membership, and explicit Check precedence are strong improvements. But the duplicate, materially different Generate revisions conflict; schemas do not consistently define selection/decomposition rules; and independent translations diverged substantially on possession handling, price constraints, bug-report attributes, and whether an open request is TASK/CONVO/GENERATE.
  - interpretability: mixed — Replacing arbitrary identifiers and prose payloads with typed handles and named ontology symbols improves inspectability. The basis is nevertheless difficult to interpret because the proposal repeatedly delegates decisive schemas and vocabulary to an unshown “complete glossary,” several symbols used in examples lack supplied entries, and two incompatible Generate definitions appear in one change set.
  - improvement: benefit — For executable tasks, explicit data flow, typed references, operation schemas, structured conditions, and symbolic conversational links would help a weaker agent plan more reliably than raw requests. The benefit is limited for complex open and software tasks until the promised schemas are made concrete enough to determine what action or artifact structure is required.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `dev-embodied-2` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Put both pillows from the sofa onto the armchair, one at a time." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
  ACTION op_find(attr_target=ent_pillow,attr_source=ent_sofa,attr_quantity=2) -> $v2:LIST[REF[ENTITY]]
  FOR EACH $v3 IN $v2 { ACTION op_pick_up(attr_target=$v3) -> $v4:REF[ENTITY] ACTION op_place(attr_target=$v4,attr_destination=ent_armchair) }
}` — The revised op_find quantity rule, loop handles, op_pick_up, and op_place express the actionable core. The proposal's own Iterate example omits op_pick_up, although op_place requires held possession; the independent providers disagreed on this point.
  - `dev-email-2` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "If my manager hasn't replied by Friday, send a polite follow-up; otherwise do nothing." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 ANCHOR @d2026-09-30 {
  ACTION op_check_replied(attr_target=role_manager,attr_deadline=@d2026-10-02) -> $v2:BOOL
  IF NOT $v2 THEN { ACTION op_send_email(attr_recipient=role_manager,attr_content=int_follow_up(attr_tone=val_polite)) }
}` — Task, Check, Flow-If, atomic DATE_LITERAL, and int_follow_up capture the condition and ordering. This is one of the strongest fully symbolic encodings in the proposal.
  - `e344b89e-767a-4618-ba82-4b81cdcba280` (Mind2Web (osunlp/Mind2Web, train, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Full] NL: "add a motherboard under $200 and a compatible processor at any price to the shopping cart." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
  ACTION op_find(attr_target=ent_motherboard,attr_max_price=200,attr_currency=val_usd,attr_quantity=1) -> $v2:REF[ENTITY]
  ACTION op_add_to_cart(attr_target=$v2)
  ACTION op_find(attr_target=ent_processor,attr_requirement=int_compatible_with(attr_reference=$v2),attr_quantity=1) -> $v3:REF[ENTITY]
  ACTION op_add_to_cart(attr_target=$v3)
}` — The new op_find price fields, compatibility proposition, and cart operation address this sample. Anthropic instead found the price predicate missing, while Gemini used a different price intent and a different compatibility field name, demonstrating that the schema must be canonicalized and fully published.
  - `f118238f-ef8f-4b63-9159-a81e981ef46e` (Mind2Web (osunlp/Mind2Web, train, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Full] NL: "Add decorative LED Candles to the cart." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
  ACTION op_find(attr_target=ent_decorative_led_candle,attr_quantity=1) -> $v2:REF[ENTITY]
  ACTION op_add_to_cart(attr_target=$v2)
}` — Assumes ent_decorative_led_candle is an exact ENTITY entry in the complete glossary, as N6 directs. The operation sequence is supported by op_find and op_add_to_cart.
  - `trial_T20190906_214148_552057#1` (ALFRED (json_2.1.0, train)) [Full] NL: "get a cup ready for coffee" → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
  ACTION op_find(attr_target=ent_cup,attr_source=ent_fridge,attr_quantity=1) -> $v2:REF[ENTITY]
  ACTION op_pick_up(attr_target=$v2) -> $v3:REF[ENTITY]
  ACTION op_heat(attr_target=$v3,attr_destination=ent_microwave) -> $v4:REF[ENTITY]
  ACTION op_remove(attr_target=$v4,attr_source=ent_microwave) -> $v5:REF[ENTITY]
  ACTION op_place(attr_target=$v5,attr_destination=ent_coffee_maker_spout)
}` — The trajectory disambiguates the otherwise underspecified request. The new acquisition, heat, removal, and place operations help. Gemini instead selected an invented op_prepare, showing a remaining canonical-decomposition problem for goal-oriented household requests.
  - `trial_T20190908_102045_139402#1` (ALFRED (json_2.1.0, train)) [Full] NL: "To put the toilet paper away." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
  ACTION op_find(attr_target=ent_toilet_paper,attr_source=ent_toilet,attr_quantity=1) -> $v2:REF[ENTITY]
  ACTION op_pick_up(attr_target=$v2) -> $v3:REF[ENTITY]
  ACTION op_place(attr_target=$v3,attr_destination=ent_toilet_paper_holder)
}` — The trajectory supplies the source and destination that the terse natural-language request omits. ENTITY entries are assumed to exist under N6.
  - `sympy__sympy-15345` (SWE-bench_Verified (princeton-nlp/SWE-bench_Verified, test, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Partial] NL: "mathematica_code gives wrong output with Max: x = symbols('x'); mathematica_code(Max(x,2)) should produce Max[x,2] rather than Max(2, x)." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
  ACTION op_modify_code(attr_path=@psympy/printing/mathematica.py,attr_change=code_sequence(attr_items=[code_atom_fix_mathematica_max_bracket_rendering]),attr_regression=code_sequence(attr_items=[code_assign(attr_target=code_atom_x,attr_value=code_call_paren(attr_callee=code_atom_symbols,attr_args=[code_atom_string_x])),code_call_paren(attr_callee=code_atom_mathematica_code,attr_args=[code_call_paren(attr_callee=code_atom_Max,attr_args=[code_atom_x,code_atom_number_2])]),code_atom_expect_mathematica_max_bracket_output]))
}` — The code AST permits an entirely symbolic sketch only by assuming opaque exact code_atom entries for source tokens, expected output, and the requested edit. No supplied glossary schema defines a canonical bug-report-to-edit decomposition, test assertion node, string-code atom form, or an exact code-edit representation sufficient to recover the issue.
  - `django__django-15098` (SWE-bench_Verified (princeton-nlp/SWE-bench_Verified, test, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Partial] NL: "Django i18n_patterns fails for locales containing script and region, including en-latn-us and en-Latn-US, which should return 200 rather than 404." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
  ACTION op_modify_code(attr_path=@pdjango/utils/translation/trans_real.py,attr_change=code_sequence(attr_items=[code_atom_fix_locale_script_region_parsing]),attr_regression=code_sequence(attr_items=[code_atom_setup_django_i18n_script_region_locales,code_atom_request_locale_en_latn_us,code_atom_expect_http_200,code_atom_request_locale_en_Latn_US,code_atom_expect_http_200]))
}` — PATH_LITERAL and CODE provide a non-prose shell, but source setup, locale values, HTTP status assertions, observed failures, and expected behavior are represented only as assumed atomic code entries. The supplied code vocabulary lacks a sufficiently typed, compositional schema for these facts.
  - `c5957` (PRISM (HannahRoseKirk/prism-alignment, conversations config, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Partial] NL: "Please provide a recipe for high protein flatbread; change the recipes to one with high reviews; how much protein is in these recipes; how long will this take me to make?" → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
CONVO $v1 {
  TURN $v2 SPEAKER=USER { UTTER int_recipe_request(attr_target=ent_flatbread,attr_nutrient=val_high_protein) -> $v3:PROPOSITION }
  TURN $v4 SPEAKER=USER REVISES $v2 { UTTER int_recipe_request(attr_target=ent_flatbread,attr_nutrient=val_high_protein,attr_requirement=val_high_reviews) -> $v5:PROPOSITION }
  TURN $v6 SPEAKER=USER REPLY_TO $v4 { UTTER int_nutrition_question(attr_reference=$v5,attr_nutrient=val_protein) }
  TURN $v7 SPEAKER=USER REPLY_TO $v4 { UTTER int_duration_question(attr_reference=$v5,attr_measure=val_preparation_time) }
}` — Conversation, REVISES, recipe, nutrition, and duration intents address the requested meta-structure. It is not fully valid under the stated schemas: attr_reference requires REF[PROPOSITION], while UTTER binds PROPOSITION; and CONVO permits UTTER only, so there is no legal in-conversation GENERATE step for the requested recipe artifact.

The expression depends on asserted-but-unpublished entries and also has the PROPOSITION-versus-REF reference mismatch.

The second Generate revision claims these domains but does not define their fields or canonical composition.

TASK $v1 {

  GENERATE(attr_specification=int_generate_artifact(attr_artifact=val_email_response,attr_specification=int_email_exchange(attr_sender=role_other_party,attr_recipient=role_user,attr_topic=ent_collaboration,attr_relation=val_next_recipient_response),attr_reference=$v2)) -> $v3:PROPOSITION

TASK $v1 {
