}` — The complete-glossary contract says medical prognosis is covered, but the actual ontology has no medical condition, prognosis uncertainty, age-audience, risk, or compassionate-register schema. A deterministic, safe representation needs more than an asserted future entry.
- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=low — Independent translations agreed only that the new direction seeks symbolic handles and operations. They diverged on whether pillows require pick-up, how price and compatibility constraints are represented, how household goals decompose, whether bug reports have usable schemas, whether recipes are TASK/CONVO/GENERATE, and whether complex persona constraints are expressible. These are semantic and canonical-form divergences, not merely harmless formatting differences.
- **Required changes:**
  - `Generate`: Merge the two duplicate Generate revisions into one normative entry. Define one grammar and one set of semantics for attr_reference; either bind generated artifacts as REF[PROPOSITION] or change every reference schema to accept PROPOSITION, then update all worked examples consistently.
  - `Types & Lexicon`: Publish the complete lexical definition needed by the claimed closed language: define every lexical terminal used by literal productions and clarify whether grammar metasymbols are non-source notation. Add an explicit rule that every code_atom is an exact glossary member with a declared atomic subtype, rather than using the phrase “string atom” ambiguously.
  - `ontology-symbol`: Replace the untestable blanket assertion that complete-glossary entries cover recipes, reviews, travel, personas, scripts, sermons, email exchanges, CRUD, meal plans, and medical prognosis with concrete category entries and schemas for the sampled domains. Each must specify fields, types, permitted value categories, canonical field order, defaults, duplicate prohibition, result/reference type, and construct-selection rule.
  - `attribute-symbol and value subcategories`: Add independently look-upable entries for every attribute used by supplied schemas and examples, including attr_recipient, attr_content, attr_duration, attr_artifact, attr_specification, attr_reference, attr_source, attr_destination, attr_quantity, attr_currency, attr_max_price, attr_deadline, attr_nutrient, attr_measure, attr_requirement, attr_audience, attr_register, and code-AST fields. Define each permitted value category rather than relying on an enclosing schema statement alone.
  - `Conversation and Generate`: Define a legal canonical bridge for a conversational request that requires generation. Either permit a typed generation step in TURN under explicit semantics or define a task invocation/reference mechanism from CONVO; ensure revision and follow-up references use a consistent proposition identity type.
  - `code-form and SWE-bench coverage`: Add typed, compositional code AST nodes for source strings, identifiers, literals, calls, imports, function definitions, assertions, HTTP status/response facts, locale tags, expected/actual outputs, and structured edits/tests. Define int_bug_report and op_modify_code decomposition so both sampled SWE-bench reports can be recovered without opaque catch-all code_atom assertions.
  - `Action and household canonical decomposition`: State a canonical rule for goal descriptions such as “ready for coffee” and for source-location retrieval: specify when op_find, op_pick_up, op_heat, op_remove, and op_place are required. Correct the Iterate worked example to satisfy op_place's held-possession precondition.
  - `basis`: Revalidate every worked example against the final merged grammar and schemas. Fix the missing attr_recipient in the Types & Lexicon extension request example, the invalid unbound $v3 in the structural-symbol example, the Check example that is not a document/step, and all PROPOSITION-versus-REF mismatches.
- **Logic issues:** Generate is revised twice with different normative semantics in the same proposal, making the final language state ambiguous.; The first Generate semantics require attr_reference:REF[PROPOSITION], but generation results and UTTER results are bound as PROPOSITION; its own worked examples pass incompatible handles.; The Types & Lexicon worked example calls int_request_extension without its schema-required attr_recipient field.; The structural-symbol worked example uses $v3 without any preceding declaration or binding.; The Check worked example is a standalone expression even though document grammar requires ENTRYPOINT plus TASK or CONVO.; The Iterate worked example passes a merely found pillow to op_place even though the declared op_place schema requires a held entity and op_pick_up is the declared acquisition operation.; The ontology membership rule promises exact glossary membership but does not provide the actual category entries, schemas, or canonical mappings necessary to verify most sampled symbols.; The code_atom gloss calls code atoms “string atom” while N4 forbids natural-language fragments; without a precise atomic subtype grammar and exact-entry discipline this is an unclear prose re-entry channel.; Conversation confines TURN bodies to UTTER, while generated-artifact requests require GENERATE; no legal connection is defined between the conversational request and requested generation.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N1: violated — The removal of STRING, comments, unrestricted IDENT, and prefix-only namespace acceptance is real progress, and literal forms plus canonical handles are substantially better defined. N1 is not yet upheld because many symbols needed for valid expressions are delegated to an unspecified complete glossary rather than being traceable in the supplied glossary/category entries, and the conflicting Generate definitions prevent a reader from determining the final legal source language.
  - N2: violated — The added structural-symbol and ontology-symbol categories are a major move beyond one construct entry per glossary item. The glossary still does not actually contain the full descriptive lexicon, its category members, value sets, and worked examples required for the many asserted domains.
  - N3: partial — Several operation and intent entries now name narrow value subcategories such as duration-unit, tone-value, currency-value, and nutrient-value, rather than accepting generic VALUE. Numerous attributes used by schemas and examples have no own glossary entries or permitted values, and the claimed remaining subcategories are not enumerated or defined.
  - N4: upheld — The proposed source syntax removes quoted strings, comments, and free-text payloads, and simulations use only structural symbols, literals, handles, and ontology/code symbols. This status depends on enforcing the proposal's exact-entry rule for code_atom and ontology entries; the ambiguous “string atom” wording must be tightened to avoid reopening a prose channel.
  - N5: upheld — Atomic NUMBER, DATE, TIME, URL, PATH, NAME, and opaque-handle forms are explicitly constrained, while descriptive tokens are intended to acquire meaning only through exact glossary entries. The literal and handle rules are a meaningful self-contained improvement over English-derived identifiers and strings.
  - N6: partial — The grammar is now stated over ontology categories and the simulations may assume a complete glossary within established categories. However, many needed categories themselves remain only an assertion in a membership-rule paragraph rather than defined compositional categories, so translators cannot reliably tell whether a missing symbol belongs to an existing category or represents a language gap.
  - N7: partial — Canonical handles, ordered fields, duplicate rejection, fixed Check precedence, and exact symbol membership improve encoding determinism. The duplicate Generate definitions, missing canonical decomposition/selection rules for many domains, and low independent-translator agreement mean one canonical expression per meaning is not yet achieved.
  - N8: partial — Typed propositions and code trees seek to retain substantially more structure than opaque prose, including quantities, relations, branches, and revisions. Richness remains aspirational for most open domains because their schemas are neither concrete nor compositional enough to preserve detailed meaning.
- **Decision:** needs-rework
- **Documenter summary:** Shaper proposed a large N1-compliance rewrite (steering note: Closed vocabulary) replacing free identifiers, strings, comments, and prefix-only acceptance with glossary-governed handles, atomic literals, ontology entries, and typed code trees across Types & Lexicon, Action, Utterance, Generate, Task, Bind, Iterate, Check, Conversation, and Flow-If. Critic ruled needs-rework despite passing doc hygiene: the decisive issues were two duplicate, mutually incompatible Generate definitions (ambiguous attr_reference typing vs PROPOSITION binding) and several worked examples violating their own stated schemas (unbound handle, missing required field, held-possession precondition broken in Iterate), plus most of the 'complete glossary' coverage for sampled domains (SWE-bench, PRISM, ThoughtTrace) being only asserted rather than concretely defined. All eight steering-note dimensions were marked improved-but-still-violated/partial, so the Shaper's compliant position was accepted in direction but rejected pending concrete required fixes.
- **Cost this sprint:** $0.9993

## Sprint 13 (attempt 2/3) — 2026-09-30

- **Language version:** 9.0.0 → 9.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N1 [hard] Closed vocabulary: every symbol used in a BrainCode expression is defined in the glossary.
- **Shaper proposal (model: openai/gpt-5.6-terra):** This revision replaces free identifiers, strings, prose payloads, and duplicate generation rules with one closed symbolic basis: opaque handles, atomic literal forms, exact glossary membership, typed propositions, and ordered schemas. It also supplies a single proposition-reference model and a legal GENERATE step in conversations.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — Every revised expression position now accepts only a structural token, a glossary member, a glossary-defined atomic literal, or a canonical handle. Grammar metavariables are explicitly non-source notation; no free-text or unrestricted identifier production remains.
- **Changes:** `Types & Lexicon` (revise, MAJOR), `structural-symbol` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `ontology-symbol` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `Utterance` (revise, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `Task` (revise, MAJOR), `Bind` (revise, MAJOR), `Iterate` (revise, MAJOR), `Check` (revise, MAJOR), `Flow-If` (revise, MAJOR), `Conversation` (revise, MAJOR)
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — This attempt makes substantial, sound progress on the focus note by closing free-text and free-identifier channels, so N1 is now upheld in the grammar. It cannot be accepted because the narrow replacement ontology causes broad coverage and richness regressions, including most sampled open tasks and both SWE-bench fixes, and because several previously required concrete schema additions remain incomplete. The core direction is sound, but the next revision must expand closed symbolic composition rather than rely on unsupported approximations.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: harm — The closed symbolic basis improves representability for a narrow set of household, email, recipe, and simple recommendation tasks, but removes the current language's ability to retain the actionable core of most sampled web, SWE-bench, planning, and open-generation tasks. Missing operations and schemas include sorting/filtering, rinsing/slicing, code bug reports and arbitrary structured edits, legal documents, channel schedules, mortgage advice, and story generation.
  - expressivity: mixed — Typed propositions, dates, paths, quantities, handles, and revision links preserve more structure than prior strings where a supplied schema fits. However, most sample meanings cannot be represented without omitting essential relations, constraints, requested artifact form, or substantive content; under N4 those omissions are failures rather than permissible opaque payloads.
  - determinism: benefit — Canonical handles, ordered schema fields, typed values, and an explicit structural vocabulary materially reduce prior identifier and string variation. Determinism remains incomplete because the ontology does not define canonical construct selection for many meanings and providers disagree on operation/field choice, such as source versus location, recommendations versus explain requests, and unsupported code edits.
  - interpretability: mixed — Closed literal forms and typed schemas are easier to inspect than free strings, and the revised grammar distinguishes source notation from source tokens. But densely grouped glossary entries obscure individual attribute/category meanings, schemas omit many needed concepts, and the claimed complete ontology is not actually published, so a reader cannot recover omitted task meaning from many expressions.
  - improvement: mixed — For supported operational tasks, explicit find/pick-up/place sequencing and typed conversational propositions would aid a weaker agent. For unsupported tasks, the only legal approximation loses the requested work, so the expression would make an agent worse than the original request.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `dev-embodied-2` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Put both pillows from the sofa onto the armchair, one at a time." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
ACTION op_find(a_target=ent_pillow,a_source=ent_sofa,a_quantity=2)->$v2:LIST[REF[ENTITY]]
FOR EACH $v3 IN $v2 { ACTION op_pick_up(a_target=$v3)->$v4:REF[ENTITY] ACTION op_place(a_target=$v4,a_destination=ent_armchair) }
}` — Action and Iterate support this task, assuming the complete entity category contains the three entity symbols as N6 directs.
  - `dev-embodied-1` (seed_tasks) [Fail] NL: "Rinse the mug in the sink, then put it in the coffee maker." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
ACTION op_find(a_target=ent_mug,a_quantity=1)->$v2:REF[ENTITY]
ACTION op_pick_up(a_target=$v2)->$v3:REF[ENTITY]
ACTION op_place(a_target=$v3,a_destination=ent_coffee_maker)
}` — The closest legal program omits rinsing. op_rinse is absent from the proposed closed operation vocabulary, so the actionable core is not representable.
  - `86afd67c-1bff-455c-baa7-e18dcb64b0f3` (Mind2Web) [Fail] NL: "Find comedy tv shows on netflix sorted by audience score." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
ACTION op_find(a_target=ent_tv_show,a_source=ent_netflix,a_quantity=1)->$v2:REF[ENTITY]
}` — op_find has neither genre nor ordering fields, and no op_sort schema exists. The legal approximation loses comedy, audience-score ordering, and plural result intent.
  - `bf469f30-6628-4017-b963-672645d7feab` (Mind2Web) [Fail] NL: "Find the highest rated dog collar under 10 dollar." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
ACTION op_find(a_target=ent_dog_collar,a_max_price=10,a_currency=val_usd,a_quantity=1)->$v2:REF[ENTITY]
}` — Price is representable, but no proposition or canonical sorting operation encodes highest rated. val_high_reviews cannot occur directly in op_find's a_requirement field because that field requires PROPOSITION.
  - `trial_T20190907_223758_523581#1` (ALFRED) [Partial] NL: "Microwave a slice of apple and place it on the table." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
ACTION op_find(a_target=ent_apple_slice,a_quantity=1)->$v2:REF[ENTITY]
ACTION op_pick_up(a_target=$v2)->$v3:REF[ENTITY]
ACTION op_heat(a_target=$v3,a_destination=ent_microwave)->$v4:REF[ENTITY]
ACTION op_remove(a_target=$v4,a_source=ent_microwave)->$v5:REF[ENTITY]
ACTION op_place(a_target=$v5,a_destination=ent_table)
}` — The requested end-state is represented, but the demonstrated trajectory requires slicing an apple and no op_slice exists. The language cannot distinguish an existing slice from a slice that must be created.
  - `trial_T20190908_224836_919680#2` (ALFRED) [Partial] NL: "Put a microwaved slice of apple on the table in the corner." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
ACTION op_find(a_target=ent_apple_slice,a_quantity=1)->$v2:REF[ENTITY]
ACTION op_pick_up(a_target=$v2)->$v3:REF[ENTITY]
ACTION op_heat(a_target=$v3,a_destination=ent_microwave)->$v4:REF[ENTITY]
ACTION op_remove(a_target=$v4,a_source=ent_microwave)->$v5:REF[ENTITY]
ACTION op_place(a_target=$v5,a_destination=ent_corner_table)
}` — This captures heating and placement if ent_corner_table is a complete-glossary entity, but it cannot encode that the slice must be produced from an apple when that procedural requirement is intended.
  - `scikit-learn__scikit-learn-11578` (SWE-bench_Verified) [Fail] NL: "Fix LogisticRegressionCV multinomial probabilistic scoring so the scoring LogisticRegression receives multi_class and related constructor parameters." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
ACTION op_modify_code(a_path=@psklearn/linear_model/logistic.py,a_change=code_edit_replace(a_target=code_identifier(a_value=code_atom_x),a_value=code_identifier(a_value=code_atom_x)))
}` — The only supplied code atoms cannot encode LogisticRegression, multi_class, fit_intercept, constructor arguments, scoring behavior, regression facts, or a test. The legal expression is semantically vacuous.
  - `django__django-13112` (SWE-bench_Verified) [Fail] NL: "Fix makemigrations crashing for a ForeignKey with a mixed-case Django app name." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
ACTION op_modify_code(a_path=@pdjango/db/models/fields/related.py,a_change=code_edit_replace(a_target=code_identifier(a_value=code_atom_x),a_value=code_identifier(a_value=code_atom_x)))
}` — No symbolic bug-report, mixed-case-name, ForeignKey, lazy-reference, exception, or test schema exists. The code AST is too small to state the requested fix.
  - `c6516` (PRISM) [Partial] NL: "Can you give me some great places to visit in the Netherlands?" → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
CONVO $v1 {
TURN $v2 SPEAKER=USER { UTTER int_recommend(a_target=ent_place,a_location=ent_netherlands)->$v3:PROPOSITION }
}` — The recommendation and location are represented, but no schema captures the requested quality criterion "great" or the desired plurality/list of destinations.
  - `c3388` (PRISM) [Fail] NL: "hi how hs your day been?" → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
CONVO $v1 {
TURN $v2 SPEAKER=USER { UTTER int_capability_question(a_subject=role_agent,a_capability=val_capability_converse)->$v3:PROPOSITION }
}` — This is only a legal conversational approximation. There is no greeting, wellbeing inquiry, social-response, or day-status proposition, and val_capability_converse is not among the supplied exact value members.

CONVO $v1 {
TURN $v2 SPEAKER=USER { UTTER int_explain(a_topic=ent_american_politics,a_audience=role_user)->$v3:PROPOSITION }
}` — int_explain captures the topic and audience, but the requested brevity has no defined depth value; val_simple does not mean brief.
  - `c2904` (PRISM) [Partial] NL: "Tell me about what is happening in New Zealand Politically" → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
CONVO $v1 {
TURN $v2 SPEAKER=USER { UTTER int_explain(a_topic=ent_new_zealand_politics,a_audience=role_user)->$v3:PROPOSITION }
}` — The topic is representable under the assumed entity category, but the current-events/freshness requirement has no symbolic field or value.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-73283` (PATHs) [Fail] NL: "Introduce the topic of the impact of dam construction on seasonal precipitation, land use/cover, and socio-economic conditions in Eastern Sudan." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
CONVO $v1 {
TURN $v2 SPEAKER=USER { UTTER int_explain(a_topic=ent_dam_construction,a_audience=role_user)->$v3:PROPOSITION }
}` — The approximation drops the requested introductory genre and every required relation: seasonal precipitation, land use/cover, socio-economic impact, and Eastern Sudan. N4 forbids retaining them as prose.

CONVO $v1 {

}` — There is no comparison, literary-work relation, thematic-parallel, or constrained recommendation schema. The expression loses both compared works and the requested criterion.

}` — The proposal has no text artifact, first-person decision/travel relation, Japan/SGI learning relation, or exact 100-word constraint. The illustrated expression is not valid under the listed exact value members.

CONVO $v1 {
TURN $v2 SPEAKER=USER { UTTER int_explain(a_topic=ent_mortgage_employment)->$v3:PROPOSITION }
}` — The expression omits job-contract acquisition, mortgage qualification, cash-versus-financing comparison, local unemployment, and low property cost; there is no financial-advice or causal-planning schema.
- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=low — Providers agree that important areas are unavailable, including arbitrary SWE-bench edits and academic-topic generation. Where they attempted translations, they diverged materially: Gemini invents a_location, a_genre, a_ordering, op_sort, and undefined entity forms; Anthropic reports gaps; the proposal instead permits op_find only with its listed fields. This is evidence that construct selection and category schemas are not yet canonical.
- **Required changes:**
  - `ontology-symbol`: Replace grouped and partly aspirational domain coverage with independently lookupable category entries for entity-symbol, role-symbol, each attribute, and each value category. For each category state membership rule, canonical synonym mapping, subtype, permitted consuming fields, and a worked example; retain N6's complete-glossary assumption only for missing members of these already-defined categories.
  - `Action`: Add closed schemas and canonical selection rules for the missing demonstrated operations: op_rinse, op_slice, filtering by genre/category, and sorting/ranking by a defined criterion. Add typed relational propositions such as genre, rating/order, location, and source so web tasks do not require invented attributes.
  - `code-form and SWE-bench coverage`: Add int_bug_report and compositional CODE schemas for module/class/function/member identifiers, keyword arguments, booleans, assignment, exception/observed-versus-expected facts, calls, tests, and structured replacement/insertion. Define a closed code-atom membership rule or exact atomic code-token category sufficient to encode both sampled fixes without prose.
  - `artifact specifications`: Add symbolic artifact/specification schemas for at least story, short constrained text, technical introduction, legal contract, structured plan/schedule, financial advice, and comparison/recommendation requests. Model participants, relations, ordered items, exact word/count constraints, audience, genre, and requested content propositions symbolically rather than replacing them with int_explain.
  - `int_plan_trip`: Make unspecified trip fields optional or introduce a minimal trip-request schema. Do not require origin, budget, currency, and criterion when the source does not state them; define canonical defaults only if a default has explicit semantics.
  - `N7 canonical selection rules`: For every overlapping intent and operation family, publish a deterministic construct-selection table and canonical field/value mappings. It must resolve recommendation versus explanation, quality/ranking criteria, source versus location, singular versus list result, and artifact generation versus utterance.
  - `attribute-symbol and value subcategories`: Complete the previous required change by giving each listed attribute and each value subcategory its own lookupable glossary entry rather than one bulk member declaration. Explicitly define values needed by the supplied schemas, including depth/length, freshness, availability, quality/ranking, relation, artifact, criterion, capability, and code-AST value categories.
  - `basis`: Revalidate all proposal examples and all corpus translations against exact-member legality. Do not use purported symbols such as val_story, val_legal_document, val_channel_plan, val_text, or val_capability_converse unless their exact glossary entries and consuming schemas have been added.
- **Logic issues:** The ontology entry calls its listed value symbols 'Exact members' while multiple required artifact and semantic categories have no member or schema; the future-member rule does not make an absent artifact specification legal today.; op_find's a_requirement accepts only PROPOSITION, but no supplied proposition schema expresses high rating, comedy genre, audience-score ordering, or similar common search constraints.; The proposed household vocabulary omits op_rinse and op_slice although both are necessary in sampled ALFRED/seed trajectories and were part of the earlier language's coverage.; The claimed SWE-bench code coverage is not achieved: the finite supplied code atoms cannot express either sampled patch, and int_bug_report is absent despite being required previously.; int_plan_trip has required origin and budget fields, making a simple destination-and-duration request unfaithful unless a translator invents information.; The worked Conversation example is internally legal on proposition handles, but its artifact/reference design does not solve the broader absence of schemas for most generated artifact content.; The current grouped attribute list is not independently lookupable per attribute or value category, so it does not fully satisfy the prior required change or N2's glossary organization requirement.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N1: upheld — The revised grammar eliminates STRING, unrestricted IDENT, comments, and quoted payloads; source positions are structural symbols, exact ontology members, atomic literals, or canonical handles. Subject to N6's stipulated complete-glossary assumption, every token in the supported simulations has a glossary entry or a glossary-defined production rule.
  - N2: partial — Structural and ontology vocabulary categories are newly added and substantially improve on the construct-only glossary. However, entity, role, and value categories are largely asserted rather than independently enumerated or separately lookupable, and significant descriptive domains remain absent.
  - N3: upheld — Fields now require typed literals, handles, proposition trees, code trees, or ontology symbols, and schemas state allowed categories. The remaining problem is insufficient category richness, not an unrestricted attribute-value channel.
  - N4: upheld — The revised productions exclude quoted text and prose payloads, and all descriptive content must occur as symbolic proposition/code structure or atomic literals. This is real progress despite the substantial missing ontology needed to encode many requests.
  - N5: upheld — Literal forms are explicitly atomic and locally defined, while descriptive symbols require exact glossary membership. No expression production depends on English sentence grammar.
  - N6: partial — The grammar now targets ontology categories and the simulations appropriately assume entity members within an existing entity category. It remains partial because several required categories and compositional schemas do not exist at all; N6 cannot cure missing categories.
  - N7: partial — Fixed field order, handle allocation, typing, and precedence substantially improve encoding discipline. Independent translations still diverge over available fields and construct choices, and canonical mappings for many meanings are not supplied.
  - N8: partial — The supported subset gains useful typed richness, but compared with the current prose-capable language, most sampled requests now lose their core content or constraints outright. The necessary N4 closure must be accompanied by a much broader symbolic ontology rather than a collapse to a few coarse schemas.
- **Decision:** needs-rework
- **Documenter summary:** Addressing steering note N1 (closed vocabulary), the Shaper proposed replacing all free identifiers, strings, and prose payloads across Action, Utterance, Generate, Task, Bind, Iterate, Check, Flow-If, Conversation, and Types & Lexicon with a closed basis of structural symbols, exact glossary ontology members, atomic literals, and canonical handles, fully complying with N1. The Critic agreed N1 itself was upheld and improved (no STRING/IDENT/prose channels remain), but rejected the overall change as needs-rework because the narrow supplied ontology caused broad coverage losses on simulated items (e.g. missing op_rinse/op_slice, no sorting/filtering, both SWE-bench fixes semantically vacuous, story/legal-document/plan generation all failing) and left several previously required schema additions incomplete; doc hygiene passed but did not override the Critic's substantive rejection.
- **Cost this sprint:** $0.8886

## Sprint 13 (attempt 3/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 9.0.0 → 9.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N1 [hard] Closed vocabulary: every symbol used in a BrainCode expression is defined in the glossary.
- **Shaper proposal (model: openai/gpt-5.6-terra):** This revision replaces free strings and identifiers with handles, atomic literals, and glossary-governed symbolic schemas while expanding the ontology for household, web, dialogue, artifact, planning, finance, and code-fix requests. It also supplies canonical selection and field-order rules so equivalent requests do not choose competing encodings.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — All source identifiers become generated HANDLE tokens, all descriptive tokens must be exact members of glossary vocabulary categories, and all open-ended atomic values use explicitly defined literal forms. No grammar production permits prose, STRING, comments, unrestricted IDENT, or undocumented attributes.
- **Changes:** `Types & Lexicon` (revise, MAJOR), `structural-symbol` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `entity-symbol` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `role-symbol` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `semantic-symbol` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `Utterance` (revise, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `Task` (revise, MAJOR), `Bind` (revise, MAJOR), `Iterate` (revise, MAJOR), `Check` (revise, MAJOR), `Conversation` (revise, MAJOR)
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — The revision makes genuine progress toward closed vocabulary by removing free strings and adding symbolic categories, so its core direction is sound. It cannot be accepted because key grammar categories and fixed tokens are still undefined, the glossary is not individually lookupable, and most sampled requests lack symbolic schemas under the no-prose constraint. The deterministic-selection and handle-resolution gaps additionally regress hard note N7.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — The proposal adds useful symbolic coverage for rinse/place, retrieval/filter/sort, artifact requests, and limited code ASTs. However, several sampled closed tasks still have no operation or proposition schema (file age/deletion/archive membership, selecting a nearest store, transit schedules, movable containers), and most open substantive requests remain unrepresentable without inventing ontology schemas.
  - expressivity: mixed — Structured propositions improve preservation of recipients, durations, ranking, and some artifact constraints. But the schemas omit essential distinctions such as email drafting versus sending, file deletion, ZIP-based proximity, colors, containment, route endpoints, revisions of supplied tables, advice constraints, and most narrative/script content; several expressions therefore cannot preserve the request.
  - determinism: harm — The proposed canonical retrieval pipeline is beneficial locally, but the language does not define operation_symbol, intent_symbol, attribute_symbol, or the category-selection rules needed to select among schemas. Independent translations already diverge materially: for the red-rag task one uses a missing red entity while another uses filter/property; for the game comparison one omits versus includes a criterion; and both providers leave major gaps for code, store, and story tasks.
  - interpretability: mixed — Opaque handles, typed propositions, and schema-ordered fields are improvements over unrestricted strings. Yet the glossary combines dozens of supposedly independently lookupable attributes, values, operations, and schemas into bulk entries, leaves source categories and structural tokens undefined, and gives many values only an aspirational subtype statement rather than a usable per-symbol definition.
  - improvement: mixed — For the rinse/place and ranked-show patterns, the decomposition would help a weaker model execute ordered work. For many other tasks the model must either fail, invent undocumented symbols, or reduce the request to a vague explain/generate proposition, which does not improve planning over the original request.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `dev-code-1` (seed_tasks) [Fail] NL: "Given a list of file paths, delete every file older than 30 days unless it's in the 'archive' folder." → `No valid BrainCode expression: no schema defines file-age lookup, archive-folder membership, or file deletion.` — The proposed op_remove is a household removal schema, not a filesystem deletion schema. This was also a GAP in both independent translations.
  - `dev-email-1` (seed_tasks) [Fail] NL: "Draft a reply to my professor asking for a two-day extension on the assignment, and keep it formal." → `No valid BrainCode expression: int_request_extension lacks an assignment/topic field, and no email-draft artifact schema distinguishes drafting a reply from sending email.` — int_request_extension helps with recipient, duration, and tone, but not the requested draft artifact or its assignment referent.
  - `b7082615-e6e1-4981-b51b-9259671d1adf` (Mind2Web) [Fail] NL: "Change your store to the one nearest to 07055" → `No valid BrainCode expression: no store-setting operation or proximity-to-ZIP relation/schema is defined.` — op_find/filter/sort cannot set the selected store. Gemini likewise identified the missing change-store operation.
  - `e2adf8f1-547d-4671-96c1-4a21a56e135d` (Mind2Web) [Fail] NL: "View the upcoming schedule from Otis St@Summer St to City Point of the transit near South Station for today." → `No valid BrainCode expression: no transit-schedule retrieval operation or route/stops/today proposition schema is defined.` — The entity category can contain station names, but there is no existing category or schema for the requested route and schedule relation.
  - `trial_T20190908_120422_949453#2` (ALFRED) [Partial] NL: "Move a red rag into a sink." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
TASK $v1 {
 ACTION op_find(a_target=ent_red_rag,a_quantity=1)->$v2:REF[ENTITY]
 ACTION op_pick_up(a_target=$v2)->$v3:REF[ENTITY]
 ACTION op_place(a_target=$v3,a_destination=ent_sink)
}` — The action sequence is representable if ent_red_rag is a complete-glossary entity. It loses the compositional color/object distinction because no color attribute/value category is defined; translators may instead use ent_rag plus an invented property filter.
  - `trial_T20190906_165121_524545#3` (ALFRED) [Fail] NL: "Put a box with keys in it on a chair." → `No valid BrainCode expression: no containment relation or operation retrieves a box specifically containing keys.` — Finding and placing a box is insufficient because the required keys-in-box relation is actionable and must be preserved.
  - `sphinx-doc__sphinx-7757` (SWE-bench_Verified) [Fail] NL: "The default value for positional only argument has vanished; modify sphinx/util/inspect.py so the documented positional-only argument default value is shown." → `No valid BrainCode expression: int_bug_report and CODE constructors exist, but no symbolic propositions/code atoms encode positional-only parameters, their default value, documentation rendering, or the required inspect.py change.` — The new bug/code family helps in shape only. Gemini and Anthropic both retained a GAP for the substantive defect.
  - `django__django-15268` (SWE-bench_Verified) [Fail] NL: "Optimize multiple AlterFooTogether operations into one by removing redundant constraint-removal operations in django/db/migrations/operations/models.py." → `No valid BrainCode expression: the required migration-operation concepts, constraint relations, optimization condition, and code atoms are not in a defined category/schema.` — Generic code_insert/code_replace cannot symbolically encode which operations are redundant or the required transformation.
  - `c7033` (PRISM) [Partial] NL: "What is an objectively stronger video game, Marvel's Spider-Man 2 or Baldur's Gate 3? But you must pick one." → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
CONVO $v1 {
 TURN $v2 SPEAKER=USER { UTTER int_compare_request(a_target=ent_marvels_spider_man_2,a_reference=ent_baldurs_gate_3,a_requirement=int_property(a_target=ent_marvels_spider_man_2,a_value=val_objective_strength))->$v3:PROPOSITION }
}` — This assumes complete-glossary entities and a value category member val_objective_strength, but it does not encode the mandatory single-winner decision because no winner-selection/result schema exists. Independent translations disagree on both fields and criterion.
  - `c1182` (PRISM) [Fail] NL: "How to approach a girl; give realistic pick-up lines that would work in real life." → `No valid BrainCode expression: no advice, social-interaction, realistic-example, or pickup-line artifact schema is defined.` — int_social_inquiry is not specified with fields or a canonical meaning sufficient for this request.
  - `c224` (PRISM) [Partial] NL: "Why does it seem a lot of people are extremely right-wing the last couple of years, including in the Netherlands?" → `ENTRYPOINT $v1
CONVO $v1 {
 TURN $v2 SPEAKER=USER { UTTER int_explain(a_topic=ent_recent_right_wing_support,a_audience=role_user)->$v3:PROPOSITION }
}` — The broad explanation act is represented, but Netherlands, perceived increase, time span, and requested causal analysis have no defined symbolic relation/value representation.
  - `c1448` (PRISM) [Fail] NL: "What do you think of the outcomes of the elections in the Netherlands from yesterday? How will a coalition form, and what parties could form one?" → `No valid BrainCode expression: no opinion/analysis intent, election-result relation, relative-date resolution context for a conversation, or coalition-formation schema is defined.` — int_explain would discard the opinion and multi-question structure; no date anchor is available on CONVO.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-58610` (PATHs) [Fail] NL: "Continue Part 3 of a Power Rangers x Helltaker crossover story about Zack Taylor's flirtatious and charming nature." → `No valid BrainCode expression: int_story_spec has participants/event/constraint only and no schemas encode continuation, dialogue/script form, named-character relationships, personality traits, or requested plot content.` — N4 forbids retaining the story substance as prose. The supplied providers likewise found an unresolved story-content GAP.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-149438` (PATHs) [Fail] NL: "Write a hilarious adult comedy script where the Scooby gang reacts to a badly translated sentence about Daphne's Aunt Olivia becoming a cat creature." → `No valid BrainCode expression: there is no script artifact, comedy/register category, translation-error relation, character-dialogue representation, or symbolic encoding for the supplied sentence.` — A generic story specification would lose the requested script form, characters, reaction structure, and exact translation target.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-113201` (PATHs) [Fail] NL: "Rewrite a table of AI-automatable online earning methods to be more comprehensive, preserve the first column, remove every <br />, and output a table." → `No valid BrainCode expression: no table/document AST, source-table literal/category, rewrite operation, column-preservation constraint, line-break transformation, or SEO/detail specification is defined.` — N4 prevents treating the supplied table as an opaque text payload.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-155336` (PATHs) [Fail] NL: "Summarize and humanize an SEO-friendly article reviewing Monetag in 2024 as an AdSense alternative." → `No valid BrainCode expression: no summarize/rewrite intent, article representation, review relation, SEO constraint, date-specific claim representation, or source-document symbolic form is defined.` — The requested source content and transformations cannot be represented as glossary symbols under the proposed ontology.
  - `1776020181361` (ThoughtTrace) [Fail] NL: "My 2017 MacBook Pro gets hot and its fan is noisy while I work. What can I do, without opening it or updating beyond its supported version?" → `No valid BrainCode expression: no troubleshooting/advice schema represents device symptoms, constraints against opening/updating, software/external remedies, or follow-up correction.` — The dialogue's correction materially changes the advice; neither an applicable intent schema nor symbolic remedy vocabulary exists.
  - `1775963744772` (ThoughtTrace) [Fail] NL: "As a budget-conscious piano beginner focused on jazz and classical music, what path should I take after learning them in one month proved unrealistic?" → `No valid BrainCode expression: no learning-plan/advice schema encodes beginner level, budget, genres, timeline feasibility, or the corrective follow-up.` — This is a multi-turn refinement, not merely an int_explain topic.
  - `1775837106479` (ThoughtTrace) [Fail] NL: "Help plan a princess-themed birthday this week for a 9-year-old with 10 adults and 20 children at a play venue, then suggest gifts and invitation wording." → `No valid BrainCode expression: schedule-spec cannot encode party planning, age, theme, venue capability, guest composition, urgency, gift suggestions, or invitation text.` — The conversation includes a sequence of planning, gift, and invitation requests, none of which has a corresponding closed schema.
  - `1775973360170` (ThoughtTrace) [Fail] NL: "Plan a friendly weekly routine around school until 2 PM, work from 4 PM to 8 PM, morning cereal and strong coffee, and a late-morning snack." → `No valid BrainCode expression: schedule-spec has no time-block, recurring-week, school/work commitment, meal preference, or friendly-register schema.` — The proposed schedule artifact family is too coarse to preserve the constraints and iterative personalization.
- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=low — Independent translations agree that several tasks require gaps, but they do not converge on valid encodings. Anthropic and Gemini disagree on red-rag representation and comparison fields; Gemini invents undefined operation/attribute uses for store selection; both leave code and creative-story substance unresolved. This confirms that the current schema-selection and vocabulary rules are not deterministic.
- **Required changes:**
  - `Types & Lexicon`: Define every grammar nonterminal that denotes a source-token category: operation_symbol, intent_symbol, attribute_symbol, code_constructor, intent_symbol, MONTH, DAY, HOUR, MINUTE, SECOND, DIGIT, NAME_CHAR, URL_HOST, URL_PATH, and PATH_SEGMENT. State explicitly which are metalanguage-only and provide glossary-defined generation rules for every source token form.
  - `structural-symbol`: Add individually lookupable entries for every fixed source token omitted from the member list, including AND, OR, NOT, and all punctuation/prefixes actually admitted by the final grammar. Reconcile the list with the exact revised grammar.
  - `semantic-symbol`: Replace each bulk symbol string with one glossary entry per operation, intent, attribute, controlled-value category, value, code constructor, and code atom. Each entry must specify its exact subtype, synonyms, consuming schemas, and worked example. In particular define a_deadline and every field mentioned by any operation schema.
  - `value categories`: Create separate value-category entries and exact member/type rules for duration-unit, tone, depth, freshness, quality, ranking, genre, relation, artifact, criterion, currency, capability, and code-AST atoms. Do not use one undifferentiated val_* list with retrospective claims that consuming schemas determine its type.
  - `schema-selection table`: Publish a complete canonical construct-selection table covering TASK versus CONVO, UTTER versus GENERATE, draft versus send, recommend versus explain versus compare, scalar versus list retrieval, entity compound versus entity-plus-property filter, source versus location, and all artifact-specification families. Define canonical field ordering for every schema, including nested propositions.
  - `closed-task operations`: Add typed schemas and vocabulary categories needed by the sampled actionable cores: filesystem age, folder membership, delete; store selection/change and ZIP/proximity; transit route/schedule lookup; containment/content relations; and explicit color/property filtering. Define their result and effect semantics rather than repurposing household op_remove.
  - `artifact and conversational ontology`: Add symbolic schemas, categories, and composition rules for advice/plans, document/table rewrite, summary, review, script/dialogue, story continuation, invitations, schedules/time blocks, troubleshooting, and multi-turn corrections. Each must encode the request substance symbolically under N4, not merely label it as a generic artifact.
  - `code-form and SWE coverage`: Extend CODE and bug-report propositions with symbolic representations for function signatures, positional-only arguments, defaults, documentation rendering, migration operations, constraints, sequences, deletion/reduction, and expected transformations. Add only atomic code-token categories with exact membership and consuming rules.
  - `handles and declaration grammar`: Specify whether ENTRYPOINT's handle introduces a declaration or references a later declaration, then give a non-circular allocation algorithm consistent with 'monotonically in preorder source introduction order.' Define handle allocation across task declarations, parameters, turns, loop variables, and result sites.
  - `proposal examples`: Revalidate every example against the final grammar and exact glossary categories. Fix unsupported source forms such as action's undefined operation_symbol, int_genre's a_value despite the grouped attribute list's incomplete field typing, code examples whose CODEATOM typing is unspecified, and any values that are only asserted to exist in an aspirational complete glossary.
- **Logic issues:** The grammar uses operation_symbol, intent_symbol, attribute_symbol, code_constructor, and speaker-related categories without defining productions that connect them to glossary_symbol or a glossary category.; The structural-symbol vocabulary omits AND, OR, and NOT even though Check admits them as source tokens.; The revised semantic schemas use fields not listed in the attribute declaration, including a_deadline in op_check_replied.; The semantic-symbol entry groups many distinct symbols in one member declaration, contrary to its claim that symbols are individually lookupable and contrary to N2's per-category/per-member requirement.; The generic value grammar admits glossary_symbol without a grammar-level category/type restriction; the later statement that each schema fixes types is not a parseable or lookupable definition of each value category.; NAME_LITERAL permits arbitrary NAME_CHAR+ but NAME_CHAR is not defined, and the semantics do not mechanically restrict names to proper-name atoms rather than arbitrary English words.; The global preorder handle rule is incompatible or at least unspecified for ENTRYPOINT $v1 followed by TASK $v1: the first occurrence appears to use the handle before the declaration that introduces it.; Task calls and declaration handles have no declared task-signature registry or handle-to-task resolution procedure, so a HANDLE(...) step is not statically resolvable.; The proposal claims every schema fixes defaults, but no default values or default application rules are supplied.; Several worked examples rely on unlisted exact symbols, including ent_new_zealand_politics, ent_place, ent_netherlands, ent_japan, ent_sgi, val_objective_strength, and val_formal; N6 allows a complete glossary to contain members only after the category/subtype/consuming-schema rules are complete, which they are not here.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N1: violated — Removing STRING, unrestricted IDENT, and quoted prose is real progress. N1 is still violated because valid source grammar references undefined token categories and lexical components, structural tokens are missing from the glossary, and bulk entries do not permit direct lookup of every used symbol.
  - N2: partial — The new entity, role, semantic, and structural vocabulary categories are substantial progress over construct-only glossary entries. The glossary remains incomplete because attributes, values, operations, schemas, and code atoms are grouped rather than individually defined, and several token families have no category entry at all.
  - N3: violated — The proposal moves toward typed controlled values, but it does not define a separate allowed value set for each attribute. A generic value can be any glossary_symbol/HANDLE/list/proposition/code, while the claimed schema restrictions and value subcategories are not individually enumerated or mechanically defined.
  - N4: upheld — The revised grammar removes quoted strings and prose payloads, and the intended atomic literals are non-phrase forms. This makes meaningful progress even though the missing symbolic ontology means many requests must currently fail rather than be encoded.
  - N5: violated — Handles and literal prefixes are a useful self-contained direction. NAME_LITERAL's undefined NAME_CHAR+ rule remains an unrestricted natural-language-token channel, and multiple source token categories are undefined rather than being closed glossary rules.
  - N6: partial — The grammar is increasingly framed over entity, role, semantic, and code categories, so missing members within a complete category need not be penalized. Missing category-level schemas remain widespread, and the incomplete subtype/consuming-schema rules make the complete-glossary assumption unusable for many required members.
  - N7: violated — Some ordering rules and retrieval canonicalization were added, but the current proposal introduces unresolved category selection, undefined token nonterminals, and broad schema overlap without a complete selection table. The supplied independent translations demonstrate non-convergence, so this worsens the prior determinism position.
  - N8: partial — The ontology attempts significantly richer propositions, artifacts, and code trees. Nonetheless, its current missing schemas force loss or failure on most sampled requests, and the bulk value categories do not yet safely realize richness within determinism.
- **Decision:** needs-rework
- **Documenter summary:** Sprint 13 (steering note N1, closed vocabulary) proposed replacing free strings/identifiers with handles, atomic literals, and glossary-governed schemas across Types & Lexicon, Action, Utterance, Generate, Task, Bind, Iterate, Check, and Conversation, plus new structural/entity/role/semantic-symbol vocabulary categories; the Shaper fully complied with N1 rather than contesting it. The Critic ruled needs-rework: while it credited real progress (removing STRING/IDENT/prose, adding typed propositions), it found the grammar still references undefined nonterminals (operation_symbol, intent_symbol, attribute_symbol, etc.), bulk-grouped glossary entries violate individual-lookup requirements, and simulated examples (e.g. file-deletion, store-selection, transit-schedule, and creative-writing tasks) mostly failed coverage or diverged nondeterministically across independent translations, which the Critic weighted most heavily as a regression on hard note N7 (determinism).
- **Cost this sprint:** $0.8902

## Sprint 14 (attempt 1/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 9.0.0 → 9.0.0 (MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N2 [hard] The glossary is the complete lexicon of the language, covering every kind of word, not only operators.
- **Shaper proposal (model: gemini/gemini-3.1-pro-preview):** Adds descriptive vocabulary categories to the glossary to cover operations, search criteria, entities, roles, code structures, artifact types, and tone values, directly addressing the Sprint 13 gaps.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — Directly implements the N2 requirement to add descriptive vocabulary as separate glossary categories, covering all the specific gaps identified in Sprint 13 (operations, search criteria, entities, roles, code structures, and artifact specifications) without removing existing free-text channels.
- **Changes:** `operation-name` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `search-criterion` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `entity-category` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `role-recipient` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `code-structure` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `artifact-type` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `tone-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR)
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — The proposal makes genuine N2 progress by adding distinct descriptive vocabulary categories, but it does not connect them to the grammar or make the glossary complete. More seriously, the five-value tone category contradicts the existing fourteen-value tone claim and creates a hard-note determinism regression. The core approach is sound, so precise integration and reconciliation—not rejection—are required.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — The added categories name several real gaps, notably code structures, artifact types, search criteria, and roles. However, they are not connected to Action, GENERATE, UTTER, or attribute grammars, so their members cannot replace the existing STRING payloads or express criteria such as compatibility, source location, nutrition, persona, or code behavior.
  - expressivity: neutral — No existing channel is removed, so the current prose-based expressions remain available. The new vocabulary is not semantically consumable by the constructs: for example, search-criterion members do not define attributes or values accepted by search_web/select_filter, and code-structure/artifact-type members do not supply structured code or generation semantics.
  - determinism: harm — The proposal introduces competing representations without a canonical selection rule: a filter may remain content="max_price<200", appear as an unsupported max_price attribute, or be represented by search_web plus select_filter. It also conflicts with the existing claimed 14-value tone enum by defining a five-member closed tone-value category, while leaving tone typed as STRING and quoted in examples.
  - interpretability: mixed — Separate glossary categories and member glosses improve lookupability for the listed words. But the open membership rules rely on undefined external lists or broad English judgments, categories overlap (genre/category/artifact-type and entity-category/artifact-type), and worked examples use quoted strings rather than category symbols, leaving readers unable to determine how vocabulary participates in valid expressions.
  - improvement: neutral — A weaker model gains useful labels such as expected_output and technical_introduction, but no construct accepts those labels with defined data-flow or execution semantics. Consequently the operational plans in the simulations remain driven by opaque content strings rather than the proposed structure.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `dev-embodied-2` (seed_tasks) [Partial] NL: "Put both pillows from the sofa onto the armchair, one at a time." → `ENTRYPOINT MovePillows

TASK MovePillows {
  LET pillows : LIST[STRING] = ["pillow1", "pillow2"]
  FOR EACH pillow_item IN pillows {
    ACTION pick_up(target=pillow_item) -> pillow : REF[STRING]
    ACTION place(target=pillow, destination="armchair")
  }
}` — FOR EACH captures one-at-a-time ordering, but the source relation "from the sofa" has no Action attribute and pillow descriptors remain prose strings. entity-category is not accepted by pick_up and adds no source-location vocabulary.
  - `dev-email-2` (seed_tasks) [Partial] NL: "If my manager hasn't replied by Friday, send a polite follow-up; otherwise do nothing." → `ENTRYPOINT FollowUpManager

TASK FollowUpManager {
  ACTION check_replied(target="manager", deadline="Friday") -> replied : BOOL
  IF NOT replied THEN {
    ACTION send_email(recipient="manager", content="follow-up", tone="polite")
  }
}` — role-recipient and tone-value identify relevant concepts, but recipient and tone remain STRING attributes; the proposed closed tone symbol is not integrated. The date lacks the required resolved UTC instant/context.
  - `e344b89e-767a-4618-ba82-4b81cdcba280` (Mind2Web (osunlp/Mind2Web, train, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Partial] NL: "add a motherboard under $200 and a compatible processor at any price to the shopping cart." → `ENTRYPOINT AddPartsToCart

  ACTION select_filter(target=motherboards, content="max_price<200") -> filtered_motherboards : LIST[REF[STRING]]

  ACTION search_web(target="processor") -> processors : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  ACTION select_filter(target=processors, content="compatible_with motherboard") -> compatible_processors : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  ACTION extract(target=compatible_processors, limit=1) -> processor : REF[STRING]
  ACTION add_to_cart(target=motherboard)
  ACTION add_to_cart(target=processor)
}` — search-criterion supplies max_price conceptually, but its members are not Action attributes and compatibility is not a listed criterion. The actionable restrictions are still opaque content strings; independent providers also disagree on whether max_price belongs directly on search_web.

  RETURN guide

  RETURN email
}` — The artifact type is too coarse and does not identify email. Sender identity, recipient, job position, and collaboration request have no symbolic representation; role-recipient is not usable by GENERATE.

  RETURN itinerary

Can you help me plan a simple meal prep for 3 days that includes breakfast, lunch, and dinner? I prefer high-protein options." → `ENTRYPOINT PlanMealPrep

TASK PlanMealPrep : STRING {

TASK ExplainHeartFailure : STRING {
  GENERATE(target="technical_introduction", audience="user", content="explain end-stage heart failure and prognosis in language suitable for a five-year-old") -> explanation : STRING

- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=low — Independent translations diverge materially: the pillow task is either a loop or a declared gap; the shopping task alternates between opaque filter content and unsupported direct search attributes; the SymPy report is either a gap or an unsupported UTTER attribute bundle; and the technical explanation is either a gap or prose-heavy GENERATE. The proposal does not establish canonical mappings that resolve these alternatives.
- **Required changes:**
  - `basis`: Add a complete glossary Lexicon section organized into structural words, key terms, and descriptive vocabulary. Include every currently usable keyword, delimiter, type, lexical primitive, operator, construct name, attribute name, operation name, and literal-category rule, rather than only seven new descriptive categories.
  - `operation-name`: Replace the membership rule "Any normative operation defined in the Action construct signature list" with either an exhaustive synchronized operation list or a precise extension rule, and revise Action grammar/semantics so operation-name is the required operation token category. Do not duplicate an operation inventory in two unsynchronized places.
  - `search-criterion`: Define criterion-value categories and criterion relations, then revise search_web, select_filter, and apply_filters signatures to accept a canonical structured criteria representation. Add compatibility/compatible_with, rating scale, genre values, price amount, currency, comparator/bound, and deterministic ordering/ranking direction.
  - `entity-category`: Split entity type/category from entity instance/descriptor and add a glossary-defined composition or literal rule for product/entity descriptions. Add location/source relations required by the pillow and household tasks; do not rely on quoted English descriptors as the supposed vocabulary encoding.
  - `role-recipient`: Define whether each member is a role, a participant identity, or a recipient value, and revise recipient/audience/speaker attribute signatures to consume the applicable category. Add a defined atomic proper-name/organization identifier rule for non-role recipients.
  - `code-structure`: Add a structured software-issue/fix construct or structured modify_code attributes for module/path, symbol kind/name, call, arguments, assertion, expected result, actual result, and reproduction/test. Define atomic code/path/URL literal categories rather than placing code fragments in STRING content.
  - `artifact-type`: Revise GENERATE so target is artifact-type rather than STRING, remove overlap between artifact type and genre/category, and add needed artifact members/rules including recipe, email, script, guide, itinerary, opinion/answer, and response. Define structured attributes for subject, audience, format, examples, length, and constraints.
  - `tone-value`: Reconcile this five-member closed set with the existing Action/Utterance claim of a closed 14-value enum. Publish one authoritative exhaustive value table with synonym normalization, revise tone signatures to reference tone-value rather than STRING, and update every worked example to use unquoted glossary symbols if symbols are intended.
  - `all proposed vocabulary categories`: Replace broad English-dependent membership rules such as "any noun" and "any structural element" with explicit membership governance, canonical symbol formation, synonym-to-one-symbol mappings, exclusion boundaries, and collision rules. Add canonical attribute ordering and a single mapping rule where an existing STRING representation competes with a vocabulary representation.
- **Logic issues:** All proposed worked examples retain quoted STRING values for proposed vocabulary members, so they do not demonstrate that the category is usable by the grammar.; search_web has the normative signature search_web(target=STRING), but the search-criterion example uses unsupported genre and minimum_rating attributes.; The operation-name category duplicates operations already normatively defined by Action signatures; its membership rule points to that list while its examples and members do not establish which source is authoritative.; tone-value says it is the allowed closed tone set of five values, while Action and Utterance semantics claim a shared closed 14-value enum and a normalization table not supplied in the glossary.; entity-category maps cup to mug, although cup and mug can be distinct requested physical objects; this synonym choice can change task meaning.; artifact-type's membership rule overlaps genre/category and does not distinguish an artifact class from a format, while story is used as a proxy for a script.; Open category membership rules depend on undefined English notions such as noun, normative operation, artistic composition, and structural software element, so they do not yield deterministic membership or canonical symbols.; The proposal does not supply category entries for structural words, construct names/key terms, attributes, attribute values beyond tone, literal forms, identifiers, or the numerous pre-existing operation and attribute symbols; N2 therefore remains substantially incomplete.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — The proposal makes real progress by adding separately named descriptive vocabulary categories with members, glosses, and examples across several requested gap areas. It nevertheless does not make the glossary the complete lexicon: structural words, key terms, most attribute/value categories, literal rules, and most existing language words remain absent, and the new categories are not integrated into syntax.
  - N3: violated — tone-value is a useful attempt at a value set, but it conflicts with the existing claimed 14-value Action/Utterance enum and leaves tone typed as STRING. The new competing authorities make the permissible tone values less determinate than the current asserted single enum.
  - N7: violated — No canonical mapping is defined between legacy STRING payloads and the new symbols, and unsupported structured attributes compete with existing content-string filters. The independent translations expose these unresolved alternatives.
  - N1: violated — The build phase correctly retains STRING and identifiers, but the proposed categories do not close any free channels or define all expression symbols. This is not itself a required closure sprint.
  - N4: violated — The proposal retains free-text channels as directed for the build phase. Its examples continue to place natural-language fragments in content and quoted attributes, but it does not remove or expand those channels beyond the current language.
  - N5: violated — The proposal adds some atomic symbolic members but neither defines literal forms for open values nor removes dependence on English prose, broad English membership judgments, and STRING payloads.
  - N6: partial — The categories are a move toward grammar over categories, but current grammar still uses IDENT and STRING for the relevant positions and does not reference the new categories. Missing members can sometimes be imagined within a category, but missing semantic categories remain common.
  - N8: partial — The added distinctions for operations, code concepts, roles, and artifacts increase potential richness. Their lack of compositional semantics and the coarse/overlapping categories prevent that richness from preserving most simulation constraints.
- **Decision:** needs-rework
- **Documenter summary:** Steering sprint on N2 (glossary as complete lexicon): the Shaper complied, proposing seven new descriptive vocabulary categories (operation-name, search-criterion, entity-category, role-recipient, code-structure, artifact-type, tone-value) to cover operations, criteria, entities, roles, code, artifacts, and tone. The Critic ruled needs-rework, judging the additions a genuine but incomplete step toward N2 since none were wired into Action/GENERATE/UTTER grammar (members still surface only as quoted STRING content), and most critically finding the new 5-value closed tone-value set contradicted the existing claimed 14-value tone enum, a determinism regression under hard note N3 that outweighed the coverage gains; doc hygiene passed but the decision still required rework.
- **Cost this sprint:** $0.7179

## Sprint 14 (attempt 2/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 9.0.0 → 9.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N2 [hard] The glossary is the complete lexicon of the language, covering every kind of word, not only operators.
- **Shaper proposal (model: gemini/gemini-3.1-pro-preview):** Restructures the glossary into structural words and descriptive vocabulary categories per N2, delegates operations and attributes to these categories, and introduces unquoted canonical symbols.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — These changes fully realize N2 by establishing the required vocabulary categories (structural-word, literal-form, attribute-name, operation-name, artifact-type, tone-value, role-recipient, search-criterion, code-structure, entity-category) with explicit membership rules, canonical symbol formation, and unquoted IDENT usage, directly integrating them into Action and Generate.
- **Changes:** `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `operation-name` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `search-criterion` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `code-structure` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `artifact-type` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `attribute-name` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `tone-value` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `role-recipient` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `entity-category` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `literal-form` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `structural-word` (add vocabulary, MINOR)
- **Shaper rebuttals:**
  - `basis`: I have added `structural-word`, `literal-form`, `attribute-name`, and `operation-name` to cover keywords, primitives, and attributes. I contest the requirement to create a separate `key-term` vocabulary category for construct names, because the existing glossary entries (Task, Action, Utterance, Check, Flow-If, Iterate, Bind, Conversation, Generate) already fulfill the 'Key terms' group defined in N2. Duplicating them into a vocabulary list would violate the single-source-of-truth principle.
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — This is meaningful progress on N2 because it adds multiple descriptive vocabulary categories and begins moving syntax toward category-directed terms. It cannot be accepted because the Action revision regresses N7 and N2's required no-coverage-reduction guarantee: most previously defined operations lose their contracts, while the new unquoted vocabulary symbols have no valid value syntax. Restoring a complete operation registry and defining typed glossary-symbol values would make the core approach viable.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — The additions name useful missing domains—search criteria, artifact classes, roles, entity categories, and code fields—but revising Action removes the current exhaustive operation signatures and replaces them with an undefined open-ended operation rule. Existing operations such as rinse, place, heat, sort, and search_travel no longer have defined signatures or result behavior, so previously expressible closed tasks are no longer reliably expressible.
  - expressivity: mixed — Structured criteria, source/destination, artifact type, and code-issue fields improve preservation of several requests. However, criterion values, ranking direction, code calls/arguments, location relations, and generation constraints remain unstructured; moreover, vocabulary symbols such as pillow and script have no defined value-level syntax or typing, making the intended unquoted encoding invalid under the retained value grammar.
  - determinism: harm — The new operation membership rule—'Any valid operation symbol' and 'prefer the most specific domain operation'—does not determine what is valid or which operation is most specific. It also leaves signatures outside the four listed members undefined. Independent translations already diverge on search targets, source versus target, ranking direction, and acquisition decomposition; the proposal does not resolve those divergences and removes the prior explicit operation inventory.
  - interpretability: mixed — The category names and member glosses make several concepts more discoverable. But structural-word claims to be closed while omitting many reserved words, operators, delimiters, and types, and its membership rule points to an unspecified grammar inventory. Readers cannot determine whether an unquoted token is a bound IDENT, a glossary symbol, or an invalid identifier, nor can they recover signatures for most actions.
  - improvement: mixed — A weaker model would benefit from explicit artifact, role, tone, entity, and code-field labels when they are usable. In practice it would be harmed on operational planning because it cannot safely infer the contract of common actions after Action's signature table is removed, and the current examples demonstrate invalid or underspecified symbol use.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `dev-embodied-2` (seed_tasks) [Fail] NL: "Put both pillows from the sofa onto the armchair, one at a time." → `ENTRYPOINT MovePillows
TASK MovePillows {
  ACTION pick_up(target=pillow, source=sofa) -> pillow : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(target=pillow, destination=armchair)
  ACTION pick_up(target=pillow, source=sofa) -> pillow2 : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(target=pillow2, destination=armchair)
}` — The intended entity symbols are unbound IDENTs under the retained value grammar, and place has no signature in the proposed operation-name members. Action's former place signature is removed.
  - `dev-embodied-1` (seed_tasks) [Fail] NL: "Rinse the mug in the sink, then put it in the coffee maker." → `ENTRYPOINT RinseThenPlace
TASK RinseThenPlace {
  ACTION rinse(target=mug, destination=sink) -> mug : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(target=mug, destination=coffee_maker)
}` — rinse and place are absent from the new operation-name member list and have no defined signature under its open rule; mug, sink, and coffee_maker also lack valid glossary-symbol value syntax.
  - `86afd67c-1bff-455c-baa7-e18dcb64b0f3` (Mind2Web) [Fail] NL: "Find comedy tv shows on netflix sorted by audience score." → `ENTRYPOINT FindComedyShows
TASK FindComedyShows : LIST[REF[STRING]] {

  ACTION sort(target=tv_shows, ordering=audience_score_desc) -> sorted_tv_shows : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  RETURN sorted_tv_shows
}` — search_web has no defined source attribute, sort and ordering are undefined after the Action revision, and neither audience_score nor descending ranking is represented by search-criterion.
  - `bf469f30-6628-4017-b963-672645d7feab` (Mind2Web) [Fail] NL: "Find the highest rated dog collar under 10 dollar." → `ENTRYPOINT FindDogCollar

  ACTION sort(target=dog_collars, ordering=rating_desc) -> ranked_dog_collars : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  RETURN ranked_dog_collars
}` — The proposal helps with price and rating dimensions, but supplies neither rating-scale semantics nor rank/order categories. sort has no proposed signature, and entity/value symbols are not legal literals.
  - `trial_T20190907_223758_523581#1` (ALFRED) [Fail] NL: "Microwave a slice of apple and place it on the table." → `ENTRYPOINT MicrowaveAppleSlice
TASK MicrowaveAppleSlice {
  ACTION pick_up(target=apple) -> apple : REF[STRING]
  ACTION slice(target=apple) -> apple_slices : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  ACTION extract(target=apple_slices, limit=1) -> apple_slice : REF[STRING]
  ACTION heat(target=apple_slice, destination=microwave) -> apple_slice : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(target=apple_slice, destination=table)
}` — Only pick_up remains listed. slice, extract, heat, and place lost their explicit contracts, and re-binding apple_slice after heat violates the no-shadowing rule retained in Bind.
  - `trial_T20190908_224836_919680#2` (ALFRED) [Fail] NL: "Put a microwaved slice of apple on the table in the corner." → `ENTRYPOINT MicrowaveAppleSlice

  ACTION heat(target=apple_slice, destination=microwave) -> heated_apple_slice : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(target=heated_apple_slice, destination=corner_table)
}` — The intended decomposition is clear, but slice, extract, heat, and place have no defined operation signature; corner_table is an unsupported location-composition value.
  - `scikit-learn__scikit-learn-11578` (SWE-bench_Verified) [Partial] NL: "For probabilistic scorers, LogisticRegressionCV(multi_class='multinomial') uses OvR to calculate scores." → `ENTRYPOINT FixLogisticRegressionScoring

}` — code-structure supplies useful field names and modify_code remains listed, but calls, constructor arguments, assertions, tests, and exact expected code are still opaque STRING payloads. No atomic code literal/category is defined.
  - `django__django-13112` (SWE-bench_Verified) [Partial] NL: "makemigrations crashes for ForeignKey with mixed-case app name." → `ENTRYPOINT FixMixedCaseAppName
TASK FixMixedCaseAppName {

}` — The module and expected/actual labels help, but the affected relation, lazy-reference call, installed-app configuration, reproduction command, and asserted behavior remain prose strings.
  - `c6516` (PRISM) [Fail] NL: "Can you give me some great places to visit in the Netherlands?" → `ENTRYPOINT NetherlandsPlaces
TASK NetherlandsPlaces : LIST[STRING] {
  GENERATE(target=guide, subject="places to visit in the Netherlands", audience=user) -> places : LIST[STRING]
  RETURN places
}` — guide and user are intended vocabulary symbols but are unbound IDENT values under the retained grammar. Quoting guide would violate the revised Generate rule that target must be an artifact-type symbol.
  - `c3388` (PRISM) [Fail] NL: "hi how hs your day been?" → `ENTRYPOINT GreetingReply
TASK GreetingReply : STRING {
  GENERATE(target=response, audience=user, tone=friendly, subject="reply to a greeting and ask how the user's day has been") -> reply : STRING
  RETURN reply
}` — The requested social response and friendly register are represented in intent, but Generate's unquoted artifact/role/tone values have no valid value-level symbol semantics.
  - `c2067` (PRISM) [Fail] NL: "Can you breifly explain American politics to me?" → `ENTRYPOINT ExplainAmericanPolitics
TASK ExplainAmericanPolitics : STRING {
  GENERATE(target=response, audience=user, subject="American politics", constraints="brief explanation") -> explanation : STRING
  RETURN explanation
}` — The artifact target is invalid as an unbound IDENT, and brevity is merely prose in constraints because no length/conciseness value category exists.
  - `c2904` (PRISM) [Fail] NL: "Tell me about what is happening in New Zealand Politically" → `ENTRYPOINT ExplainNewZealandPolitics
TASK ExplainNewZealandPolitics : STRING {
  GENERATE(target=response, audience=user, subject="current political events in New Zealand") -> explanation : STRING
  RETURN explanation
}` — The information-request structure is present, but response and user have no legal symbol-literal interpretation and the current-events requirement remains prose.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-73283` (PATHs) [Fail] NL: "Introduce the topic of Impact of Dam Construction on Seasonal Precipitation Pattern, Land Use/Cover, and Socio-economic in the semi-arid Region, Eastern Sudan." → `ENTRYPOINT IntroduceDamImpact

  RETURN introduction
}` — technical_introduction is a valuable new artifact type, but it cannot be used as an unbound value under the grammar. The substantive topic remains a permitted build-phase STRING payload.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-76731` (PATHs) [Fail] NL: "which of dostoevsky works has most parallels with "The Holy Blood and the Holy Grail" book?" → `ENTRYPOINT CompareDostoevskyWorks

  GENERATE(target=response, audience=user, subject="which Dostoevsky work has the strongest parallels with The Holy Blood and the Holy Grail") -> comparison : STRING
  RETURN comparison

  - `wildchat1m_en3u-137561` (PATHs) [Fail] NL: "Write a story of when they met the scientist, who is amazed they can speak." → `ENTRYPOINT GloopiterScientistStory

  GENERATE(target=story, audience=user, subject="Gloopy and Glitter meet a scientist who is amazed that they can speak") -> story_output : STRING
  RETURN story_output
}` — story is a useful proposed artifact type, but the revised Generate construct cannot legally consume it as currently specified; character, event, and reaction content are still opaque prose.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-78000` (PATHs) [Fail] NL: "Write me a legal document and contract for a high demand speaker, saying not enough time and our speaker is in very high demand." → `ENTRYPOINT HighDemandSpeakerContract

  RETURN contract_output
