  - `1776009617894` (ThoughtTrace) [Fail] NL: "Can you suggest a posting schedule to help the channel grow quickly in its first month?" → `ENTRYPOINT ChannelPostingSchedule
TASK ChannelPostingSchedule : STRING {
  GENERATE(target=guide, audience=user, subject="first-month posting schedule for the Hamoudi children's educational YouTube channel", constraints="support rapid channel growth") -> schedule : STRING
  RETURN schedule

  - `1775429344935` (ThoughtTrace) [Fail] NL: "Help me to create a text about my decision to go to Japan to learn about SGI. Only 100 words." → `ENTRYPOINT JapanSGIText

  RETURN text

  - `1775752130482` (ThoughtTrace) [Fail] NL: "hello, i want to plan a 3 day safari in eastern cape south africa" → `ENTRYPOINT PlanEasternCapeSafari
TASK PlanEasternCapeSafari : STRING {

  RETURN itinerary_output

  - `1775479438859` (ThoughtTrace) [Fail] NL: ""Gain a short certification if it opens a stable field" examples in UK context?" → `ENTRYPOINT UKCertificationExamples
TASK UKCertificationExamples : STRING {
  GENERATE(target=guide, audience=user, subject="short UK certifications leading to stable mortgage-friendly employment") -> examples : STRING
  RETURN examples
}` — The response has the right broad intent, but guide and user are not valid value symbols and the vocabulary has no employment, certification, jurisdiction, lending, or stability categories.
- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=low — Independent translations disagree materially: the pillow task differs on whether the second target is pillow or pillow2; comedy-show translations disagree on target/source and score direction; the apple task differs between full acquisition/slicing and direct heating; code translations differ on structured fields versus content; and Netherlands recommendations differ between GENERATE and search_web. The proposal leaves these choices unresolved and further makes the intended unquoted vocabulary values syntactically/semantically undefined.
- **Required changes:**
  - `Action and operation-name`: Retain or move every existing normative operation signature, result category, and attribute contract into operation-name before deleting Action's table. Make operation-name either a finite complete registry or an explicit extension mechanism that defines each operation's signature and result category; remove 'Any valid operation symbol' and 'prefer the most specific domain operation.' Add current operations including rinse, place, heat, slice, extract, sort, search_travel, communication, filesystem, and support operations.
  - `Types & Lexicon, value, and all descriptive categories`: Add a value production and static typing rule for glossary symbols, distinct from bound IDENT references, e.g. value ::= glossary_symbol with category-directed validation. Define whether each vocabulary token is an atomic symbol, a bound identifier, or a literal, and revise all examples so unquoted values such as pillow, script, user, polite, and sci_fi parse and type-check.
  - `structural-word`: Make the closed structural inventory actually complete: list every retained keyword, type token, operator, delimiter, and lexical primitive, including ENTRYPOINT, CONVO, TURN, THEN, ELSE, FOR, EACH, IN, ANY, ALL, SATISFIES, GENERATE, UTTER, REPLY_TO, REVISES, DECREASES, TRUE, FALSE, punctuation delimiters, arrows, colon, assignment, all comparison operators, and COMMENT; remove the circular 'any reserved word ... defined in the grammar' membership rule.
  - `search-criterion`: Define typed values and relations for genre, category, ratings and rating scale, price amount, currency, comparator/bounds, source/provider, ranking field, and ranking direction. Revise search_web, select_filter/apply_filters, and sort signatures and give one canonical search/filter/ranking representation.
  - `code-structure and literal-form`: Add glossary-defined atomic literal forms/categories for paths, code symbols, calls, argument/value assignments, assertions, commands, and test identifiers. Define modify_code and run_tests signatures that consume module/path, symbol kind/name, call, arguments, expected result, actual result, reproduction/test, and ensure the SWE examples can avoid opaque English payloads when the closing phase arrives.
  - `artifact-type and Generate`: Add missing artifact classes/rules for contract/legal document, plan/schedule, personal statement, comparison/recommendation, and any other format distinguished by the request. Define structured values for length, duration, locale/jurisdiction, platform, audience, and constraints; preserve the current valid quoted-target form until glossary-symbol values have a valid grammar, or make the symbol-literal rule in the same revision.
  - `entity-category and location vocabulary`: Separate entity type, instance descriptor, location/receptacle, spatial relation, and descriptor composition. Add a defined location category and relations sufficient for sofa, armchair, sink, microwave, coffee maker, table, corner table, and positional requests; make composition rules type-safe rather than treating arbitrary modifier strings as symbols.
  - `attribute-name, role-recipient, and tone-value`: Give every attribute an explicit permitted value category and operation applicability table, including recipient/audience/tone/source/destination/subject/format/constraints. Define role versus participant identity versus recipient identity and a precise proper-name/organization atomic identifier rule. Revise tone examples to use the new symbol-value syntax consistently.
  - `basis`: Add a glossary Lexicon organization that explicitly identifies existing construct entries as the single authoritative Key Terms group, while adding cross-references from structural and descriptive categories. Audit every token usable in every retained grammar production against a glossary member or exact literal rule.
- **Logic issues:** Action's revised grammar requires operation-name and attribute-name categories, but operation-name's membership rule is circular and its four members do not cover the prior Action operation inventory.; The proposal's unquoted canonical symbols are not legal value literals: value currently treats IDENT as a variable/reference-like identifier, while no grammar or semantics makes a vocabulary member a typed value.; The Action example uses book, sci_fi, and minimum_rating=4.0 without defining book/sci_fi as valid values or defining the scale and type of minimum_rating.; The Generate example uses target=script, audience=user, genre=comedy, and rating=seventeen_plus, but genre and rating are not declared Generate attributes/value categories and the symbols are unbound IDENTs.; structural-word is declared closed but omits most of the existing lexical inventory and uses an open circular membership rule; its worked example uses THEN, braces, TRUE, and numeric literals that are not all listed as members.; literal-form labels STRING, URL, and PATH as literal forms but supplies no exact lexical pattern for IDENT, URL, PATH, NUMBER, or STRING beyond references to pre-existing grammar; URL and PATH are additionally said to be STRING literals rather than distinct literal forms.; role-recipient permits examples such as Alice and Acme_Corp but its alphanumeric rule contradicts Acme_Corp and does not state case normalization or collision rules.; The source/target and target/location semantics overlap across search and household operations without an applicability table, so translators can make incompatible but grammatical choices.; The phrase 'target must be a symbol from artifact-type, never a generic STRING' breaks existing valid Generate expressions before a value-level artifact-symbol syntax exists, violating the N2 build-phase no-coverage-reduction instruction.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — The proposal makes real progress by introducing structural, literal, attribute, operation, entity, role, tone, search, code, and artifact categories with examples. It does not fully realize a complete glossary: structural-word omits much of the language's lexical inventory, operation-name removes definitions for most existing operation words, key-term coverage is only implicit, and many category membership/value rules remain incomplete.
  - N3: violated — Although tone-value is now explicit, nearly all other attributes still accept unrestricted value, STRING, or IDENT forms. Search criterion values, subject, source, destination, recipient, audience, format, constraints, code fields, rating, genre, currency, and ordering do not have authoritative value sets or derivation rules.
  - N7: violated — Canonical attribute ordering is a useful addition, but determinism regresses because the prior explicit Action operation/signature registry is replaced by 'Any valid operation symbol' and a non-algorithmic preference for the most specific operation. Common operations now have no defined signature, and criteria/ranking mappings remain noncanonical.
  - N1: violated — Free STRING and unrestricted IDENT channels remain, as required in this build phase. The proposed categories do not yet make every usable expression token traceable because category symbols lack a valid lexical/value integration rule.
  - N4: violated — STRING content, subject, constraints, expected_output, and actual_output continue to contain natural-language fragments. Retaining them is permitted during the stated N2 build phase, but the proposal does not yet provide symbolic replacements.
  - N5: violated — literal-form and several category rules are progress toward self-containment, especially for names and descriptor composition. They remain incomplete and depend on English notions such as 'valid operation,' semantic category, and standard URL/POSIX formats without exact self-contained token rules.
  - N6: partial — The revised Action and Generate grammars begin to quantify over glossary categories rather than fixed words. Missing categories remain real gaps—especially locations, rank/order, code literals, schedules/plans, and contracts—and the symbol categories cannot yet be used as values.
  - N8: partial — The added categories preserve distinctions among artifacts, roles, tones, code fields, and search dimensions. Richness is still lost or hidden in prose for ranking, location, software calls/arguments, constraints, and several artifact forms; the undefined operation rule also makes the added richness unusable for planning.
- **Rebuttal rulings:**
  - `basis`: upheld — A separate duplicate key-term vocabulary list is unnecessary if the existing construct glossary entries are explicitly retained as the authoritative Key Terms group. The single-source-of-truth argument is sound, but the basis still needs an explicit Lexicon organization/cross-reference and a complete structural/descriptive token audit.
- **Decision:** needs-rework
- **Documenter summary:** Shaper proposed restructuring the glossary per N2 into structural-word, literal-form, attribute-name, operation-name, artifact-type, tone-value, role-recipient, search-criterion, code-structure, and entity-category vocabularies, revising Action and Generate to delegate operations/attributes to these categories, and rebutted (successfully) a required duplicate key-term list. The Critic ruled needs-rework: while the new categories advance N2's coverage, the Action revision replaces the prior exhaustive operation registry with a circular 'any valid operation symbol' rule that leaves common operations (rinse, place, heat, slice, sort, etc.) undefined, and the new unquoted vocabulary symbols (e.g. pillow, script, user) have no valid value-level grammar, causing 18 of 21 simulated items to fail coverage and regressing N7 determinism. The Critic upheld the Shaper's key-term rebuttal but required a complete operation registry and typed glossary-symbol value rules before acceptance.
- **Cost this sprint:** $0.8110

## Sprint 14 (attempt 3/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 9.0.0 → 9.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N2 [hard] The glossary is the complete lexicon of the language, covering every kind of word, not only operators.
- **Shaper proposal (model: gemini/gemini-3.1-pro-preview):** Introduces the SYMBOL type and category-directed value resolution to make unquoted descriptive vocabulary valid syntax, addressing all coverage and typing regressions. Retains the complete Action operation registry with updated SYMBOL signatures, and adds the required exhaustive structural, literal, and descriptive vocabulary categories.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — These changes realize N2 by establishing the Lexicon organization, providing the SYMBOL type for unquoted vocabulary, and adding the required descriptive categories (artifacts, entities, roles, tones, attributes, search values, and code structures) while preserving existing operation coverage.
- **Changes:** `Types & Lexicon` (revise, MAJOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `structural-word` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `literal-form` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `code-structure` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `artifact-type` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `attribute-name` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `search-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `tone-value` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `role-recipient` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `entity-category` (add vocabulary, MINOR)
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — This is a substantive N2 increment: it introduces descriptive glossary categories, a SYMBOL direction, and retains much of the operation registry. It cannot be accepted because the claimed lexicon is not complete and several additions are not connected to valid grammar or typed operation signatures; PATH-like forms in particular regress currently expressible filesystem and code tasks. The determinism regression from open coinage and undefined criteria also violates hard-note no-regression gating.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — The new SYMBOL mechanism and operation registry improve household and web-task coverage, including rinse, slice, sorting, and structured search criteria. However, revised signatures make existing filesystem and software tasks ill-typed because PATH, URL, ISO_DATE, and CODE_SYMBOL are not types or value productions, and modify_code no longer accepts several needed bug-report fields. Store-selection, transit routing, and several code-report requirements still lack operations or applicable attributes.
  - expressivity: mixed — The additions preserve more descriptive distinctions than the prior string-only form for entities, tones, artifact types, ranking, and roles. But several proposed examples still encode meaningful material as STRING, the code-action signature excludes reproduction/environment/assertion material, and entity-category conflates object classes, locations, providers, and composed descriptions; this prevents faithful structured encoding of several sampled requests.
  - determinism: harm — Unbound IDENT resolution is a useful direction, but arbitrary open-category membership rules such as any lowercase underscore-joined word leave translators free to coin different symbols. `criteria...`, `attribute-name`, and the absence of a defined canonical route/store-selection operation invite divergent encodings. Independent translations already disagree sharply on whether store change and bug reports are expressible and on TASK versus CONVO for comparison requests.
  - interpretability: harm — The proposal claims an exhaustive structural and literal lexicon while omitting grammar tokens and lexical forms it relies on, including CONTEXT, ISO_DATE syntax, PATH syntax, URL syntax, CODE_SYMBOL syntax, UNSIGNED_INT, HEX, comparison punctuation as individually glossed members, and grammar meta-operators. Literal-form also duplicates NUMBER, STRING, BOOL, and IDENT with incompatible roles, while entity-category combines categories that need distinct lookup and typing.
  - improvement: mixed — Typed symbolic targets, roles, tones, ranking fields, and artifact classes would help a weaker agent plan ordinary household, search, and generation requests. Yet an agent cannot reliably execute expressions using undefined PATH/URL/ISO_DATE forms or infer whether arbitrary attributes are legal, and the lost code-report structure makes planning software fixes worse for affected requests.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `dev-code-1` (seed_tasks) [Fail] NL: "Given a list of file paths, delete every file older than 30 days unless it's in the 'archive' folder." → `ENTRYPOINT DeleteOldFiles
TASK DeleteOldFiles(files: LIST[PATH]) {
  FOR EACH file IN files {
    ACTION file_age_days(target=file) -> age : NUMBER
    ACTION path_in_folder(target=file, destination="archive") -> in_archive : BOOL
    IF age > 30 AND NOT in_archive THEN {
      ACTION delete_file(target=file)
    }
  }
}` — The intended expression requires PATH as a type, but PATH is only named as a literal-form glossary member and is absent from `type` and `value`; quoted paths are STRING, not PATH. The new Action signatures therefore make the previously expressible filesystem task ill-typed.
  - `dev-email-1` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Draft a reply to my professor asking for a two-day extension on the assignment, and keep it formal." → `ENTRYPOINT DraftExtensionReply
TASK DraftExtensionReply : STRING {
  GENERATE(target=email, audience=professor, subject="assignment extension request", constraints="request a two-day extension", tone=formal) -> reply : STRING
  RETURN reply
}` — Generate, artifact-type, role-recipient, and tone-value help. The request substance remains in permitted STRING during this build phase.
  - `b7082615-e6e1-4981-b51b-9259671d1adf` (Mind2Web) [Partial] NL: "Change your store to the one nearest to 07055" → `ENTRYPOINT ChangeStore
TASK ChangeStore {
  ACTION search_web(target=store, location="07055") -> stores : LIST[REF[SYMBOL]]
  ACTION sort(target=stores, rank_field=distance, rank_direction=asc) -> nearest_stores : LIST[REF[SYMBOL]]
  ACTION extract(target=nearest_stores, limit=1) -> nearest_store : REF[SYMBOL]
  ACTION click(target=nearest_store)
}` — Search, sort, and extract approximate discovery, but no operation commits the selected store as the active store. `location` and `distance` also have no explicit value/applicability definitions; Action's arbitrary criteria notation is insufficiently specified.
  - `e2adf8f1-547d-4671-96c1-4a21a56e135d` (Mind2Web) [Fail] NL: "View the upcoming schedule from Otis St@Summer St to City Point of the transit near South Station for today." → `ENTRYPOINT ViewTransitSchedule
TASK ViewTransitSchedule {
  ACTION search_web(target=transit_schedule, source=South_Station, origin="Otis St@Summer St", destination=City_Point, date="today") -> schedules : LIST[REF[SYMBOL]]
  ACTION extract(target=schedules, limit=1) -> schedule : REF[SYMBOL]
  ACTION read_page(target=schedule) -> schedule_text : STRING
}` — No transit-route or schedule operation exists, `date` is not a valid search_web criterion with an ISO_DATE rule, and the required route selection semantics are absent. This cannot faithfully represent the finite actionable core.
  - `trial_T20190908_120422_949453#2` (ALFRED) [Full] NL: "Move a red rag into a sink." → `ENTRYPOINT MoveRedRag
TASK MoveRedRag {
  ACTION pick_up(target=red_rag) -> red_rag : REF[SYMBOL]
  ACTION place(target=red_rag, destination=sink)
}` — The entity-category composition rule provides a usable red_rag symbol and the action registry preserves the required acquisition and placement sequence.

- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=low — Anthropic and Gemini diverge on whether the store-change, rag, Sphinx bug report, game comparison, and crossover story are expressible. Gemini uses undefined attributes and GAP placeholders; Anthropic identifies category gaps. Both dev-code translations also conflict with the proposal's PATH typing because PATH is not actually admitted by the proposed type/value grammar. These disagreements substantiate the remaining canonicalization and lexicon defects.
- **Required changes:**
  - `Types & Lexicon and literal-form`: Make every literal form operational rather than merely named: add PATH, URL, ISO_DATE, ISO_DATETIME if retained, CODE_SYMBOL, COMMAND, and UNSIGNED_INT either to `type` plus `value` productions or define their contextual subtype/coercion rules. Give each exact lexical grammar and static type. Resolve the duplicate use of NUMBER/STRING/BOOL/IDENT as both structural/type names and literal-form members.
  - `structural-word`: Audit every retained grammar production and list every structural token and lexical primitive explicitly, including CONTEXT, comparison operators, COMMENT, IDENT, STRING escape components, NUMBER components, UNSIGNED_INT, ISO_DATE syntax, PATH/URL delimiters if any, and all grammar delimiters. Do not claim an exhaustive inventory while relegating tokens to an unenumerated punctuation set.
  - `Key Terms / glossary organization`: Add an actual Key Terms glossary category with a membership rule or enumerated cross-references covering every retained construct, including Task, Action, Generate, Utterance, Check, Flow-If, Iterate, Bind, Conversation, and their deprecated aliases or remove deprecated aliases. Give the category its own worked example and make it the declared authoritative construct index.
  - `Action, attribute-name, and search-value`: Replace `<criteria>...` with a typed criterion grammar and an applicability table: define location/zip code, distance, route, date, source/provider, rating scale, price plus currency, genre/category, ranking field, and direction. Specify one canonical representation for filtering/sorting and add a set_store or equivalent operation for committing the nearest selected store.
  - `Action and code-structure`: Restore all previously supported operation contracts while extending them. Define a structured modify_code request accepting typed module/path, class/function, code call, argument list, assertion, reproduction step, expected result, actual result, environment/platform, and test identifier; define typed atomic value forms for each. Do not narrow existing `content` support before structured equivalents cover its actionable uses.
  - `entity-category and new vocabulary categories`: Split entity-category into separately lookupable entity-type, location/receptacle, provider, instance-descriptor, spatial-relation, and descriptor-modifier categories. Define type-safe composition that states which modifiers may apply to which category, rather than allowing any underscore-joined word to become an entity, artifact, search value, or attribute.
  - `Utterance and speech-act vocabulary`: Add a descriptive speech-act category containing the retained UTTER operation symbols (ask, respond, inform, propose, correct, acknowledge, confirm, decline) with canonical synonym mappings, and revise UTTER so its IDENT is validated against that category.
  - `attribute-name, artifact-type, and Generate`: Give every Generate attribute a closed or precisely typed category-based value rule and operation applicability. Add artifact types for invitation and other distinct existing generation targets, or an explicit deterministic artifact-type extension rule with synonym mapping. Replace STRING-permitted `format`, `length`, `duration`, `locale`, `platform`, and `constraints` alternatives with named symbolic categories where their values are descriptive.
  - `Types & Lexicon and all identifier-bearing constructs`: Specify the namespace and lookup status of top-level task/conversation names, operation names, attribute names, result bindings, and glossary symbols. Prevent a glossary symbol from silently colliding with a visible variable or a structural token, and define a deterministic escaping or namespace convention.
  - `basis`: Perform the promised complete-token audit against all unchanged grammar, semantics, and worked examples. Every token accepted by a valid expression must resolve to a structural member, Key Term, bound identifier, exact literal-form rule, or descriptive category; update stale examples and semantics that still require quoted tone values or undefined register_note/content attributes.
- **Logic issues:** The proposal says BrainCode has exactly six closed value types but `value` omits a glossary-symbol production distinct from IDENT; SYMBOL values are inferred through negative variable lookup, making lexical role depend on scope and leaving namespace collisions unspecified.; PATH, URL, ISO_DATE, CODE_SYMBOL, and COMMAND are used in Action signatures but do not appear in `type` or `value`; their literal-form glossary labels do not create syntax or static types.; The structural-word category is not exhaustive despite its gloss: CONTEXT and COMMENT are omitted from members, and the unenumerated punctuation set omits or inadequately distinguishes lexical primitives and grammar-level notation.; The `literal-form` category duplicates NUMBER, STRING, BOOL, and IDENT, but its gloss calls them atomic literal forms even though NUMBER/STRING are type names and IDENT is a lexical class rather than a literal value.; Action's `criteria...` grammar is not grammar: it supplies no allowed names, value types, ordering, duplicate rule, or applicability rule, while attribute-name's open underscore-word rule permits arbitrary incompatible attributes.; The revised modify_code signature rejects reproduction_step, assertion, environment data, calls, and arguments despite code-structure listing several of those names; listed vocabulary is not connected to a consuming operation signature.; entity-category's membership rule treats arbitrary modifier_category strings as symbols and conflates entities, locations, receptacles, and providers, contrary to the requested separate individually look-upable categories.; The Generate worked example uses STRING subject and constraints even though the proposal presents structured attributes as the solution; no symbolic values or membership rules exist for the detailed generation constraints.; The coreference naming rule says a REF binding must be the normalized descriptor noun, but the worked example binds `mug` after rinse and then returns it as `mug`; the grammar still lacks a way to distinguish a descriptor symbol from a bound value when the spelling is identical.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — The proposal makes real progress by adding a Lexicon organization, SYMBOL, structural-word, literal-form, and several descriptive categories with examples. It remains violated because the claimed complete lexicon omits usable structural and lexical forms, lacks a real Key Terms category, leaves UTTER speech acts open, and fails to provide separate, connected categories for several required code, location, and artifact concepts.
  - N3: violated — Many newly named attributes still permit STRING values, including subject, format, constraints, length, duration, locale, and platform; existing content and recipient STRING channels remain. This is allowed by the N2 build phase, but it does not realize N3 and is not a sound value-set definition.
  - N7: violated — The proposal introduces category-directed symbols and attribute ordering, but its open underscore-word membership rules, ungrammatical arbitrary criteria, and unresolved identifier/symbol collisions expand translator choice. The supplied independent translations demonstrate materially divergent encodings and expressibility judgments.
  - N1: violated — Free STRING and identifier channels remain by the prescribed build phase. Although the proposal adds categories, top-level identifiers, several lexical forms, and open UTTER names remain not fully traceable to glossary definitions.
  - N4: violated — STRING payloads remain intentionally available in this build phase, including subject, constraints, code expected/actual outputs, UTTER content, and generated artifact content. The proposal does not remove them, as N2 correctly instructs it not to do yet.
  - N5: violated — Although several atomic categories are proposed, the exact syntax of PATH, URL, CODE_SYMBOL, COMMAND, and ISO_DATE is not defined, and open underscore-word rules still rely on unbounded English-like token invention.
  - N6: partial — The syntax begins to refer to categories such as artifact-type, role-recipient, entity-category, and search-value instead of reshaping grammar around every listed member. However, missing categories for transit scheduling, store activation, structured code calls, and speech acts still force gaps rather than category-directed encoding.
  - N8: partial — Fine-grained tones, artifact types, search values, code fields, and entity examples improve richness. But the missing typed code, location, transit, schedule, and constraint vocabulary causes meaning to collapse back into STRING, and arbitrary composition sacrifices reliable rich decoding.
- **Decision:** needs-rework
- **Documenter summary:** Steering sprint on N2 (glossary must cover the entire lexicon, not just operators): Shaper proposed a SYMBOL type, category-directed IDENT resolution, and new structural-word/literal-form/entity-category/tone-value/etc. vocabulary categories while retaining the Action operation registry, claiming full compliance. Critic ruled needs-rework (not a rebuttal dispute, since Shaper filed none): despite genuine progress toward N2, the 'exhaustive' lexicon still omitted usable tokens (CONTEXT, COMMENT, PATH/URL/ISO_DATE/CODE_SYMBOL syntax) and left open underscore-word coinage rules, which the dev-code-1 (PATH typing breaks a previously working filesystem task) and Mind2Web store-change/transit simulations showed caused real coverage regressions and low cross-model agreement, violating the hard no-regression gate on determinism.
- **Cost this sprint:** $0.9155

## Sprint 15 (attempt 1/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 9.0.0 → 9.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N2 [hard] The glossary is the complete lexicon of the language, covering every kind of word, not only operators.
- **Shaper proposal (model: anthropic/claude-sonnet-5):** This increment builds the N2 descriptive-vocabulary lexicon with operational literal types (PATH/URL/ISO_DATE/CODE_SYMBOL/COMMAND/UNSIGNED_INT wired into type/value grammar), an exhaustive structural-word inventory, a Key Terms index, a restored+extended Action registry with typed search criteria and structured code-fix fields, split entity/location/provider categories, and a closed speech-act vocabulary for UTTER — fixing the coverage regressions the Critic flagged (filesystem PATH typing, code-fix structure, store-selection, transit search) while keeping STRING payload fallback per the build-phase rule.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — Every change adds or completes a glossary category (structural, key-term, or descriptive) or wires a construct to draw from one, per N2's three-group requirement; free-text channels remain available where not yet replaced, per the build-phase carve-out.
- **Changes:** `Types & Lexicon` (revise, MAJOR), `literal-form` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `structural-word` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `key-term` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `search-criterion` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `code-structure` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `entity-category` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `location-provider` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `speech-act` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `Utterance` (revise, MAJOR), `attribute-name` (add vocabulary, MAJOR)
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — The increment makes real progress on N2 and improves practical planning structure, especially for code and search tasks. It cannot be accepted because several claimed typed forms invalidate their own examples, important closed-task constraints remain unexpressible, and the newly introduced COMMAND form regresses hard note N5. Fixing the concrete lexical, category, and namespace gaps would preserve the promising vocabulary-building direction.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — The new operations improve coverage for transit searches, store selection, typed code-fix fields, and several household operations. However, sampled closed tasks still lose actionable constraints: pick_up has no source attribute, search_web has no gender/size/color criteria, and PATH rejects the relative repository paths used by both SWE-bench samples. Artifact, role-recipient, spatial-relation, and several attribute-value categories remain absent.
  - expressivity: mixed — Structured code fields, ranking fields, and typed date/path slots preserve more request structure than the current spec. But the proposal still requires opaque STRING payloads for essential closed-task constraints, cannot express the sofa source or ordered pair of pillows, cannot represent the refrigerator-on-table nesting, and lacks structured artifact specifications for the generation samples.
  - determinism: mixed — Canonical attribute ordering and the speech-act registry are genuine improvements. Determinism remains weak because the same search request can be represented either through named criteria or an opaque target/content STRING; category membership rules permit broad invented underscore identifiers; `UNSIGNED_INT_LIT` overlaps NUMBER lexically; and operation, task, turn, and attribute namespaces are still not comprehensively specified.
  - interpretability: mixed — The structural-word, key-term, literal-form, search-criterion, code-structure, and speech-act indexes make much more of the language discoverable. Yet the claimed exhaustive glossary is not exhaustive: operation names, many grammar-bearing identifiers, role-recipient values, tone values, artifact types, descriptor-modifier members, spatial relations, and Generate-specific semantics are not fully indexed. Several proposed examples are statically invalid under the proposed grammar.
  - improvement: benefit — A weaker agent can plan store selection, code repair, route lookup, and structured filtering better from the added Action signatures and criterion/code fields than from the current broad STRING interfaces. The benefit is limited for generation and many conversational tasks because their central substance remains opaque STRING content.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `dev-embodied-2` (seed_tasks) [Fail] NL: "Put both pillows from the sofa onto the armchair, one at a time." → `GAP("pick_up lacks source=sofa and the vocabulary/grammar has no deterministic representation for two distinct pillows acted on one at a time")` — location-provider helps name sofa and armchair, but Action does not accept source on pick_up; separate unqualified pillow acquisitions would not preserve the source or determinate identity.
  - `dev-email-1` (seed_tasks) [Partial] NL: "Draft a reply to my professor asking for a two-day extension on the assignment, and keep it formal." → `ENTRYPOINT DraftExtension

TASK DraftExtension : STRING {

  RETURN reply
}` — The request is retained through STRING content. There is no role-recipient category for professor, no artifact-specification category for reply/email, and no symbolic request_extension/duration representation.
  - `42657330-bfc5-425d-ae21-396a9ba1fb12` (Mind2Web (osunlp/Mind2Web, train, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Partial] NL: "Find yellow t-shirts for women small size that are less than $20." → `ENTRYPOINT FindTShirts

  RETURN t_shirts

  - `wildchat1m_en3u-81276` (PATHs (microsoft/prototypical-hai-collaborations, wildchat1m_en3u-task_utterance_wintent_anns, model=gpt-3.5-turbo-0613)) [Partial] NL: "Write a detailed story about Sadie Adler taking off her hat, and dropping it on the ground. She reaches up to her scalp, and begins peeling away her skin. Sadie Adler, now Mary Beth, sighs in relief as she tears away the remainders of her old face." → `ENTRYPOINT WriteStory

TASK WriteStory : STRING {
  GENERATE(target="story", content="Write a detailed story about Sadie Adler taking off her hat, dropping it on the ground, peeling away her skin, becoming Mary Beth, and sighing in relief while removing the old face.") -> story : STRING
  RETURN story

  RETURN explanation

  - `wildchat1m_en3u-122879` (PATHs (microsoft/prototypical-hai-collaborations, wildchat1m_en3u-task_utterance_wintent_anns, model=gpt-4-1106-preview)) [Partial] NL: "Emma was having the best summer ever, working as a counselor at Camp Sludge. The campers - and Emma - would always be covered in a wonderful medley of muck by lunchtime. Emma strolled through the camp, wearing the same uniform the campers wore - shorts, a T-shirt and absolutely no shoes!

Emma finds a group of campers enjoying themselves in the sludge pit. She teases them and asks if anyone fancies having a food fight. She tempts the campers to join in by mentioning the camp has just received a huge quantity of rotten cheese sauce and some other "surprises".

Please continue this story in a fun, entertaining and creative way. Avoid flowery language or talking about liberation and freedom - the campers just think it’s fun getting messy." → `ENTRYPOINT ContinueCampSludge

- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=low — Independent translations disagree materially on source handling for pillows/goblet, whether women's category is representable, target-path spelling, inclusion of expected-output details, and even whether a CONVO needs ENTRYPOINT. The proposal resolves neither pick_up source nor relative PATH validity, and leaves opaque STRING alternatives that preserve divergent encodings.
- **Required changes:**
  - `Types & Lexicon and literal-form`: Revise PATH_LIT to accept a precisely defined repository-relative path form in addition to absolute POSIX and Windows forms, or add a separate REPO_PATH type and use it in modify_code/run_tests/add_file/delete_file. Make the stated PATH definition validate every retained and newly added example.
  - `Types & Lexicon`: Eliminate lexical ambiguity between NUMBER and UNSIGNED_INT_LIT: define longest/priority tokenization or make UNSIGNED_INT a contextual numeric constraint rather than a competing value token. Add UNSIGNED_INT to the type/value account consistently, including Generate.
  - `Action`: Add source=SYMBOL|REF[T] to pick_up with a defined category/applicability rule, and add a deterministic representation for selecting multiple same-type source objects sequentially (for example ordinal/quantity selection plus per-item iteration).
  - `Action, search-criterion, entity-category, and attribute-name`: Add typed search/filter criteria and value categories for color, size, gender/audience class, product type, rating scale, route/provider, and multi-criterion ranking. Specify canonical representation for two or more ranking dimensions rather than falling back to target/content STRING.
  - `entity-category and new vocabulary categories`: Complete the required split by adding individually lookupable role-recipient, provider, instance-descriptor, spatial-relation, and descriptor-modifier categories. Define which modifiers apply to which entity/location categories; add refrigerator, freezer, counter, armchair, goblet, potato, and other required members through the category rules.
  - `Generate, artifact-type, and attribute-name`: Add artifact-type and generation-specification categories for reply/email, story, story continuation, character sheet, technical explanation/introduction, report, list, and invitation. Define typed symbolic values for format, length/paragraph count, audience/recipient role, style, exclusions, locale, duration, and constraints while retaining STRING fallback only where the build-phase carve-out requires it.
  - `structural-word, key-term, and complete-token audit`: Perform an actual retained-grammar audit. Add all operation identifiers and grammar-used symbolic names to descriptive vocabulary categories; account for IDENT and exact lexical subcomponents by a rule rather than claiming an exhaustive token list; reconcile COMMENT '#' with examples using '--'; and either index deprecated construct aliases in key-term or remove the deprecated construct entries.
  - `all identifier-bearing constructs`: Define disjoint namespaces and lookup/escaping rules for top-level task/conversation names, turn names, operation symbols, attribute names, variables, result bindings, glossary symbols, and reserved words. The current variable-versus-glossary shadowing rule does not cover the other collisions.
  - `N3 attribute-value categories`: Give every attribute currently accepted by Action, Utterance, and Generate an explicit category-based value set or derivation rule, including recipient, audience, target categories, tone, register_note, format, constraints, content, deadline, subject, origin, source, destination, and all code/search attributes. Mark transitional STRING channels as build-phase fallbacks, not as satisfying N3.
  - `COMMAND literal-form`: Replace COMMAND's undefined English-like 'shell-token sequence' with a self-contained formal token grammar and escaping/quoting rules, or defer COMMAND until such a grammar exists. A space-separated command language is not an atomic literal under N5.
- **Logic issues:** The proposed literal-form worked example ACTION modify_code(target="sphinx/util/inspect.py") is invalid because PATH_LIT accepts only absolute POSIX paths beginning with '/' or Windows DRIVE:\ paths.; The Types & Lexicon worked example passes destination="archive" to path_in_folder even though the revised Action signature requires destination=PATH; "archive" is not a PATH_LIT.; NUMBER and UNSIGNED_INT_LIT both lexically accept values such as 1 and 0, but no lexer-priority or contextual disambiguation rule is provided.; COMMAND is described using an undefined shell-token language rather than an exact lexical grammar, and it permits multiword natural-language-like content despite N5's atomic-literal direction.; The structural-word category claims to be exhaustive but Action operation names, attribute identifiers, task/call identifiers, and several lexical grammar components are not structural members or members of another complete category.; key-term excludes deprecated aliases while the specification retains deprecated types-lexicon, action, and bind construct entries; this does not satisfy the stated 1:1 authoritative-index rule.; entity-category's descriptor-modifier rule references descriptive-modifier vocabulary, but no such vocabulary category is added.; location-provider's membership rule permits arbitrary common lowercase underscore words and arbitrary capitalized proper-name forms without a canonical synonym mapping, undermining deterministic encoding.; Action signatures use broad SYMBOL positions without saying which descriptive category is accepted in each position, so a reader cannot determine whether a value is an entity, location, provider, role, criterion value, or arbitrary symbol.; The Action revision claims all prior supported signatures remain intact, but it changes target and result descriptive typing from STRING/REF[STRING] to SYMBOL/REF[SYMBOL] without a migration/coercion rule and removes the prior default-operation fallback.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — This is substantial progress: literal forms, structural words, key terms, speech acts, search criteria, code structures, and some entity/location vocabulary are now explicit glossary categories. It remains violated because the glossary still lacks complete categories for operation words, roles/recipients, artifact specifications, tone values, spatial relations, descriptor modifiers, and many grammar-bearing symbols; several categories also have non-deterministic membership rules.
  - N3: violated — attribute-name now identifies many attributes and cross-references some value rules, which improves the basis. But numerous attributes retain unrestricted STRING values and lack per-attribute glossary value sets; the proposal expressly leaves content, constraints, format, register_note, subject, origin, code fields, and other values outside N3-compliant symbolic definitions.
  - N7: violated — Canonical attribute ordering, speech-act closure, and some typed criteria improve encoding convergence. It is still violated because broad composition/membership rules, STRING-versus-structured alternatives, unresolved namespace rules, and NUMBER/UNSIGNED_INT lexical overlap prevent one canonical encoding and, in the overlap case, one unambiguous parse.
  - N1: violated — The build phase correctly retains STRING and free-text channels, so N1 is not expected to be realized. The new categories help future closure, but valid expressions still contain unrestricted identifiers and free-text content not traceable to glossary members or a sufficiently complete glossary-defined rule.
  - N4: violated — Prose remains in STRING content, constraints, code-report fields, read fields, and COMMAND-like values. Retaining those channels is permitted by N2's current build-phase instruction, so this increment does not worsen the pre-existing closing-phase violation.
  - N5: violated — PATH, URL, ISO_DATE, and CODE_SYMBOL move toward self-contained literal forms, but COMMAND newly defines a value as a shell-token sequence using undefined external shell/English token conventions and permits multi-token text. That expands rather than contains dependence on an external language, worsening the current state.
  - N6: partial — The grammar now begins to use categories such as speech-act, search criterion, and code structure instead of only today's members. It remains partial because many slots are plain SYMBOL or STRING without a category, and missing category cases in the simulations require prose or gaps rather than a category-symbol assumption.
  - N8: partial — Structured code-fix and search fields increase meaningful distinctions. Richness is still lost for source relations, nested locations, artifact constraints, roles, multiple ranking criteria, and generation content because those concepts are left as prose or cannot be expressed.
- **Decision:** needs-rework
- **Documenter summary:** Steering note N2 (glossary must cover the full lexicon, not just operators) was addressed by a large Shaper pass adding literal-form, structural-word, key-term, search-criterion, code-structure, entity-category, location-provider, and speech-act vocabularies plus revised Action/Utterance/Types constructs; the Shaper complied with N2 rather than contesting it. The Critic found genuine progress (mixed-to-benefit KPI impact) but ruled needs-rework because several worked examples were invalid under the proposal's own PATH/attribute grammar, key constraints (pick_up source, multi-criterion search, spatial relations, artifact types) remained unexpressible, and the new COMMAND literal-form regressed hard note N5 by depending on an undefined external shell grammar—no rebuttals were offered to weigh.
- **Cost this sprint:** $1.1691

## Sprint 15 (attempt 2/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 9.0.0 → 9.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N2 [hard] The glossary is the complete lexicon of the language, covering every kind of word, not only operators.
- **Shaper proposal (model: anthropic/claude-sonnet-5):** Fixes the lexical bugs that invalidated prior examples (PATH vs repo-relative paths, NUMBER/UNSIGNED_INT overlap, undefined COMMAND form removed for N5), and adds the concrete missing vocabulary the Critic identified: spatial-relation, descriptor-modifier, tone-value, artifact-type, plus expanded entity/location/search-criterion members and Action attributes (source, relation, nested destination) so the Fail-graded ALFRED/embodied/SWE-bench items become expressible.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — This sprint closes the concrete lexical bugs (PATH/REPO_PATH, NUMBER/UNSIGNED_INT overlap, undefined COMMAND) that made prior examples statically invalid, adds the missing categories needed for the Fail-graded closed-task items (spatial-relation for nested containment, descriptor-modifier and expanded search-criterion for color/size/audience filters, expanded entity-category/location-provider membership, source/relation attributes on Action), and adds tone-value and artifact-type to start closing two previously-undefined-but-referenced attribute value sets. Deeper categories (role-recipient, instance-descriptor, full generation-specification depth, a full operation-identifier index) are deferred one more increment, per rebuttals below, to keep this step coherent and fully specified rather than an overreaching redesign.
- **Changes:** `Types & Lexicon` (revise, MAJOR), `literal-form` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `location-provider` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `entity-category` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `spatial-relation` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `search-criterion` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `descriptor-modifier` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `key-term` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `structural-word` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `tone-value` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `artifact-type` (add vocabulary, MINOR)
- **Shaper rebuttals:**
  - `entity-category and new vocabulary categories`: No simulated Fail-coverage item required role-recipient or instance-descriptor (only the Partial dev-email-1 touches recipient roles); every simulated Fail item (dev-embodied-2, both ALFRED items, both SWE-bench items) is fixed by the source/relation/entity/location/spatial-relation changes made this sprint. Adding two more categories now within the 12-change cap would force cutting one of those Fail-fixing changes, net-reducing coverage rather than improving it. Deferring keeps this step coherent and leaves no regression (STRING recipient remains usable under N2's build-phase carve-out).
  - `structural-word, key-term, and complete-token audit`: Every operation identifier is already enumerated, with its exact signature, inside the Action construct entry itself, so a reader can trace any operation token to a spec location today (no N1 regression). Promoting this into a separate glossary category is a valuable interpretability polish but adds no new expressible coverage and no simulated item was Failed by its absence; given the 12-change cap it is deferred to preserve room for the coverage-fixing changes above.
  - `Generate, artifact-type, and attribute-name`: This sprint adds the artifact-type category itself (the prerequisite), covering story/story_continuation/technical_explanation/structured_report/character_description/email_reply/contract/list as a growable, closed:false set. Fully typing every generation attribute (paragraph counts, exclusions, locale, style) in the same sprint would be exactly the overreaching redesign the steering guidance warns against; it is scheduled for the next increment once artifact-type is validated against sampled items.
  - `N3 attribute-value categories`: N2's own build-phase text is explicit that free-text alternatives for these attributes are removed 'later, under N1 and N4' once the vocabulary is rich enough, and that this build phase must 'not remove STRING or other free-text channels' nor 'reduce coverage.' This sprint substantially grows that vocabulary (tone-value, descriptor-modifier, spatial-relation, artifact-type, expanded search-criterion, location-provider, entity-category) without yet retiring every STRING fallback, which is the correct sequencing under the note's own phasing; retiring all of them in one sprint risks exactly the coverage loss the note forbids.
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — This is meaningful progress on N2: it fixes important lexical defects and adds useful descriptive categories that make several closed tasks expressible without prose. It cannot be accepted because the Action revision can remove previously available default operations, while the claimed complete glossary still omits operation and attribute vocabularies and retains concrete lexical inconsistencies. Fixing those issues would preserve the coverage gains and make this a sound accepted increment.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — The new source, spatial-relation, entity/location, descriptor, search, and repository-path vocabulary materially improves several previously failing household and code tasks. However, Action's revised normative registry no longer states the prior default-operation rule and omits previously usable operations such as offer_discount, offer_expedited_shipping, and itinerary/domain operations; this can reduce previously available coverage. Search still lacks symbolic shape and availability criteria, so the mirror task retains a prose filter fallback.
  - expressivity: benefit — Nested placement, acquisition source, multi-field sorting, artifact kind, and a real tone vocabulary preserve distinctions that previously collapsed into strings. The sampled cup, warm-cup, and repository-path tasks are substantially better represented. But many meaningful constraints remain STRING payloads, including code assertions and reproduction, generated-artifact requirements, recipients, and content.
  - determinism: mixed — REPO_PATH, contextual UNSIGNED_INT, the new namespace account, canonical relation symbols, and ordered rank_field lists improve convergence. Yet search translators still have competing representations for filters (dedicated fields versus apply_filters(content=STRING)); gray/round/in-stock have no canonical symbolic encoding; and operation/attribute identifiers are not independently indexed as glossary vocabulary. The independent mirror translations demonstrate these divergent choices.
  - interpretability: benefit — The glossary becomes substantially more useful through literal-form, structural-word, key-term, location-provider, entity-category, spatial-relation, descriptor-modifier, search-criterion, tone-value, and artifact-type categories. Nonetheless, the claimed exhaustive structural inventory is not actually exhaustive (# is the comment token but only COMMENT is listed), action operations and attribute names remain scattered in a construct signature rather than glossary categories, and several category rules are imprecise or self-contradictory.
  - improvement: benefit — A weaker executor can now distinguish 'in refrigerator on table', product color/size/audience, and relative repository paths without parsing prose. The remaining prose criteria and unsupported availability/shape constraints mean that improvements are incomplete rather than merely cosmetic.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `held-trip-1` (seed_tasks) [Partial] NL: "Plan a 3-day itinerary in Kyoto that includes at least one temple per day and avoids anything more than a 20-minute walk from the last stop." → `ENTRYPOINT PlanKyoto

TASK PlanKyoto : STRING {
  GENERATE(target=structured_report, content="Plan a 3-day Kyoto itinerary with at least one temple each day and walking time between successive stops no greater than 20 minutes.") -> itinerary : STRING
  RETURN itinerary
}` — Generate/artifact-type can carry the request, but itinerary days, per-day cardinality, and successive-stop walking constraints remain an opaque STRING. No itinerary, travel-leg, duration, or constraint category exists.
  - `held-support-1` (seed_tasks) [Fail] NL: "If the customer's order is late and they're a first-time buyer, offer a 10% discount; if they're a repeat buyer, offer free expedited shipping instead." → `ENTRYPOINT ResolveLateOrder

TASK ResolveLateOrder {
  ACTION check_support_status(target="customer_order", metric=is_late) -> late : BOOL
  ACTION check_support_status(target="customer", metric=is_first_time_buyer) -> first_time : BOOL
  ACTION check_support_status(target="customer", metric=is_repeat_buyer) -> repeat_buyer : BOOL
  IF late AND first_time THEN {
    ACTION offer_discount(target="customer_order", content="10 percent discount")
  } ELSE {
    IF late AND repeat_buyer THEN {
      ACTION offer_expedited_shipping(target="customer_order", content="free")
    }
  }
}` — The current Action semantics allowed an unlisted operation through its default signature. The proposed Action registry lists no offer_discount or offer_expedited_shipping and does not retain that default-operation rule, so this formerly expressible task has no defined Action behavior after the revision.
  - `6ca55141-9ba6-4ed7-8c07-77df6c24029d` (Mind2Web) [Partial] NL: "Find the lowest-priced gray-colored round-shaped mirror in stock in Atlanta, Georgia, with a maximum price of 20 dollars." → `ENTRYPOINT FindMirror

TASK FindMirror : REF[SYMBOL] {
  ACTION search_web(target=mirror, location="Atlanta, Georgia", color=gray, max_price=20, currency=usd) -> mirrors : LIST[REF[SYMBOL]]
  ACTION apply_filters(target=mirrors, content="round-shaped; in stock") -> filtered_mirrors : LIST[REF[SYMBOL]]
  ACTION sort(target=filtered_mirrors, rank_field=price, rank_direction=asc) -> sorted_mirrors : LIST[REF[SYMBOL]]
  ACTION extract(target=sorted_mirrors, limit=1) -> mirror : REF[SYMBOL]
  RETURN mirror
}` — N6 permits gray and mirror as members of existing descriptor/entity categories, and price sorting is symbolic. Shape and stock availability have no criterion/value category, forcing apply_filters content STRING. This matches the independent translators' disagreement: one omitted these filters, while the other retained them as prose.
  - `62806bef-eeeb-4892-b4d5-6a8d2005c58d` (Mind2Web) [Partial] NL: "Browse the trending searches in Columbus." → `ENTRYPOINT BrowseTrendingColumbus

TASK BrowseTrendingColumbus : STRING {
  ACTION open_page(target=trending_searches)
  ACTION search_web(target=trending_searches, location="Columbus") -> trends : LIST[REF[SYMBOL]]
  ACTION read_page(target=trends, content="trending searches") -> result : STRING
  RETURN result
}` — The query/result topic and Columbus location remain strings or assumed generic symbols; no trend/search-topic category specifies the requested information.
  - `trial_T20190907_134455_194210#0` (ALFRED) [Full] NL: "Place a white cup with a knife in it on the counter in front of the toaster." → `ENTRYPOINT PlaceCupWithKnife

TASK PlaceCupWithKnife {
  ACTION pick_up(target=knife, source=table) -> knife : REF[SYMBOL]
  ACTION place(target=knife, destination=white_cup, relation=in, destination_location=sink, destination_relation=in)
  ACTION pick_up(target=white_cup, source=sink) -> white_cup : REF[SYMBOL]
  ACTION place(target=white_cup, destination=counter, relation=on, destination_location=toaster, destination_relation=in_front_of)
}` — source and nested spatial relations directly enable the actionable core. cup, knife, toaster, and white_cup are admissible assumed members/compositions under N6 and the entity/descriptor rules.

TASK WarmCoffeeCup {

  ACTION heat(target=coffee_cup, destination=microwave) -> coffee_cup : REF[SYMBOL]

TASK FixMinCountReduction {
  ACTION modify_code(target="xarray/core/nanops.py", function="_maybe_null_out", argument="axis", assertion="replace mask.shape[axis] with np.take(a.shape, axis).prod()", reproduction_step="da.sum([\"dim_0\", \"dim_1\"], min_count=1)", expected_output="sum succeeds with min_count across multiple dimensions", actual_output="sum errors when more than one dimension is passed")
