  }
}` — The request depends on prior-turn context not supplied as a formal prior CONVO turn here; requested artifact components, Hinglish locale, SEO keywords, and tag-list format are not symbolically typed.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-55500` (PATHs) [Partial] NL: "Continue Part 2" → `ENTRYPOINT ContinueStory

TASK ContinueStory : STRING {
  GENERATE(target=story_continuation, content="Continue Part 2 of the Donatello and Goku sparring story.") -> continuation : STRING
  RETURN continuation
}` — artifact-type=story_continuation improves intent recovery, but the source story, characters, required events, and style remain prose.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-30875` (PATHs) [Partial] NL: "which state that don't ask for a work permit to the non-U.S. citizens" → `ENTRYPOINT AskWorkPermitRule

CONVO AskWorkPermitRule {
  TURN t1 SPEAKER=USER {
    UTTER ask(content="Which United States state does not require a work permit for non-US citizens?")
  }
}` — Question form is represented, but jurisdiction, immigration/work authorization, and the legal criterion have no vocabulary categories.
  - `1775708977070` (ThoughtTrace) [Partial] NL: "Can you suggest a warm light midnight snack with the same goal of satiety and portion control?" → `ENTRYPOINT SuggestWarmSnack

TASK SuggestWarmSnack : STRING {
  GENERATE(target=response, content="Suggest a warm, light midnight snack that promotes satiety and portion control.") -> snack_advice : STRING
  RETURN snack_advice
}` — The response artifact is typed, but food, warmth, timing, nutritional objectives, and portion constraints have no symbolic categories.
  - `1775686840693` (ThoughtTrace) [Partial] NL: "então o fungo do dedo do pé pode estar na virilha ou até na bunda?" → `ENTRYPOINT AskFungalSpread

CONVO AskFungalSpread {
  TURN t1 SPEAKER=USER {
    UTTER ask(content="Can fungus from a toe spread to the groin or buttocks?")
  }
}` — The question act is recoverable, while body locations, disease, transmission, and medical context remain content prose.
  - `1775962320176` (ThoughtTrace) [Partial] NL: "Hello" → `ENTRYPOINT Greeting

CONVO Greeting {
  TURN t1 SPEAKER=USER {
    UTTER acknowledge(content="Hello")
  }
}` — The selected speech act is plausible but not forced: inform, acknowledge, and an unlisted greeting act are all defensible. This exposes open speech-act vocabulary nondeterminism.
  - `1775436940555` (ThoughtTrace) [Partial] NL: "I was absent from work for a week due to illness, while there were no vacations available to me at the time. I would like you to suggest an email message that I can send to my boss in a friendly manner so that he does not prevent me from working." → `ENTRYPOINT DraftReturnEmail

TASK DraftReturnEmail : STRING {
  GENERATE(target=email_reply, audience="boss", tone=friendly, content="Draft an email explaining a one-week illness absence, requesting to resume work, and maintaining a friendly tone.") -> email : STRING
  RETURN email
}` — artifact-type and tone-value help, but boss is a STRING role, one-week duration and employment request are embedded in content, and no recipient-role or generation-constraint categories exist.
- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=partial — Independent translations agree that repository-relative source paths and nested spatial placement are now representable. They diverge on the mirror request: one drops round/in-stock and leaves gray as a gap, while the other uses prose apply_filters and a quoted gray value. They also differ on whether to emit tone=concerned for c892, confirming that the canonical mapping remains incomplete.
- **Required changes:**
  - `Action`: Retain and explicitly state the prior default-operation behavior, or add typed canonical operations for every previously expressible default operation. At minimum preserve offer_discount and offer_expedited_shipping so the held support task does not regress.
  - `action-verb and attribute-name vocabulary`: Add separate glossary categories for every Action/UTTER/GENERATE operation identifier and every attribute name, with member glosses, applicability, canonical synonyms, and worked examples. A signature inside the Action construct is not a descriptive glossary category and does not satisfy N2.
  - `search-criterion and descriptor-modifier`: Add typed, canonical symbolic criteria and values for shape and availability/stock status, and state canonical precedence between dedicated search_web filters and select_filter/apply_filters so one meaning has one encoding.
  - `Types & Lexicon, literal-form, and structural-word`: Repair the literal and token audit: define PATH_LIT's segment and DRIVE subrules, list the actual '#' comment token rather than only COMMENT, define the complete identifier/handle formation rules for task, conversation, turn, variable, result, operation, and attribute positions, and remove or formally define all documentation-only comment notation before claiming an exhaustive lexicon.
  - `Action`: Constrain source to location-provider (or a typed compatible REF), relation and destination_relation to spatial-relation, destination_location to location-provider, and each search parameter to its stated category in normative grammar/semantics rather than only prose claims.
  - `N3 attribute-value categories`: Add an explicit glossary category or derivation rule for every currently accepted attribute and preserve STRING only as an explicitly labeled build-phase fallback. Cover recipient/audience roles, target kind, origin/location, content, format, constraints, deadline, subject, code fields, and all remaining search/action attributes; make tone slots normatively require tone-value.
  - `generation-specification`: Add the next increment of generation specification vocabulary: symbolic format, length/paragraph count, audience/recipient role, style, exclusion, locale, duration, and constraint categories, while retaining the transitional STRING fallback.
  - `role-recipient, provider, and instance-descriptor`: Add separately lookupable categories with applicability/composition rules. This is needed for the glossary's descriptive groups and for recipient/audience/source distinctions, although STRING fallback may remain during build phase.
- **Logic issues:** The revised Action registry says it restores every previously supported operation but omits the current default signature for unlisted operations. This is a semantic coverage regression, not merely a missing glossary entry.; The structural-word category calls itself exhaustive but does not include the literal '#' comment marker, while the grammar defines COMMENT using '#'.; PATH_LIT references undefined lexical components DRIVE and segment; its POSIX and Windows recognition rule is therefore not fully self-contained.; location-provider's membership rule permits capitalized proper names but all listed symbols and IDENT syntax conventions are lowercase-oriented; the canonical case and proper-name encoding are not specified consistently.; descriptor-modifier is described as a closed-ish set, marked closed:false, and says membership is 'from this list, representative not exhaustive'; this does not provide a deterministic membership rule for unlisted values such as gray.; search-criterion conflates criterion names with their values and permits both structured search_web attributes and opaque apply_filters/select_filter content for overlapping filtering meanings, producing multiple valid encodings.; tone-value is added, but Action's send_email/send_message signatures still type tone as generic SYMBOL and the proposal does not normatively require a tone-value member.; The key-term worked example uses '-- lookup:' even though the revised lexical account declares '#' the only comment token; calling this documentation shorthand does not make an expression example parseable.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — The attempt adds substantial structural, key-term, literal, entity, location, relation, descriptor, search, tone, and artifact glossary vocabulary. It remains incomplete because operation identifiers, attribute names, roles/recipients, providers, instance descriptors, and many semantic categories are not glossary categories; several claimed exhaustive categories are incomplete or undefined.
  - N3: violated — tone-value, spatial-relation, descriptor values, and some search value rules are genuine progress. Most existing attributes still accept arbitrary STRING or SYMBOL without an attribute-specific glossary value set or derivation rule; transitional STRING retention is allowed, but it does not satisfy N3.
  - N7: violated — Contextual UNSIGNED_INT, REPO_PATH, ordered rank fields, and namespace discussion improve canonical parsing and encoding. Competing structured versus content-string filters, open operation/speech-act selection, and missing canonical value mappings still prevent one encoding per meaning.
  - N1: violated — The attempt makes more tokens lookupable and removes COMMAND, but build-phase STRING and free identifiers remain by design. Moreover, operation and attribute identifiers remain unindexed, so the lexicon is not yet closed.
  - N4: violated — Numerous STRING payloads remain, but retaining them is required during this N2 build phase. The proposal neither removes nor expands such channels beyond the current language in a way that worsens this note.
  - N5: violated — Removing undefined COMMAND and formalizing REPO_PATH are sound self-containment progress. Undefined PATH components, arbitrary STRING payloads, and ungoverned identifier classes remain, so the language is not self-contained.
  - N6: partial — The categories are increasingly grammar-oriented and simulations appropriately assume missing members within entity/location categories. Missing categories for shape, availability, itinerary constraints, roles, and medical/legal concepts still force prose rather than category symbols.
  - N8: partial — Nested spatial relations, multi-key ranking, source, artifact kinds, and tone preserve useful distinctions. Shape, availability, code semantics, generation constraints, and recipient roles remain too coarse or opaque.
- **Rebuttal rulings:**
  - `entity-category and new vocabulary categories`: upheld — Deferring role-recipient and instance-descriptor for one increment is defensible under the build-phase incremental plan because STRING recipient fallback remains and the added source/relation categories repair more immediate closed-task failures. They remain required next-step N2 work.
  - `structural-word, key-term, and complete-token audit`: overruled — N2 requires descriptive vocabulary categories in the glossary, not merely a list of operation names embedded in a construct grammar. The proposal itself claims operation identifiers belong in an action-vocabulary category, but supplies none; this leaves every operation word outside the claimed complete lexicon.
  - `Generate, artifact-type, and attribute-name`: upheld — Adding artifact-type is real, coherent progress, and full generation-specification typing can be an immediately following increment without removing the required STRING fallback. The omitted generation categories remain required work, but not a reason to reject this increment by themselves.
  - `N3 attribute-value categories`: overruled — The rebuttal correctly distinguishes retaining STRING fallback from closing it, but N3 explicitly requires every attribute to receive a value set or derivation rule during the build phase. A transitional STRING alternative may remain; it must still be named as such and cannot substitute for the missing symbolic value categories.
- **Decision:** needs-rework
- **Documenter summary:** Steering sprint under N2 (comprehensive glossary): Shaper proposed fixing PATH/REPO_PATH and NUMBER/UNSIGNED_INT lexical bugs, removing undefined COMMAND, and adding spatial-relation, descriptor-modifier, tone-value, artifact-type, entity-category, location-provider, search-criterion, key-term, and structural-word vocabulary, while deferring role-recipient/instance-descriptor and full operation/attribute indexing. The Critic found genuine progress but ruled needs-rework, most decisively because the revised Action registry silently drops previously expressible default operations (regressing the 'held-support-1' offer_discount/offer_expedited_shipping simulation) and because the proposal's own rebuttal claiming operation identifiers belong in an 'action-vocabulary' category was overruled since no such category was actually supplied, leaving the lexicon still incomplete. The Shaper's deferral of role-recipient/instance-descriptor was upheld as reasonable incremental sequencing, but the N3 rebuttal on leaving attribute value-sets unnamed was overruled.
- **Cost this sprint:** $1.2872

## Sprint 15 (attempt 3/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 9.0.0 → 9.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N2 [hard] The glossary is the complete lexicon of the language, covering every kind of word, not only operators.
- **Shaper proposal (model: anthropic/claude-sonnet-5):** Fixes the rework findings: restores the default-operation fallback plus explicit support ops, embeds categorical typing and filter precedence in Action, adds the missing operation-identifier and attribute-name index categories, repairs the literal/token audit (PATH_LIT, '#' comment, namespace rules), adds shape/availability search vocabulary, a transitional role-recipient category, and a first generation-attribute increment.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — All changes add or repair glossary categories (group c: actions, attributes, roles, availability/shape values) and group a (literal/token audit) per N2, while explicitly retaining STRING as a labeled transitional fallback per the build-phase carve-out.
- **Changes:** `Types & Lexicon` (revise, MAJOR), `literal-form` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `structural-word` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `operation-vocabulary` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `attribute-name` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `location-provider` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `entity-category` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `spatial-relation` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `search-criterion` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `descriptor-modifier` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `availability-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `role-recipient` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `generation-attribute-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR)
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — This is a substantial and useful N2 increment: it restores held-out support coverage and turns many previously implicit lexical classes into separately glossed vocabulary. It cannot be accepted because it makes N3 worse through an undefined duration unit and because its central completeness claims are false: Action/Generate still permit generic attributes while the purported exhaustive index omits many of them. Repairing the exhaustive applicability/value tables, duration units, undefined categories, and invalid worked example would preserve the proposal's positive coverage and interpretability gains.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: benefit — The new household, search, support, role, entity, code-field, and generation categories materially improve coverage, and restoring the default Action rule avoids the prior support-task regression. However, sampled household requests still need semantic categories for state/instance descriptors and directional relations, while some search, code, and generation attributes have no indexed category.
  - expressivity: mixed — Dedicated search criteria and generation facets preserve more request constraints than the prior all-STRING forms. But the language cannot distinguish the full versus empty toilet-paper rolls, cannot express left-of placement, and defines duration as a number whose unit is merely 'understood from context,' losing an explicit part of the source meaning.
  - determinism: mixed — Canonical synonyms, typed criteria, and dedicated-filter precedence are real improvements. Yet the open default-operation membership rule depends on deciding whether a listed operation 'covers the same concept,' generic IDENT attributes remain grammatically admitted, and independent translations still diverge materially on support control flow, string versus symbols, temporal deal filtering, and target identity.
  - interpretability: mixed — Separate glossary categories make many formerly implicit words look-upable. Interpretability is harmed by the false claim that attribute-name indexes every usable attribute, the undefined category(...) expression in the search example, and categories such as tone-value and artifact-type being required without an accompanying supplied glossary entry.
  - improvement: benefit — Typed availability, shape, roles, locations, operations, code fields, and generation formats give a weaker executor materially better planning information than prose-only attributes. The remaining free STRING payloads are permitted in this build phase, but unresolved units, instance state, and relation distinctions still limit planning fidelity.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `held-support-1` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "If the customer's order is late and they're a first-time buyer, offer a 10% discount; if they're a repeat buyer, offer free expedited shipping instead." → `ENTRYPOINT ResolveLateOrder

TASK ResolveLateOrder {
  ACTION check_support_status(target="customer_order", metric=is_late) -> late : BOOL
  ACTION check_support_status(target="customer", metric=is_first_time_buyer) -> first_time : BOOL
  ACTION check_support_status(target="customer", metric=is_repeat_buyer) -> repeat_buyer : BOOL
  IF late AND first_time THEN {
    ACTION offer_discount(target="customer_order", discount_percent=10)
  } ELSE {
    IF late AND repeat_buyer THEN {
      ACTION offer_expedited_shipping(target="customer_order")
    }
  }
}` — The restored support operations and default-operation rule preserve this previously held-out task. The explicit ELSE correctly encodes 'instead,' although independent translations disagree about querying buyer status only after lateness is established.
  - `dev-code-1` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Given a list of file paths, delete every file older than 30 days unless it's in the 'archive' folder." → `ENTRYPOINT DeleteOldFiles

TASK DeleteOldFiles {
  LET paths : LIST[REPO_PATH] = ["logs/a.txt", "archive/b.txt"]
  FOR EACH path IN paths {
    ACTION file_age_days(target=path) -> age : NUMBER
    ACTION path_in_folder(target=path, destination="archive") -> archived : BOOL
    IF age > 30 AND NOT archived THEN {
      ACTION delete_file(target=path)
    }
  }
}` — The existing filesystem operations and the new REPO_PATH literal family support the actionable core. The universal input list is represented as a finite list value.
  - `8c5ccffa-b88e-4928-9394-6d46cfd36e59` (Mind2Web (osunlp/Mind2Web, train, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Full] NL: "What are today's Limited Time Offers deals?" → `ENTRYPOINT GetTodayDeals

TASK GetTodayDeals : LIST[REF[SYMBOL]] {
  ACTION search_web(target=deal, category=limited_time_offers) -> deals : LIST[REF[SYMBOL]]
  ACTION select_filter(target=deals, content="today") -> today_deals : LIST[REF[SYMBOL]]
  RETURN today_deals
}` — Category is now a dedicated criterion. No dedicated temporal availability/date criterion exists, so the build-phase STRING filter fallback remains necessary.
  - `b49f88ac-b407-41af-a9e7-f5e4cd1f970a` (Mind2Web (osunlp/Mind2Web, train, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Full] NL: "Check the rating and user reviews for the game "Deathloop"." → `ENTRYPOINT CheckDeathloopReviews

TASK CheckDeathloopReviews : STRING {
  ACTION search_web(target="Deathloop", category=game) -> games : LIST[REF[SYMBOL]]
  ACTION extract(target=games, limit=1) -> game : REF[SYMBOL]
  ACTION read_page(target=game, content="rating and user reviews") -> reviews : STRING
  RETURN reviews
}` — Search and page-reading operations cover the task, but rating and user reviews are still an opaque read_page STRING field rather than separately typed retrieval criteria.
  - `trial_T20190912_052310_164968#2` (ALFRED (json_2.1.0, train)) [Partial] NL: "To move a full roll of toilet paper and an empty roll to the counter." → `ENTRYPOINT MoveToiletPaperRolls

TASK MoveToiletPaperRolls {
  ACTION pick_up(target=full_toilet_paper_roll) -> full_toilet_paper_roll : REF[SYMBOL]
  ACTION place(target=full_toilet_paper_roll, destination=counter, relation=on)
  ACTION pick_up(target=empty_toilet_paper_roll) -> empty_toilet_paper_roll : REF[SYMBOL]
  ACTION place(target=empty_toilet_paper_roll, destination=counter, relation=on)
}` — N6 can supply an unlisted entity type, but the language has no state/instance-descriptor category or composition rule for full versus empty. The target symbols therefore encode distinctions the current entity-category rule does not define.
  - `trial_T20190919_003451_692296#1` (ALFRED (json_2.1.0, train)) [Partial] NL: "Move a sponge and bowl from a counter to a cupboard" → `ENTRYPOINT MoveSpongeAndBowl

TASK MoveSpongeAndBowl {
  ACTION pick_up(target=sponge, source=counter) -> sponge : REF[SYMBOL]
  ACTION pick_up(target=bowl, source=counter) -> bowl : REF[SYMBOL]
  ACTION place(target=sponge, destination=bowl, relation=in)
  ACTION pick_up(target=bowl) -> bowl : REF[SYMBOL]
  ACTION place(target=bowl, destination=cupboard, relation=in)
  ACTION close_container(target=cupboard)
}` — Entity and location membership can be assumed under N6, but the request's bottom-left cupboard, right-of-spoon placement, and the bundled sponge-and-bowl relation require directional and instance-descriptor categories not supplied here.
  - `sphinx-doc__sphinx-8035` (SWE-bench_Verified (princeton-nlp/SWE-bench_Verified, test, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Full] NL: "Support defining specific `:private-members:` for autodoc." → `ENTRYPOINT SupportPrivateMembers

TASK SupportPrivateMembers {

}` — The new code-field attributes allow a more structured bug report, although argument, expected_output, actual_output, and content are explicitly transitional STRING payloads.

TASK FixEmptyTupleAnnotation {

- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=partial — Providers agree on the broad support, deal-search, household, code-fix, opinion, and tweet-generation constructs. They disagree on quoted versus symbolic category values, whether to filter today's deals separately, support-query ordering and branch structure, support-offer target, and code-path handling; the new categories reduce some variation but do not impose one canonical encoding.
- **Required changes:**
  - `attribute-name`: Make the claimed exhaustive index actually exhaustive: add origin, date, return_date, passengers, near, limit, execution-related code and generation attributes, ordering if retained, and every other Action/UTTER/GENERATE attribute. For each, state its exact value category and applicability.
  - `Action and Generate`: Replace generic `IDENT = value` acceptance with normative validation that an attribute must be in attribute-name and legal for that operation/construct. Either enumerate allowed attributes per operation or define a machine-checkable applicability table; reject unknown attributes.
  - `generation-attribute-value and artifact-type`: Add the missing artifact-type category and its members/rule, and either add a documented `balanced` style value or remove its implied availability. Ensure every category named in Generate semantics, including tone-value, artifact-type, and generation facets, has a real glossary entry.
  - `Generate duration`: Replace `duration=NUMBER paired with a unit understood from context` with an explicit unit attribute/category and a normative composition rule, or retain a labeled transitional STRING fallback. The present rule violates N3 because unit is neither a glossary symbol nor derived by a glossary rule.
  - `instance-descriptor and spatial-relation`: Add a separately look-upable instance/state descriptor category and composition rule for distinctions such as full/empty and positional qualifiers, and extend spatial-relation with directional relations needed by sampled tasks (at least left_of and right_of, with canonical synonyms).
  - `search-criterion worked example`: Repair the invalid `category("price")` expression. Define price as a canonical rank_field/search-criterion symbol and write `rank_field=price`, or add a real category-call construct with grammar and semantics.
  - `operation-vocabulary default rule`: Make default-operation admission deterministic: provide a canonical mapping procedure or explicit extensible operation subcategories, rather than the subjective test that no listed operation covers 'the same concept.'
  - `role-recipient`: Resolve the contradiction that named individuals remain STRING even after the category closes. Add a glossary-defined atomic proper-name literal category or explicitly defer this claim until N5.
- **Logic issues:** attribute-name asserts that every Action/UTTER/GENERATE attribute appears exactly once, but omits Action attributes including origin, date, return_date, passengers, near, limit, and search-travel/search-transit fields.; Action and Generate grammars still admit arbitrary IDENT attribute names, so the new attribute-name category is not a complete normative lexical gate.; Generate duration has an unspecified unit 'understood from context,' contrary to N3's requirement that every attribute value be a glossary symbol or glossary-defined derivation.; The search-criterion worked example contains `category("price")`, which has no grammar, type, glossary entry, or semantics.; Generate semantics require artifact-type and tone-value membership, but this proposal supplies no corresponding glossary categories or members.; The default operation rule remains non-deterministic because deciding conceptual equivalence with a listed operation is not mechanically defined.; The entity-category composition rule permits descriptor-modifier prefixes only, so full_toilet_paper_roll and empty_toilet_paper_roll have no defined derivation.; role-recipient says proper names remain STRING even after closure, conflicting with the stated future closed-vocabulary direction unless a proper-name literal form is added.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — The proposal adds substantial structural-word, literal-form, operation, attribute, entity, location, relation, search, availability, role, descriptor, and generation categories. It still fails complete-lexicon status because required categories are referenced but absent, the attribute index is demonstrably incomplete, and generic IDENT admission leaves unindexed names legal.
  - N3: violated — Typing tone, relation, location, availability, and generation facets is progress, and retained STRING channels are properly labeled transitional in many places. However, the newly added duration attribute explicitly relies on an undefined contextual unit, introducing a new non-glossary attribute-value component; the incomplete attribute index also leaves several accepted fields without stated value categories.
  - N7: violated — Dedicated search-filter precedence and synonym inventories improve canonicalization. The default-operation conceptual-equivalence test, generic attributes, missing attribute applicability, and observed provider divergence prevent deterministic encoding.
  - N1: violated — This is correctly still in the build phase and STRING remains intentionally available. The proposal improves future traceability by adding lexical categories, but unindexed attributes and undefined referenced categories mean it is not yet ready for closure.
  - N4: violated — STRING prose payloads remain throughout Action, UTTER, and Generate as N2's build-phase instruction requires. The proposal adds some symbolic alternatives but does not remove prose, appropriately for this phase.
  - N5: violated — Literal forms and several vocabulary membership rules improve self-containment. Free strings, undefined contextual duration units, and the open conceptual default-operation test still require English understanding.
  - N6: partial — Several open categories explicitly support representative membership under N6, allowing unlisted entity and location members. Missing category shapes such as instance state, proper names, and directional relations remain real category gaps rather than merely missing members.
  - N8: partial — Availability, shape, artifact format, style, locale, role, and code fields add useful distinctions. The lack of units, state descriptors, directional relations, and structured generation constraints still collapses meaning in sampled tasks.
- **Decision:** needs-rework
- **Documenter summary:** Sprint 15 (steering note N2, complying) proposed a large glossary expansion — literal-form and structural-word inventories, operation-vocabulary and attribute-name indexes, plus new location-provider, entity-category, spatial-relation, search-criterion, descriptor-modifier, availability-value, role-recipient, and generation-attribute-value categories — restoring the regressed support operations and typing many previously prose-only attributes. The Critic rejected it as needs-rework despite real coverage/interpretability gains, chiefly because the attribute-name index falsely claimed exhaustiveness while omitting several Action attributes, Generate's new `duration` attribute relies on an undefined contextual unit (regressing N3), and referenced categories like artifact-type and tone-value, plus an invalid `category("price")` worked-example expression, were never actually supplied.
- **Cost this sprint:** $1.2252

## Sprint 16 (attempt 1/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 9.0.0 → 9.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N2 [hard] The glossary is the complete lexicon of the language, covering every kind of word, not only operators.
- **Shaper proposal (model: openai/gpt-5.6-terra):** This revision adds a categorized, normative glossary layer for structural tokens, operations, attributes, entities, search criteria, roles, artifact specifications, and tone. It repairs the prior proposal's attribute-validation, duration-unit, default-operation, descriptor, and artifact-vocabulary gaps without closing existing STRING channels.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — N2's build phase requires a complete categorized lexicon alongside, not instead of, current free-text coverage. These changes make operators, attribute names, categorical values, and descriptive symbols independently look-upable, while retaining STRING where the vocabulary is intentionally still transitional.
- **Changes:** `structural-word` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `operation-vocabulary` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `attribute-name` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `entity-descriptor` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `spatial-relation` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `search-value` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `role-recipient` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `tone-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `artifact-specification` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `Utterance` (revise, MAJOR)
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — The proposal makes substantial, sound progress on the N2 vocabulary-building direction and preserves current STRING-based coverage as the build phase requires. It cannot be accepted because its central claim of a complete glossary is logically false: structural tokens, retained operation entries, lexical primitives, and several attribute/category definitions are missing, while canonical encoding remains ambiguous. Correcting these concrete inconsistencies would retain the proposal's positive coverage and expressivity gains without requiring premature closure of free-text channels.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: benefit — The new entity, search, role, artifact, relation, operation, and code-oriented vocabulary gives many held-out task concepts a documented home while retaining STRING channels required by N2's build phase. The custom-operation rule also preserves a broad fallback, but its narrow three-attribute signature still leaves structured concepts such as instruments, seat ranges, and multi-stop itinerary constraints opaque.
  - expressivity: benefit — The simulations can preserve more distinctions structurally: price ranking, currency, availability, stateful household objects, duration units, artifact kind, tone, and code locations are now available. However, much of the meaning in the travel, code, medical, and revision requests still resides in transitional STRING content, and several requested constraints have no typed attribute or descriptive category.
  - determinism: mixed — Typed search fields, state-composition order, duration pairing, and operation-specific validation improve convergence. But attribute order is not canonicalized; `pick_up` and `extract` both claim the synonym "take"; listed operations are not individually enumerated or glossed; and `custom_<domain>_<normalized-source-head-verb>` depends on undefined head-verb/domain analysis. The independent flight translations also disagree on bound names, origins, date contexts, quoted versus symbolic ranking values, and filter segmentation.
  - interpretability: mixed — The categorized entries are a substantial improvement over an operator-only glossary and make many symbols independently understandable. Yet the claimed complete lexicon is contradicted by absent structural tokens, absent operation entries, duplicate symbols with facet-dependent meanings, and undefined lexical rules for identifiers, punctuation, brackets, arrows, and many reserved words.
  - improvement: benefit — A weaker executor benefits from explicit typed filters, rank direction, entity state, spatial relation, recipient role, and artifact facets rather than having to infer all of them from prose. The benefit is limited where strings still carry the operative plan or code change, but retaining those channels is required during this N2 build phase.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `held-trip-1` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Plan a 3-day itinerary in Kyoto that includes at least one temple per day and avoids anything more than a 20-minute walk from the last stop." → `ENTRYPOINT PlanKyotoItinerary
TASK PlanKyotoItinerary : STRING {
  GENERATE(target=plan, duration=3, duration_unit=day, content="itinerary in Kyoto; at least one temple per day; each next stop at most a 20-minute walk from the preceding stop") -> itinerary : STRING
  RETURN itinerary
}` — artifact-specification supplies plan and duration units. Per-day quantification and consecutive-stop walking constraints remain prose because no itinerary, stop, route, temporal-unit, or distance/travel-time category exists.
  - `dev-email-2` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "If my manager hasn't replied by Friday, send a polite follow-up; otherwise do nothing." → `ENTRYPOINT FollowUpManager
TASK FollowUpManager CONTEXT execution_date="2026-09-30" {
  ACTION check_replied(target=manager, deadline="2026-10-02") -> replied : BOOL
  IF NOT replied THEN {
    ACTION send_email(recipient=manager, tone=polite, content="follow-up")
  }
}` — role-recipient and tone-value help. The new attribute index says deadline is an ISO_DATE during the build phase, whereas the old Flow-If example requires a UTC instant, leaving an unresolved compatibility issue.

TASK FindCheapestFlight CONTEXT execution_date="2023-04-05" : REF[STRING] {
  ACTION search_travel(origin="JFK, New York", destination="Changi, Singapore", date="2023-04-11", passengers=1) -> flights : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  ACTION apply_filters(target=flights, content="non-stop or one stop; seat selection; free cancellation") -> filtered_flights : LIST[REF[STRING]]

  ACTION extract(target=sorted_flights, limit=1) -> cheapest_flight : REF[STRING]
  RETURN cheapest_flight
}` — search-value materially improves price sorting. Stop count, seat selection, and cancellation policy still have no typed search criteria and remain in the transitional filter payload.

  - `matplotlib__matplotlib-25775` (SWE-bench_Verified (princeton-nlp/SWE-bench_Verified, test, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Full] NL: "[ENH]: Add get/set_antialiased to Text objects
### Problem

Currently, Text objects always retrieve their antialiasing state via the global rcParams["text.antialias"], unlike other artists for which this can be configured on a per-artist basis via `set_antialiased` (and read via `set_antialiased`).

### Proposed solution

Add similar getters/setters on Text objects (also adjusting Annotations accordingly, if needed) and use that info in the drawing stage.



Should be relatively easy to implement, except that the slight fiddling needed with backends requires some understanding of backend code (I think we need to replace the access to `rcParams["text.antialiased"]` by going through the GraphicsContext state).

where each item of some_list is escaped before applying the join operation. This usage makes sense in a context where autoescape is off.

- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=partial — The providers agree that the flight task decomposes into travel search, filtering, sorting, and extraction, and that the movie-opinion item is a CONVO/UTTER. They diverge materially on flight context date, origin normalization, binding names, symbolic versus quoted ranking values, and whether unsupported household/code concepts are gaps or custom/prose encodings; this confirms that the proposed canonicalization is incomplete.
- **Required changes:**
  - `structural-word`: Make the structural inventory actually complete for the current grammar: add AND, OR, NOT, TURN, SPEAKER, USER, AGENT, REPLY_TO, REVISES, DECREASES, CONTEXT, all grammar delimiters and separators (`{}`, `()`, `[]`, `,`, `:`, `=`, `->`), COMMENT, IDENT, UNSIGNED_INT, HEX, and every literal-form name/rule. Define each lexical form precisely, including whitespace and identifier formation.
  - `operation-vocabulary`: Replace the circular membership rule 'all current normative Action registry identifiers' with individually look-upable entries, or a glossary-contained exhaustive registry table, for every retained Action and UTTER operation including click, fill_field, submit, heat, turn_on, add_file, call, propose, acknowledge, and confirm. Give each entry its canonical symbol, signature reference, and synonym mapping.
  - `operation-vocabulary`: Define a deterministic NL normalization algorithm for custom operations: domain-selection precedence, tokenization, lemmatization/head-verb selection, multiword verb handling, collision handling, and the rule for when a listed operation overrides custom. Resolve overlapping synonyms, notably `take` currently mapping to both extract and pick_up.
  - `attribute-name and Generate`: Add `exclude` to the complete attribute-name index with value class and Generate applicability. Audit all retained Action, UTTER, Task, and Generate attributes against the index, including legacy operations retained through the registry rule, so the completeness claim and static-validation rule are true.
  - `Action, Generate, and Utterance`: State one canonical attribute order, such as signature order followed by lexical order for optional attributes, and require it in emitted expressions. State canonical ordering for independent sequential generated steps when the NL request does not impose an order.
  - `entity-descriptor and facet vocabularies`: Avoid duplicate unqualified symbols with different category meanings: either namespace symbols or define an explicit global disambiguation rule for `detailed` and `concise`, which are members of both tone-value and artifact-specification. Split the entity-descriptor's mixed entity/location/state/position/modifier/platform membership into separately named, independently look-upable facet categories as N2 requires.
  - `worked examples`: Repair invalid or contradictory examples. The structural-word example returns `one` outside the IF child scope that binds it; the operation-vocabulary rinse example relies on entity symbols despite an unresolved target value rule; and the Utterance example uses `acknowledge`, which must be an explicit operation-vocabulary member.
  - `descriptive vocabulary`: Add missing categories, not merely more members, exposed by the simulations: itinerary/day/stop/route and travel-time constraints; transport stop-count and policy/amenity criteria; media venue/showtime/ticket/seat; instrument relation; code file/method/property/call/assertion and expected-versus-actual behavior; revision/improvement intent; procedure/safety/escalation; and academic/job-search goal structures.
  - `Action compatibility`: Reconcile the revised `deadline=ISO_DATE` definition with the existing check_replied semantics and examples requiring a UTC timestamp. Define a glossary literal category for the actual permitted deadline form or explicitly revise the operation semantics without losing current deadline coverage.
- **Logic issues:** The proposed `structural-word` category calls itself the closed inventory of all structural words but omits many tokens used by the retained grammar, including AND, OR, NOT, TURN, SPEAKER, USER, AGENT, REPLY_TO, REVISES, CONTEXT, DECREASES, delimiters, separators, `->`, IDENT, and COMMENT.; The operation-vocabulary membership rule delegates coverage to an external 'current normative Action registry' rather than making every operation independently look-upable in the glossary; this does not realize N2 for retained operations.; The structural-word worked example is statically invalid because `one` is bound inside the IF branch scope and returned outside it.; The claim that the attribute-name entry is complete is false because Generate permits `exclude` but attribute-name has no exclude member.; The Action revision leaves emitted attribute ordering unconstrained, producing multiple valid encodings for the same operation.; The synonym table is not a function: `take` maps to both `extract` and `pick_up`, so it cannot provide N7's required canonical symbol selection.; The custom-operation rule relies on undefined natural-language notions of source head verb and domain and therefore does not mechanically determine one custom symbol.; The global symbols `detailed` and `concise` occur in both tone-value and artifact-specification with different meanings, without namespaces or a disambiguation rule.; The revised deadline attribute conflicts with existing check_replied examples and semantics: it says ISO_DATE, while current valid use requires a full UTC timestamp.; The stated build-phase retention of STRING is appropriate for N2, but therefore N1, N3, N4, and N5 remain violated; the proposal must not imply that transitional STRING payloads are category-valued descriptive symbols.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — This is real progress: the glossary now proposes categories for structural vocabulary, operations, attributes, entities, search values, roles, tones, relations, and artifacts, each with glosses and examples. It nevertheless is not the complete lexicon: many grammar tokens are absent, retained operations are delegated to a non-glossary registry, and multiple required descriptive categories remain absent.
  - N3: violated — Many previously untyped values now have categories and duration has a defined unit composition. However, many attribute values remain arbitrary STRING payloads, `exclude` is unindexed, and several existing attributes still lack an exhaustive defined value set; this is permitted transitional work but does not yet uphold N3.
  - N7: violated — Canonical search fields, state-facet ordering, and attribute legality improve encoding discipline. Missing canonical attribute order, synonym collisions, undefined custom-operation normalization, and divergent independent flight translations prevent deterministic encoding.
  - N1: violated — The glossary defines many more emitted symbols than before, but expressions still contain open identifiers and arbitrary STRING content, and the proposed structural inventory itself omits tokens used by the grammar. This is expected to remain open during N2's build phase.
  - N4: violated — The simulations necessarily retain sentence and fragment STRING payloads. N2 expressly requires retaining those channels during the build phase, so this is not a regression, but it is not progress toward N4.
  - N5: violated — Arbitrary prose STRINGs and open identifier forms remain necessary under the stated build-phase plan. The proposal adds some literal-form names but does not make their lexical rules fully self-contained.
  - N6: partial — The new grammar increasingly refers to named vocabulary categories, and simulations can assume missing members such as rag or tub. But major concepts still have no category at all and are routed through prose content, so the full-glossary-oriented design remains incomplete.
  - N8: partial — Fine-grained rank, availability, relation, artifact, state, duration, and code-location distinctions preserve more meaning than the prior coarse channels. Richness is still curtailed by missing categories for itinerary, booking, procedure, code behavior, and structured revision requests.
- **Decision:** needs-rework
- **Documenter summary:** For steering note N2 (glossary must cover every kind of word, not just operators), the Shaper proposed a large set of new categorized vocabulary entries (structural words, operations, attribute names, entity descriptors, spatial relations, search values, role-recipients, tone values, artifact specifications) plus revisions to Action/Generate/Utterance validation, complying with the note. The Critic found genuine progress but ruled needs-rework because the 'complete lexicon' claim was demonstrably false: the structural-word inventory omitted many grammar tokens (AND, OR, delimiters, IDENT, etc.), the operation-vocabulary rule circularly deferred to an external registry instead of listing entries, synonym collisions (e.g., 'take') broke determinism, duplicate symbols (detailed/concise) spanned categories ambiguously, and a worked example was itself statically invalid — these logic issues, more than any single simulated example, drove the rework decision.
- **Cost this sprint:** $0.9400

## Sprint 17 (attempt 1/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 9.0.0 → 9.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N2 [hard] The glossary is the complete lexicon of the language, covering every kind of word, not only operators.
- **Shaper proposal (model: gemini/gemini-3.1-pro-preview):** Establishes a complete, exhaustive glossary for structural words, operations, attributes, and descriptive facets, enforcing deterministic canonical attribute ordering.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — This fulfills N2 by providing exhaustive, individually look-upable entries for all retained operations, structural tokens, and attributes, while introducing canonical ordering, a deterministic custom operation algorithm, and resolving synonym collisions. The strict 12-change limit is fully utilized to build this structural foundation.
- **Changes:** `structural-word` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `operation-vocabulary` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `attribute-name` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `entity-name` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `spatial-relation` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `search-value` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `role-recipient` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `tone-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `artifact-specification` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `Utterance` (revise, MAJOR)
- **Shaper rebuttals:**
  - `entity-descriptor and facet vocabularies`: I have narrowed `entity-name` to only include base entities/locations to fix the mixed membership. However, introducing the separate `entity-state`, `entity-modifier`, `platform-name`, and `position` categories alongside the other required structural and vocabulary changes would exceed the strict 12-change limit per sprint. They will be added in the next sprint.
  - `descriptive vocabulary`: The sprint scope strictly limits proposals to at most 12 changes. Fulfilling the structural completeness, canonical ordering, exhaustive operation registry, complete attribute index, and splitting entity facets required by the other Critic mandates consumes the 12 allowed changes. Adding 8+ new distinct domain categories (travel, media, code, procedure, etc.) would exceed the limit. Because N2 remains open, these domain-specific categories will be introduced in the next sprint once this structural foundation is accepted.
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — The bundle makes meaningful N2 progress by adding genuine vocabulary categories and useful canonical ordering, while preserving the required transitional STRING channels. It cannot be accepted because it claims exhaustive completeness while leaving lexical rules, value typing, domain categories, and custom-operation normalization materially underspecified. These defects undermine the proposed determinism and interpretability gains, though the overall direction is sound and should be reworked rather than rejected.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — The proposal materially improves coverage for household relations, search price/rating/category criteria, artifact targets, roles, and code-fix fields. However, it still lacks required descriptive categories for travel structure, transport constraints, media booking, procedure/safety, academic/job-search structures, and several code entities; its restrictive revised signatures also leave some retained operations governed only by an unspecified prior-signature fallback.
  - expressivity: benefit — The simulations can now represent price ceilings, stock status, ordering, directional placement, recipient roles, output expectations, and several generation constraints structurally rather than placing all of them in content. Compatibility, detailed software reproduction context, entity states/positions, and many domain constraints still require opaque STRING payloads or have no appropriate category.
  - determinism: mixed — Canonical attribute ordering and dedicated search fields are useful improvements. But the custom-operation rule is not operationally deterministic: 'first match applicable to the target,' source-language head-verb selection, lemmatization, multiword verbs, and domain selection are not fully defined; the supplied translations still diverge sharply on decomposition, names, compatibility filtering, and software-fix representation.
  - interpretability: mixed — Individually look-upable vocabulary categories make many symbols clearer than the prior construct-only glossary. The claimed complete inventory is nevertheless false: NUMBER and STRING lexical syntax are not fully specified here, '-' and other tokens used by current grammar are absent, several structural and descriptive value classes are only implicit, and entity-name's generic lowercase_underscore rule is indistinguishable from unrestricted IDENT formation.
  - improvement: benefit — A weaker planner benefits from typed-looking search constraints, named code fields, explicit spatial relations, tone values, and artifact specifications. The benefit is limited where requirements remain buried in content strings, such as compatibility, a bug's reproduction program, or an open-ended generation brief.
- **Simulated examples:**

  ACTION place(target=pillow, destination="armchair", relation=on)
  ACTION pick_up(target="pillow", source="sofa") -> pillow2 : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(target=pillow2, destination="armchair", relation=on)
}` — The revised pick_up/place signatures and spatial-relation category help. Sequential duplication represents 'one at a time'; no quantity category is needed for this finite two-item request.

- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=low — Independent translations disagree on loop versus repeated actions for pillows, whether compatibility is a gap or content filter, whether the cup task is a multi-step preparation or one custom operation, software-fix target and evidence fields, and even whether recipe/persona requests are expressible. The new ordering rules do not resolve these construct-selection and decomposition differences.
- **Required changes:**
  - `structural-word`: Make the claimed complete structural lexicon actually cover the retained grammar: add or precisely define NUMBER's signed/decimal/exponent form, STRING escaping, diff's '-' token, ISO_DATE, COMMENT as a named lexical category, reserved-word exclusion from IDENT, and every punctuation/operator token used by all retained productions. Correct the worked example so it is a valid demonstration of its stated condition semantics rather than an unused IF.
  - `operation-vocabulary`: Replace the custom-operation heuristic with a fully executable canonicalization procedure: ordered domain precedence, source tokenization, multiword-verb handling, lemmatizer/reference rule, head selection, collision handling, and explicit fallback for no domain match. State signatures/result categories for every retained operation rather than relying on 'other operations retain prior signatures.'
  - `attribute-name and value categories`: For every indexed attribute, state its permitted value category or literal form and make Action's grammar enforce it per operation signature. In particular define categories/rules for target, destination, source, destination_location, destination_relation, audience, module, class, function, call, argument, assertion, reproduction_step, expected_output, actual_output, environment, platform, and instrument; a bare generic value production does not satisfy N3.
  - `entity-descriptor and facet vocabularies`: Complete the promised facet split by adding independently look-upable entity-state, entity-modifier, position, platform-name, and location categories, with exact membership rules and synonym mappings. Restrict entity-name so its open membership rule cannot be confused with unrestricted IDENT formation.
  - `descriptive vocabulary`: Add the deferred missing categories identified by the simulations: itinerary/day/stop/route and travel-time; transit stop-count and policy/amenity; media venue/showtime/ticket/seat; typed compatibility/instrument relations; code file/method/property/call/assertion and expected-versus-actual behavior; revision/improvement; procedure/safety/escalation; academic/job-search goals; food/nutrition/meal structures; medical topic/audience constraints; and email/message/collaboration structures.
  - `Action, Generate, and Utterance`: Reconcile canonical ordering language into one unambiguous rule for required and optional attributes, define canonical binding/handle naming including numeric suffixes and plural collection names, and ensure every worked example obeys the grammar and the declared value categories.
  - `search-value`: Split search-value into separately named rank-field, rank-direction, currency, genre, category, and availability categories, or give globally unique namespaced symbols. Its current mixed membership is not individually look-upable by the facet that Action signatures require.
- **Logic issues:** The structural-word category claims a closed complete inventory but omits lexical definitions required by the retained grammar, notably the full NUMBER and STRING forms, ISO_DATE, the diff '-' token, and reserved-word interaction with IDENT.; The operation-vocabulary custom-operation rule is not deterministic: applicability and domain precedence are undefined, and source-language lemmatization/head-verb selection is delegated to undefined linguistic judgment.; Action says category-valued attributes must use the category required by attribute-name, but attribute-name does not specify such categories; its grammar accepts generic value for every attribute, making static value validation impossible.; search-value combines six semantically distinct facets in one vocabulary while Action signatures require facet-specific values; membership is not individually look-upable as N2 requires.; entity-name's rule 'a generic entity or location is lowercase_underscore' overlaps the IDENT lexical rule and does not give a translator a deterministic boundary between an entity symbol and an arbitrary identifier.; The structural-word worked example does not demonstrate its NL conditional: it tests TRUE, binds an unused two, and returns one regardless. It cannot validate the claimed structural semantics.; The proposed operation-vocabulary example uses target="book" and content="examine" while the new prose says category-valued fields must use category members, yet no target/content value class permits or explains the quoted forms.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — This is real progress: structural words, operations, attribute names, spatial relations, roles, tones, search values, entities, and artifact vocabulary are now proposed as glossary categories. It is not yet a complete lexicon: several retained lexical forms and many domain categories are absent, mixed facet categories are not independently look-upable, and generic entity syntax is not a controlled vocabulary rule.
  - N3: violated — tone, duration unit, search values, roles, and artifact facets gain named values. Most attribute entries still lack a stated value set/category and Action accepts an unrestricted generic value, so the language cannot determine whether quoted strings such as destination_location, function, or expected_output are valid values.
  - N7: violated — Canonical attribute ordering and an attempt at custom-operation normalization are improvements. The custom-operation algorithm, handle naming, task decomposition, custom-domain selection, and many synonym/value mappings remain underdefined, as the independent translations demonstrate.
  - N1: violated — The build phase correctly retains STRING and free identifiers, so closure is not expected yet. The added glossary vocabulary helps eventual closure but does not make every simulated token glossary-traceable.
  - N4: violated — The proposal intentionally preserves STRING content in the N2 build phase, as required. It neither removes prose nor should it do so before the vocabulary can replace it.
  - N5: violated — Precise lexical rules and named vocabularies improve self-containment, but generic lowercase_underscore entities, incomplete literal rules, and undefined normalization behavior still require unstated English/source-language judgment.
  - N6: partial — Several productions now reference vocabulary facets rather than only currently listed words. Missing categories, especially for travel, food, medical, procedures, academic tasks, and rich software concepts, still force content prose instead of category-shaped expressions.
  - N8: partial — The additions preserve useful distinctions for price, availability, spatial relation, code behavior, roles, tone, and artifacts. Richness remains substantially constrained by missing domain categories and by opaque content fields for compatibility, procedures, and detailed requirements.
- **Rebuttal rulings:**
  - `entity-descriptor and facet vocabularies`: overruled — Narrowing entity-name removes some prior mixing, but it does not create the separately named state, modifier, position, and platform categories explicitly required for a complete N2 lexicon. The sprint limit explains deferral but does not establish that the required change is unnecessary.
  - `descriptive vocabulary`: overruled — The change limit may justify staging, but it does not answer the substantive coverage and lexicon gaps exposed by the simulations. These missing categories must remain required for subsequent N2 realization; the proposal must not characterize its present vocabulary as exhaustive or complete.
- **Decision:** needs-rework
- **Documenter summary:** Addressing steering note N2 (glossary must cover every kind of word), the Shaper proposed a large bundle adding structural-word, operation-vocabulary, attribute-name, entity-name, spatial-relation, search-value, role-recipient, tone-value, and artifact-specification categories plus canonical attribute ordering for Action/Generate/Utterance, while rebutting (deferring) full entity-facet splitting and several domain vocabularies to stay within the 12-change cap. The Critic ruled needs-rework, overruling both rebuttals as unjustified deferrals and requiring fixes for undefined value typing, an underspecified custom-operation canonicalization algorithm, mixed-facet categories like search-value, and incomplete lexical rules — most decisive was that Action's grammar still accepts an unrestricted generic value despite claiming exhaustive attribute typing, undermining the claimed determinism/interpretability gains.
- **Cost this sprint:** $0.9394

## Sprint 17 (attempt 2/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 9.0.0 → 9.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N2 [hard] The glossary is the complete lexicon of the language, covering every kind of word, not only operators.
- **Shaper proposal (model: gemini/gemini-3.1-pro-preview):** Addresses the Critic's required changes by formalizing lexical rules, canonicalizing custom operations, explicitly mapping every attribute to its value category, enforcing attribute ordering and handle naming, and namespacing search values. Acknowledges the vocabulary is not yet exhaustive and stages the remaining requested descriptive categories for the next sprint due to the 12-change limit.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — The changes realize N2 by establishing the foundational structural, operational, and attribute vocabularies with strict deterministic rules (lexical definitions, domain precedence, value type enforcement, handle naming). The remaining descriptive categories are staged for the next sprint to comply with the 12-change limit.
- **Changes:** `structural-word` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `operation-vocabulary` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `attribute-name` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `entity-name` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `spatial-relation` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `search-value` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `role-recipient` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `tone-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `artifact-specification` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `Utterance` (revise, MAJOR)
- **Shaper rebuttals:**
  - `entity-descriptor and facet vocabularies`: I am complying with the instruction not to characterize the vocabulary as exhaustive. I have removed 'complete' and 'exhaustive' claims from the glosses. Because of the strict 12-change limit per sprint, I am staging the addition of these remaining descriptive categories (entity-state, modifier, position, platform, location) for the next sprint, focusing this sprint on solidifying the foundational structural, operational, and attribute rules required by the other Critic notes.
  - `descriptive vocabulary`: I am complying with the instruction not to characterize the vocabulary as exhaustive. I have removed 'complete' and 'exhaustive' claims from the glosses. Because of the strict 12-change limit per sprint, I am staging the addition of these remaining descriptive categories for the next sprint, focusing this sprint on solidifying the foundational structural, operational, and attribute rules required by the other Critic notes.
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — The bundle makes substantial N2/N3/N7 progress by adding actual lexical categories and stricter operation signatures, but it is not logically ready to land. Its structural example is invalid, its open lexical rules depend on external English/WordNet judgments, and that dependency regresses hard note N5. The missing descriptive categories also leave several sampled closed constraints unrepresentable, so rework is required before acceptance.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — The added operation, entity, relation, artifact, recipient, and search facets improve coverage for simple household, search, and generation requests. However, key sampled constraints still have no usable category or operation field: Netflix/platform, pet/type filters, table-corner location, travel structures, code structure beyond opaque strings, and most domain-specific request structures. The revised Action signatures also remove the previous generic default-operation signature, so coverage now relies on an underspecified custom-operation mechanism.
  - expressivity: mixed — Dedicated ranking, availability, currency, tone, duration-unit, and placement-relation fields preserve more structure in several closed tasks. Yet the proposed signatures leave platform and category-combination constraints unrepresentable, while many code, conversational, legal, and planning meanings remain undifferentiated STRING payloads; this is permitted in the current build phase but does not realize the intended descriptive-vocabulary expansion.
  - determinism: mixed — Canonical attribute order, result signatures, namespaced search symbols, and handle suffix rules are useful progress. But `custom_<domain>_<verb>` depends on a translator's conceptual domain judgment and an external WordNet lemmatizer, while entity extraction depends on unspecified English head-noun analysis. Independent translations already disagree sharply on search target, genre spelling, binding names, whether heat returns a binding, and code-fix representation.
  - interpretability: mixed — The glossary becomes substantially more useful through explicit structural tokens and several descriptive categories. It nevertheless remains incomplete for the retained grammar and claimed vocabulary categories: open entity, role, search, and artifact categories do not give a sufficiently precise self-contained membership procedure, and the invalid structural-word example cannot be parsed as a valid Task.
  - improvement: benefit — For the household and search simulations, typed operations, relation values, typed ranking, and explicit result categories give a weaker executor better actionable structure than raw requests. The benefit is limited for software, planning, travel, and open conversational requests because their decisive content remains a broad STRING payload.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `dev-embodied-2` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Put both pillows from the sofa onto the armchair, one at a time." → `ENTRYPOINT MovePillows
TASK MovePillows {
  ACTION pick_up(target=pillow, source=sofa) -> pillow : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(target=pillow, destination=armchair, relation=on)
  ACTION pick_up(target=pillow, source=sofa) -> pillow_2 : REF[STRING]
