- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=low — The providers agree only on the basic two-pillow expression. For comedy shows they disagree on target, missing genre representation, quoting, and rebinding; for the apple task they disagree on whether to acquire the apple and preserve the heated reference; for the software fix one emits GAP while the other uses opaque fields. The proposal's canonical rules reduce some naming variation but do not resolve these semantic choices.
- **Required changes:**
  - `structural-word`: Correct the worked example to a grammar-valid TASK: RETURN may only occur as the Task's final return_stmt, not inside an IF block. Also explicitly state that every retained lexical punctuation/form used by the full grammar is either a structural-word member or covered by a named lexical rule.
  - `operation-vocabulary`: Replace WordNet and 'conceptually fitting' domain selection with a self-contained, glossary-defined deterministic mapping procedure. Define every `custom_` component as a category-defined atomic token rule, or defer custom operations while the vocabulary is being built; do not require an English dictionary or external lemmatizer.
  - `entity-name`: Do not define open entity membership by unspecified English head-noun extraction. Add the deferred independently look-upable entity-state, entity-modifier, position, platform-name, and location categories, with category-specific membership rules and synonym mappings; restrict entity-name to a self-contained atomic-symbol rule.
  - `search-value`: Make each open facet usable deterministically: either enumerate its currently supported members or define a semantic, self-contained membership/synonym rule for the suffix after `genre_`, `cat_`, and related prefixes. Add a platform category and legal search_web platform attribute so the Netflix constraint is representable.
  - `attribute-name`: Add the deferred descriptive categories needed by the simulations, including itinerary/day/stop/route/travel-time; transit stop-count, policy, and amenity; media venue/showtime/ticket/seat; typed compatibility and instrument relations; code file/method/property/call/assertion and expected-versus-actual behavior; revision/improvement; procedure/safety/escalation; academic/job-search goals; food/nutrition/meal structures; medical topic/audience constraints; and email/message/collaboration structures.
  - `Action, Generate, and Utterance`: Audit every worked example against the revised grammar and value rules, and state one single canonical rule for result handles that resolves SAME-result rebinding without relying on translator choice. Clarify whether quoted entity strings are a transitional exception or whether entity-name symbols must be used whenever a member exists.
- **Logic issues:** The structural-word worked example is invalid: `RETURN one` occurs inside IF, but Task grammar permits RETURN only as the final `return_stmt` after steps.; operation-vocabulary calls WordNet and asks translators to choose the first domain that 'conceptually fits'; neither operation selection nor lemmatization is self-contained or deterministic.; entity-name's open membership rule requires English noun-phrase/head extraction and therefore does not define a self-contained lexical category.; search-value says facets are prefix-separated but does not define how an unlisted suffix such as `comedy` becomes a valid genre member or how its natural-language synonyms map canonically.; The Netflix/platform constraint and the table-in-the-corner location constraint cannot be represented by the legal revised Action signatures.; The proposal leaves the prior promised facet split and broad descriptive-vocabulary expansion deferred, despite those categories being directly needed by the sampled simulations.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — This is real progress: structural words, operations, attributes, entities, relations, roles, tones, search facets, and artifact specifications are now glossary categories with examples. It remains incomplete because required descriptive categories are deferred and several open categories lack a precise self-contained membership rule.
  - N3: violated — The attribute index maps many attributes to categories or literal forms and gives Action/Generate/Utterance a stricter gate. Numerous attributes still intentionally accept broad transitional STRING payloads, so the required closed value sets are not yet realized; this is build-phase-compatible progress but not compliance.
  - N7: violated — Canonical attribute ordering, signatures, namespaced facets, and collision suffixes improve encoding convergence. The custom-operation domain choice, WordNet lemmatization, and English head-noun extraction remain non-deterministic external dependencies.
  - N1: violated — The structural glossary and lexical rules define more expression tokens than before. Retained free STRING channels and open, externally interpreted custom/entity forms mean the language still has symbols and payload content that cannot be fully looked up through a closed glossary.
  - N4: violated — The proposal deliberately retains STRING payloads for content and several attributes, as N2's build phase requires. It neither removes them nor expands symbolic content enough to close them, but does not make the pre-existing free-text situation worse.
  - N5: violated — The current language already retains free text, but the new normative custom-operation procedure explicitly requires WordNet and English lemmatization, and entity-name explicitly requires English head-noun extraction. That adds a new external-English dependency to encoding rather than merely retaining the build-phase channels.
  - N6: partial — The syntax increasingly refers to category names such as artifact-specification, role-recipient, and search-value, allowing N6-style assumed members in some cases. Missing categories, especially platform and location, still force loss of structure rather than category-based encoding.
  - N8: partial — Fine-grained relations, ranking facets, availability, tone, duration units, and artifacts preserve distinctions that were formerly strings. Important distinctions remain unavailable, including platform, location, typed code relations, and the deferred domain vocabularies.
- **Rebuttal rulings:**
  - `entity-descriptor and facet vocabularies`: overruled — Removing exhaustive claims is correct, but it does not substitute for the missing entity-state, modifier, position, platform, and location categories. These are concrete N2 gaps shown by the Netflix and corner-location simulations, and the replacement entity rule is not self-contained.
  - `descriptive vocabulary`: overruled — Staging under the change limit explains why the work is incomplete but does not satisfy the required change. The listed categories remain necessary for N2 and for preserving structure in sampled travel, code, planning, medical/support, and communication requests.
- **Decision:** needs-rework
- **Documenter summary:** Steering sprint under N2 ('glossary is the complete lexicon'): Shaper proposed adding structural-word, operation-vocabulary, attribute-name, entity-name, spatial-relation, search-value, role-recipient, tone-value, and artifact-specification categories plus revised Action/Generate/Utterance signatures with canonical ordering and handle-naming rules, while complying with the letter of N2 but rebutting (deferring) the fuller descriptive-vocabulary buildout citing the 12-change limit. The Critic rejected the bundle as needs-rework and overruled both deferral rebuttals, finding the structural-word worked example grammatically invalid (RETURN misused inside IF) and, decisively, that the operation-vocabulary and entity-name membership rules illegitimately depend on external WordNet lemmatization and English head-noun extraction, which regresses hard note N5's determinism requirement; simulated Netflix-platform and table-corner-location cases further showed unrepresentable constraints that the missing descriptive categories were meant to cover.
- **Cost this sprint:** $0.9716

## Sprint 17 (attempt 3/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 9.0.0 → 10.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N2 [hard] The glossary is the complete lexicon of the language, covering every kind of word, not only operators.
- **Shaper proposal (model: gemini/gemini-3.1-pro-preview):** Adds the required lexical, operational, and descriptive vocabularies to satisfy N2/N3/N7, with self-contained atomic rules replacing external dependencies. Resolves the handle rebinding ambiguity and introduces platform, location, and a unified descriptive category to capture the requested domain constraints within the 12-change limit.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — The changes realize N2 and N3 by fully defining the structural words, operations, attributes, and descriptive values, while satisfying N7 and N5 by replacing external English/lemmatizer dependencies with self-contained atomic token rules and explicit handle suffixing rules.
- **Changes:** `structural-word` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `operation-vocabulary` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `attribute-name` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `entity-name` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `search-value` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `spatial-relation` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `platform-name` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `location-name` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `descriptive-value` (add vocabulary, MAJOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `Utterance` (revise, MAJOR)
- **Shaper rebuttals:**
  - `attribute-name`: The strict 12-change limit prevents adding a dozen independent categories for travel, code, medical, etc., alongside the mandatory structural and operational vocabularies. I have added `descriptive-value` as a unified category to hold these domain concepts, roles, tones, and artifacts, and added `platform-name` and `location-name` as requested, fulfilling the requirement within the sprint budget.
  - `entity-descriptor and facet vocabularies`: Due to the strict limit of 'at most 12 changes this sprint', I cannot add 5+ new independent categories while also resubmitting the base vocabulary. I have added `platform-name` and `location-name` as independent categories, restricted `entity-name` to a self-contained atomic rule, and incorporated states and modifiers into the unified `descriptive-value` category to comply as much as possible within the budget.
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — [Forced acceptance after 3 attempt(s) without a clean accept — the required changes below are known issues carried forward for a future sprint to address, not resolved.] This is meaningful progress on the N2 glossary objective, especially through structural vocabulary and spatial/search categories, and it does not regress the build-phase permission for STRING channels. It cannot be accepted because the central unified descriptive category is type-unsound, the open-token rules do not determine canonical encoding, and revised Action removes previously available fallback coverage. The self-contained spatial-relation vocabulary is sound and may land independently.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — The new operation, entity, search, platform, location, and spatial-relation inventories cover several previously unsupported household, shopping, and code concepts. However, revised Action removes the previous default-operation fallback and does not supply signatures for many existing or newly listed concepts, while the generic file-list input task remains inexpressible; this creates coverage regressions or failures.
  - expressivity: mixed — The additions preserve more distinctions for search ranking, spatial placement, code reports, and generated-artifact requests. But descriptive-value is an over-broad union: tone, role, artifact, state, domain concept, and modifier values are interchangeable under the stated attribute mapping, so it cannot preserve the intended type of a constraint such as a tone versus an artifact or entity state.
  - determinism: mixed — Canonical attribute ordering and numeric rebinding suffixes improve convergence where a valid operation signature exists. Determinism remains poor for arbitrary custom_<domain>_<action>, entity-name, platform-name, location-name, descriptive-value, and facet suffixes: their rules specify token shape but no canonical NL-to-token mapping, segmentation rule, synonym table, or criterion for choosing a domain/category.
  - interpretability: mixed — A categorized glossary is a substantial improvement over construct-only entries, and spatial/search members are readable. The unified descriptive-value category harms lookupability because a reader cannot infer which conceptual value set a given attribute permits, and several attribute mappings group unrelated semantic types into one category.
  - improvement: benefit — Typed operation signatures, relation values, filter/rank values, and explicit code-report attributes would give a weaker agent useful planning structure for supported requests. The benefit is limited by invalid examples, missing signatures, and residual opaque content strings.

  RETURN script
}` — art_script and a 17+ rating value are absent; target and content therefore rely on open invented/prose values.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-113201` (PATHs) [Partial] NL: "Rewrite this table of profitable online earning methods to be comprehensive, preserve the first column, remove br tags, and output a table." → `ENTRYPOINT RewriteMethodsTable
TASK RewriteMethodsTable : STRING {

  RETURN structured_report

  - `wildchat1m_en3u-155336` (PATHs) [Partial] NL: "Give me a summarize." → `ENTRYPOINT SummarizeMonetag
TASK SummarizeMonetag : STRING {

  RETURN short_text
}` — The requested speech act is only implicit in prose; no summarize operation, source-reference relation, or summary artifact category is defined.

  }

  RETURN plan

- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=low — Independent translations diverge materially. For the store task Anthropic reports a gap while Gemini uses search/sort/extract/click; for the red-rag task one omits red and one identifies a missing modifier; for open requests Anthropic reports vocabulary gaps while Gemini emits prose payloads. The proposal's broad open-token rules do not resolve these choices.
- **Required changes:**
  - `attribute-name and descriptive-value`: Replace the unified descriptive-value union with independently look-upable, attribute-specific categories at minimum for role/recipient, tone/register, artifact format/type, entity modifier, entity state, travel/transit, media/venue, code structure/behavior, medical topic/audience, planning/education, and message/collaboration structure. For every attribute, state its exact permitted category or literal rule; do not let tone accept artifact/state/domain values.
  - `operation-vocabulary`: Replace custom_<domain>_<action>'s token-shape-only rule with a deterministic mapping algorithm that specifies canonical domain selection, canonical action decomposition, component boundaries, and synonym mapping; alternatively defer custom operations. A regex alone does not define how a translator maps an NL operation to a canonical token.
  - `entity-name, platform-name, location-name, search-value`: Give each open category a category-specific canonical membership and synonym procedure, not merely '[a-z_]+' plus an English semantic description. Define how proper names, postal codes, paths, and multiword names are represented, and define canonical tokens for supported values such as red, formal, script, table, and ZIP/postal-code locations.
  - `Action`: Restore coverage for all operations expressible under the current Action default signature, or enumerate valid signatures for every operation-vocabulary member. Add a defined operation for selecting/setting a store and define signatures for any listed operation usable as ACTION. Do not silently make formerly expressible default operations invalid.
  - `Action, Generate, and Utterance`: Audit and correct all examples and cross-references against revised grammar/value rules. In particular, define tone_formal before using it, define art_script before using it, state a complete value grammar, and make the entity-string transitional exception consistent with each operation's typed signature.
  - `structural-word`: Make the lexical inventory actually exhaustive for the retained grammar: reconcile ISO_DATE's quoted-string grammar with its lexical definition, include or define UNSIGNED_INT/HEX and every referenced lexical nonterminal, and give reserved-word collision treatment for category members and IDENT.
  - `basis`: Add a typed external-input/parameter mechanism for entrypoint tasks, or a defined document-input binding, so requests over a supplied list such as file paths can be expressed by a valid executable entrypoint.
- **Logic issues:** descriptive-value makes semantically incompatible values legal for the same mapped attributes: tone can receive art_story, role_manager, state_microwaved, or dom_safari under the stated rules.; The custom operation, entity, platform, location, descriptive, and search suffix rules define only syntax, not deterministic natural-language membership or canonicalization; arbitrary translators can emit distinct valid symbols for the same meaning.; Revised Action's closed operation-id production and limited signature table remove the previous default signature for unlisted operations, regressing existing coverage without a migration rule.; operation-vocabulary includes speech acts such as ask and inform but Action has no signatures for them, while Utterance accepts any operation-vocabulary member rather than a separately constrained speech-act category.; The Action worked example uses entity values and list/result handles whose category/value typing is not fully defined by the proposal; its claimed exhaustive validation therefore cannot be mechanically applied.; Generate's worked example uses tone/formal-like semantics elsewhere, while the supplied descriptive-value inventory lacks tone_formal; the PATHs script requires art_script, also absent.; The structural-word proposal calls ISO_DATE a bare lexical form although the retained Task grammar defines it as a matching STRING literal, leaving two incompatible readings.; The store task can locate and click a store but has no operation that changes the current store selection, so the requested final state is not encoded.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — The glossary moves substantially toward a complete lexicon by adding structural, action, entity, attribute, relation, search, platform, location, and descriptive categories. It remains incomplete and insufficiently categorized: several retained lexical forms are unresolved, attribute value categories are conflated, and required descriptive domains are not independently look-upable.
  - N3: violated — Many attributes now point to a named category, which is progress. The mapping is not complete or type-safe: broad groups permit incompatible values, some needed values are absent, and quoted transitional strings remain without attribute-specific literal rules.
  - N7: violated — Canonical attribute ordering and numeric handle suffixes are real improvements. Open category regexes and custom operation construction lack a canonical NL mapping and synonym policy, so one meaning can still yield many distinct expressions.
  - N1: violated — The proposal supplies glossary categories and lexical rules for more tokens, but STRING and open identifiers remain by design in this build phase. This is not a regression because N2 explicitly preserves those channels until the closing phase.
  - N4: violated — Prose content remains in Action, GENERATE, and UTTER STRING channels as required during N2's build phase; the proposal does not add further prose channels beyond those already retained.
  - N5: violated — Replacing WordNet/head-noun extraction with token-shape rules is progress toward self-containment. The semantic instructions 'representing' an entity, platform, location, or domain still leave English-dependent classification and canonicalization undefined.
  - N6: partial — The grammar begins to refer to vocabulary categories rather than individual words. It still reshapes behavior around missing members and falls back to prose strings for many missing category/value distinctions instead of defining the categories needed by the simulations.
  - N8: partial — Search facets, placement relations, code attributes, and artifact values add useful distinctions. The merged descriptive category collapses distinctions at the type level, and missing categories lose important task constraints.
- **Rebuttal rulings:**
  - `attribute-name`: overruled — The twelve-change budget does not justify replacing independently look-upable semantic categories with one category that permits invalid cross-category values. The stated N2 requirement expressly requires descriptive vocabulary categories and look-upable entries; category-specific membership rules are necessary for N3 and N7 as well.
  - `entity-descriptor and facet vocabularies`: overruled — platform-name and location-name are useful partial compliance, but states/modifiers and positions folded into descriptive-value are not independently constrained or look-upable by their attributes. The entity-name regex also does not establish canonical semantic membership or synonym mapping.
- **Decision:** accepted
- **Documenter summary:** Under steering note N2 (glossary must cover every kind of word), the Shaper proposed a large vocabulary expansion—structural-word, operation-vocabulary, attribute-name, entity-name, search-value, spatial-relation, platform-name, location-name, and a unified descriptive-value category—plus revisions to Action/Generate/Utterance for canonical ordering and handle rules, contesting the 12-change budget via two rebuttals that folded many domain categories into descriptive-value. The Critic found this 'needs-rework' overall (both rebuttals overruled: descriptive-value is type-unsound, letting tone/role/state/domain values be interchangeable, and open-token regex rules lack canonical NL-to-symbol mapping), and revised Action regressed coverage by dropping the prior default-operation fallback; after repeated rounds only the self-contained spatial-relation vocabulary was accepted outright, with the rest forced through as documented but known-incomplete progress toward N2/N3/N7, carrying the required fixes forward to a future sprint.
- **Cost this sprint:** $0.9529

## Sprint 18 (attempt 1/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 10.0.0 → 10.1.0 (MAJOR/MINOR/PATCH)
- **Steering note:** N3 [hard] Every attribute value is a glossary symbol, or is derived from glossary symbols by a glossary-defined rule.
- **Shaper proposal (model: anthropic/claude-sonnet-5):** Gives tone, format, duration-unit, currency, and recipient attributes their own closed/open glossary value-sets, and rewrites attribute-name's membership rule as an explicit per-attribute mapping to those categories, so attribute values are now traceable to glossary symbols rather than ad-hoc strings.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — N3 requires every attribute to have a defined value set in the glossary. This sprint adds four new attribute-value categories (tone-value, format-value, duration-unit-value, currency-value, recipient-value) and rewrites attribute-name's membership_rule to state, per attribute, exactly which category or literal rule governs its values. Existing free-text examples (tone="polite", recipient="manager", duration_unit=dom_day) are updated to canonical symbols to demonstrate the closure, while content remains a transitional STRING channel (already documented as such) pending N4's closing phase.
- **Changes:** `tone-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `format-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `duration-unit-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `currency-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `recipient-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `search-value` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `descriptive-value` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, PATCH), `Flow-If` (revise, PATCH), `attribute-name` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR)
- **Partial acceptance:** applied 1 of 10 changes (`format-value`); the rest were sent back for rework.
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — The bundle makes substantive progress on N3 by introducing dedicated value categories and an explicit attribute-to-category index, but it cannot be accepted because doc hygiene fails and the index is incomplete and internally contradictory. It also regresses hard note N7 by creating duplicate canonical encodings for roles, tones, currencies, and day units. The standalone format-value category is self-contained and useful, but the remaining changes need an atomic, exhaustive migration and mapping revision.
- **Doc hygiene:** FAIL — Doc hygiene FAILED — `Generate` missing glossary_gloss; `Flow-If` missing glossary_gloss
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — The new value categories improve coverage for tone, duration units, currencies, roles, and formats, but the revised attribute-name mapping omits existing actionable attributes such as relation and metric and removes numerous currently indexed attributes. It also lacks categories for color, size, gender, price retrieval, ranking by ease/output, and several travel and code concepts, so sampled requests cannot retain all constraints.
  - expressivity: mixed — role_manager, tone_formal, unit_day, and curr_usd preserve distinctions that were previously ad-hoc strings. However, the proposed attribute mapping makes existing spatial-relation values and several Action attributes ungoverned, and the Generate semantics contradict the mapping by allowing artifact symbols for target while target is mapped only to entity-name.
  - determinism: mixed — Dedicated canonical categories and synonym lists improve convergence for the newly covered values. But tone_, role_, and curr_ symbols are duplicated across old and new categories unless the corresponding old entries are removed atomically; duration has both dom_day and unit_day; open regex rules permit translators to invent distinct symbols; and the Generate target rule is internally contradictory.
  - interpretability: mixed — The explicit per-attribute index is a substantial improvement over the former catch-all descriptive-value rule. Its incompleteness is harmful: a reader cannot determine valid values for relation, metric, origin, source, or the attribute members omitted by the revision, while examples use attributes and values that contradict operation signatures or the proposed mapping.
  - improvement: benefit — For requests involving a known recipient, tone, duration unit, currency, or output layout, canonical symbols provide useful planning structure rather than merely preserving prose. This benefit is limited until every existing attribute has a coherent value rule and the duplicate value vocabularies are resolved.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `dev-embodied-2` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Put both pillows from the sofa onto the armchair, one at a time." → `ENTRYPOINT MovePillows
TASK MovePillows {
  ACTION pick_up(target=pillow, source=sofa) -> pillow : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(target=pillow, destination=armchair)
  ACTION pick_up(target=pillow, source=sofa) -> pillow_2 : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(target=pillow_2, destination=armchair)
}` — The item remains expressible without the proposed categories. I omit relation=on because the revised attribute-name membership rule fails to govern relation, although the existing placement semantics would otherwise support it.

  RETURN short_text
}` — recipient-value, tone-value, duration-unit-value, and format-value help. The request's operative extension meaning remains in transitional content prose, and Generate's target=art_short_text conflicts with the revised mapping that declares target to be entity-name.

- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=low — The independent translations agree closely only for c2762. For the pillow item they disagree over whether relation=on is required; for the goblet item they choose incompatible goals; for django they select different target representations and different bug fields; and one provider treats the story as unencodable while another uses GENERATE with prose. The proposed canonical categories would improve only recipient/tone/currency agreement, not these unresolved choices.
- **Required changes:**
  - `doc hygiene (scripted — blocks acceptance)`: Doc hygiene FAILED — `Generate` missing glossary_gloss; `Flow-If` missing glossary_gloss
  - `basis`: Fix the doc-hygiene failure: add glossary_gloss fields to the Generate and Flow-If revisions, so the revised glossary entries preserve their required glosses.
  - `attribute-name`: Replace the incomplete membership_rule with an exhaustive mapping for every attribute currently listed in the glossary and every attribute used by any Action, Generate, Utterance, Task CONTEXT, or worked example. At minimum add governing rules for relation, metric, origin, source, itinerary, route, travel_time, stop_count, policy, amenity, venue, showtime, ticket, seat, compatibility, revision, procedure, safety, escalation, academic_goal, job_search_goal, food, nutrition, meal, medical_topic, medical_audience, message_structure, and collaboration_structure; do not silently delete those current attributes.
  - `attribute-name`: Resolve the target contradiction explicitly: either map Generate.target to an artifact-value category while Action.target maps to entity-name, or split the attribute names by construct. The current global target -> entity-name rule conflicts with valid GENERATE(target=art_plan/art_story) examples and semantics.
  - `descriptive-value`: Make the vocabulary split atomic and non-overlapping. Remove or deprecate role_ and tone_ members from descriptive-value when recipient-value and tone-value become normative, remove curr_ from search-value when currency-value becomes normative, and define migration/canonicalization rules. Also remove dom_day or explicitly declare unit_day as its sole canonical replacement.
  - `Generate`: Align Generate's legal attributes and examples with the global attribute mapping and Action-style signatures. Define whether Generate may use target artifact symbols, and ensure every worked example is statically valid under that definition.
  - `Action`: Correct the tone-value worked example or the send_email signature: it currently uses format=format_plain_text even though send_email permits recipient, content, and optional tone only.
  - `vocabulary basis`: Add missing attribute categories needed by sampled closed tasks before restricting mappings: at least color, size, gender/audience demographic, product-price retrieval or price-result representation, construction difficulty, production/output/yield, and source/link request. Give each category canonical symbols and a deterministic membership rule.
  - `format-value`: Clarify its open membership rule: either every format_[a-z_]+ token is valid directly under the rule, or new layouts require prior glossary addition. The present statements say both, yielding contradictory validity criteria.
  - `recipient-value`: Preserve existing recipient coverage during the build phase by defining the permitted literal/category for named individual recipients, or explicitly retain the existing quoted recipient exception until a proper-name literal category is introduced. Do not make ordinary named recipients inexpressible.
- **Logic issues:** Doc hygiene failed because the proposed Generate and Flow-If revisions omit glossary_gloss; this independently prevents acceptance.; The attribute-name revision maps target to entity-name, but Generate semantics and every Generate example use art_ symbols from descriptive-value.; The revision omits governing rules for existing relation and metric attributes and removes many existing attribute-name members, despite Action requiring every Action attribute to conform to a declared category/rule.; send_email's declared signature does not allow format, but the tone-value worked example supplies format=format_plain_text.; tone_, role_, and curr_ values have duplicate meanings across the newly added categories and their old categories; dom_day and unit_day provide two competing encodings for the same duration unit.; format-value says arbitrary matching tokens are valid but also says layouts are valid only after being added as members; recipient-value has the same practical uncertainty for unlisted roles.; The revised Flow-If worked example is improved for tone and recipient but still relies on deadline as a quoted UTC timestamp while the general value grammar does not independently define UTC_TIMESTAMP as a value literal form.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — Five descriptive value categories and a more explicit attribute index are real glossary growth, but the glossary is not yet the complete lexicon: many existing attributes lack value categories and free STRING channels remain by design in this build phase.
  - N3: violated — tone, format, duration_unit, currency, recipient/audience, and several search facets gain explicit governing categories. N3 remains violated because relation, metric, and numerous existing attribute names have no governing rule after the revision, and target's rule contradicts Generate's artifact values.
  - N7: violated — Canonical symbols and synonyms improve some mappings, but adding duplicate role_/tone_/curr_ categories and retaining dom_day versus unit_day introduces multiple valid encodings for the same meanings. Contradictory target and format rules also prevent a single canonical encoding.
  - N1: violated — Free identifiers and STRING payloads remain permitted in this build phase. The proposal does not close them, and it neither improves nor worsens the existing general closure gap.
  - N4: violated — The proposal expressly retains content and several code fields as STRING payloads, as permitted during this pre-closing build phase. No new prose channel is introduced.
  - N5: violated — Open category suffixes and free STRING/IDENT channels remain dependent on unbounded external naming and prose. The new categories provide some self-contained atomic conventions but do not close the general gap.
  - N6: partial — The proposal continues to use category-shaped vocabulary for several new values, but it does not supply a category for several required closed-task concepts, such as color, size, and gender. Missing symbols within entity-name are tolerable; missing categories are not.
  - N8: partial — Fine-grained tone, format, duration-unit, currency, and recipient distinctions preserve useful constraints rather than collapsing them into a single descriptive bucket. Richness is still limited by missing filter and generation-constraint categories.
- **Decision:** partially-accepted
- **Documenter summary:** Steering note N3 ('every attribute value must be a glossary symbol or a rule-derived value') was addressed by a Shaper proposal adding tone-value, format-value, duration-unit-value, currency-value, and recipient-value categories plus a rewritten per-attribute mapping in attribute-name, with revisions to Generate and Flow-If examples; the Shaper fully complied with the note. The Critic ruled needs-rework/partially-accepted: doc hygiene failed (missing glossary_gloss on Generate and Flow-If), the attribute-name mapping was incomplete (dropping relation, metric, and other existing attributes) and self-contradictory (target mapped to entity-name yet Generate examples use art_ symbols), and the split created duplicate canonical encodings for roles/tones/currencies/day-units, regressing hard note N7 on determinism; only the self-contained format-value category was accepted.
- **Cost this sprint:** $0.9403

## Sprint 18 (attempt 2/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 10.1.0 → 11.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR/PATCH)
- **Steering note:** N3 [hard] Every attribute value is a glossary symbol, or is derived from glossary symbols by a glossary-defined rule.
- **Shaper proposal (model: anthropic/claude-sonnet-5):** Fixes doc-hygiene (added glossary_glosses), resolves the target-attribute contradiction by splitting artifact-value out from descriptive-value, de-duplicates tone/role/currency symbols across categories, gives attribute-name a complete per-attribute mapping (including previously-omitted attributes), and adds a product-attribute-value category as a first step on color/size/gender coverage.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — Every attribute touched now resolves to exactly one glossary-defined value category (or an explicitly-tracked transitional STRING gap, never silent free text), satisfying N3's build-phase requirement without duplicating canonical symbols across categories.
- **Changes:** `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `Flow-If` (revise, PATCH), `tone-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `duration-unit-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `currency-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `recipient-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `artifact-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `descriptive-value` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `search-value` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `format-value` (revise vocabulary, PATCH), `product-attribute-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `attribute-name` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR)
- **Partial acceptance:** applied 9 of 12 changes (`Flow-If`, `tone-value`, `duration-unit-value`, `currency-value`, `recipient-value`, `artifact-value`, `descriptive-value`, `search-value`, `product-attribute-value`); the rest were sent back for rework.
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — This bundle makes meaningful N3 progress by separating several attribute value sets, fixing the Generate target contradiction, and eliminating duplicate canonical symbols. It cannot be accepted as a whole because category validity and Action signatures are not aligned, the format rule remains internally inconsistent, and broad residual/STRING mappings do not yet give many attributes a precise symbolic governing rule. The listed self-contained vocabulary and Flow-If changes can land now without depending on the broken mappings or signature revisions.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — Dedicated tone, duration-unit, currency, recipient, artifact, and product-facet categories improve coverage for several existing request shapes. However, sampled closed tasks still lack signature-permitted attributes and value categories for shape, product construction/state, itinerary constraints, walking-time constraints, source/link requests, and structured code facts; product-attribute-value is not wired into search_web, so its example is not statically valid.
  - expressivity: mixed — Generate.target is correctly distinguished from Action.target, and role/tone/currency values preserve more intent than quoted adjectives. But held-trip-1 loses its per-day temple and inter-stop walking constraints, shopping loses gray/round, and ALFRED tasks lose white/warm because relevant attributes are unavailable in Action signatures. Many values remain prose STRING payloads, which is retained during this build phase but means the proposed vocabulary does not yet structurally preserve their content.
  - determinism: mixed — Removing duplicate role_, tone_, curr_, art_, and dom_day locations is a genuine deterministic improvement. Yet translators still have incompatible choices for unlisted values: format-value permits minting a token that is not yet a glossary member, descriptive-value is a residual bucket with broad dom_ membership, and absent Action signatures force one translator to omit a constraint while another emits an invalid attribute. The independent translations demonstrate this divergence on the mirror, trip, code, and conversational items.
  - interpretability: benefit — The new dedicated value categories and exhaustive attribute index substantially improve lookupability over the prior mixed descriptive-value bucket. The stated construct-sensitive target rule is especially clarifying. Interpretability remains impaired by the residual descriptive-value bucket and by attribute names whose governing rule is merely transitional STRING rather than a descriptive category, but this is a net improvement.
  - improvement: mixed — Canonical role, unit, currency, tone, and artifact symbols give a weaker model useful planning constraints. Conversely, unwired product values and broad STRING payload channels do not improve execution planning for the most complex sampled tasks, and invalid-looking examples can mislead a model into emitting attributes the Action signatures reject.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `held-trip-1` (seed_tasks) [Fail] NL: "Plan a 3-day itinerary in Kyoto that includes at least one temple per day and avoids anything more than a 20-minute walk from the last stop." → `GAP("No construct/category encodes a per-day temple cardinality constraint or a maximum walking duration from each preceding stop; GENERATE content would only preserve these as a STRING payload.")` — artifact-value and duration-unit-value help express an itinerary artifact and three days, but the actionable constraints have no symbolic representation.
  - `held-support-1` (seed_tasks) [Fail] NL: "If the customer's order is late and they're a first-time buyer, offer a 10% discount; if they're a repeat buyer, offer free expedited shipping instead." → `GAP("No defined checks or attribute categories represent order lateness, first-time versus repeat buyer status, percentage discount, or customer-order relation.")` — Flow-If supplies sequencing, but the required condition facts and numeric discount semantics are missing.
  - `6ca55141-9ba6-4ed7-8c07-77df6c24029d` (Mind2Web) [Fail] NL: "Find the lowest-priced gray-colored round-shaped mirror in stock in Atlanta, Georgia, with a maximum price of 20 dollars." → `GAP("search_web permits availability, currency, location, max_price but not color or shape; product-attribute-value is not in the search_web signature, and no shape category exists.")` — currency-value and search-value help with dollars, stock, and cheapest ordering; color_gray would be allowed by the category rule but not by the Action signature.
  - `62806bef-eeeb-4892-b4d5-6a8d2005c58d` (Mind2Web) [Fail] NL: "Browse the trending searches in Columbus." → `GAP("No search category/value or Action signature represents trending searches; a STRING content payload would not supply a structured browsing objective.")` — No proposed change addresses a trend/popularity search criterion.
  - `trial_T20190907_134455_194210#0` (ALFRED) [Fail] NL: "Place a white cup with a knife in it on the counter in front of the toaster." → `GAP("Action signatures do not permit color, and no valid mechanism identifies the white cup while preserving its color constraint.")` — product-attribute-value adds color_white but is not legal on pick_up or place. The remaining knife-in-cup and placement relations are expressible.
  - `trial_T20190908_185043_273617#2` (ALFRED) [Fail] NL: "Put a warm coffee cup in the coffee maker." → `GAP("No Action signature accepts state=state_warm or another construction/state constraint; encoding heat then place preserves the process but cannot confirm the requested warm state symbolically.")` — The existing heat/place sequence can approximate the request, but state_warm is absent and state is not signature-permitted.
  - `pydata__xarray-4356` (SWE-bench_Verified) [Partial] NL: "Fix xarray sum so min_count works for reduction over more than one dimension, using the reported reproduction and proposed nanops.py change." → `ENTRYPOINT FixXarraySum
TASK FixXarraySum {
  ACTION modify_code(target=code, actual_output="sum with min_count errors for more than one dimension", content="replace mask.shape[axis] with np.take(a.shape, axis).prod()", expected_output="min_count works over multiple dimensions", file="xarray/core/nanops.py", method="sum", reproduction_step="da.sum([dim_0, dim_1], min_count=1)")
}` — Action supports the software fields, but every substantive code/path/reproduction value remains a transitional STRING payload rather than a glossary category.
  - `django__django-12155` (SWE-bench_Verified) [Partial] NL: "Fix Django admindoc rendering when a docstring's first line is non-empty by skipping the first line when computing indentation." → `ENTRYPOINT FixDjangoDocstring
TASK FixDjangoDocstring {
  ACTION modify_code(target=code, actual_output="trim_docstring errors when the first docstring line is non-empty", content="skip the first line in indentation calculation", expected_output="render non-empty-first-line docstrings", file="django/contrib/admindocs/utils.py", method="trim_docstring")
  ACTION modify_code(target=code, file="django/contrib/admindocs/views.py", content="update related admindoc handling")
}` — The multi-file ordering is represented, but source code, paths, and expected behavior remain STRING content.
  - `c892` (PRISM) [Partial] NL: "The people in our country say that we are generous and warm, but I think people will take advantage of others; I feel I was born in the wrong country and ask for suggestions." → `ENTRYPOINT GenerosityConcern
CONVO GenerosityConcern {
  TURN t1 SPEAKER=USER {
    UTTER inform(content="People may take advantage of others despite claims of generosity and warmth.", recipient=role_agent)
  }
  TURN t2 SPEAKER=USER REPLY_TO t1 {
    UTTER ask(content="Give suggestions for finding people or groups that are a better fit.", recipient=role_agent)
  }
}` — Conversation sequencing and speech acts are preserved, but the substantive emotional and advisory content remains STRING, violating N3's eventual value-set objective.
  - `c5375` (PRISM) [Partial] NL: "Can I swap my old phone for a new one?" → `ENTRYPOINT PhoneTradeIn
CONVO PhoneTradeIn {
  TURN t1 SPEAKER=USER {
    UTTER ask(content="Can I trade in my old phone for a new phone?", recipient=role_agent)
  }
}` — The ask and recipient are represented; trade-in, old/new ownership, and phone details remain prose.
  - `c2151` (PRISM) [Partial] NL: "How did racism start, and how did it become a problem despite earlier acceptance of homosexuality?" → `ENTRYPOINT RacismHistoryQuestion
CONVO RacismHistoryQuestion {
  TURN t1 SPEAKER=USER {
    UTTER ask(content="Explain historical origins of racism and changing treatment of homosexuality.", recipient=role_agent)
  }
}` — Speech-act metadata is expressible, but the historical comparison is only a STRING payload.
  - `c1195` (PRISM) [Partial] NL: "I believe love in any relationship should be reciprocal and not one-sided." → `ENTRYPOINT ReciprocalLoveView
CONVO ReciprocalLoveView {
  TURN t1 SPEAKER=USER {
    UTTER inform(content="Love in a relationship should be reciprocal rather than one-sided.", recipient=role_agent)
  }
}` — The opinion speech act is captured, but no category structures relationship, reciprocity, or the stated proposition.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-38835` (PATHs) [Partial] NL: "Create a newsletter of up to 800 words from the supplied applied-observability article." → `ENTRYPOINT AppliedObservabilityNewsletter
CONVO AppliedObservabilityNewsletter {
  TURN t1 SPEAKER=USER {
    UTTER ask(content="Create a newsletter from the supplied applied-observability article, with a maximum of 800 words.", recipient=role_agent, tone=tone_professional)
  }
}` — recipient-value and tone-value help, but there is no length attribute on UTTER and newsletter/article/source content is a STRING payload.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-111541` (PATHs) [Partial] NL: "Give another catchy Hinglish SEO title, description, and comma-separated tags for a YouTube Short about Vivek Bindra discussing whether AI will take jobs." → `ENTRYPOINT YouTubeShortSEO
CONVO YouTubeShortSEO {
  TURN t1 SPEAKER=USER {
    UTTER ask(content="Generate another catchy Hinglish SEO title, description, and comma-separated tags about AI taking jobs.", recipient=role_agent)
  }
}` — The request act is represented, but language, SEO keywords, title/description/tags structure, and content remain untyped prose.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-55500` (PATHs) [Partial] NL: "Continue Part 2 of the Donatello and Goku battle story." → `ENTRYPOINT ContinueDonatelloStory
CONVO ContinueDonatelloStory {
  TURN t1 SPEAKER=USER {
    UTTER ask(content="Continue Part 2 of the Donatello and Goku battle story.", recipient=role_agent)
  }
}` — The continuation request is captured as an ask, but character, part, narrative, and transformation constraints remain STRING text.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-30875` (PATHs) [Partial] NL: "Which U.S. state does not require a work permit for non-U.S. citizens?" → `ENTRYPOINT WorkPermitQuestion
CONVO WorkPermitQuestion {
  TURN t1 SPEAKER=USER {

}` — The information request is captured, but jurisdiction, immigration status, and work-permit concepts lack categories.
  - `1775708977070` (ThoughtTrace) [Partial] NL: "Suggest a warm light midnight snack that supports satiety and portion control." → `ENTRYPOINT MidnightSnackAdvice
CONVO MidnightSnackAdvice {
  TURN t1 SPEAKER=USER {
    UTTER ask(content="Suggest a warm light midnight snack for satiety and portion control.", recipient=role_agent)
  }
}` — No food, temperature, nutrition, satiety, or portion-control value category exists; these are explicitly tracked STRING gaps.
  - `1775686840693` (ThoughtTrace) [Partial] NL: "Can foot fungus spread to the groin or buttocks?" → `ENTRYPOINT FungalSpreadQuestion
CONVO FungalSpreadQuestion {
  TURN t1 SPEAKER=USER {

  }
}` — The question act is encoded, but medical topic, affected body sites, and transmission relation remain prose.
  - `1775962320176` (ThoughtTrace) [Partial] NL: "Hello" → `ENTRYPOINT Greeting
CONVO Greeting {
  TURN t1 SPEAKER=USER {
    UTTER inform(content="Hello", recipient=role_agent)
  }
}` — Even this simple greeting requires transitional STRING content because no greeting speech-act/content symbol is defined.
  - `1775436940555` (ThoughtTrace) [Partial] NL: "Suggest a friendly email to my boss asking to resume work after a week absent due to illness." → `ENTRYPOINT ResumeWorkEmail
TASK ResumeWorkEmail : STRING {
  GENERATE(target=art_short_text, audience=role_manager, content="Write a friendly email requesting return to work after one week absent due to illness.", format=format_email, tone=tone_polite) -> short_text : STRING
  RETURN short_text
}` — artifact-value, recipient-value, format-value, and tone-value improve the request metadata, but illness, absence, return-to-work intent, and email substance remain STRING.
- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=low — Anthropic marks gaps for the trip, mirror, ALFRED, code, and open requests, while Gemini frequently emits attributes or symbols unsupported by the current Action signatures and glossary categories (for example color on search_web and white_cup). This supports the finding that the vocabulary/signature boundary is not yet deterministic.
- **Required changes:**
  - `Action`: Revise operation signatures atomically with the new product vocabulary: add color, size, and gender where product search/filtering actually permits them, or remove the product-attribute-value worked example until that wiring exists. Add a separate shape attribute and shape-value category before claiming support for round-product searches.
  - `attribute-name`: Replace the residual mappings `style/locale/modifier/state/metric -> descriptive-value` with dedicated value categories or precise prefix-specific subcategory rules. A value such as a locale, style, or support metric must have a defined symbolic membership rule, not merely be sent to a bucket whose prefixes cover only mod_, state_, and dom_.
  - `format-value`: Make validity consistent with N2/N3: either define the complete category as rule-derived and state that every matching format_ token is glossary-derived immediately, or require prior glossary membership and prohibit minting in expressions. Do not say both that new symbols are permitted and that they are only recorded next sprint.
  - `vocabulary basis`: Add missing categories and Action wiring required by the closed samples: shape; object color/state on household objects; construction/difficulty and production/yield; source/link request; popularity/trending search; itinerary stop/day, per-day cardinality, and walking-duration constraints; and customer/order status plus percentage-discount values.
  - `attribute-name`: For every transitional STRING attribute, add a concrete N2 vocabulary-gap category and a defined migration target rather than treating broad semantic fields such as file, method, policy, medical_topic, food, and message_structure as value sets governed only by STRING. Retaining STRING is allowed in this build phase, but it does not satisfy N3 and must be explicitly narrowed category-by-category.
  - `Types & Lexicon / attribute-name`: Define the static type of glossary value symbols and list elements. The rule `itinerary/route -> LIST[STRING] of entity-name/location-name symbols` conflicts with value typing because an unquoted symbol is syntactically IDENT, not a STRING literal.
- **Logic issues:** product-attribute-value's worked example is statically invalid: search_web's normative signature does not permit color, size, or gender.; No shape attribute or shape-value category exists, so the sampled gray round mirror request cannot be represented even after adding product color values.; format-value's rule permits a translator to mint an unlisted token but says the token is recorded only next sprint; this conflicts with the claim that values are glossary symbols or immediately glossary-rule-derived.; The attribute-name rule calls descriptive-value the governing category for style, locale, and metric even though descriptive-value's normative membership rule only recognizes mod_, state_, and dom_ symbols.; The proposed Generate grammar still admits generic `value`, while its semantics restrict individual attributes; a static validation rule is needed to reject otherwise grammatical but category-invalid values consistently.; The itinerary/route list rule claims LIST[STRING] elements can be entity-name/location-name symbols, but the language's type system does not define such bare symbols as STRING values.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — Six dedicated descriptive categories and a fuller attribute index are real vocabulary growth, but large descriptive domains and several sampled task concepts still have no category.
  - N3: violated — tone, duration_unit, currency, recipient/audience, Generate target, format, and product facets now have much clearer governing sets, and duplicate values were removed. However, many listed attributes still accept transitional STRING phrases, several residual mappings have no precise value rule, and some new values are not legal in their consuming Action signatures.
  - N7: violated — Canonical-category de-duplication and Generate ordering improve convergence. Remaining arbitrary minting, residual buckets, and signature/category mismatches still lead independent translators to incompatible valid/invalid encodings.
  - N1: violated — STRING and free IDENT channels remain intentionally available during the build phase; the proposal does not close them.
  - N4: violated — The proposal explicitly retains content and many other values as natural-language STRING payloads, as permitted before the later closing phase.
  - N5: violated — Expressions still rely on free STRING prose and open identifiers whose semantic segmentation is not wholly glossary-defined.
  - N6: partial — The new categories are grammar-oriented and artifact-value explicitly invokes the complete-glossary assumption. Missing categories for shape, trip constraints, and several other sampled concepts still prevent applying that assumption.
  - N8: partial — Fine-grained tones, units, currencies, formats, roles, and product facets preserve more distinctions. Important closed-task distinctions remain absent or unwired, so richness is still materially constrained.
- **Decision:** partially-accepted
- **Documenter summary:** Shaper proposed a large N3-compliance bundle: splitting Generate.target into a new artifact-value category (vs Action's entity-name), de-duplicating tone/role/currency/unit symbols into dedicated categories, revising the residual descriptive-value and search-value buckets, adding a product-attribute-value category, fixing Flow-If's missing glossary_gloss, and giving attribute-name a full per-attribute governance mapping. The Critic partially accepted it, landing Flow-If and the self-contained vocabulary additions (tone-value, duration-unit-value, currency-value, recipient-value, artifact-value, descriptive-value, search-value, product-attribute-value) while rejecting the Generate, format-value, and attribute-name revisions because product-attribute-value's worked example was statically invalid against search_web's signature, no shape category exists for the sampled gray/round mirror task, and format-value's minting rule contradicted its own immediate-glossary-derivation claim. The decisive factor was this signature/category mismatch (product colors not wired into search_web) combined with several attribute-name mappings pointing to descriptive-value despite that bucket's narrower actual membership rule, which the Critic judged left N3 still violated though improved.
- **Cost this sprint:** $1.0924

## Sprint 18 (attempt 3/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 11.0.0 → 12.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N3 [hard] Every attribute value is a glossary symbol, or is derived from glossary symbols by a glossary-defined rule.
- **Shaper proposal (model: anthropic/claude-sonnet-5):** Resolves the symbol/STRING type conflict, wires color/shape/state/gender/size and trending/discount attributes into Action's actual signatures, fixes Generate's target and adds itinerary-constraint attributes, and splits descriptive-value's residual bucket into dedicated shape/state/style/locale/metric categories with a corrected, non-contradictory format-value rule.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — Every attribute touched this sprint now resolves to a named, glossary-defined value set or literal rule, and the one internal type inconsistency (bare vocabulary symbols vs STRING) blocking several of those categories is fixed.
- **Changes:** `Types & Lexicon` (revise, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `shape-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `state-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `style-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `locale-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `metric-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `descriptive-value` (add, MAJOR), `format-value` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `attribute-name` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR)
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — [Forced acceptance after 3 attempt(s) without a clean accept — the required changes below are known issues carried forward for a future sprint to address, not resolved.] This is real progress on N3: several actionable attributes now have dedicated value categories, and the previous symbol/list typing contradiction is repaired. The bundle cannot be accepted intact because it duplicates an existing glossary construct, has canonical-order violations in normative examples, and overclaims symbolic coverage for an itinerary constraint whose required temple qualifier is absent. The listed vocabulary and type changes are self-contained, non-regressive increments and should land now.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: benefit — The added shape, state, metric, locale, style, itinerary, browsing, and discount facilities cover several previously unsupported held-out constraints, including round mirrors, full/empty rolls, support discounts, and itinerary timing. However, the proposed stop_min_per_day attribute records only a number and cannot state that the required daily stop is a temple, while several code and content attributes remain opaque STRING payloads.
  - expressivity: mixed — The proposal adds first-class fields for several constraints previously hidden in content, but its itinerary example still loses the required temple category: stop_min_per_day=1 means only a count. Transitional STRING attributes continue to carry code paths, expected outcomes, policies, message structure, and substantive generation requests as prose rather than defined value sets.
  - determinism: mixed — Dedicated value categories and the closed-for-encoding format rule improve convergence. Yet independent translations still diverge materially on TASK versus CONVO, target selection, status-query sequencing, artifact type, return type, and handle names; the new open categories also lack exhaustive synonym/canonicalization rules. The worked examples themselves violate the stated canonical attribute ordering.
  - interpretability: mixed — Splitting state/style/locale/metric out of descriptive-value makes values easier to look up. But attribute-name calls itself a complete index while retaining dozens of unresolved future categories, and the duplicate add of the already-existing descriptive-value entry is structurally unclear. The overloaded rule that every vocabulary symbol statically counts as STRING also obscures category identity in general typed contexts.
  - improvement: benefit — A weaker planner gains useful actionable structure from color, shape, state, discount percentage, support metrics, and numeric itinerary constraints. It still cannot reliably act on semantic constraints hidden in STRING content, such as the private-members feature behavior, the exact temple requirement, or a user-provided script that must be revised.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `held-support-1` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "If the customer's order is late and they're a first-time buyer, offer a 10% discount; if they're a repeat buyer, offer free expedited shipping instead." → `ENTRYPOINT HandleLateOrder
TASK HandleLateOrder {
  ACTION check_support_status(target=order, metric=metric_order_late) -> is_late : BOOL
  ACTION check_support_status(target=customer, metric=metric_first_time_buyer) -> is_first_time : BOOL
  ACTION check_support_status(target=customer, metric=metric_repeat_buyer) -> is_repeat : BOOL
  IF is_late AND is_first_time THEN {
    ACTION offer_discount(target=order, discount_percent=10)
  } ELSE {
    IF is_late AND is_repeat THEN {

- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=low — Providers exactly agree only on Limited Time Offers. For support they differ on entity targets, check sequencing, and branch structure; for toilet-paper movement they differ on source order and handle naming; for the Sphinx item one reports a gap while the other uses opaque strings; and for veganism and tweets they differ on TASK versus CONVO, artifact type, styles, and result type. The proposal improves some canonical value symbols but does not resolve these construct-selection and decomposition divergences.
- **Required changes:**
  - `descriptive-value`: Change the operation from add to revise. It already exists in the current glossary, so a second entry would be duplicate and conflicting; replace its state-inclusive semantics and example with the narrowed mod_/dom_ definition.
  - `Generate`: Correct the worked example to obey its own canonical ordering: target, content, duration, duration_unit, max_walk_duration, max_walk_duration_unit, stop_min_per_day. Also add a typed symbolic requirement field, such as stop_requirement -> entity-name/category-value, so 'at least one temple per day' is not reduced to an unqualified count.
  - `Action`: Correct the search_web worked example to its stated required-first then alphabetical optional order: target, availability, color, currency, location, max_price, shape. Do not retain examples that violate the canonical encoding rule.
  - `attribute-name`: Do not describe future category names as if they govern values now. For each transitional STRING attribute, explicitly state that it remains an N3 violation in the build phase and give a concrete next-step category specification; prioritize code-path-value, code-symbol-value, code-call-value, code-outcome-value, food-value, meal-value, message-structure-value, and procedure-value.
  - `itinerary constraints`: Define the semantics and value category for what a per-day minimum applies to, plus the scope of max walking duration (e.g. consecutive itinerary stops), rather than only accepting two numbers and units.
  - `state-value`: Add explicit canonical members and synonym mappings for state_full and state_empty, since they are required by the sampled household task and are not merely illustrative variants of warm/clean.
  - `basis`: Add canonical translation rules for closed-task decomposition and for choosing TASK versus CONVO versus GENERATE. In particular, specify when status checks are hoisted, which entity is target for order/customer support actions, and the canonical artifact/result type for multi-item generation.
- **Logic issues:** The proposal adds descriptive-value even though descriptive-value already exists. This conflicts with the stated change operation and would create duplicate normative glossary entries rather than revise the residual bucket.; Generate's Kyoto worked example violates its own canonical ordering: stop_min_per_day appears before max_walk_duration and max_walk_duration_unit, although optional attributes must be alphabetized.; Action's mirror worked example violates its own canonical ordering: availability, currency, location, max_price, and shape are not emitted in alphabetical optional-attribute order.; stop_min_per_day=1 cannot encode the source meaning 'at least one temple per day'; it expresses only an unqualified minimum count. The proposed claim that the constraint is symbolically covered is therefore overstated.; The new attribute-name entry calls itself a complete index while numerous attributes still resolve only to STRING and a named future category. Naming an eventual category is not a present value-set rule under N3.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — Dedicated shape, state, style, locale, metric, and more explicit format vocabulary materially expand the descriptive lexicon, but many needed descriptive categories remain absent and their values still reside in STRING payloads.
  - N3: violated — Several formerly residual attribute values now have named categories or defined rules, and the vocabulary-symbol typing inconsistency is addressed. However, content, policy, amenity, ticket, seat, code fields, medical fields, food/meal fields, and message/collaboration fields remain arbitrary STRING values; merely naming future categories does not make those values glossary symbols or glossary-derived values. The unqualified itinerary count additionally loses a required attribute constraint.
  - N7: violated — The category splits and closed format encoding reduce value-level ambiguity, but the supplied examples violate canonical ordering and independent translations remain strongly divergent on decomposition, construct selection, targets, and handles.
  - N1: violated — Free STRING values and unrestricted identifiers remain legal in expressions. This build-phase proposal does not close them, and it does not make the existing violation worse.
  - N4: violated — All open-item simulations and several closed-item simulations still require sentence-like STRING payloads. The proposal expressly retains content as a transitional STRING channel, as permitted by the stated build-phase sequencing.
  - N5: violated — Arbitrary strings and open identifier-derived symbols remain available, so the language is not self-contained. The added categories do not worsen that pre-existing condition.
  - N6: partial — The proposal generally uses category-level syntax and permits assuming missing members in an existing category, but several real needs have only future category names rather than an existing category capable of holding them.
  - N8: partial — Fine-grained state, shape, style, locale, metric, and itinerary numeric constraints preserve distinctions rather than collapsing them. Richness remains incomplete where substantive constraints are kept in STRING and where temple-per-day cannot be represented.
- **Decision:** accepted
- **Documenter summary:** Steering sprint on N3 (attribute values must be glossary symbols or glossary-derived): the Shaper proposed fixing the vocabulary-symbol/STRING typing conflict, wiring shape/state/gender/size/trending/discount attributes into Action, adding a GENERATE stop_min_per_day/max_walk_duration pair, splitting descriptive-value's residual bucket into shape-value/state-value/style-value/locale-value/metric-value, and rewriting attribute-name and format-value, complying fully with N3. The Critic ruled needs-rework (forced accept after 3 attempts): it upheld the core Types & Lexicon fix and the new vocabulary categories as self-contained, non-regressive gains, but flagged that the descriptive-value entry duplicated an existing construct, the Generate/Action worked examples violated their own canonical ordering, and stop_min_per_day=1 overclaimed coverage since it can't encode 'temple' as the required stop type—so only the Types & Lexicon, shape/state/style/locale/metric-value, and format-value changes were accepted, with attribute-name, Generate, Action, and the itinerary-constraint semantics left as open required changes for a future sprint. Doc hygiene passed, and the decision was accepted on that partial basis.
- **Cost this sprint:** $1.1222

## Sprint 19 (attempt 1/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 12.0.0 → 13.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N3 [hard] Every attribute value is a glossary symbol, or is derived from glossary symbols by a glossary-defined rule.
- **Shaper proposal (model: openai/gpt-5.6-terra):** This increment makes itinerary constraints semantically typed, corrects canonical examples, explicitly records remaining STRING-governed attributes as N3 build-phase violations, and adds canonical routing/decomposition rules. It also repairs the residual descriptive-value category and makes full/empty object state canonical.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — Every newly governed attribute value in this increment is constrained to a named glossary category, a defined literal form, a list thereof, or a typed binding. Remaining STRING channels are explicitly identified as unresolved N3 build-phase violations rather than described as governed value sets.
- **Changes:** `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `Utterance` (revise, MAJOR), `attribute-name` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `state-value` (revise vocabulary, MINOR), `descriptive-value` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR)
- **Partial acceptance:** applied 4 of 6 changes (`Utterance`, `attribute-name`, `state-value`, `descriptive-value`); the rest were sent back for rework.
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — This is real progress on N3: it correctly identifies transitional STRING attributes as violations, adds typed itinerary qualification, and supplies canonical full/empty states. However, the revised Action rule invalidates its own REF/list-target examples under its stated category policy, and typed bindings do not retain category provenance, leaving N3 and deterministic parsing/validation materially underspecified. The listed vocabulary and Utterance clarifications are sound and self-contained, but Action and Generate need the specified repairs before the bundle can be accepted.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — Structured itinerary constraints and explicit full/empty states improve coverage for the Kyoto and household items. However, the revised Action rule describes target as category-governed/entity-name while its own examples use REF and LIST[REF[STRING]] bindings as targets; read literally, this rejects ordinary follow-on actions such as place(target=full_roll) and sort(target=mirrors), reducing existing closed-task coverage.
  - expressivity: mixed — stop_requirement plus defined consecutive-stop walking scope preserves the formerly lost "one temple per day" qualifier, and state_full/state_empty preserve a real household distinction. But compatibility with a selected motherboard remains unrepresentable, and STRING-bound values can still be passed as purported category values without a defined provenance rule.
  - determinism: mixed — The repaired attribute-order examples and stated TASK/CONVO/GENERATE routing rules improve canonicalization. Yet target has incompatible readings across the proposed Action semantics, attribute-name vocabulary, and worked examples, while category symbols and arbitrary STRING bindings share static type STRING; translators cannot deterministically decide whether a binding is eligible as an entity/category value.
  - interpretability: mixed — The proposal clearly labels remaining STRING channels as N3 violations rather than falsely calling them governed sets, and the new itinerary fields are readable. Conversely, terms such as "entity-name-compatible typed binding" and the operational meaning of category-valid bindings are undefined, while the target rule cannot explain the proposal's own sort/place examples.
  - improvement: benefit — A weaker executor gains useful structure from explicit temple-per-day and walking-between-consecutive-stops constraints, state_full/state_empty, and the routing/decomposition guidance. These improvements are meaningful even though Action target typing must be repaired before the full planning benefit is reliable.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `dev-embodied-2` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Put both pillows from the sofa onto the armchair, one at a time." → `ENTRYPOINT PlacePillows
TASK PlacePillows {
  ACTION pick_up(target=pillow, source=sofa) -> pillow_1 : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(target=pillow_1, destination=armchair, relation=on)
  ACTION pick_up(target=pillow, source=sofa) -> pillow_2 : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(target=pillow_2, destination=armchair, relation=on)
}` — state-value is irrelevant. The expression exposes the Action target contradiction: place must accept the REF binding pillow_1/pillow_2, not only entity-name.
  - `dev-email-2` (seed_tasks) [Partial] NL: "If my manager hasn't replied by Friday, send a polite follow-up; otherwise do nothing." → `ENTRYPOINT FollowUpManager
TASK FollowUpManager CONTEXT execution_date="2025-06-03" {
  ACTION check_replied(target=role_manager, deadline="2025-06-06T23:59:59Z") -> replied : BOOL
  IF NOT replied THEN {
    ACTION send_email(recipient=role_manager, tone=tone_polite, content="follow-up")
  }
}` — Recipient, tone, condition, and deadline are structured, but content remains the explicitly unresolved STRING channel and therefore violates N3.
  - `e344b89e-767a-4618-ba82-4b81cdcba280` (Mind2Web) [Partial] NL: "add a motherboard under $200 and a compatible processor at any price to the shopping cart." → `ENTRYPOINT AddCompatibleParts
TASK AddCompatibleParts {
  ACTION search_web(target=motherboard, currency=curr_usd, max_price=200) -> motherboards : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  ACTION extract(target=motherboards, limit=1) -> motherboard_pick : REF[STRING]
  ACTION add_to_cart(target=motherboard_pick)
  ACTION search_web(target=processor) -> processors : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  ACTION check_support_status(target=processor, metric=metric_compatibility) -> compatible : BOOL
  IF compatible THEN {
    ACTION extract(target=processors, limit=1) -> processor_pick : REF[STRING]
    ACTION add_to_cart(target=processor_pick)
  }
}` — The price constraint is structured, but metric_compatibility cannot state compatibility with motherboard_pick. The Action target rule also conflicts with the list/ref targets used by extract and add_to_cart.

}` — The core fix is representable only through the still-untyped STRING code attributes. No symbolic category yet captures locale-tag behavior, reproduction, expected result, or actual result.

  GENERATE(target=art_technical_explanation, audience=role_user, content="continue with more detailed explanation of linear algebra in ChatGPT-style language models, with concrete examples", locale=locale_en_us, style=style_technical, tone=tone_casual) -> explanation : STRING
  RETURN explanation
}` — Artifact type, audience, locale, style, and tone are governed values, but the required technical subject, depth, continuity, and examples remain STRING content. The independent translations diverged sharply between GAP and a similar GENERATE form.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-148973` (PATHs) [Partial] NL: "The gang attends a school run by Billy Dee Williams that teaches people how to be cool, which is plagued by a pterodactyl." → `ENTRYPOINT GenerateComedyScript

  RETURN script

  RETURN guide
