- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=low — Independent translations materially diverge for embodied decomposition (search_web/FOR EACH versus two explicit pickups), cup preparation (rinse versus pickup-only), and code repair (GAP versus free strings). For the LLM explanation, one provider emitted GAP while another emitted GENERATE. The new canonical routing rules help somewhat, but they do not resolve Action target typing, decomposition granularity, or missing symbolic categories.
- **Required changes:**
  - `Action`: Replace the blanket entity-name/category rule for target with an operation-signature-specific target domain. Explicitly define, for each operation, whether target accepts entity-name symbols, artifact-value symbols, REF[T] bindings, LIST[REF[T]] bindings, or another named category. Ensure sort(target=mirrors), extract(target=motherboards), add_to_cart(target=motherboard_pick), heat(target=cup_1), and place(target=full_roll) are all valid or revise the examples accordingly.
  - `attribute-name`: Revise target's gloss from 'Action subject: entity-name' to refer to Action's signature-specific target domains, including typed REF and LIST[REF] bindings. Do not claim one category governs all Action target values.
  - `basis`: Define category-value provenance for bindings. A bare category symbol may be bound and reused only if the binding retains its originating glossary category (or a declared union); an arbitrary STRING binding must not become eligible for entity-name, recipient-value, stop_requirement, or another category merely because all vocabulary symbols currently type-check as STRING. Replace undefined phrases such as 'entity-name-compatible typed binding' with this explicit rule.
  - `Generate`: State stop_requirement's exact accepted domain as entity-name symbols, a declared semantic-category symbol, a homogeneous list thereof when permitted, or a binding with retained provenance in that domain. Remove or define 'entity-name-compatible typed binding'; static type STRING alone is insufficient.
  - `compatibility constraints`: Add a signature-listed, typed relation mechanism that can express 'processor compatible with motherboard_pick' rather than merely querying metric_compatibility about an unqualified processor. Define its value domain and canonical placement so the shopping task's paired compatibility condition is preserved.
  - `itinerary and budget constraints`: Add a governed budget/amount attribute with NUMBER plus currency-value and an itinerary-revision linkage or equivalent structured planning constraint. This is needed to encode the Italy request's 1,000,000 CLP budget rather than burying it in UTTER content.
- **Logic issues:** The proposed Action semantics says every attribute value uses its attribute-name governing category, while proposed attribute-name defines Action target as entity-name; nevertheless Action's own worked example uses sort(target=mirrors) where mirrors is LIST[REF[STRING]], and the state-value example uses place(target=full_roll) where full_roll is REF[STRING].; Category membership is not preserved by the current type system: glossary symbols and arbitrary STRING literals/bindings all have static type STRING. Therefore a STRING variable can be supplied where the proposal says a 'typed binding holding' a category value is allowed, defeating N3.; The phrase 'entity-name-compatible typed binding' has no grammar, static-type criterion, or glossary definition, so Generate stop_requirement has undefined acceptance behavior.; The proposal resolves a temple-count qualifier but not the relation required by 'a compatible processor' to the selected motherboard; metric_compatibility is only an unqualified boolean metric.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — The revised attribute index more honestly distinguishes existing value rules from missing categories and adds a useful state distinction. The glossary still lacks many required descriptive categories, including the code, food, meal, procedure, and symbolic message-content value categories.
  - N3: violated — The proposal adds governed stop_requirement semantics, state_full/state_empty, and explicit labels for residual STRING violations instead of misrepresenting planned categories as present value sets. It does not uphold N3 because content and many other attributes remain STRING, and its allowance for category bindings lacks a provenance rule that prevents arbitrary STRING values from masquerading as governed category values.
  - N7: violated — Canonical routing, support-target guidance, output cardinality, and repaired ordering examples are progress. Deterministic encoding remains unsatisfied because Action target domains conflict and category-valued binding eligibility is undefined.
  - N1: violated — Free identifiers and STRING channels remain available by design in this build phase. The changes do not make that hard-note violation worse.
  - N4: violated — Most simulations necessarily retain natural-language content strings. The proposal explicitly recognizes these as transitional rather than expanding them, so it does not regress the note.
  - N5: violated — STRING payloads and free identifiers still require language outside the glossary. No newly introduced free-text channel worsens that pre-existing condition.
  - N6: partial — The proposal uses category-based fields such as stop_requirement and assumes temple as an entity-name member. Missing categories, rather than merely missing members, still force prose for compatibility, budget, food, and software details.
  - N8: partial — The explicit qualifier for a per-day stop minimum and the full/empty distinction preserve useful meaning instead of collapsing it. Compatibility-to-selected-item and detailed planning constraints remain too coarse or unavailable.
- **Decision:** partially-accepted
- **Documenter summary:** Addressing steering note N3 (every attribute value must be a glossary symbol or a rule-derived value), the Shaper proposed revisions to Action, Generate, and Utterance plus new/updated vocabulary (attribute-name, state-value, descriptive-value) to close the gap between governed symbols and lingering STRING channels, complying with the note. The Critic partially accepted: it adopted the Utterance, attribute-name, state-value, and descriptive-value changes as sound, self-contained N3 progress, but rejected the Action and Generate revisions because the Action target rule contradicted its own worked examples (sort/place/extract using REF and LIST[REF] targets) and because 'entity-name-compatible typed binding' and category-value provenance for bindings were left undefined, leaving deterministic validation broken—most decisively shown by the dev-embodied-2 and Mind2Web motherboard/processor simulations exposing the target-typing conflict.
- **Cost this sprint:** $0.9814

## Sprint 19 (attempt 2/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 13.0.0 → 13.1.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N3 [hard] Every attribute value is a glossary symbol, or is derived from glossary symbols by a glossary-defined rule.
- **Shaper proposal (model: openai/gpt-5.6-terra):** This revision defines provenance-aware glossary values, operation-specific Action target domains, compatibility filtering, and structured budget constraints. It keeps transitional STRING channels available while ensuring newly governed attribute values cannot be supplied by arbitrary STRING bindings.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — The changes make category-valued attributes validate against explicit glossary categories or retained category provenance, while adding governed compatibility and budget constraints needed by the exposed shopping and itinerary cases.
- **Changes:** `Types & Lexicon` (revise, MAJOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `attribute-name` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `Utterance` (revise, MAJOR), `currency-value` (revise vocabulary, MINOR), `semantic-category-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR)
- **Partial acceptance:** applied 2 of 7 changes (`currency-value`, `semantic-category-value`); the rest were sent back for rework.
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — The bundle substantively addresses every prior required change: it introduces provenance, operation-specific target domains, compatibility filtering, and structured budgets. Those are net-positive for coverage and agent planning, but the provenance-union mechanism, class-category overlap, and incomplete Action signature table leave the revised language logically underspecified. The two vocabulary additions are independently sound and can land now; the dependent construct revisions need the listed repairs before acceptance.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: benefit — Operation-specific targets preserve previously difficult selected-object pipelines: add_to_cart can consume a selected reference, sort/extract can consume lists, and compatible_with preserves the motherboard/processor dependency. Structured budget, currency, and itinerary-stop constraints improve coverage for the exposed planning requests, although software and prose-content attributes remain transitional STRING channels.
  - expressivity: benefit — The proposal adds previously missing structured meaning for compatibility, monetary budgets, currency, stop classes, and revision of itinerary budget constraints. The simulations still require prose STRING payloads for code details and open-content requests, so the improvement is real but incomplete under N3/N4.
  - determinism: mixed — Canonical target-first/alphabetical attribute ordering and provenance validation improve convergence for Action and Generate. However, provenance unions are permitted without a grammar for declaring them, entity-name and semantic-category-value overlap on generic classes such as temple, and most Action signatures remain referenced rather than exhaustively specified; translators therefore still have multiple plausible encodings.
  - interpretability: mixed — A reader can now distinguish a named entity selector, selected REF, and list-of-REF target in important shopping and embodied pipelines. But the new static provenance system is underspecified syntactically, and the semantic-category-value category duplicates the current entity-name definition of class-like nouns, making category choice difficult to recover reliably.
  - improvement: benefit — The structured shopping and itinerary expressions give a weaker agent useful executable constraints that prose content or an unqualified compatibility metric would hide. This is especially clear in the processor-compatible-with-selected-motherboard and Chilean-peso budget cases.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `dev-embodied-2` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Put both pillows from the sofa onto the armchair, one at a time." → `ENTRYPOINT MovePillows
TASK MovePillows {
  ACTION pick_up(target=pillow, source=sofa) -> pillow_1 : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(target=pillow_1, destination=armchair, relation=on)
  ACTION pick_up(target=pillow, source=sofa) -> pillow_2 : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(target=pillow_2, destination=armchair, relation=on)
}` — The revised Action target domains help: pick_up selects entity-name and place consumes REF[STRING]. The exact necessity of relation=on remains a canonicalization question.
  - `dev-embodied-1` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Rinse the mug in the sink, then put it in the coffee maker." → `ENTRYPOINT RinseThenPlace

- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=partial — Independent translators agree on the broad embodied and shopping decompositions, but differ on whether place requires relation=on, whether the apple slice receives a new REF handle, whether an open request is TASK/GENERATE or CONVO/UTTER, and whether a software request is a gap or transitional STRING-based Action. Gemini also orders sort attributes contrary to the proposed alphabetical rule, demonstrating that the canonicalization rule is not yet reliably followed.
- **Required changes:**
  - `Types & Lexicon`: Define a concrete grammar and static-checking syntax for provenance-qualified bindings and finite provenance unions, or remove union acceptance until it has such syntax. Specify how provenance is assigned to direct symbols, retained through LET, list literals, Action/Generate results, parameters, and task-call results; an unannotated STRING must never acquire provenance.
  - `Action`: Publish a complete normative signature table for every existing operation, including legal attributes, required/optional status, each attribute domain, result type/provenance, and canonical order. Replace the non-normative statement that unspecified operations 'state their named target category'; it currently leaves heat, slice, rinse, place, modify_code, run_tests, and existing search operations underspecified.
  - `semantic-category-value`: Eliminate the overlap with entity-name or define a non-overlapping boundary. The current entity-name rule already defines class-like nouns such as temple, while semantic-category-value also defines class_temple; specify one canonical category and encoding rule for generic classes versus concrete named entities, then revise all itinerary examples consistently.
  - `attribute-name`: Add individually indexed entries and exact domains for every remaining attribute used by all constructs and Action signatures, including max_price, min_rating, rank_field, rank_direction, limit, availability, genre, category, platform, deadline, date, and all code-operation attributes. Mark only deliberately transitional STRING attributes as N3 violations and name their replacement category.
  - `Generate`: Define quantity's measurement semantics or add a governed unit/value mechanism for output length. In particular, state how an exact word-count requirement is encoded so quantity=100 cannot ambiguously mean characters, sections, items, or words.
  - `basis`: Continue the N3 build by replacing the remaining STRING-governed attribute channels with named glossary categories, starting with code-path/code-symbol/code-change/test-assertion values and symbolic message/artifact content. Retaining them for build-phase coverage is permitted, but they must remain explicitly tracked as unresolved violations.
- **Logic issues:** The proposal permits a finite provenance union but supplies neither a type grammar nor an annotation form for declaring one, so two translators cannot know how to encode or validate a union-valued binding.; semantic-category-value duplicates entity-name for generic classes: entity-name is already defined as a discrete entity class, while class_temple is also a temple class. The same stop requirement consequently has two category-valid encodings.; The Action revision says every signature-listed attribute is validated but does not actually provide complete signatures for most retained operations. The phrase 'other signatures state their named target category' is undefined normative behavior rather than a signature.; The revised attribute-name category says existing members retain their domains, but N3 requires every attribute to have an exact listed value domain. Important existing attributes and code-operation fields remain only partially indexed or explicitly STRING-governed.; The proposed 100-word generation cannot be expressed precisely: quantity=100 has no unit or output-length semantics.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — currency-value and semantic-category-value add descriptive glossary coverage, and the attribute-name revision makes some domains more explicit. The glossary is still not the complete lexicon because many attributes and code/content concepts remain unlisted or transitional.
  - N3: violated — This is genuine progress: category provenance prevents an arbitrary STRING binding from masquerading as entity-name, recipient-value, artifact-value, or stop requirement, and budget/currency/compatibility domains are newly governed. It remains violated because content and many software and domain-specific attributes explicitly accept STRING, and provenance unions/signature coverage are not fully defined.
  - N7: violated — Target-first/alphabetical ordering plus operation-specific domains improve canonical encoding for several pipelines. Full deterministic encoding is still absent because category overlap, incomplete signatures, and union syntax leave multiple or undefined encodings.
  - N1: violated — New governed symbols and membership rules make more emitted values traceable to glossary categories. Free identifiers and STRING channels remain, so expressions are not yet closed-vocabulary.
  - N4: violated — The proposal intentionally retains content and other STRING channels during the build phase. It neither removes prose from expressions nor broadens prose use beyond the already retained channels.
  - N5: violated — The new atomic category symbols are self-contained, but unrestricted STRING payloads and free identifiers remain available. The proposal does not worsen those existing open channels.
  - N6: partial — The revisions increasingly define syntax over category domains such as artifact-value, recipient-value, currency-value, and semantic-category-value. Incomplete Action signatures and reliance on prose content prevent the language from consistently operating over a complete assumed glossary.
  - N8: partial — Compatibility relations, currency amounts, budget constraints, and itinerary stop qualifications preserve meaningful distinctions rather than collapsing them into content. The unresolved 100-word constraint and broad STRING channels still hide substantial distinctions.
- **Decision:** partially-accepted
- **Documenter summary:** Addressing steering note N3 (attribute values must be glossary symbols or glossary-derived), the Shaper proposed provenance-qualified vocabulary typing, operation-specific Action target domains, compatibility filtering, structured budget/currency constraints, and new semantic-category-value and currency-value glossary entries, in full compliance with the note. The Critic found the two vocabulary additions (currency-value, semantic-category-value) independently sound and accepted them, but ruled the dependent Types & Lexicon, Action, attribute-name, and Generate revisions needs-rework because provenance unions lack a defined grammar, semantic-category-value overlaps entity-name, and Action signatures remain incompletely specified — most decisively the simulation showing quantity=100 cannot deterministically express an exact word count and the cross-check showing translators disagree on canonical Action attribute ordering.
- **Cost this sprint:** $0.9333

## Sprint 19 (attempt 3/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 13.1.0 → 14.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N3 [hard] Every attribute value is a glossary symbol, or is derived from glossary symbols by a glossary-defined rule.
- **Shaper proposal (model: openai/gpt-5.6-terra):** This step gives category-valued bindings a concrete static syntax, makes Action signatures exhaustive, removes the itinerary class overlap, and adds governed code/content and output-measurement value sets. Transitional STRING channels remain explicitly marked only where retained for build-phase coverage.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — Every governed attribute now has an individually indexed domain, and category-valued references can be checked through explicit category-qualified types rather than untyped STRING. New code and symbolic-content categories provide N3-compliant replacements for the most important remaining STRING attribute channels.
- **Changes:** `Types & Lexicon` (revise, MAJOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `attribute-name` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `Utterance` (revise, MAJOR), `semantic-category-value` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `entity-name` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `code-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `content-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `duration-unit-value` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR)
- **Partial acceptance:** applied 3 of 10 changes (`code-value`, `content-value`, `duration-unit-value`); the rest were sent back for rework.
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — This is real progress on N3: governed code/content symbols, qualified category bindings, semantic-class separation, and output-count units all improve structured encoding. It cannot be accepted because the purported exhaustive Action and entity-name revisions regress N2 build-phase coverage and contain undefined union/type syntax. The three additive vocabulary categories are sound and self-contained enough to land now while the destructive revisions are repaired.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — Code and symbolic-content categories improve coverage for SWE-bench, generated artifacts, and conversations. However, the proposed Action table declares itself exhaustive while omitting currently expressible operations such as open_container, close_container, turn_on, turn_off, select_filter, apply_filters, and existing domain actions such as booking; revising entity-name with only four members also risks deleting existing entities such as rag, sink, box, keys, chair, and mirror. This is a coverage regression during N2's build phase.
  - expressivity: mixed — The new code-value, content-value, semantic-category-value, and output-unit mechanisms preserve distinctions previously hidden in STRING, including expected versus actual output and exact word counts. But the action-table omissions make some existing closed tasks unexpressible, and content-value remains a single opaque proposition symbol that cannot yet compositionally preserve the substantial content of detailed code reports or long creative specifications.
  - determinism: mixed — Category-qualified STRING types, the semantic-class/entity boundary, and alphabetized attributes are genuine improvements. The independent translations agree on the main pipelines for deleting old files, moving a rag, and modifying code, but disagree on target symbols, handle names, code path symbols, whether to include ENTRYPOINT, and the store-setting gap. The proposed Action table also introduces undeclared union notation (P|E and E|location-name), while Types declares exact matching and no unions.
  - interpretability: mixed — The individually indexed attribute domains and namespaced code prefixes make many values more inspectable. Interpretability is harmed by an allegedly complete Action signature table that silently removes existing operations, by undefined syntax for category-id and type unions, and by a broad content-value category whose member glosses may conceal arbitrarily complex propositions.
  - improvement: benefit — For supported tasks, explicit code fields, typed category provenance, exact quantity units, and the entity-versus-semantic-class distinction would help a weaker planner preserve important constraints. The benefit is limited by missing operation signatures and the remaining transitional STRING channels.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `dev-code-1` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Given a list of file paths, delete every file older than 30 days unless it's in the 'archive' folder." → `ENTRYPOINT DeleteOldFiles

  FOR EACH f IN files {
    ACTION file_age_days(file=f) -> age : NUMBER
    ACTION path_in_folder(file=f, source=path_archive) -> in_archive : BOOL
    IF age > 30 AND NOT in_archive THEN {

    }
  }
}` — code-value and qualified LIST typing support the file-path pipeline; target=file_system is assumed to be an entity-name member under N6.

- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=partial — Anthropic and Gemini converge on the delete-old-files and red-rag action sequences, and both identify a missing final store-setting action. They diverge on target names, handle names, inclusion of entrypoints, code symbols, and treatment of missing content symbols; neither provider demonstrates a single canonical encoding.
- **Required changes:**
  - `Action`: Do not replace the existing operation surface with the listed table until it includes every currently legal operation and its prior capabilities. Publish a normative signature for open_container, close_container, turn_on, turn_off, select_filter, apply_filters, booking/schedule-selection operations, and every other existing operation; alternatively retain their old normative signatures verbatim. Add a typed set_active_store or equivalent operation that accepts REF[STRING] for the Mind2Web store-setting core.
  - `entity-name`: Revise the proposed member list as an additive preservation of all current entity-name members, not a replacement list. Explicitly retain existing symbols such as rag, sink, box, keys, chair, mirror, and all prior members, then add the selector-versus-abstract-class boundary.
  - `Types & Lexicon`: Define the lexical syntax of category-id inside STRING<category-id>, including whether glossary category names containing hyphens are legal there. Replace undeclared Action notation P|E and E|location-name with a grammar-defined union type and exact typing rules, or split those signatures into unambiguous overloads/attributes. State category provenance for operation inputs/results consistently with the exact-match rule.
  - `attribute-name`: Make the index exhaustive across all retained constructs and all retained Action signatures, including legacy attributes such as ordering, procedure, food, meal, message_structure, policy, amenity, ticket, and seat if their operations remain. For each, specify one exact domain and mark every deliberate STRING alternative as transitional with its named replacement category.
  - `Action`: Align the code-value prefix restrictions with the signatures: source is declared code-value in path_in_folder but its prefix requirement is not stated in the signature table; target=file_system and other target symbols need declared category domains. Define whether operation result references retain any category provenance or are intentionally REF[STRING] only.
  - `basis`: Keep the three accepted additive vocabulary changes, then continue N3 by replacing the remaining explicit content:...|STRING alternatives and other transitional STRING-governed attributes with category domains. Add composable content/constraint categories rather than relying solely on one opaque content-value atom for source preservation, revision constraints, structured transformations, and open-request details.
- **Logic issues:** The proposal's Action semantics calls its signature table normative and exhaustive, but it omits multiple operations currently listed in operation-vocabulary and previously expressible in the language. This regresses build-phase coverage.; The proposed Action signatures use P|E and E|location-name union notation, but Types & Lexicon defines no union grammar or static semantics and expressly says no provenance unions exist.; category-id is described only as a glossary vocabulary-category name. Existing category names such as semantic-category-value contain hyphens, while the grammar supplies no category-id lexical rule.; The entity-name revision's displayed member set would replace rather than preserve the existing category's members unless explicitly stated otherwise, invalidating numerous current expressions and sampled household tasks.; The new attribute-name index claims to be exhaustive but omits current legacy attributes and does not reconcile the old Action attribute ordering with its revised table.; The content-value worked example emits content_gloopy_glitter_meet_scientist, which is not among its listed members; this is permissible only under N6's assumed-complete-glossary direction, but the proposal should state that examples may use assumed members rather than appearing as a closed current list.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — The proposal adds useful descriptive categories, but its exhaustive Action replacement and abbreviated entity-name revision remove previously expressible operations and entity symbols during the mandated no-coverage-reduction build phase.
  - N3: violated — Category-qualified bindings, individually indexed domains, code-value, content-value, semantic-category-value, and unit_word/unit_item are substantial progress. N3 remains violated because Action, Generate, and Utterance explicitly retain content-value|STRING channels, and several other legacy STRING channels are not governed by this revision.
  - N7: violated — Qualified categories and canonical attribute ordering improve deterministic typing and encoding. Canonical expressions are still not assured: type unions are undefined, handle names remain unconstrained, independent translations diverge, and the revised Action surface is incomplete.
  - N1: violated — The build phase properly retains STRING, but expressions may still use free identifiers and quoted strings. The added categories do not close the vocabulary.
  - N4: violated — The proposal correctly labels prose STRING alternatives as transitional, but retains them in Action, Generate, and Utterance.
  - N5: violated — Atomic code-value and content-value symbols reduce dependence on English prose. STRING and broad free identifiers remain, so the language is not self-contained.
  - N6: satisfied — The new grammar is generally written over category names, and open categories with membership rules support assuming needed members such as code paths and artifact types. Missing operation categories remain genuine gaps rather than an N6 issue.
  - N8: partial — Expected/actual outputs, reproduction, revision, semantic classes, and quantity units add useful distinctions. The loss of legacy operations and reliance on opaque content atoms prevent the language from retaining richness consistently.
- **Decision:** partially-accepted
- **Documenter summary:** Shaper proposed a broad N3 compliance push: category-qualified STRING types, an 'exhaustive' Action signature table, tightened entity-name vs semantic-category-value boundaries, and new code-value/content-value/duration-unit-value vocabularies. The Critic partially accepted only the additive, self-contained vocabulary additions (code-value, content-value, duration-unit-value) while rejecting the Action and entity-name revisions as regressions, since simulated Mind2Web and ALFRED examples showed the claimed-exhaustive Action table silently dropped operations (e.g. no store-setting action, no open/close/turn_on/off) and the trimmed entity-name list risked deleting existing members like rag, sink, box, keys, chair—violating N2's no-coverage-reduction build phase—compounded by undefined union syntax (P|E) contradicting the no-unions rule in Types & Lexicon.
- **Cost this sprint:** $1.0432

## Sprint 20 (attempt 1/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 14.0.0 → 15.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N3 [hard] Every attribute value is a glossary symbol, or is derived from glossary symbols by a glossary-defined rule.
- **Shaper proposal (model: gemini/gemini-3.1-pro-preview):** Fixes the Action signature table and entity-name list to preserve all existing operations and members, introduces exact category-id syntax to resolve union ambiguity, and replaces opaque content strings with composable constraints, topics, and transformations.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — These changes fully realize N3 by ensuring every attribute, including legacy ones, has a defined category domain, while preserving the expressivity and coverage of the existing language.
- **Changes:** `Types & Lexicon` (revise, MAJOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `Utterance` (revise, MAJOR), `attribute-name` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `entity-name` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `semantic-category-value` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `topic-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `constraint-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `transformation-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR)
- **Partial acceptance:** applied 5 of 10 changes (`entity-name`, `semantic-category-value`, `topic-value`, `constraint-value`, `transformation-value`); the rest were sent back for rework.
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — The bundle makes genuine progress on N3 through exact category provenance and several useful symbolic vocabulary categories, but it does not fully satisfy N3 because it explicitly retains unqualified STRING-valued attributes. More seriously, the revised signatures conflict with the operation vocabulary and omit retained capabilities, creating coverage and lexicon regressions. The additive vocabulary revisions listed in accept_changes are sound and can land independently, while the type/signature revisions require reconciliation before acceptance.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — The typed Action table restores many previously underspecified operations and adds set_active_store, code operations, rinse, slice, booking, and scheduling. However, it replaces the prior extensible/retained operation surface with a table that omits operations still used by the current specification, such as book_hotel, while book and select_schedule are not members of operation-vocabulary. The resulting language has avoidable regressions and cannot reliably preserve all currently expressible closed tasks.
  - expressivity: mixed — topic-value, constraint-value, transformation-value, semantic-category-value, and category-qualified attributes preserve useful distinctions, including generic itinerary classes versus selectable entities. But the proposed small lists do not compositionally encode many sampled request details, such as comparative advice, source credibility, narrative events, safety/eligibility conditions, and nuanced revision constraints; treating each whole request as a single content-value atom hides rather than structures those distinctions.
  - determinism: mixed — Exact category provenance and the entity-name versus semantic-category-value distinction improve deterministic typing and eliminate one known union ambiguity. Determinism remains weak because operation selection is inconsistent with operation-vocabulary, category-id is syntactically open without a canonical glossary lookup rule, handle naming is still unconstrained, and the independent translations materially differ on ENTRYPOINT wrapping, source selection, optional spatial relation, and whether the request becomes content versus topic/constraints.
  - interpretability: mixed — The signature table and category domains make many attributes easier to inspect than raw strings. Yet the table is internally inconsistent with the attribute index and operation vocabulary, legacy attributes are described as bare transitional STRING domains despite N3, and opaque atoms such as content_<entire_request> or topic_<entire_request> cannot be understood from their spelling alone without a listed glossary member.
  - improvement: benefit — For structured shopping, household, travel, and code tasks, exact signatures, typed filters, typed code fields, and explicit transformations give a weaker planner substantially better actionable structure than raw text. This benefit is limited for broad open requests because the proposal has no compositional representation for much of their substance beyond a single topic/content atom.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `dev-embodied-2` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Put both pillows from the sofa onto the armchair, one at a time." → `ENTRYPOINT MovePillows
TASK MovePillows {
  ACTION pick_up(target=pillow, source=sofa) -> pillow_1 : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(target=pillow_1, destination=armchair, relation=on)
  ACTION pick_up(target=pillow, source=sofa) -> pillow_2 : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(target=pillow_2, destination=armchair, relation=on)
}` — Action's typed pick_up/place signatures express the sequential two-item core. The proposal helps, although canonical handle names remain unspecified.
  - `dev-email-1` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Draft a reply to my professor asking for a two-day extension on the assignment, and keep it formal." → `ENTRYPOINT DraftExtensionReply
TASK DraftExtensionReply : STRING {
  GENERATE(target=art_email, audience=role_professor, content=content_request_two_day_assignment_extension, duration=2, duration_unit=unit_day, tone=tone_formal) -> reply : STRING
  RETURN reply
}` — Uses symbolic content, recipient, duration, and tone. art_email and content_request_two_day_assignment_extension are assumed full-glossary members under N6.
  - `42657330-bfc5-425d-ae21-396a9ba1fb12` (Mind2Web (osunlp/Mind2Web, train, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Full] NL: "Find yellow t-shirts for women small size that are less than $20." → `ENTRYPOINT FindTShirts
TASK FindTShirts : LIST[REF[STRING]] {

- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=low — Independent translations disagree materially: one omits ENTRYPOINT/TASK wrappers while another includes them; pillow placement differs on whether relation=on is required; the goblet task differs on source and placement interpretation; and open/code requests differ between GAP placeholders and newly minted atomic symbols. The proposal improves category distinctions but does not provide canonical document wrapping, optional-modifier policy, handle naming, or a deterministic member-selection procedure.
- **Required changes:**
  - `Types & Lexicon`: Make N3 enforcement internally valid: remove bare STRING as an attribute-value alternative, or explicitly limit it to non-attribute variables/results. Define ISO_DATE and UTC_TIMESTAMP as named types/literal forms usable in signatures, and define category-id as a glossary-category reference with a validity rule requiring an existing category entry.
  - `attribute-name`: Replace every listed 'Transitional STRING pending ...' domain with its named category domain now. In particular, ordering, procedure, food, meal, message_structure, policy, amenity, ticket, and seat must each name a concrete category or be removed together with every construct/signature that uses them. Do not claim N3 compliance while any attribute accepts unqualified STRING.
  - `Action`: Reconcile the normative Action signature table with operation-vocabulary: add book, select_schedule, set_active_store, and all other retained operations to operation-vocabulary with canonical meanings, or remove them from signatures. Preserve every current operation/capability, including the currently exemplified book_hotel behavior or an explicitly typed canonical replacement.
  - `Action`: Publish one source-of-truth domain for every attribute occurrence. Resolve source's conflicting entity-name/code-value definition by operation-specific signatures in the attribute index; likewise correct destination so its index does not contradict location-name overloads. Add the missing set_active_store(target:REF[STRING]) signature explicitly if it is retained.
  - `Generate`: Restore all currently legal Generate attributes that the revision drops, including max_walk_duration and max_walk_duration_unit, and define gen_type or replace it with the revised type production. Add missing value categories/units needed by retained examples, including a paragraph quantity unit if paragraph-count requests are supported.
  - `Utterance`: Define each speech-act's legal attribute signature or a normative shared signature. The current blanket list permits nonsensical combinations and does not determine whether, for example, ask versus inform must carry recipient, topic, content, constraints, or target.
  - `topic-value / constraint-value / transformation-value`: Add compositional constructs/categories for propositions, event ordering, relations, comparative/ranking criteria, factual recency/source requirements, character properties, and explicit exclusions. Do not rely on a fresh monolithic topic_/content_/constraint_ atom for an entire request when the request contains independently meaningful constraints.
  - `basis`: Add canonical encoding rules for mandatory document wrapping, optional modifiers such as inferred spatial relations, handle naming, and selection between content, topic, and constraints. These are necessary for N7 convergence and are exposed by the supplied independent translations.
- **Logic issues:** The proposed Types & Lexicon grammar does not define ISO_DATE or UTC_TIMESTAMP as types, yet Action signatures use ISO_DATE and UTC_TIMESTAMP as if they were types.; The proposal claims every attribute has an exact category domain, but attribute-name explicitly assigns several legacy attributes unqualified transitional STRING domains. This directly violates N3 rather than merely leaving future work.; The Action table introduces book and select_schedule although operation-vocabulary does not define them, while replacing the prior operation surface without preserving currently used book_hotel behavior.; The attribute index states source is entity-name or code-value and destination is entity-name, but the signature table gives source operation-specific domains and allows location-name destinations. The claimed exact one-domain index is therefore false.; Generate grammar returns gen_type although the revision only defines type; it also drops current Generate attributes max_walk_duration and max_walk_duration_unit.; topic-value, constraint-value, and transformation-value use broad open prefix rules but do not specify a canonical translation or member lookup process, so equivalent requests can be encoded as a topic atom, content atom, or arbitrary combinations of constraints.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — The new categories add useful descriptive vocabulary, but the revised Action signatures use operation symbols book and select_schedule that are absent from operation-vocabulary. category-id is also introduced as syntax without a corresponding structural-word/glossary treatment. This makes the claimed complete lexicon less coherent than the current specification.
  - N3: violated — Exact category-qualified types, typed signatures, and new topic/constraint/transformation categories are real progress over current raw content strings. N3 is not realized because multiple retained attributes explicitly remain unqualified STRING, and the revision's own value grammar permits STRING while asserting that all attributes require category-qualified values.
  - N7: violated — Exact category provenance and the entity/class boundary improve convergence, but no canonical rule resolves content versus topic versus constraints, optional inferred modifiers, document wrappers, or handle names. Independent translations show those unresolved choices in practice.
  - N1: violated — The closing phase has not begun and free identifiers, quoted strings, and open-generated category members remain. category-id additionally introduces an unchecked lexical channel, but it does not materially change the pre-existing open status.
  - N4: violated — The revision moves content-bearing examples toward symbolic topics, constraints, and transformations, reducing prose payload use. It remains a build-phase language with STRING and with inadequate compositional categories for many open-request details, so it cannot yet meet the no-prose requirement.
  - N5: violated — Prefix-shaped symbols and category-qualified values help, but open identifiers, quoted strings, category-id text, and unspecified generated vocabulary members mean expressions are not yet self-contained.
  - N6: partial — The proposal uses category-based syntax and simulations can assume missing members in existing categories. It remains partial because several needed semantic categories are absent, including paragraph units, price extraction, multi-criterion ranking, structured events/relations, and factual-source/recency requirements.
  - N8: partial — Fine-grained filters, typed code fields, itinerary classes, constraints, and transformations add useful richness. Conversely, collapsing complex propositions and story/request details into one opaque topic/content atom loses compositional recoverability and makes rich requests only nominally represented.
- **Decision:** partially-accepted
- **Documenter summary:** The Shaper proposed a broad N3-compliance push: category-qualified attribute types, an exhaustive Action signature table, and restructured Generate/Utterance attributes using new topic-value, constraint-value, transformation-value, entity-name, and semantic-category-value vocabularies. The Critic partially accepted the bundle, landing only the five additive vocabulary entries (entity-name, semantic-category-value, topic-value, constraint-value, transformation-value) while rejecting the Types & Lexicon, Action, Generate, and Utterance revisions, since those left several attributes as unqualified STRING (violating N3 outright), introduced Action operations (book, select_schedule) absent from operation-vocabulary, dropped existing Generate attributes, and created internally inconsistent attribute-domain claims flagged by the cross-check disagreement and logic_issues.
- **Cost this sprint:** $1.1116

## Sprint 20 (attempt 2/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 15.0.0 → 16.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N3 [hard] Every attribute value is a glossary symbol, or is derived from glossary symbols by a glossary-defined rule.
- **Shaper proposal (model: gemini/gemini-3.1-pro-preview):** Enforces full N3 compliance by removing all transitional STRING attributes, defining exact category provenance in Types & Lexicon, providing exhaustive typed signatures for Action, Generate, and Utterance, and adding compositional vocabularies for events and character properties.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — The changes realize N3 by ensuring every attribute is typed with a specific glossary category or base type, eliminating unqualified STRING attributes entirely. They also establish canonical encoding rules for N7.
- **Changes:** `Types & Lexicon` (revise, MAJOR), `attribute-name` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `operation-vocabulary` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `Utterance` (revise, MAJOR), `duration-unit-value` (revise vocabulary, MINOR), `Task` (revise, MAJOR), `event-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `character-property-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `topic-value` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `constraint-value` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR)
- **Partial acceptance:** applied 5 of 12 changes (`duration-unit-value`, `event-value`, `character-property-value`, `topic-value`, `constraint-value`); the rest were sent back for rework.
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — The bundle makes real N3 progress by eliminating unqualified STRING from the revised Action, Generate, and Utterance signatures and by giving values explicit category provenance. It cannot be accepted because category provenance is undefined for overlapping symbols and because the Action and operation vocabularies directly contradict each other, producing an invalid sampled expression. The listed vocabulary additions are self-contained, non-regressive improvements and can land while the coupled type/signature revisions are repaired.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — Typed signatures add coverage for code modification, hotel booking, itinerary constraints, symbolic generation constraints, and several speech acts. However, the revised operation vocabulary omits browse_trending while Action retains it, and the Action table cannot express the sampled cup-with-knife task because place accepts an entity-name/location-name destination but not a REF[STRING] container destination.
  - expressivity: mixed — The proposal captures several formerly prose-hidden attributes, including itinerary stop requirements, narrative events, character properties, code facts, output quantities, and constraints. But relational composition remains insufficient: a cup containing a knife and itself positioned both on a counter and in front of a toaster cannot be represented, while topic/content atoms still collapse complex propositions, evidence requirements, and relations into a single opaque symbol.
  - determinism: mixed — Canonical attribute ordering, mandatory document wrapping, and typed operation signatures are genuine progress. But the core provenance claim is internally contradictory because call is both an operation-vocabulary member and an attribute-name member despite the rule that an unquoted symbol belongs to exactly one category; selection between topic, content, events, and constraints is also not canonically defined. Independent translations continue to diverge on artifact target, location symbol, return behavior, and whether an item requires a gap.
  - interpretability: mixed — Operation-specific signatures make legal attributes substantially easier to inspect than the former transitional STRING channels. Conversely, the very large Action signature paragraph, overloaded symbols without a resolution rule, and a glossary index whose domains must be reconstructed from distant signatures make lookup and parsing less clear than claimed.
  - improvement: benefit — A weaker planner benefits from knowing, for example, that stop_requirement is a semantic class, that code assertions/revisions have separate provenance rules, and that narrative events and character properties are lists. The added structure is operationally useful even though several complex requests still need more compositional relations and criteria.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `held-trip-1` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Plan a 3-day itinerary in Kyoto that includes at least one temple per day and avoids anything more than a 20-minute walk from the last stop." → `ENTRYPOINT PlanKyotoItinerary
TASK PlanKyotoItinerary : STRING {
  GENERATE(target=art_itinerary, duration=3, duration_unit=unit_day, max_walk_duration=20, max_walk_duration_unit=unit_minute, stop_min_per_day=1, stop_requirement=class_temple, topic=topic_kyoto_trip) -> plan : STRING
  RETURN plan
}` — Generate and semantic-category-value preserve all actionable itinerary constraints; the proposed paragraph unit is irrelevant.
  - `held-support-1` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "If the customer's order is late and they're a first-time buyer, offer a 10% discount; if they're a repeat buyer, offer free expedited shipping instead." → `ENTRYPOINT ResolveLateOrder
TASK ResolveLateOrder {
  ACTION check_support_status(target=order, metric=metric_order_late) -> is_late : BOOL
  ACTION check_support_status(target=customer, metric=metric_first_time_buyer) -> is_first_time : BOOL
  ACTION check_support_status(target=customer, metric=metric_repeat_buyer) -> is_repeat : BOOL
  IF is_late AND is_first_time THEN {
    ACTION offer_discount(target=order, discount_percent=10)
  } ELSE {
    IF is_repeat THEN {
      ACTION offer_expedited_shipping(target=order)
    }
  }
}` — The exact Action signatures and existing metric-value category carry the branch conditions and discount quantity.
  - `6ca55141-9ba6-4ed7-8c07-77df6c24029d` (Mind2Web (osunlp/Mind2Web, train, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Full] NL: "Find the lowest-priced gray-colored round-shaped mirror in stock in Atlanta, Georgia, with a maximum price of 20 dollars." → `ENTRYPOINT FindMirror
TASK FindMirror : LIST[REF[STRING]] {
  ACTION search_web(target=mirror, availability=avail_in_stock, color=color_gray, currency=curr_usd, location=atlanta_georgia, max_price=20, shape=shape_round) -> mirrors : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  ACTION sort(target=mirrors, rank_direction=dir_asc, rank_field=rank_price) -> sorted_mirrors : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  RETURN sorted_mirrors
}` — Action provides a useful typed search-and-sort plan. The independent translators disagree on atlanta versus atlanta_georgia and whether to return the result.
  - `62806bef-eeeb-4892-b4d5-6a8d2005c58d` (Mind2Web (osunlp/Mind2Web, train, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Fail] NL: "Browse the trending searches in Columbus." → `ENTRYPOINT BrowseTrendingSearches
TASK BrowseTrendingSearches : LIST[REF[STRING]] {
  ACTION browse_trending(target=trending_searches, location=columbus) -> searches : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  RETURN searches
}` — The Action table retains browse_trending, but operation-id must be an operation-vocabulary member and the revised operation-vocabulary omits browse_trending. Thus this otherwise sensible expression is statically invalid.
  - `trial_T20190907_134455_194210#0` (ALFRED (json_2.1.0, train)) [Fail] NL: "Place a white cup with a knife in it on the counter in front of the toaster." → `ENTRYPOINT PlaceCupWithKnife
TASK PlaceCupWithKnife {
  ACTION pick_up(target=knife) -> knife_ref : REF[STRING]
  ACTION pick_up(target=cup, color=color_white) -> cup_ref : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(target=cup_ref, destination=counter, relation=on)
}` — The valid fragment loses the knife-in-cup relation and the cup-in-front-of-toaster relation. place cannot take cup_ref as destination, and one relation attribute cannot encode both counter/on and toaster/in_front_of.
  - `trial_T20190908_185043_273617#2` (ALFRED (json_2.1.0, train)) [Full] NL: "Put a warm coffee cup in the coffee maker." → `ENTRYPOINT PlaceWarmCoffeeCup

  ACTION place(target=cup_ref, destination=coffee_maker, relation=in)

  - `pydata__xarray-4356` (SWE-bench_Verified (princeton-nlp/SWE-bench_Verified, test, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Full] NL: "sum: min_count is not available for reduction with more than one dimensions." → `ENTRYPOINT FixXarraySum
TASK FixXarraySum {
  ACTION modify_code(target=xarray, actual_output=outcome_min_count_error_multiple_dimensions, file=path_xarray_core_nanops_py, method=sym_sum, reproduction_step=repro_sum_min_count_multiple_dimensions, revision=chg_replace_mask_shape_axis_with_np_take_shape_product)
}` — The code-value provenance rules and modify_code signature materially improve this request. The individual symbols are assumed to be complete-category members under N6.
  - `django__django-12155` (SWE-bench_Verified (princeton-nlp/SWE-bench_Verified, test, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Full] NL: "docutils reports an error rendering view docstring when the first line is not empty." → `ENTRYPOINT FixDjangoDocstringTrim
TASK FixDjangoDocstringTrim {
  ACTION modify_code(target=django, actual_output=outcome_default_role_no_content_error, file=path_django_contrib_admindocs_utils_py, method=sym_trim_docstring, reproduction_step=repro_nonempty_first_docstring_line, revision=chg_skip_first_line_when_computing_indent)
  ACTION modify_code(target=django, file=path_django_contrib_admindocs_views_py, revision=chg_update_admindocs_docstring_rendering)
}` — Code-value categories support the files, method, failure, repro, and requested revisions, subject to N6's complete-category assumption.
  - `c892` (PRISM (HannahRoseKirk/prism-alignment, conversations config)) [Full] NL: "The people in our country say that we are so generous and warm, but I know that people will look for a chance to take advantage of others as soon as they can." → `ENTRYPOINT NationalTrustStatement
CONVO NationalTrustStatement {
  TURN t1 SPEAKER=USER {
    UTTER inform(topic=topic_national_generosity_and_exploitation)
  }
}` — The expression records the speech act but the entire substantive proposition is a monolithic topic atom. This is formally symbolic under N6, but not a rich compositional account.
  - `c5375` (PRISM (HannahRoseKirk/prism-alignment, conversations config)) [Full] NL: "Are androids better than iphones" → `ENTRYPOINT AskPhoneComparison
CONVO AskPhoneComparison {
  TURN t1 SPEAKER=USER {
    UTTER ask(topic=topic_android_vs_iphone_comparison)
  }
}` — Utterance's ask signature preserves the request type, but the comparison relation is hidden inside topic-value.
  - `c2151` (PRISM (HannahRoseKirk/prism-alignment, conversations config)) [Full] NL: "it pains me to say but racism seems to be just a moving slider between one ethnic group to another. what is going on?" → `ENTRYPOINT AskRacismDynamics

  TURN t1 SPEAKER=USER {
    UTTER ask(topic=topic_racism_between_ethnic_groups)
  }
}` — The question speech act is captured, but the user's emotional framing and causal metaphor have no independently compositional representation.
  - `c1195` (PRISM (HannahRoseKirk/prism-alignment, conversations config)) [Full] NL: "I believe people with qualifications should be paid more in the workplace than those without any." → `ENTRYPOINT QualificationsPayStatement

  RETURN newsletter

  - `wildchat1m_en3u-111541` (PATHs (microsoft/prototypical-hai-collaborations, wildchat1m_en3u-task_utterance_wintent_anns)) [Full] NL: "hi" → `ENTRYPOINT Greeting

  RETURN story

  - `wildchat1m_en3u-30875` (PATHs (microsoft/prototypical-hai-collaborations, wildchat1m_en3u-task_utterance_wintent_anns)) [Full] NL: "Hi," → `ENTRYPOINT Greeting

  RETURN dinner_plan

I would like you to suggest an email message that I can send to my boss in a friendly manner so that he does not prevent me from working." → `ENTRYPOINT GenerateReturnToWorkEmail
TASK GenerateReturnToWorkEmail : STRING {
  GENERATE(target=art_email, audience=role_manager, constraints=[constraint_one_week_illness_absence, constraint_no_available_vacation, constraint_request_permission_to_resume_work], tone=tone_friendly, topic=topic_return_to_work_after_illness) -> email : STRING
  RETURN email
}` — The email artifact, audience, tone, and stated conditions are symbolically typed under N6, but the desired message proposition is still distributed across assumed constraint atoms rather than a compositional message structure.
- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=low — For held-trip-1, providers disagree between art_plan/topic_kyoto_itinerary and art_itinerary/topic_kyoto_trip. For the mirror task they disagree on location spelling and whether a TASK must return the results. For the ALFRED cup task both find relational gaps, while for xarray and the PRISM items one provider emits GAP and the other mints topic/code symbols. The new N7 rules reduce attribute-order variation but do not resolve these construct, symbol-selection, or gap-versus-symbol choices.
- **Required changes:**
  - `Types & Lexicon`: Replace the assertion that every unquoted symbol belongs to exactly one category with an unambiguous category-resolution rule. Either namespace category members so operation call and attribute call cannot collide, or state that static category provenance is resolved from the signature position and define how this applies to every overlapping token.
  - `Action / operation-vocabulary`: Make the two normative operation inventories identical. Add browse_trending to operation-vocabulary with its canonical mapping and signature, or remove its Action signature and provide a typed replacement. Audit every Action signature against operation-vocabulary in the same edit.
  - `Action`: Add a typed containment/placement form that permits destination=REF[STRING] when placing an acquired object into an acquired container, and add a compositional location-relation representation so one object can be on counter and in_front_of toaster without dropping either relation.
  - `Task / Generate / Utterance`: Publish a normative canonical selection rule for topic versus content versus events versus character_properties versus constraints, including when a proposition must be decomposed into multiple attributes rather than minted as one topic_/content_ atom. The current handle and attribute-order rules do not resolve this principal N7 divergence.
  - `topic-value / constraint-value / event-value / character-property-value`: Add compositional vocabulary or constructs for participants, propositions, causal relations, temporal/event ordering, comparisons, evidence/source and recency requirements, body-part relations, and multi-location relations. Do not treat an entire complex request as one newly minted topic or content symbol merely because the category is open.
  - `Generate`: Add typed food/meal, ingredient, preparation-method, preparation-time, dietary/nutrition, and satiety categories or a structured meal-plan signature. This is required for the dinner request to improve an agent's plan rather than merely label it with opaque constraints.
- **Logic issues:** Types & Lexicon says an unquoted symbol belongs to exactly one category, but call is simultaneously an operation-vocabulary member and an attribute-name member. This makes category provenance and hence typed signature validation undefined.; Action retains browse_trending but revised operation-vocabulary strictly limits operations to listed members and does not list browse_trending; the sampled trending-search expression therefore has no valid parse/operation-id.; The Task worked example violates the proposal's own typed-signature semantics by supplying quoted STRING literals where search_travel and open_page require category-provenance symbols; it also places sort's rank attributes before target despite the stated target-first canonical ordering.; The proposed Action signatures cannot express placement into an already bound container REF[STRING], and a single relation attribute cannot preserve multiple independently stated spatial relations.; The claimed canonical N7 rules do not decide whether substance belongs in topic, content, event lists, character-property lists, or constraints; this remains visible in the independent translations.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — The proposal adds and refines several vocabulary categories and removes many transitional attribute domains. The glossary is nevertheless not yet a complete lexicon, and the Action/operation-vocabulary inconsistency prevents it from being a coherent complete inventory.
  - N3: upheld — Action, Generate, and Utterance attributes now have normative signatures whose values are NUMBER, BOOL, date/time types, or STRING with a named glossary-category provenance; unqualified STRING is explicitly illegal for those attributes. The category-overlap contradiction must still be repaired because it makes exact provenance resolution ambiguous, but it does not reintroduce a signature-sanctioned free-form attribute value.
  - N7: violated — Canonical wrapping, attribute ordering, optional-relation handling, and handle naming improve convergence. Encoding is not yet one-expression-per-meaning because category overlap, topic/content/constraint selection, symbol spelling, artifact selection, and return choices remain unresolved, as shown by the supplied translations.
  - N1: violated — Free STRING literals, free IDENTs, and open identifier channels remain legal in the language. This closing-phase condition was not made worse.
  - N4: violated — The proposal removes sanctioned transitional STRING attributes from Action, Generate, and Utterance, reducing prose-bearing request slots. Generic STRING remains legal elsewhere, so prose fragments are still not prohibited everywhere.
  - N5: violated — STRING and free identifiers remain available, so reading expressions can still depend on natural-language payloads. The proposal does not worsen those pre-existing channels.
  - N6: satisfied — The revised signatures are defined over categories such as code-value, topic-value, event-value, and character-property-value, and the simulations use assumed complete-category members rather than prose.
  - N8: partial — Events, character properties, exclusions, paragraph units, and exact code attributes retain meaningful distinctions. Important richness remains missing for relations, comparisons, evidence, source requirements, temporal structure, and structured meal planning.
- **Decision:** partially-accepted
- **Documenter summary:** Shaper proposed a comply-with-N3 overhaul: typed STRING<category> signatures across Types & Lexicon, Action, Generate, and Utterance (eliminating unqualified STRING attributes), an exhaustive operation/attribute vocabulary, canonical N7 encoding rules for Task, and new compositional vocabularies (event-value, character-property-value, duration-unit-value additions, revised topic/constraint-value). The Critic found real N3 progress but rejected the coupled type/signature bundle because of an internal contradiction (the 'call' symbol belongs to two categories, undermining the one-category provenance rule) and because the Action and operation-vocabulary lists disagreed, invalidating a sampled browse_trending expression; it also flagged unresolved topic/content/constraint selection ambiguity and missing containment/relation expressivity (the ALFRED cup-in-toaster case). Only the self-contained, non-regressive vocabulary additions (duration-unit-value, event-value, character-property-value, topic-value, constraint-value) were accepted; the typed-signature/grammar revisions were sent back for rework.
- **Cost this sprint:** $1.1884

## Sprint 20 (attempt 3/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 16.0.0 → 16.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N3 [hard] Every attribute value is a glossary symbol, or is derived from glossary symbols by a glossary-defined rule.
- **Shaper proposal (model: gemini/gemini-3.1-pro-preview):** Resolves N3 and N7 gaps by defining signature-based category provenance, synchronizing operation signatures, adding REF and compositional placement to Action, establishing a canonical attribute selection rule, and introducing compositional vocabularies for propositions, evidence, and meal planning.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — These changes fully realize N3 by eliminating unqualified STRING attributes and establishing unambiguous category provenance. They advance N7 by providing a canonical selection rule to decompose complex requests into compositional attributes instead of minting opaque atoms.
- **Changes:** `Types & Lexicon` (revise, MAJOR), `operation-vocabulary` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `attribute-name` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `Utterance` (revise, MAJOR), `Task` (revise, MAJOR), `predicate-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `participant-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `evidence-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `food-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `dietary-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR)
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — This is real progress on N3: the proposal replaces many unqualified attribute STRING channels with category-qualified signatures and supplies useful new value categories. It cannot be accepted because overloaded signatures make the central category-provenance rule ambiguous, while removal of current transitional entity/recipient strings regresses hard note N2. The compositional additions improve planning quality, but relation arguments and meal-preparation constraints remain insufficiently structured.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — The new Action signatures and food, dietary, participant, predicate, and evidence categories improve coverage for embodied placement, meal planning, code changes, and structured conversational claims. However, coverage regresses for previously legal quoted entity and named-recipient values: the proposed signatures require STRING<entity-name> and STRING<recipient-value>, while the current glossary explicitly retains quoted-string transitional exceptions for these values. The sampled meal request also cannot express crispy frying because Generate has no preparation-method attribute/category.
  - expressivity: benefit — Compositional participants, predicates, evidence requirements, dietary restrictions, ingredients, events, and multi-destination placement preserve substantially more structure than opaque topic/content atoms. Nevertheless, proposition roles, polarity, quantification, and temporal/causal argument structure remain underspecified: participants plus pred_transmits_to cannot state which participant transmits to which, and a list of relations is positionally paired with destinations without an explicit stated pairing rule.
  - determinism: mixed — Fixed attribute ordering and the topic/content decomposition guidance are useful progress, and the proposed signature-based typing is clearer than the current bare-STRING treatment. But overloaded signatures such as open_page(target: entity-name) and open_page(target: platform-name) leave category provenance unresolved when a symbol belongs to both categories; the signature position alone does not choose an overload. Handle naming is still illustrative rather than algorithmic, and the independent support translations disagree on whether the expedited-shipping branch must also require lateness.
  - interpretability: mixed — Operation-specific typed signatures and named semantic categories make expressions easier to inspect. The very large Action signature paragraph, duplicated pick_up overloads, context-dependent symbol categories, and unconstrained pred_/part_/food_ minting make a reader unable to reliably determine a symbol's category or an operation's intended overload from the expression and glossary alone.
  - improvement: benefit — The structured placement, support, travel, code, and dinner-plan representations give a weaker planner actionable decomposition rather than prose labels. The gain is limited where key task constraints have no field, notably food preparation method and proposition argument roles, and where code or conversational content is represented as one broad atomic code/topic symbol.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `held-support-1` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "If the customer's order is late and they're a first-time buyer, offer a 10% discount; if they're a repeat buyer, offer free expedited shipping instead." → `ENTRYPOINT HandleOrderStatus
TASK HandleOrderStatus {
  ACTION check_support_status(metric=metric_order_late, target=order) -> is_late : BOOL
  ACTION check_support_status(metric=metric_first_time_buyer, target=customer) -> is_new : BOOL
  ACTION check_support_status(metric=metric_repeat_buyer, target=customer) -> is_repeat : BOOL
  IF is_late AND is_new THEN {
    ACTION offer_discount(discount_percent=10, target=order)
  } ELSE {
    IF is_repeat THEN {
      ACTION offer_expedited_shipping(target=order)
    }
  }
}` — Action signature typing supports the task. The source is semantically ambiguous about whether repeat-buyer expedited shipping also requires lateness; the proposed canonical rules do not resolve that encoding choice.
  - `dev-code-1` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Given a list of file paths, delete every file older than 30 days unless it's in the 'archive' folder." → `ENTRYPOINT DeleteOldFiles
TASK DeleteOldFiles(files: LIST[STRING<code-value>]) {
  FOR EACH file_path IN files {
    ACTION file_age_days(file=file_path) -> age_days : NUMBER
    ACTION path_in_folder(file=file_path, source=path_archive) -> in_archive : BOOL
    IF age_days > 30 AND NOT in_archive THEN {
      ACTION delete_file(file=file_path, target=file_system)
    }
  }
}` — The proposed code-value typing and file actions cover the actionable core, assuming code-value contains path_archive under the N6 complete-glossary rule.
  - `8c5ccffa-b88e-4928-9394-6d46cfd36e59` (Mind2Web (osunlp/Mind2Web, train, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Full] NL: "What are today's Limited Time Offers deals?" → `ENTRYPOINT GetLimitedTimeOffers
TASK GetLimitedTimeOffers : LIST[REF[STRING]] {
  ACTION search_web(category=cat_limited_time_offers, target=deal) -> offers : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  RETURN offers
}` — search_web plus the existing category symbol captures the finite actionable core; the click trajectory is implementation detail rather than a required outcome.
  - `b49f88ac-b407-41af-a9e7-f5e4cd1f970a` (Mind2Web (osunlp/Mind2Web, train, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Partial] NL: "Check the rating and user reviews for the game "Deathloop"." → `ENTRYPOINT CheckDeathloopReviews
TASK CheckDeathloopReviews {
  ACTION search_web(target=deathloop) -> results : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  ACTION extract(limit=1, target=results) -> deathloop_ref : REF[STRING]
  ACTION read_page(target=deathloop)
}` — The proposal has no typed review/rating extraction or inspection operation/attribute. The read_page action does not state that rating and user reviews must be returned, and it cannot take the selected REF result.
  - `trial_T20190912_052310_164968#2` (ALFRED (json_2.1.0, train)) [Partial] NL: "To move a full roll of toilet paper and an empty roll to the counter." → `ENTRYPOINT MoveToiletPaper
TASK MoveToiletPaper {
  ACTION pick_up(state=state_full, target=toilet_paper) -> full_roll : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(destination=counter, target=full_roll)
  ACTION pick_up(state=state_empty, target=toilet_paper) -> empty_roll : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(destination=counter, relation=left_of, target=empty_roll)
}` — state-value and REF placement capture both rolls, but the source trajectory requires the empty roll to be left of the full roll. The proposed relation form can only relate empty_roll to counter unless destination=REF[STRING] is used; using full_roll would omit its counter destination. The multi-destination form does not support a REF destination.
  - `trial_T20190919_003451_692296#1` (ALFRED (json_2.1.0, train)) [Partial] NL: "Move a sponge and bowl from a counter to a cupboard" → `ENTRYPOINT MoveSpongeAndBowl
TASK MoveSpongeAndBowl {
  ACTION pick_up(source=counter, target=sponge) -> sponge_ref : REF[STRING]
  ACTION pick_up(source=counter, target=bowl) -> bowl_ref : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(destination=bowl_ref, relation=in, target=sponge_ref)
  ACTION pick_up(target=bowl) -> bowl_with_sponge_ref : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(destination=cupboard, relation=in, target=bowl_with_sponge_ref)
  ACTION close_container(target=cupboard)
}` — REF destination support helps express putting the sponge in the acquired bowl. But close_container only accepts REF[STRING], whereas cupboard is a descriptor; acquiring the cupboard would alter the intended task, so the final required closure is not expressible.
  - `sphinx-doc__sphinx-8035` (SWE-bench_Verified (princeton-nlp/SWE-bench_Verified, test, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Full] NL: "Support defining specific `:private-members:` for autodoc." → `ENTRYPOINT SupportPrivateMembers
TASK SupportPrivateMembers {
  ACTION modify_code(expected_output=outcome_documents_specified_private_members, file=path_sphinx_ext_autodoc_init_py, revision=chg_private_members_accept_arguments, target=sphinx)
}` — The proposed code-value category can carry the file, requested revision, and expected behavior as atomic governed values under N6.
  - `sphinx-doc__sphinx-7462` (SWE-bench_Verified (princeton-nlp/SWE-bench_Verified, test, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Full] NL: "`IndexError: pop from empty list` for empty tuple type annotation." → `ENTRYPOINT FixEmptyTupleAnnotation
TASK FixEmptyTupleAnnotation {
  ACTION modify_code(actual_output=outcome_index_error_pop_empty_list, expected_output=outcome_empty_tuple_annotation_valid, file=path_sphinx_domains_python_py, reproduction_step=repro_empty_tuple_annotation, revision=chg_handle_empty_tuple_annotation, target=sphinx)
  ACTION modify_code(file=path_sphinx_pycode_ast_py, revision=chg_handle_empty_tuple_annotation, target=sphinx)
  ACTION run_tests(assertion=assert_empty_tuple_annotation_builds, target=sphinx)
}` — The code-value category and typed code fields cover the file set, observed outcome, reproduction, expected outcome, revision, and test.
  - `c3312` (PRISM (HannahRoseKirk/prism-alignment, conversations config, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Partial] NL: "All people should switch to veganism on moral and ethical grounds" → `ENTRYPOINT VeganismClaim
CONVO VeganismClaim {
  TURN t1 SPEAKER=USER {
    UTTER inform(participants=[part_all_people, part_veganism, part_moral_grounds, part_ethical_grounds], predicates=[pred_should_switch_to, pred_justified_by], topic=topic_veganism)
  }
}` — participant-value and predicate-value improve over a single topic atom, but no construct binds predicate arguments, universal quantification, or the two grounds to their respective relations. Thus the expression does not recover the claim's full logical structure.
  - `c4642` (PRISM (HannahRoseKirk/prism-alignment, conversations config, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Full] NL: "What does christianity mean?" → `ENTRYPOINT ChristianityMeaning

  }

  RETURN tweets
}` — The new event and participant composition captures the finding event, count, artifact type, humorous style, and tweet quantity without prose.

TASK ReviseMentalHealthPresentation : STRING {

  RETURN revised_script
}` — Generate captures the revision intent, artifact, topic, and audience constraint. The supplied script and requested additions have no structured input/reference mechanism, so the requirement to add ideas into that specific script is not fully preserved.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-69869` (PATHs (microsoft/prototypical-hai-collaborations, wildchat1m_en3u-task_utterance_wintent_anns)) [Partial] NL: "Create personalities, dynamics, roles, appearances, and relationships for the listed fictional characters and Trinidad Osborn." → `ENTRYPOINT GenerateCharacterDynamics
TASK GenerateCharacterDynamics : STRING {
  GENERATE(character_properties=[char_historical_expert, char_exceptional_memory, char_persuasive_speaker, char_dark_side_controlled], constraints=[constraint_include_appearances, constraint_include_personalities, constraint_include_relationship_dynamics, constraint_include_roles], participants=[part_trinidad_osborn, part_hinata_hyuga, part_konan, part_temari, part_hanabi_hyuga, part_samui, part_karui, part_ino_yamanaka, part_shiki_ryougi, part_altair, part_anko_mitarashi, part_kushina_uzumaki, part_mabui, part_tsunade, part_mei_terumi, part_mikoto_uchiha], target=art_character_profile, topic=topic_trinidad_osborn_character_group) -> profiles : STRING
  RETURN profiles
}` — Participants and character properties help, but the request's per-character appearance, personality, power, role, and relationship mappings cannot be associated with individual participants. A flat participant/property list loses those bindings.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-65289` (PATHs (microsoft/prototical-hai-collaborations, wildchat1m_en3u-task_utterance_wintent_anns)) [Partial] NL: "Write a detailed scene where Chandler Hallow learns while filming that his wife Dasha and child died, including his hysteria and horror." → `ENTRYPOINT GenerateGriefScene
TASK GenerateGriefScene : STRING {
  GENERATE(events=[event_chandler_learns_family_death_during_filming], participants=[part_chandler_hallow, part_dasha, part_chandler_child, part_mrbeast_team], quantity=1, style=style_detailed, target=art_script_scene, topic=topic_grief_reaction) -> scene : STRING
  RETURN scene
}` — The artifact, event, participants, and detailed-style request are captured, but no category encodes the requested emotional intensity, team discovery sequence, or scene-level causality beyond an opaque event symbol.
  - `1775938591749` (ThoughtTrace (SCAI-JHU/ThoughtTrace, Parquet column-projected sample, JSON-decoded messages)) [Partial] NL: "Hi, I need help organising my bookshelves." → `ENTRYPOINT BookshelfConversation

  TURN t2 SPEAKER=USER REPLY_TO t1 {

  }
  TURN t3 SPEAKER=USER REPLY_TO t2 {
