}` — Conversation order and follow-ups are preserved. The desired organization criterion and book-category questions are represented largely as opaque predicate/topic atoms, with no category taxonomy or classification relation.
  - `1775498956373` (ThoughtTrace (SCAI-JHU/ThoughtTrace, Parquet column-projected sample, JSON-decoded messages)) [Partial] NL: "Problem solving from work." → `ENTRYPOINT DifficultCoworkerConversation

  RETURN plan

TASK PlanPorkChopDinner : STRING {

  RETURN dinner_plan
}` — food-value, quantity, and max_prep_time improve meal-plan structure. No preparation-method, cooking-technique, texture, or serving-count category/signature field captures fried crispy pork chops; quantity_unit=unit_person also requires a missing unit member/category.
- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=partial — Anthropic and Gemini agree on the core action sequence for limited-time offers and toilet-paper movement, differing only in noncanonical handles. They differ materially on held-support-1: Gemini requires lateness for expedited shipping while Anthropic does not. They also diverge between GAP fallback and newly minted symbols for the Sphinx feature request, and between opaque topic versus structured event/participant encodings for quahog tweets; the proposed selection rule does not yet force convergence.
- **Required changes:**
  - `Action / Types & Lexicon`: Replace overload-dependent provenance with a uniquely resolvable rule. Either prohibit category overlap in a single parameter position, namespace categories, require an explicit typed cast/category tag, or collapse same-operation overloads into one declared union/category rule. Apply this specifically to open_page, read_page, book_hotel, modify_code, run_tests, add_file, delete_file, check_support_status, pick_up, and place.
  - `Action / operation-vocabulary`: Restore no-regression transitional support for previously legal quoted entity-name and recipient-value inputs during the N2/N3 build phase, or add glossary-defined proper-name/entity literal forms accepted by every affected signature. Do not make named recipients or quoted entity selectors inexpressible before N1/N4/N5.
  - `Action`: Repair retained embodied coverage: make close_container accept a selected container REF or provide a typed select/open-container route; extend the multi-location relation form to permit REF destinations; and state that destinations[i] pairs with relations[i], including equal-length validation.
  - `Generate / food-value`: Add preparation-method, cooking-technique, texture/result, serving-count unit, and optionally appliance categories/attributes. A dinner plan must encode fried/crispy pork chops, not merely ingredients and an overall time bound.
  - `predicate-value / participant-value / Utterance / Generate`: Add a glossary-defined proposition/relation construct with typed argument roles (for example subject, object, ground, time, modality, polarity, quantifier) and use LIST[proposition] rather than independent participant and predicate lists. Define how causal, comparison, location, temporal, and evidence relations bind their arguments.
  - `Task`: Make canonical handle generation algorithmic, not illustrative, and expand canonical attribute selection to state when a request requires a proposition/event relation object rather than a newly minted topic/predicate atom. Add a canonical rule for conditional scope, including whether shared antecedents such as 'order late' apply to an 'instead' branch.
  - `Action / attribute-name`: Audit every formerly retained Action attribute and signature against the new exhaustive signatures before removal. Restore or explicitly migrate any current attributes such as procedure, food, meal, message_structure, policy, amenity, ticket, and seat that remain supported by retained constructs, so the N2 build-phase no-coverage-regression requirement is met.
- **Logic issues:** The claim that signature position resolves overlapping glossary symbols is false for overloaded signatures with the same attribute name and different categories. `ACTION open_page(target=x)` has two valid candidate signatures if x belongs to entity-name and platform-name, and the expression contains no category discriminator.; The proposed placement list form has no normative rule that relations[i] applies to destinations[i], no equal-length requirement, and cannot name REF destinations even though the scalar placement form can.; The proposed Action table types close_container as close_container(target: REF[STRING]), while ALFRED-style tasks may require closing a fixed named cupboard that was never selected as a movable object.; The N3 revision removes current transitional quoted-string acceptance from entity and recipient positions, contrary to N2's explicit build-phase requirement not to reduce coverage before the free-text closing phase.; participant-value plus predicate-value is not a proposition representation: it cannot determine argument roles, scope, quantification, modality, negation, or which participants a predicate relates.; The operation-vocabulary calls its member list both an initial/open index and 'strictly limited to enumerated members,' then permits custom_<domain>_<action>; this is internally contradictory about whether operations are closed and does not define deterministic NL-to-domain/action segmentation.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — The proposal expands descriptive vocabulary but removes current quoted-string transitional exceptions for entity and recipient values and drops legacy attributes from its exhaustive index/signatures. That reduces currently expressible build-phase requests, which N2 expressly forbids.
  - N3: violated — Most revised Action, Generate, and Utterance attributes now have named category or base-type signatures, and the new categories provide concrete governed values. N3 is still violated because overload ambiguity prevents reliably establishing category provenance for an attribute value, and retained transitional or un-audited constructs cannot be shown to have all been migrated.
  - N7: violated — Canonical attribute order and a useful topic/content/event/constraint selection direction improve convergence. Ambiguous overload resolution, non-algorithmic handle derivation, underspecified proposition encoding, and the divergent support-branch readings prevent one canonical expression per meaning.
  - N1: violated — Free identifiers, STRING literals, and open-category token minting remain. This is expected during the build phase, but context-only provenance does not make every emitted token independently lookupable.
  - N4: violated — The proposal appropriately removes unqualified STRING attributes from revised signatures, but STRING results and the Task rule allowing literal text payloads remain, and the language is not yet in its closing phase.
  - N5: violated — The custom operation rule relies on translator-selected domain/action English-derived fragments, and free identifiers/quoted strings remain. The proposed atomic category patterns help but do not yet make the language self-contained.
  - N6: partial — The signatures are increasingly defined over categories, allowing simulations to use complete-glossary category members. Several needed meanings still have no category at all, including preparation technique and proposition argument roles, so N6 cannot cover them merely by assuming missing members.
  - N8: partial — The added compositional values, typed code attributes, food/dietary fields, and multi-placement attempt increase richness. Flat participant/predicate lists and opaque newly minted atoms still collapse substantial relational meaning.
- **Decision:** needs-rework
- **Documenter summary:** Shaper proposed a large N3-compliance package: signature-position category provenance for all attribute types, an exhaustive rewrite of Action/Generate/Utterance signatures with typed categories, and new compositional vocabularies (predicate-value, participant-value, evidence-value, food-value, dietary-value) to decompose propositions and meal plans instead of using opaque topic/content atoms. The Critic ruled needs-rework despite crediting genuine N3 progress: overloaded signatures (e.g. open_page accepting both entity-name and platform-name targets) leave category provenance undecidable from position alone, and removing previously-legal quoted entity/recipient strings regressed hard note N2's no-coverage-loss requirement during the build phase — these two issues, plus unresolved proposition argument-role structure (participant+predicate lists can't bind roles, as shown in the Boris Johnson and veganism-claim simulations), were decisive. Doc hygiene passed, but the Critic's required changes (unique provenance rule, restored transitional string support, proposition argument roles, algorithmic handle naming) sent the proposal back for rework.
- **Cost this sprint:** $1.2052

## Sprint 21 (attempt 1/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 16.0.0 → 16.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N7 [hard] Encoding is deterministic; decoding should be as deterministic as richness allows.
- **Shaper proposal (model: anthropic/claude-sonnet-5):** Adds a global Canonical-Form construct fixing (1) how bound handles are named, (2) the TASK-vs-CONVO construct-selection rule, and (3) the step-ordering rule for decomposing a goal into steps, then wires Bind, Task, and Conversation to reference it so encoding converges deterministically.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — N7's build-phase items — which construct a request maps to, how a household goal decomposes into steps, and how handles are named — were previously left to translator judgment. Canonical-Form gives each a fixed, mechanical rule, and Bind/Task/Conversation now cite it as normative, closing the largest remaining sources of encoding non-determinism without touching decoding.
- **Changes:** `Canonical-Form` (add, MINOR), `Bind` (revise, MAJOR), `Task` (revise, MAJOR), `Conversation` (revise, MAJOR)
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — The bundle has the right N7 objective and makes real progress through a shared construct-selection rule and result-handle rule. It cannot be accepted because the proposed canonical rules are incomplete, contain undefined valid cases, and are contradicted by their own examples. No individual revision is self-contained and sound enough to land separately: Bind, Task, and Conversation all depend on the defective Canonical-Form definitions.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — The TASK/CONVO rule preserves the existing broad task/conversation split and does not itself remove coverage. However, SOR can impose source-wording order on operationally independent steps without defining how implied causal dependencies become Binds, and the proposal leaves several sampled closed-task gaps unchanged.
  - expressivity: mixed — The CSR makes one-shot messages consistently TASKs and preserves multi-turn structures as CONVOs. But the mandated SOR can encode an incidental mention order rather than intended operational order, as in examining a book by lamp light, and the proposal's own examples violate its handle rule.
  - determinism: mixed — HNR and CSR make genuine progress on N7 by removing two major translator choices. They do not yield one canonical expression: TASK, CONVO, TURN, and LET identifiers remain unconstrained; Action attribute ordering is still not globally specified; decomposition into steps is not specified; HNR is undefined when target is a REF or STRING; and the supplied independent translations still diverge on payload choice, handle names, task names, and decomposition.
  - interpretability: mixed — A shared Canonical-Form rule is conceptually easier to locate than duplicated rules. But phrases such as "primary semantic symbol," "purely a speech-act," "computed outcome," and "no discernible relative mention order" are not mechanically decidable from an expression or source request, while the normative rule conflicts with the proposal's own worked examples.
  - improvement: benefit — A weaker executor benefits when dependent selected objects have predictable handles and when a multi-turn correction is consistently represented as CONVO. The benefit is limited because the rule does not yet reliably determine step decomposition or all identifiers.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `held-trip-1` (seed_tasks) [Fail] NL: "Plan a 3-day itinerary in Kyoto that includes at least one temple per day and avoids anything more than a 20-minute walk from the last stop." → `<no valid BrainCode expression: GENERATE's declared legal attributes omit stop_requirement, and its published signatures conflict with the existing worked examples using that attribute>` — Canonical-Form is irrelevant because the current Generate signature/attribute rules remain internally inconsistent.
  - `dev-email-2` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "If my manager hasn't replied by Friday, send a polite follow-up; otherwise do nothing." → `ENTRYPOINT FollowUpManager

  IF NOT role_manager THEN {
    ACTION send_email(content=content_follow_up, recipient=role_manager, tone=tone_polite)
  }
}` — HNR forces the awkward but deterministic BOOL handle role_manager from the Action target. This demonstrates that HNR needs a namespace-safe rule when a target symbol is also used as a role/value.

Independent providers likewise disagreed between GAP and an ad-hoc expression.

- **Cross-check:** used=True, agreement=low — Independent translations disagree materially: held-trip uses topic versus content and itinerary versus art_itinerary handles; the flight and matplotlib items split between GAP and ad-hoc encodings; the book item differs on whether lamp activation is encoded; and the movie-opinion item differs on topic versus content. The new HNR would reject several supplied handles, but cannot resolve the payload/decomposition and identifier differences.
- **Required changes:**
  - `Canonical-Form`: Replace subjective CSR predicates with a mechanically testable decision table. Define whether a one-turn USER request with UTTER is always CONVO, whether agent-response turns must be emitted, and define the treatment of a request that both asks for information and requires an effectful action.
  - `Canonical-Form`: Specify canonical identifiers for TASK, CONVO, TURN, and LET bindings, or replace free identifiers with glossary-defined/derived identifiers. N7 cannot claim one expression per meaning while these names remain translator-chosen.
  - `Canonical-Form`: Define deterministic decomposition before SOR: state when a request maps to one Action versus multiple Actions, how implicit prerequisite/causal relations are identified, and how an implied dependency is represented. Do not use source mention order as a substitute for causal ordering.
  - `Canonical-Form`: Revise HNR to define a primary symbol for every valid target form, including REF identifiers, STRING descriptors, absent targets, lists, and non-entity Action targets. Define namespace collision handling with vocabulary symbols and specify whether suffix counts include all enclosing scopes or only the current lexical scope.
  - `Task`: Add a normative canonical Action attribute-order rule and apply it to every Action signature. Existing Action semantics say canonical ordering is unchanged but do not provide a complete global ordering rule.
  - `Bind`: Correct all worked examples to obey HNR. In particular, results of search_travel with no target must derive from operation-id, and GENERATE(target=art_itinerary) must derive art_itinerary rather than itinerary; explain how this avoids collision with vocabulary symbols.
  - `Conversation`: Correct the worked example to include the mandatory ENTRYPOINT declaration, and define canonical TURN names rather than retaining arbitrary t1/t2/t3 labels.
  - `basis`: Reconcile Generate's legal-attribute list with the attribute glossary and existing examples, especially topic and stop_requirement, before relying on deterministic encoding of itinerary tasks.
- **Logic issues:** The Canonical-Form worked example violates its own SOR/HNR framework only partially: it relies on repeated target-derived names but does not establish a general way to distinguish repeated same-kind entities beyond source order.; The revised Task worked example violates HNR: search_travel has no target, so its result should derive from operation-id search_travel, not raw_flights; sort should similarly derive from operation-id or a defined REF rule, not sorted_flights.; The existing and revised Generate examples violate HNR: GENERATE(target=art_itinerary) should bind art_itinerary under the stated rule, not plan or itinerary.; The revised Conversation worked example is not a valid document because the grammar requires exactly one ENTRYPOINT but the example begins directly with CONVO.; CSR's categories are not a partition defined over formal request features: "purely a speech-act," "computed outcome," and an "explicit reply/follow-up/correction relationship" require unconstrained semantic judgment.; SOR only orders already chosen steps. It does not canonically decide whether an instruction is decomposed into steps, which N7 expressly requires for household goals.; HNR says Action target is used when an entity/artifact symbol is present but gives no defined behavior for valid target=REF identifiers, target=STRING descriptors, or operations with target omitted.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — The proposal adds no descriptive vocabulary or glossary categories and continues to depend on free identifiers and STRING channels.
  - N3: violated — No attribute value sets are added; content and numerous code/domain attributes remain STRING-governed transitional violations.
  - N7: violated — HNR and the TASK/CONVO selection attempt make real progress over the current unconstrained choices. N7 remains violated because canonical naming excludes LET/task/conversation/turn identifiers, HNR has undefined valid cases, attribute order is incomplete, and step decomposition is still translator judgment.
  - N1: violated — Free STRING and free IDENT channels remain, as permitted during the build phase but not compliant with N1.
  - N4: violated — All open-item simulations necessarily retain natural-language content strings; this proposal neither removes nor symbolically replaces them.
  - N5: violated — STRING content and translator-chosen identifiers remain dependent on natural-language-derived material outside a closed symbolic rule.
  - N6: partial — The simulations use assumed complete members where established categories exist, but several real gaps are missing action attributes/categories rather than merely missing members.
  - N8: partial — The proposal preserves existing richness, but SOR risks flattening causal intent into textual order and does not add compositional structures for the sampled missing domains.
- **Decision:** needs-rework
- **Documenter summary:** Shaper proposed a new Canonical-Form construct (Handle-Naming, Construct-Selection, Step-Ordering rules) and wired Bind, Task, and Conversation to it, complying with steering note N7 by fixing translator choices in handle naming, TASK-vs-CONVO selection, and step decomposition. Critic ruled needs-rework: N7 showed genuine improvement but the rules were left with undefined cases (e.g. HNR undefined for REF/STRING/list targets, SOR conflating source mention order with causal decomposition), CSR predicates were not mechanically decidable, and the proposal's own worked examples (search_travel/sort handles, GENERATE art_itinerary, missing ENTRYPOINT in the Conversation example) violated the very rules being introduced — most decisively the simulated ALFRED lamp-examine item showing SOR substituting mention order for causal precondition ordering, plus multiple cross-check disagreements on identifiers/decomposition.
- **Cost this sprint:** $1.1155

## Sprint 21 (attempt 2/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 16.0.0 → 16.0.0 (MAJOR/PATCH)
- **Steering note:** N7 [hard] Encoding is deterministic; decoding should be as deterministic as richness allows.
- **Shaper proposal (model: anthropic/claude-sonnet-5):** Rewrites Canonical-Form into five explicit, mechanically-testable sub-rules (document/turn naming, handle naming with full target-form coverage, construct selection via a transcript/effect test, bounded step-ordering, and a single attribute-ordering rule), and propagates the fixes into Bind, Task, Conversation, Flow-If, Action, Generate and Types & Lexicon so every worked example actually obeys the rules it cites.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — Every previously-vague predicate ("purely a speech-act", "primary semantic symbol", "discernible mention order") is replaced with a test over a request's transcript structure and operation-vocabulary membership, so two translators run the same procedure and land on the same TASK/CONVO choice, the same bind names, and the same top-level/turn identifiers. Decoding is untouched (still one parse per expression); decomposition is explicitly scoped so the rule does not silently invent unstated steps, which is a deliberate, documented limitation rather than a hidden gap.
- **Changes:** `Canonical-Form` (add, MAJOR), `Bind` (revise, MAJOR), `Task` (revise, MAJOR), `Conversation` (revise, MAJOR), `Flow-If` (revise, MAJOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `Types & Lexicon` (revise, PATCH)
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — This is a meaningful N7-oriented improvement: it makes several formerly discretionary choices mechanically specified and repairs multiple prior example inconsistencies. It cannot be accepted because the central step-order rule directly contradicts its own example and leaves repeated actions, transcript scope, decomposition, and lexical collision resolution non-deterministic. The prior decomposition requirement was not made; it was explicitly deferred, so the bundle is not yet logically sound.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — Generate now declares several previously undeclared itinerary and generation attributes, which helps express itinerary constraints. However, SOR says only one step is emitted per distinct named operation-vocabulary member, while its own rinse-two-mugs example emits two rinse and two place steps; repeated operations, multiple favorite saves, and other multiplicity requests therefore have no consistent encoding rule.
  - expressivity: mixed — The proposal preserves the current transitional STRING channels and improves explicit generation attributes such as topic and stop_requirement. But deterministic decomposition is explicitly declined: it cannot say when repeated instances of an operation become repeated Actions, a loop, or one plural Action, and it does not represent unstated but necessary causal prerequisites. Several multi-turn samples also remain only partially preserved because transcript scope is not defined.
  - determinism: benefit — CSR, ordinal TURN names, AOR, and most HNR cases materially reduce translator discretion relative to the current specification. Nevertheless, exact convergence remains unlikely for repeated actions, transcript boundaries, source synonym segmentation, target-less operations, and lexical collisions between a bound handle and a vocabulary symbol; these defects prevent N7 from being upheld.
  - interpretability: mixed — A single global attribute-order rule and T1/T2 naming are easy to read. In contrast, permitting a BOOL binding named role_manager beside the recipient vocabulary symbol role_manager leaves name resolution dependent on expected type without a normative resolution rule, and mechanically derived document names such as OpenPage or RoleManager do not reliably identify the task's intent.
  - improvement: mixed — Canonical ordering and explicit data-flow names can help a weaker executor follow straightforward web, household, and generation tasks. The SOR conflict can instead make a weaker model omit required repeated operations or invent a loop, and the refusal to identify necessary causal decomposition leaves important household execution planning under-specified.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `dev-web-1` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Go to the flight booking site, search flights from Tel Aviv to Berlin next Tuesday, and list the three cheapest options." → `ENTRYPOINT OpenPage

TASK OpenPage CONTEXT execution_date="2025-06-03" : LIST[REF[STRING]] {
  ACTION open_page(target="flight_booking_site")
  ACTION search_travel(date="2025-06-10", destination="Berlin", origin="Tel Aviv") -> search_travel : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  ACTION sort(target=search_travel, ordering="price_asc") -> sort : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  ACTION extract(target=sort, limit=3) -> extract : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  RETURN extract
}` — Canonical-Form supplies the Task choice, source-order sequence, absent-target handle search_travel, and AOR. Independent translators can still differ over whether opening the site is an explicit operation required by the terse request or an implicit prerequisite.
  - `dev-web-2` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "On the electronics store, filter laptops to 16 GB RAM or more under $1,200, and add the best-rated one to the cart." → `ENTRYPOINT SelectFilter

TASK SelectFilter {
  ACTION select_filter(target=laptop, max_price=1200, min_ram_gb=16) -> laptop : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  ACTION sort(target=laptop, rank_direction=dir_desc, rank_field=rank_rating) -> sort : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  ACTION extract(target=sort, limit=1) -> extract : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  ACTION add_to_cart(target=extract)
}` — The action signatures for min_ram_gb and the electronics-store context are assumed to exist under N6. HNR and AOR help, but whether filtering maps to select_filter, apply_filters, or search_web remains under-specified by synonym mappings.
  - `479bdc82-ec52-447b-a577-fa2bcdc3886f` (Mind2Web (osunlp/Mind2Web, train, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Full] NL: "Show offers on home essentials under $20 and add the first 3 items to favorites." → `ENTRYPOINT SearchWeb

TASK SearchWeb {
  ACTION search_web(target=home_essentials, category=cat_home_essentials, currency=curr_usd, max_price=20) -> home_essentials : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  ACTION extract(target=home_essentials, limit=3) -> extract : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  FOR EACH item IN extract {
    ACTION save_favorite(target=item)
  }
}` — The actionable core is representable if the full glossary supplies home_essentials. SOR is inconsistent here: the request names save_favorite once but requires three effect instances; it does not say whether FOR EACH is mandated, permitted, or prohibited.
  - `4af615be-4472-4357-8737-5286b06b385d` (Mind2Web (osunlp/Mind2Web, train, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Full] NL: "Browse listings for a used 2006 Honda Civic." → `ENTRYPOINT SearchWeb

TASK SearchWeb {
  ACTION search_web(target=honda_civic, condition=state_used, year=2006) -> honda_civic : LIST[REF[STRING]]
}` — A complete glossary is assumed to provide honda_civic and state_used. The proposal does not determine whether the trajectory's four UI filter operations must be retained as four steps or normalized to one search_web Action.
  - `trial_T20190911_135649_366423#0` (ALFRED (json_2.1.0, train)) [Partial] NL: "Place a hot slice of tomato inside the trash can." → `ENTRYPOINT PickUp

TASK PickUp {
  ACTION pick_up(target=knife) -> knife : REF[STRING]
  ACTION slice(target=tomato, destination=trash_can) -> tomato : REF[STRING]
  ACTION pick_up(target=tomato, state=state_sliced) -> tomato_2 : REF[STRING]
  ACTION heat(target=tomato_2) -> heat : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(target=heat, destination=trash_can, relation=in)
}` — The requested final state is represented, but the supplied trajectory requires repeated opening, closing, movement, and object handling steps that are not stated in the request and whose inclusion is expressly forbidden by SOR. The meaning of heating a slice in a microwave is also not preserved without an appliance/location attribute.
  - `trial_T20190908_050921_727654#1` (ALFRED (json_2.1.0, train)) [Full] NL: "Put a heated apple in the fridge." → `ENTRYPOINT PickUp

TASK PickUp {

It loses the supplied proposal text, all agent turns, and the later request to review again; representing every source message is required by CSR but the proposal does not define transcript extraction from the dataset record.

  }

  RETURN art_work_schedule

No canonical policy chooses between a generated schedule artifact and a Task that solves a scheduling problem.

TASK ArtStudyPlan : STRING {

  RETURN art_study_plan
}` — The initial request is expressible as generation. The trajectory adds SQL, one month, one hour daily, night preference, and low motivation, but the proposal does not specify whether later elicited constraints revise a prior Generate request or create subsequent turns.

Notice that i cant tolerate studying for long time and i want you to assure that i have a fair free time to work and to learn new soft skill." → `ENTRYPOINT ArtStudyPlan

TASK ArtStudyPlan : STRING {
  GENERATE(target=art_study_plan, constraints=[constraint_short_study_sessions, constraint_fair_free_time, constraint_include_soft_skill, constraint_friday_full_rest, constraint_thursday_light_day], duration=2, duration_unit=unit_week, topic=topic_medical_subject_study_plan) -> art_study_plan : STRING
  RETURN art_study_plan
}` — The main constraints and subsequent Friday-rest/Thursday-light correction can be represented as atomic constraints, assuming the full glossary. The actual five-subject allocation and day-by-day timetable remain unstructured; moreover, nothing says whether the correction is a REVISES Turn or a new generation request.
- **Cross-check:** used=False, agreement=partial — No independent translations were supplied. Self-simulation finds high expected agreement for AOR and ordinal TURN labels, but low agreement for transcript boundaries, repeated operations, loop introduction, UI-trajectory normalization, and whether causal prerequisites should be explicit; capable translators will not reliably converge on equivalent expressions for those items.
- **Required changes:**
  - `Canonical-Form`: Replace SOR's contradictory 'one per distinct operation-vocabulary member' rule with a normative decomposition and multiplicity algorithm. It must state when repeated quantified instances emit repeated Actions, a FOR EACH, or one plural operation; define how per-instance ordering and HNR suffixing work; and correct the rinse-two-mugs example to follow that rule.
  - `Canonical-Form`: Define the exact CSR input unit. State whether translation consumes only the supplied NL field, the entire recorded transcript, or an explicitly selected request span; for a transcript define whether every displayed assistant message is emitted as an AGENT TURN and how system/evaluation/feedback records are excluded.
  - `Canonical-Form`: Add a deterministic normalization/decomposition policy for action granularity and causal relations. It must decide, for example, whether a UI trajectory's clicks/filters are preserved or normalized to search_web, when a request's explicit final-state wording entails pick_up/slice/heat/place, and how necessary but unmentioned preconditions are recorded or rejected.
  - `Bind`: Remove or repair the vocabulary-symbol/handle collision exception. Define one unambiguous lexical resolution rule, preferably require a distinct derived handle such as role_manager_result when HNR's base collides with any vocabulary symbol in scope, and revise the Flow-If example accordingly. Expected static type alone cannot define which same-token binding a parser/name resolver selects.
  - `Canonical-Form`: Define canonical mapping from a source operation phrase to exactly one operation-id, including compound requests and the choice among overlapping existing operations such as select_filter, apply_filters, and search_web. The current membership rule only provides some synonyms and leaves operation segmentation to translator judgment.
  - `Generate`: Reconcile the new legal attributes with attribute-name vocabulary: add topic, constraints, transformations, events, and character_properties as governed attribute-name members with their stated categories, and add quantity_unit if Generate uses it. Do not claim the signature matches glossary categories while those attribute names are absent from the attribute glossary.
  - `Canonical-Form`: State the canonical representation of conversational follow-ups that alter a prior generation request: distinguish REPLY_TO, REVISES, and a fresh request, including whether replacing a length constraint or requesting continuation replaces the whole earlier body.
- **Logic issues:** SOR requires one step per distinct named operation-vocabulary member, yet Canonical-Form's own worked example uses rinse twice and place twice. Thus the same request is both required to have two repeated steps and prohibited from having them.; The claimed vocabulary/handle collision disambiguation is not defined by static type. In `check_replied(target=role_manager) -> role_manager : BOOL`, later `recipient=role_manager` can denote either a BOOL binding or a recipient vocabulary symbol; expected type is not a lexical or name-resolution rule.; CSR refers to 'the source' being a transcript but does not define whether dataset trajectories, assistant responses, feedback, and the displayed NL prompt constitute that source. This materially changes TASK/CONVO selection and turn count.; The proposed Generate signature names topic, constraints, transformations, events, and character_properties, but current attribute-name vocabulary does not define those attribute names or their governing categories.; The DNR first-step rule depends on SOR, while SOR cannot consistently determine the first step for repeated, compound, or trajectory-normalized operations; consequently ENTRYPOINT and document identifiers remain non-deterministic in those cases.; The previous required change concerning deterministic decomposition was not actually completed: the proposal explicitly says SOR never invents unnamed preparatory steps, but it supplies no rule for explicit final-state requests whose required operation sequence is not enumerated.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — The proposal references more vocabulary categories through Generate but adds no descriptive glossary categories and leaves newly used Generate attribute names absent from attribute-name vocabulary. Existing incomplete vocabulary remains incomplete, without a demonstrated regression in the intended build phase.
  - N3: violated — STRING content and many STRING-governed attributes remain transitional by design. The proposal does not add undefined free-form attribute values beyond the current regime, but its new Generate attributes are not fully governed in the attribute glossary.
  - N7: violated — CSR, DNR/TNR, HNR coverage for several target forms, and AOR are real progress over translator-chosen names and order. N7 remains violated because SOR contradicts its own example, transcript scope is undefined, operation normalization/decomposition remains subjective, and collision resolution is not mechanically specified.
  - N1: violated — Free IDENT and STRING channels remain, as permitted before the closing phase. The proposal's new signature references undeclared attribute-name tokens, so it does not advance complete look-upability.
  - N4: violated — All content channels remain transitional STRING prose, consistent with the stated build-phase ordering; no new removal or worsening occurs.
  - N5: violated — Expressions still rely on arbitrary STRING and free identifiers. The proposal preserves rather than expands these existing channels.
  - N6: partial — Generate is written over category names, which is aligned with the direction, but the new attributes lack entries in the attribute-name category and deterministic custom operation naming still relies on source-language segmentation.
  - N8: partial — Additional Generate attributes preserve useful itinerary and generation distinctions. The broken multiplicity/decomposition rule loses distinctions required by repeated-action tasks, so the overall richness position is not improved.
- **Decision:** needs-rework
- **Documenter summary:** Shaper proposed splitting Canonical-Form into five mechanical sub-rules (CSR, DNR/TNR, HNR, SOR, AOR) and propagated them into Bind, Task, Conversation, Flow-If, Action, Generate, and Types & Lexicon to address steering note N7 (decoding determinism), complying fully with the note's intent. Critic rejected the bundle as needs-rework: despite genuine determinism gains (ordinal turn names, unified attribute ordering, expanded handle-naming coverage), the proposal's own worked example (rinse-two-mugs) directly contradicted its stated SOR 'one step per operation member' rule, and it left transcript-scope boundaries, repeated-action/decomposition policy, and vocabulary/handle-name collision resolution undefined—so N7 remains violated (improved but not upheld); doc hygiene passed but did not override the logical-consistency failure.
- **Cost this sprint:** $0.5921

## Sprint 21 (attempt 3/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 16.0.0 → 17.0.0 (MAJOR)
- **Steering note:** N7 [hard] Encoding is deterministic; decoding should be as deterministic as richness allows.
- **Shaper proposal (model: anthropic/claude-sonnet-5):** Fixes the Critic's required changes: Canonical-Form now specifies an explicit multiplicity/decomposition algorithm, a defined CSR input scope, a causal-chain decomposition policy, an operation-selection tie-break rule, and a conversational-follow-up (REPLY_TO vs REVISES) rule; Bind's vocabulary-symbol collision is resolved with a mandatory _ref suffix instead of relying on static type; Generate's new attributes are added to attribute-name vocabulary.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — N7 is a hard note; all required changes are implemented rather than rebutted.
- **Changes:** `Canonical-Form` (add, MAJOR), `Bind` (revise, MAJOR), `Flow-If` (revise, MAJOR), `Task` (revise, MAJOR), `Conversation` (revise, MAJOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `attribute-name` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR)
- **Partial acceptance:** applied 1 of 8 changes (`attribute-name`); the rest were sent back for rework.
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — This bundle makes real progress on N7 through explicit request scope, multiplicity, ordering, and conversational normalization, but it cannot be accepted because doc hygiene failed and its canonical rules contradict their own examples. More importantly, the causal-chain policy fails ordinary placement requests and OSR still relies on an undefined synonym-distance judgment, so independent encoders would not yet converge. The attribute-name vocabulary addition is self-contained and sound enough to land separately.
- **Doc hygiene:** FAIL — Doc hygiene FAILED — `Flow-If` missing glossary_gloss
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — The proposed ISR correctly prevents recorded trajectories from being mistaken for request content, and OSR usefully normalizes retrieval requests. However, CCR gives no usable acquisition rule for imperative placement requests: "Put two forks in the sink" reaches PLACE from its destination but supplies neither a preceding pick_up nor a valid REF target. The unsupported relative date in held-trip Mind2Web input also has no deterministic resolution without a supplied execution date.
  - expressivity: mixed — The new multiplicity, collection, correction, and generation-attribute rules preserve several constraints that were previously translator choices. But CCR loses the required acquisition action for ALFRED placement tasks, and the new rule that UI trajectories are never preserved can erase explicitly requested navigation or UI operations if they are part of the supplied request rather than merely evaluation logs.
  - determinism: benefit — ISR, fixed TURN naming, AOR, bounded-literal versus collection multiplicity, and the REPLY_TO/REVISES policy are substantial progress toward N7. Nevertheless, N7 is not yet upheld: operation-vocabulary's "nearest-synonym lookup" has no defined distance, tie-break, phrase segmentation, or synonym coverage, while HNR examples contradict the mandatory collision rule. Different translators can still choose different operation segments and handles.
  - interpretability: mixed — The explicit ISR and named subrules make the intended normalization policy easier to inspect. Conversely, the phrase "as before" in HNR leaves key handle derivation outside this rule, and several worked examples violate the stated collision rule, so a reader cannot reliably infer which form is normative.
  - improvement: mixed — Canonical ordering and explicit data-flow naming would help a weaker model execute search, sorting, and conversational corrections. The missing acquisition policy produces an under-specified household plan, however, and is worse than a decomposition that retains pick_up followed by place.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `held-trip-1` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Plan a 3-day itinerary in Kyoto that includes at least one temple per day and avoids anything more than a 20-minute walk from the last stop." → `ENTRYPOINT ArtItinerary

  GENERATE(target=art_itinerary, duration=3, duration_unit=unit_day, max_walk_duration=20, max_walk_duration_unit=unit_minute, stop_min_per_day=1, stop_requirement=class_temple, topic=topic_kyoto_trip) -> art_itinerary_ref : STRING
  RETURN art_itinerary_ref
}` — Generate and attribute-name additions encode duration, temple minimum, and walking bound. HNR requires _ref because art_itinerary is also a bare vocabulary symbol in this body; the proposal's own Generate example does not follow that rule.
  - `dev-email-2` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "If my manager hasn't replied by Friday, send a polite follow-up; otherwise do nothing." → `ENTRYPOINT CheckReplied

  IF NOT role_manager_ref THEN {

- **Cross-check:** used=False, agreement=n/a — No independent provider translations were supplied. Self-simulation indicates likely agreement on ISR and basic CSR, but low convergence on operation segmentation, nearest-synonym selection, omitted causal prerequisites, and HNR collision handling.
- **Required changes:**
  - `doc hygiene (scripted — blocks acceptance)`: Doc hygiene FAILED — `Flow-If` missing glossary_gloss
  - `Canonical-Form`: Repair HNR and every affected worked example. Define the computed base without referring to unspecified prior rules, and apply the collision rule consistently: if a bare vocabulary symbol is used as target/value in the body, a result handle derived from it must be suffixed _ref. Correct the rinse, Action search, and Generate examples, which currently violate their own mandatory rule.
  - `Canonical-Form`: Replace "nearest-synonym lookup" with a complete deterministic operation-mapping algorithm: define source phrase segmentation, the finite synonym table or a glossary-defined normalization relation, how compound clauses map to operations, and tie-breaking when multiple operation members match. Do not rely on an undefined notion of semantic nearness.
  - `Canonical-Form`: Repair CCR for imperative movement/placement. State whether a direct request to put/place an entity entails ACQUIRE followed by PLACE; if so, require pick_up and bind its REF before place. Define how plural per-unit sequences bind and consume each distinct REF. If acquisition is intentionally not implicit, reject direct placement as unrepresentable rather than emitting a place action with an unbound descriptor target.
  - `Canonical-Form`: Define deterministic treatment of relative temporal expressions when no execution_date is supplied. Either require translation input to supply an execution-date anchor and reject otherwise, or add a governed relative-date value representation; never emit a prose value such as "this weekend" for a date attribute.
  - `Conversation`: Reconcile UTTER's legal-attribute signature with the proposed and existing Conversation examples. Either add topic and constraints to UTTER with governed categories and canonical ordering, or rewrite those examples to use only UTTER's legal attributes.
  - `Flow-If`: Add Flow-If's glossary_gloss as the required top-level change field, not nested inside worked_example, so the scripted doc-hygiene check passes.
  - `duration-unit-value`: If Generate is meant to encode character counts, add unit_character with an exact rendering/counting definition, or prohibit quantity_unit=unit_character and retain the length constraint through a dedicated governed alternative.
- **Logic issues:** Doc hygiene failed because Flow-If has no top-level glossary_gloss; acceptance is blocked independently of the substantive review.; Canonical-Form's rinse example binds -> mug although bare mug appears as target in the same body, directly contradicting its mandatory _ref collision rule.; The revised Action example binds -> laptop despite bare target=laptop, and the revised Generate example binds -> art_itinerary despite bare target=art_itinerary; both also contradict HNR.; CCR says only explicitly stated stages are included, but gives no trigger for ACQUIRE in ordinary "put X in Y" requests; PLACE then receives no selected REF.; OSR's "nearest-synonym lookup" is not mechanically defined and therefore cannot guarantee one operation-id per source phrase.; Conversation's existing/proposed example uses UTTER ask(topic=...), while UTTER semantics permits only audience, content, recipient, target, and tone.; The proposed attribute-name example uses topic and quantity_unit successfully, but duration-unit-value does not define unit_character needed for common character-length generation constraints.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — attribute-name gains several missing governed entries, which is genuine glossary progress. The glossary still does not cover all descriptive words or all existing expression channels.
  - N3: violated — The newly listed Generate attributes now name their intended value categories, improving attribute governance. Many attributes remain explicitly STRING-governed violations, and unit_character is absent.
  - N7: violated — ISR, CSR, CFR, SOR, AOR, deterministic TURN naming, and a lexical collision strategy are substantive progress. Undefined nearest-synonym selection, unclear operation segmentation, inconsistent HNR examples, and unresolved acquisition decomposition prevent deterministic encoding.
  - N1: violated — STRING and free IDENT channels remain intentionally open during the build phase. This proposal neither closes them nor materially broadens the rule that defines arbitrary identifiers.
  - N4: violated — Transitional content STRING remains, including in most simulations. That is pre-existing build-phase behavior and is not worsened by the proposal.
  - N5: violated — Expressions still depend on free STRING content and open atomic identifier minting rules. No new English-grammar dependency is introduced, but self-containment is not yet reached.
  - N6: partial — The proposal continues to define new Generate attributes over glossary categories, and simulations assume missing members within existing categories. Missing categories and UTTER/topic inconsistency still prevent fully grammar-over-category treatment.
  - N8: partial — New constraints and generation attributes add richness, but CCR's failure to retain acquisition for direct placement requests loses an essential actionable relation that the current language could express with pick_up followed by place.
- **Decision:** partially-accepted
- **Documenter summary:** Shaper attempted to fully comply with steering note N7 by adding a seven-sub-rule Canonical-Form (ISR, CSR, CFR, OSR, HNR with mandatory _ref suffix, SOR/CCR, AOR) plus revisions to Bind, Flow-If, Task, Conversation, Action, and Generate, but the Critic ruled needs-rework and the final decision was partially-accepted, landing only the attribute-name vocabulary addition. The deciding factors were a scripted doc-hygiene failure (Flow-If missing a top-level glossary_gloss) and substantive self-contradictions the Critic's simulations exposed: several worked examples (rinse, laptop, art_itinerary) violated the proposal's own mandatory HNR collision rule, OSR's 'nearest-synonym lookup' was left undefined, and CCR gave no acquisition step for ordinary placement requests like 'Put two forks in the sink,' so independent translators still would not converge deterministically under N7.
- **Cost this sprint:** $0.5155

## Sprint 22 (attempt 1/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 17.0.0 → 17.1.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N7 [hard] Encoding is deterministic; decoding should be as deterministic as richness allows.
- **Shaper proposal (model: openai/gpt-5.6-terra):** This increment establishes a single canonical-form rulebook for request scope, construct selection, operation mapping, decomposition, ordering, and naming. It also makes placement acquisition explicit, permits governed topic/constraint attributes on UTTER, and defines character-count units.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — The changes replace translator discretion with mechanical scope, naming, ordering, operation-selection, multiplicity, placement, and temporal-anchor rules. Every resulting expression remains uniquely parseable while preserving the current build-phase STRING channels.
- **Changes:** `canonical-form` (add, MAJOR), `Bind` (revise, MAJOR), `Task` (revise, MAJOR), `Conversation` (revise, MAJOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `Utterance` (revise, MAJOR), `Flow-If` (revise, MAJOR), `duration-unit-value` (revise vocabulary, MINOR)
- **Partial acceptance:** applied 1 of 9 changes (`duration-unit-value`); the rest were sent back for rework.
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — The bundle makes genuine, substantial progress toward deterministic encoding and fixes each previously identified worked-example, placement, temporal, UTTER-signature, hygiene, and character-unit issue. It cannot be accepted because the new OSR fallback contradicts the actual operation-vocabulary rule and regresses hard notes N1 and N5, while common utterance-only conversations still lack a canonical declaration name. The duration-unit-value addition is sound, self-contained, and should land now.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — Explicit acquisition-and-placement expansion improves embodied-task coverage, and topic/constraint-capable UTTER improves conversational coverage. However, the new OSR fallback emits custom_general_x<bytes>, which is not a member form permitted by the current operation-vocabulary category, while relative-date requests without an externally supplied anchor are now translation errors; both create avoidable expressibility failures.
  - expressivity: mixed — The changes preserve useful distinctions for multiplicity, physical identity, correction, relative dates, topic, constraints, and character-count limits. But OSR reduces an unmatched requested operation to an opaque byte-derived token without defined attributes or operation signature, and the TASK-versus-CONVO rule does not operationally distinguish information seeking, advice, and artifact generation in difficult cases.
  - determinism: benefit — This is substantial progress on N7: AOR, SOR, turn numbering, revision selection, date anchoring, placement expansion, repeated-object decomposition, and much of handle naming replace prior encoder discretion. Determinism remains incomplete because operation fallback conflicts with the actual vocabulary rule, HNR leaves key terms such as 'bare vocabulary-symbol target' and 'first step handle base' insufficiently defined, and canonical declaration naming is undefined for utterance-only conversations.
  - interpretability: mixed — Explicit REF acquisition before PLACE and governed UTTER topic/constraints make generated expressions easier to read. Conversely, byte-encoded custom operations are not human-readable, and Canonical Form is a very dense cross-cutting rulebook whose terms are not all linked to stable lexical definitions.
  - improvement: benefit — The canonical decomposition of physical placement, ordering of dependent operations, explicit correction links, and normalized length units give a weaker planning model materially more actionable structure than raw requests. The benefit is limited for custom-domain operations because the generated opaque fallback gives no useful planning semantics.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `held-trip-1` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Plan a 3-day itinerary in Kyoto that includes at least one temple per day and avoids anything more than a 20-minute walk from the last stop." → `ENTRYPOINT ArtItinerary

TASK ArtItinerary : STRING {
  GENERATE(target=art_itinerary, duration=3, duration_unit=unit_day, max_walk_duration=20, max_walk_duration_unit=unit_minute, stop_min_per_day=1, stop_requirement=class_temple, topic=topic_kyoto_trip) -> art_itinerary_ref : STRING
  RETURN art_itinerary_ref
}` — Generate, AOR, HNR, and duration-unit-value encode the closed actionable core; Canonical Form fixes the artifact handle.
  - `dev-embodied-2` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Put both pillows from the sofa onto the armchair, one at a time." → `ENTRYPOINT Pillow

TASK Pillow {
  ACTION pick_up(source=sofa, target=pillow) -> pillow_ref : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(destination=armchair, target=pillow_ref)
  ACTION pick_up(source=sofa, target=pillow) -> pillow_ref_2 : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(destination=armchair, target=pillow_ref_2)
}` — Canonical multiplicity and mandatory acquisition directly help. The rule should explicitly state that an explicit count of two denotes two distinct selected entities.
  - `b30b9f84-0541-4826-a3af-98220b851f7c` (Mind2Web (osunlp/Mind2Web, train, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Full] NL: "Search for women's sports wear, size S." → `ENTRYPOINT SportsWear

TASK SportsWear {
  ACTION search_web(gender=gender_women, size=size_small, target=sports_wear) -> sports_wear_ref : LIST[REF[STRING]]
}` — AOR makes the attribute order deterministic. This assumes the complete glossary's entity-name category contains sports_wear under N6.
  - `33b57a14-f469-405c-8843-f7e8ceb5b1ed` (Mind2Web (osunlp/Mind2Web, train, Parquet column-projected sample)) [Full] NL: "Add 10 of the cheapest green shirt to my cart" → `ENTRYPOINT Shirt

TASK Shirt {
  ACTION search_web(color=color_green, target=shirt) -> shirt_ref : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  ACTION sort(rank_direction=dir_asc, rank_field=rank_price, target=shirt_ref) -> sort : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  ACTION extract(limit=1, target=sort) -> extract : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  ACTION add_to_cart(quantity=10, target=extract)
}` — SOR preserves search, rank, choose, then cart addition. The current Action signature index must confirm quantity is legal on add_to_cart; Canonical Form itself does not supply that signature.
  - `trial_T20190907_164924_809101#1` (ALFRED (json_2.1.0, train)) [Full] NL: "Place a washed tomato on a table." → `ENTRYPOINT Tomato

TASK Tomato {
  ACTION pick_up(target=tomato) -> tomato_ref : REF[STRING]
  ACTION rinse(destination=sink, target=tomato_ref) -> rinse : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(destination=table, relation=on, target=rinse)
}` — The proposed ACQUIRE < TRANSFORM < CONDITION < PLACE rule supplies a deterministic high-level plan. It still needs explicit operation-result typing for rinse and a rule for selecting the required sink when none is named in the user request.
  - `trial_T20190906_230132_658755#2` (ALFRED (json_2.1.0, train)) [Full] NL: "Put a warm glass on the shelf." → `ENTRYPOINT Glass

TASK Glass {
  ACTION pick_up(target=glass) -> glass_ref : REF[STRING]
  ACTION heat(destination=microwave, target=glass_ref) -> heat : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(destination=shelf, relation=on, target=heat)
}` — Canonical stage ordering improves the household plan. The inferred microwave is not licensed by the proposed rules, so independent encoders could diverge between a heat operation with no destination and an inferred microwave.

- **Cross-check:** used=False, agreement=n/a — No independent translations were supplied. Simulated convergence is substantially better for the itinerary and direct-placement items because AOR, SOR, multiplicity, and REF naming are specified; it remains low for unmatched operations, utterance-only CONVO names, source-scope selection, and inferred equipment/locations.
- **Required changes:**
  - `canonical-form`: Replace OSR's `custom_general_x<bytes>` fallback with a token form that is explicitly legal under operation-vocabulary, has a glossary-defined membership rule, a static signature/attribute policy, and a human-readable deterministic domain/action decomposition. Alternatively remove fallback emission and make unmatched operations a translation error until a glossary operation is added. Do not claim an undefined `custom-token form`.
  - `canonical-form`: Define HNR completely and mechanically for every Bind site: define 'bare vocabulary-symbol target,' distinguish a literal vocabulary value from an identifier reference, specify whether `_ref` is part of the first-step handle base used for declaration names, and give a declaration-name rule for a first step with no result, including UTTER-only CONVOs.
  - `canonical-form`: Reconcile ISR/CSR scope rules. State exactly whether a supplied dataset transcript includes all human turns or only the final human message, and remove the contradiction between 'through its final human-authored message' and 'a multi-message supplied input ... emits one CONVO TURN per supplied message.' Specify how final-request artifact generation interacts with preceding corrections.
  - `canonical-form`: Define deterministic implicit-resource policy for decompositions: when a request says washed/warm but names no sink or microwave, either require a source/destination symbol in the request, define a canonical abstract resource symbol, or reject the translation. Also specify result types and REF identity preservation for transform operations such as rinse and heat.
  - `Task`: Update stale Task/Action/Bind/Generate/Conversation worked examples and glossary examples to obey the adopted canonical handle and ordering rules, including required `_ref` collisions and canonical TASK/CONVO declaration names. Normative examples that violate canonical form undermine deterministic encoding.
- **Logic issues:** OSR says custom_general_x<bytes> 'matches operation-vocabulary's custom-token form,' but operation-vocabulary instead permits only custom_<domain>_<action>; no such custom-token form exists.; HNR does not define a declaration name for a CONVO whose first step is an unbound UTTER, yet CSR maps every single non-effectful request to exactly that form.; Canonical scope is internally contradictory: it limits application to the final human-authored message while separately requiring one TURN per supplied message for multi-message inputs.; The direct-placement stage rule is useful but underspecified for requests that imply washing/heating without naming an appliance or source.; Several retained normative examples become noncanonical under the new mandatory HNR/AOR rules, so the specification gives users conflicting instructions.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — The new duration member is a proper glossary category addition, but the glossary still does not completely cover every expression word and Canonical Form introduces an asserted but undefined custom-token form.
  - N3: violated — Build-phase STRING channels remain as allowed by the staged note, but content and several other attributes still lack governed symbolic value sets. The changes do not worsen this state.
  - N7: violated — AOR, SOR, OSR matching, HNR, placement decomposition, temporal anchoring, TURN naming, and correction classification are meaningful deterministic-encoding progress. It remains violated because OSR's fallback is incompatible with the operation lexicon, declaration names are undefined for common CONVO forms, and source-scope rules conflict.
  - N1: violated — The proposal's new custom_general_x<bytes> operation is not defined by the current operation-vocabulary membership rule, and its alleged custom-token rule is absent from the glossary. It therefore introduces a new channel whose emitted symbols cannot be traced to a valid glossary-defined member or rule.
  - N4: violated — Transitional STRING content remains, as required during the present build phase, and no new prose-bearing channel is added.
  - N5: violated — OSR derives arbitrary operation tokens from normalized natural-language predicate spans while citing no actual glossary-defined literal/category rule for that generated form. This makes the language less self-contained than the prior explicit custom_<domain>_<action> rule.
  - N6: satisfied — The simulations consistently use existing categories and assume required members of those categories, rather than reshaping syntax around absent individual members.
  - N8: partial — The proposal retains useful distinctions for multiplicity, placement, temporal anchors, conversation corrections, topics, constraints, and character counts. Opaque custom byte operations and unresolved implicit-resource semantics still reduce meaningful richness.
- **Decision:** partially-accepted
- **Documenter summary:** Steering note N7 prompted a large Canonical-Form rulebook (scope selection, operation mapping, decomposition, ordering, handle naming, placement expansion, temporal anchoring, revision classification) spanning Task, Conversation, Action, Generate, Utterance, Bind, and Flow-If, which the Shaper proposed in full compliance. The Critic found substantial determinism gains but ruled needs-rework/partial-accept: only the standalone duration-unit-value addition (including unit_character) landed, because the OSR custom-operation fallback contradicted the existing operation-vocabulary rule (regressing hard notes N1 and N5) and HNR left declaration naming for utterance-only CONVOs undefined, plus ISR/CSR scope rules were self-contradictory.
- **Cost this sprint:** $0.4186

## Sprint 22 (attempt 2/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 17.1.0 → 17.2.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N7 [hard] Encoding is deterministic; decoding should be as deterministic as richness allows.
- **Shaper proposal (model: openai/gpt-5.6-terra):** This step adds a normative Canonical Form rulebook and applies it to request scope, declaration names, handle names, attribute order, operation selection, embodied decomposition, and transcript structure. It also repairs affected constructs and examples so the specification gives one consistent encoding path.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — The changes replace translator discretion with explicit canonical rules while retaining one unambiguous parse. Unmatched operations remain legal through the existing custom operation category, but now have a deterministic readable token derivation and a fixed signature policy.
- **Changes:** `canonical-form` (add, MAJOR), `operation-vocabulary` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `entity-name` (revise vocabulary, MINOR), `Task` (revise, MAJOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `Bind` (revise, MAJOR), `Generate` (revise, MAJOR), `Utterance` (revise, MAJOR), `Conversation` (revise, MAJOR)
- **Partial acceptance:** applied 1 of 9 changes (`entity-name`); the rest were sent back for rework.
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — The bundle makes substantial, concrete progress on N7 through fixed ordering, names, transcript scope, and embodied-resource defaults, but it is not yet logically sound enough to accept as a whole. The custom-operation path is internally inconsistent and underspecified, while multiplicity and coordinated speech acts remain nondeterministic. The entity-name resource addition is self-contained and beneficial, so it can land independently.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — Canonical embodied decomposition and abstract resource entities improve coverage for household requests such as washed or heated objects. However, the custom-operation rule does not provide a usable deterministic encoding for requests whose required attributes have no governed category (for example RAM capacity and cuisine), and several sampled web tasks remain inexpressible.
  - expressivity: mixed — The changes preserve object identity through pick_up/transform/place and retain transcript turns, which improves preservation of embodied and conversational structure. But numeric quantity is expanded into repeated indistinguishable operations without an explicit count or selection identity, and unsupported request criteria cannot be represented; single-message multi-question requests also have no rule requiring their distinct speech acts to be retained.
  - determinism: mixed — AOR, HNR, turn names, declaration names, resource defaults, and dependency ordering are substantive progress toward N7. Yet OSR leaves predicate-span selection underspecified, its fallback can generate illegal tokens after Unicode normalization or digit-bearing predicates, and synonym/category selection remains non-canonical for many vocabulary categories; capable encoders would still diverge on unsupported criteria, operation boundaries, and whether a state denotes selection or a required transformation.
  - interpretability: mixed — Named canonical resources and predictable result handles improve readability. Conversely, custom_general_<normalized_predicate> is only a lossy source-string transformation rather than a defined operation meaning, and repeated identical physical sequences do not visibly identify which distinct requested unit they refer to.
  - improvement: benefit — Explicit pick_up/transform/place expansion, REF preservation, dependency ordering, and retained correction chains provide operational structure a weaker planner can use. The benefit is limited for web and software tasks lacking required attribute categories or operation signatures.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `dev-web-2` (seed_tasks) [Fail] NL: "On the electronics store, filter laptops to 16 GB RAM or more under $1,200, and add the best-rated one to the cart." → `FAIL: no governed attribute/category represents minimum RAM capacity; custom operations may only use otherwise governed attributes.` — Canonical Form cannot encode the actionable RAM>=16 criterion. price and rating can be represented, but the closed-task core cannot.
  - `held-trip-1` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Plan a 3-day itinerary in Kyoto that includes at least one temple per day and avoids anything more than a 20-minute walk from the last stop." → `ENTRYPOINT ArtItinerary

TASK ArtItinerary : STRING {
  GENERATE(target=art_itinerary, duration=3, duration_unit=unit_day, max_walk_duration=20, max_walk_duration_unit=unit_minute, stop_min_per_day=1, stop_requirement=class_temple, topic=topic_kyoto_trip) -> art_itinerary_ref : STRING
  RETURN art_itinerary_ref
}` — Task, AOR, HNR, and declaration naming apply coherently. Assuming topic_kyoto_trip is available under N6.
  - `4bc70fa1-e817-405f-b113-0919e8e94205` (Mind2Web) [Full] NL: "Add the cheapest Women's Sweaters to my shopping cart." → `ENTRYPOINT SearchWeb

TASK SearchWeb {
  ACTION search_web(category=cat_sweater, gender=gender_women, target=sweater) -> sweater_ref : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  ACTION sort(rank_direction=dir_asc, rank_field=rank_price, target=sweater_ref) -> sort : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  ACTION extract(limit=1, target=sort) -> extract : LIST[REF[STRING]]
  ACTION add_to_cart(target=extract)
}` — The canonical operation sequence is plausible, but HNR's base for list-returning search is target=sweater, yielding sweater_ref; another encoder may choose plural inventory semantics absent from the rules.
  - `7ad37a91-c4c2-42d6-9b87-1f3e6a81647e` (Mind2Web) [Fail] NL: "check reservation availability for an Indian restaurant with price below $30 in East village" → `FAIL: no governed cuisine criterion/category or reservation-availability operation signature is defined.` — location, currency, and max_price are insufficient to preserve Indian cuisine and reservation availability.
  - `trial_T20190906_224234_994763#2` (ALFRED) [Full] NL: "Put a spoon in the sink." → `ENTRYPOINT Spoon

TASK Spoon {
  ACTION pick_up(target=spoon) -> spoon_ref : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(destination=sink, relation=in, target=spoon_ref)
}` — Canonical direct-placement expansion and REF use are clear.
  - `trial_T20190909_065023_650475#2` (ALFRED) [Full] NL: "Retrieve plate from cupboard, insert it into microwave, take the plate to the fridge" → `ENTRYPOINT Plate

TASK Plate {
  ACTION pick_up(source=cupboard, target=plate) -> plate_ref : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(destination=microwave, relation=in, target=plate_ref)
  ACTION pick_up(source=microwave, target=plate) -> plate_ref_2 : REF[STRING]
  ACTION place(destination=refrigerator, relation=in, target=plate_ref_2)
}` — The request does not explicitly require heating, so no heat expansion is required. The second pick_up reselects plate rather than consuming the first REF, which is a semantic ambiguity not resolved by Canonical Form.
  - `django__django-15957` (SWE-bench_Verified) [Full] NL: "Prefetch objects don't work with slices." → `ENTRYPOINT Django

TASK Django {
  ACTION modify_code(file=path_django_db_models_fields_related_descriptors_py, revision=chg_fix_prefetch_sliced_queryset, target=django)
}` — Assumes code-value members under N6. The proposed modify_code synonym mapping helps only if the imperative is treated as fix rather than its broader reported-bug wording.
  - `django__django-16502` (SWE-bench_Verified) [Full] NL: "After #26052 runserver returns response body for HTTP HEAD requests." → `ENTRYPOINT Django

TASK Django {
  ACTION modify_code(actual_output=outcome_head_response_includes_body, expected_output=outcome_head_response_excludes_body, file=path_django_core_servers_basehttp_py, revision=chg_strip_head_response_body, target=django)
}` — Assumes the code-value symbols exist under N6. A full bug report's reproduction details are not captured because no deterministic rule says when each report field must be emitted.
  - `c1957` (PRISM) [Partial] NL: "I think something important we should discuss is that it is important not to be negative. Negativity is a choice, every time. It is not related to depression. People who have the option to be negative should not be like that! I don't want to hear about bias, I am simply saying it is important to stay positive or you become negative. It's very important." → `ENTRYPOINT Inform

CONVO Inform {

    UTTER inform(content=content_positivity_choice_not_depression_reject_bias)

CONVO Inform {

  - `c1044` (PRISM) [Full] NL: "Do you think its important for people to have healthy relationships in life ?" → `ENTRYPOINT Ask

CONVO Ask {
  TURN t1 SPEAKER=USER {
    UTTER ask(topic=topic_importance_healthy_relationships)
  }

  - `wildchat1m_en3u-45091` (PATHs) [Partial] NL: "How would someone discibe the surface texture of the catwalk greatings on Oil Rigs? Then secondley how would you discribe the Specular Highlights on such gratings?" → `ENTRYPOINT Ask

CONVO Ask {
  TURN t1 SPEAKER=USER {
    UTTER ask(topic=topic_oil_rig_catwalk_grating_surface_texture_and_specular_highlights)
  }
}` — One topic atom can name the combined subject, but the requested two-part explanatory structure is not represented as distinct requests or output sections.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-136118` (PATHs) [Partial] NL: "answer with 80 to 110 words as you are in a speaking IELTS test. Don't reveal that you are an A.I. In your country (Iran), how much time do people spend on work and how much time on leisure? Is this a good balance, do you think?" → `ENTRYPOINT Ask

CONVO Ask {
  TURN t1 SPEAKER=USER {
    UTTER ask(constraints=[constraint_word_count_80_110, constraint_ielts_speaking_register, constraint_do_not_claim_ai_identity], topic=topic_iran_work_leisure_balance)
  }
}` — The language has no minimum/maximum word-count attributes, no speaking-test register value, and no reliable representation of the requested human persona; assumed atomic constraints conceal rather than structurally encode these distinctions.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-105894` (PATHs) [Full] NL: "Make a report about Peterhof" → `ENTRYPOINT ArtStructuredReport

TASK ArtStructuredReport : STRING {
  GENERATE(target=art_structured_report, topic=topic_peterhof) -> art_structured_report_ref : STRING
  RETURN art_structured_report_ref
}` — Generate selection, AOR, HNR, and task naming are deterministic for this simple artifact request.
  - `wildchat1m_en3u-135133` (PATHs) [Partial] NL: "overview of the football industry in egypt. include credible apa 7 reference" → `ENTRYPOINT ArtTechnicalReport

TASK ArtTechnicalReport : STRING {
  GENERATE(constraints=[constraint_credible_apa_7_references], format=format_structured_report, target=art_technical_report, topic=topic_egypt_football_industry) -> art_technical_report_ref : STRING
  RETURN art_technical_report_ref
}` — The required reference count and APA-7 citation structure have no first-class attributes. An assumed atomic constraint is not a rich deterministic encoding of the citation requirement.
  - `1775448628523` (ThoughtTrace) [Partial] NL: "I will be in Paris for two days for a conference, and I'll be free on day 2. I want to go to a museum and see some nice art. I don't want anything too crowded. What do you suggest?" → `ENTRYPOINT Ask

  }
}` — The request's day-2 timing is only an assumed opaque constraint; no itinerary/date-relative scheduling construct captures the conference context or recommendation ranking.
