TASK ArtPlan : STRING {
  GENERATE(constraints=[constraint_four_star_hotel, constraint_traditional_food_restaurants, constraint_heritage_sightseeing, constraint_other_attractions], target=art_trip_plan, topic=topic_china_trip) -> art_trip_plan_ref : STRING
  RETURN art_trip_plan_ref
}` — An artifact plan can be requested, but hotel rating, restaurant recommendation, heritage activities, and attraction relations are merely opaque atomic constraints rather than structured itinerary requirements.
- **Cross-check:** used=False, agreement=low — No independent translations were supplied. Self-simulation finds likely agreement for simple direct placement and single GENERATE requests, but low convergence for custom operations, unsupported criteria, operation-span selection, repeated physical objects, and rich conversational requests.
- **Required changes:**
  - `canonical-form`: Make OSR total and legal: define normalized_predicate over an explicitly permitted ASCII tokenization/escaping rule, reject source predicates that cannot be represented rather than emitting an illegal token, and define how the predicate span is selected when the request contains a verb phrase, coordination, negation, or a noun-derived imperative. The generated custom symbol must have a glossary-defined semantic domain/action decomposition, not only a normalized source spelling.
  - `operation-vocabulary`: Align the membership rule exactly with Canonical Form OSR. Either permit the same Unicode-derived token form with a precise escaping rule or constrain OSR to the vocabulary rule's [a-z_]+ output; do not casefold arbitrary source text and then require an ASCII-only result.
  - `canonical-form`: Define a canonical representation for explicit physical multiplicity that preserves unit identity. Either add a count/quantity selector to pick_up and an explicit per-item reference rule, or require a LIST plus FOR EACH whenever multiple indistinguishable entities are requested. Specify how a second pick_up of the same entity is distinguished from reacquiring the already-held REF.
  - `canonical-form`: Define whether and how one supplied single message containing coordinated independent speech acts/questions is decomposed into multiple UTTER steps. The current 'never split at punctuation' rule does not determine treatment of semantic coordination and loses the second question in the Conversation worked example.
  - `basis`: Add governed vocabulary/categories and operation signatures for currently unrepresentable closed-task criteria exposed by the sample: hardware capacity (including RAM with numeric bounds), cuisine, reservation availability, and a deterministic relation between ranking/filtering criteria and search operations.
  - `Task`: Repair the Task worked example: its stated AOR requires target first, but the example writes GENERATE(target=...) correctly only after a task declaration whose return handle is canonical; ensure all revised examples are checked against the complete signature table. Also define the GENERATE format symbol used by technical/report artifacts consistently with the glossary.
  - `Conversation`: Repair the Conversation worked example or its source text. The source has two questions in one supplied message, while the example encodes only one UTTER and Canonical Form says a plain single text is never split at punctuation.
- **Logic issues:** OSR NFC-normalizes and Unicode-casefolds arbitrary source predicates, but operation-vocabulary permits custom predicates only when the result matches [a-z_]+. Inputs containing digits, non-ASCII letters, or many ordinary technical predicates have no defined legal output.; OSR says to match maximal operation-vocabulary synonym spans but does not define the candidate predicate spans, syntactic role assignment, or handling of coordinated verbs; longest-span tie breaking cannot resolve that prior ambiguity.; Canonical Form mandates one distinct acquire/transform/place sequence per explicit numeric physical count, yet Action has no canonical per-unit selector, count attribute, or identity mechanism. Repeated operations may acquire the same object twice.; The revised Conversation example violates Canonical Form's preservation claim: two distinct questions in one supplied message become one UTTER about friendship.; The proposal states that custom operations permit governed attributes whose categories match their values, but it gives no per-custom-operation signature semantics beyond mandatory target; this is insufficient to determine which requested arguments are representable or meaningful.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — The resource entities and revised operation category add glossary material, but the glossary still lacks complete descriptive vocabulary and several sampled closed-task categories are absent.
  - N3: violated — The proposal retains transitional STRING channels and does not supply value sets for their attributes. It does not materially worsen the existing build-phase violation.
  - N7: violated — AOR, HNR, SOR, transcript scope, resource defaults, and synonym tie rules are real progress. Encoding remains non-deterministic or undefined for custom predicate extraction, illegal normalization outputs, multiplicity identity, and semantic decomposition of coordinated speech acts.
  - N1: violated — Free identifiers and STRING remain legal by design in the build phase. The custom-operation mismatch can additionally produce tokens not valid under the stated category rule, but this does not materially worsen the pre-existing open-lexicon state.
  - N4: violated — Transitional STRING is expressly retained, so natural-language payload channels remain. The proposal does not add new mandatory prose payloads.
  - N5: violated — The source-derived custom predicate depends on natural-language tokenization and Unicode casefolding, and free text remains available. The new canonical rules do not close this dependency.
  - N6: partial — The simulations can assume missing members within existing categories, but genuinely missing categories for RAM and cuisine remain gaps; the proposal does not resolve them.
  - N8: partial — Transform identity and explicit resources retain useful distinctions, but custom-operation normalization and opaque atomic constraint/topic encodings do not preserve rich internal structure for multi-criterion requests.
- **Decision:** partially-accepted
- **Documenter summary:** Addressing steering note N7 (decoding determinism), the Shaper proposed a large 'comply' bundle adding a normative Canonical Form rulebook (AOR/HNR/SOR/OSR ordering and naming rules, transcript scope, embodied decomposition, custom-operation fallback) plus revisions to Task, Action, Bind, Generate, Utterance, and Conversation. The Critic found real determinism progress but ruled needs-rework overall due to logic gaps—an illegal-token-producing custom-operation fallback, undefined multiplicity identity for repeated physical objects, and loss of coordinated speech acts in the Conversation example (simulated item c1957/Conversation worked example were decisive)—so only the self-contained entity-name (canonical resource symbols) addition was accepted; the rest requires rework before resubmission.
- **Cost this sprint:** $0.3332

## Sprint 22 (attempt 3/3) — 2026-10-01

- **Language version:** 17.2.0 → 18.0.0 (MAJOR/MINOR)
- **Steering note:** N7 [hard] Encoding is deterministic; decoding should be as deterministic as richness allows.
- **Shaper proposal (model: openai/gpt-5.6-terra):** This step makes canonical encoding concrete for document selection, step and attribute order, handles, physical multiplicity, speech-act coordination, and safe custom operations. It also adds governed search criteria for RAM, cuisine, reservation availability, and ranking/filter pipelines.
- **Shaper's position on the note:** comply — The changes impose one parseable canonical form: fixed target-first/alphabetical attributes, deterministic handles and turn names, defined action decomposition, canonical multi-question preservation, and a restricted legal custom-operation fallback. Synonyms map only to listed canonical operation symbols.
- **Changes:** `canonical-form` (add, MAJOR), `operation-vocabulary` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `attribute-name` (revise vocabulary, MAJOR), `capacity-unit-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `cuisine-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `reservation-status-value` (add vocabulary, MINOR), `Action` (revise, MAJOR), `Task` (revise, MAJOR), `format-value` (revise vocabulary, MINOR), `Utterance` (revise, MAJOR), `Conversation` (revise, MAJOR)
- **Critic decision:** needs-rework — [Forced acceptance after 3 attempt(s) without a clean accept — the required changes below are known issues carried forward for a future sprint to address, not resolved.] This is a real but incomplete N7 improvement: it adds valuable canonical ordering intentions, multiplicity, criteria vocabulary, and multi-question preservation. The core canonical rules cannot be accepted while AOR contradicts its own example and transform references have no type- and scope-consistent encoding. The four standalone value-category additions are sound, self-contained vocabulary progress and can land now without inheriting those defects.
- **Doc hygiene:** pass — All changes carry a complete glossary entry (gloss + worked example), and every revise/remove targets an existing entry.
- **Pre-KPI assessment:**
  - coverage: mixed — The added RAM, cuisine, reservation, ranking, multiplicity, and multi-question rules improve coverage for several closed samples. However, the restricted custom-operation rule rejects many previously encodable multiword, coordinated, negated, or non-imperative operations, and the revised Action signatures leave important operations such as booking without a governed signature. Transform actions also become difficult to encode consistently because they require and preserve a REF but have no canonical binding rule compatible with no-shadowing.
  - expressivity: mixed — The proposal preserves physical multiplicity and independently scoped questions better than the current language, and search/ranking criteria are materially more expressive. But it does not define containment relations such as a box already containing a card, source-specific game-review retrieval, booking details, or the complete semantic structure of several open requests; its custom-operation restriction can turn previously expressible actionable requests into translation errors.
  - determinism: mixed — Target-first attribute ordering, deterministic turn names, HNR bases, list acquisition, and explicit multi-UTTER splitting are real N7 progress. Determinism remains broken by conflicting rules: AOR requires target first but the canonical-form Pillow example puts target last; HNR requires scalar Action-result names while transforms must preserve an existing REF and no visible binding may be shadowed; and 'independently scoped speech act clause' is semantic rather than operationally defined. Different capable translators will also disagree on predicate-span boundaries, whether a request is a custom imperative, and what counts as a replacement message.
  - interpretability: mixed — Dedicated criteria vocabulary and explicit resource symbols improve readability. Conversely, custom_general_<predicate> has only a source-word-derived label and an intentionally opaque 'external general-domain action' meaning, so a reader cannot determine its effect from the glossary; the new canonical rulebook is also internally inconsistent with its own worked example.
  - improvement: benefit — For supported search, shopping, household multiplicity, and multi-question tasks, explicit filters, ranking pipelines, references, and ordered utterances give a weaker agent substantially more actionable structure than raw language. The benefit is limited by the undefined/custom operation cases and transform-reference inconsistency, which must be repaired before the core canonical rules can safely land.
- **Simulated examples:**
  - `dev-embodied-1` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Rinse the mug in the sink, then put it in the coffee maker." → `ENTRYPOINT Mug

TASK Mug {
  ACTION pick_up(target=mug) -> mug_ref : REF[STRING]
  ACTION rinse(destination=sink, target=mug_ref)
  ACTION place(destination=coffee_maker, target=mug_ref)
}` — Canonical target-first ordering and REF reuse help. The expression exposes the unresolved transform problem: rinse is said to return the same REF identity, while HNR would require a result name and Bind forbids rebinding mug_ref.
  - `held-trip-1` (seed_tasks) [Full] NL: "Plan a 3-day itinerary in Kyoto that includes at least one temple per day and avoids anything more than a 20-minute walk from the last stop." → `ENTRYPOINT ArtItinerary

  RETURN art_itinerary

- **Cross-check:** used=False, agreement=n/a — No independent translations were supplied. Self-simulation indicates partial agreement only: canonical naming/order rules help, but translators can diverge on speech-act independence, predicate spans, custom-operation eligibility, and transform-result naming.
- **Required changes:**
  - `canonical-form`: Repair AOR and every worked example to one rule. If AOR is target first then alphabetical remaining attributes, change the Pillow example to `pick_up(target=pillow, quantity=2, source=sofa)`; audit all revised examples against that rule.
  - `canonical-form`: Define a type-correct canonical transform protocol. State whether rinse/heat/chill/slice/pour are written without `->` and retain the input REF, or bind a distinct mandatory result handle derived from the operation plus target; make that choice consistent with HNR, Bind no-shadowing, and the Action signatures. Define how an unbound transform target is canonically expanded to pick_up before the transform.
  - `Action`: Give every retained and custom operation a complete signature discipline. In particular, define custom_general_<predicate> as accepting exactly target plus an enumerated set of attributes and state its result type/effect; add governed signatures for booking and the sampled travel criteria or retain a deterministic legal fallback for coordinated requests.
  - `canonical-form`: Replace 'independently scoped speech-act clause' with an operational segmentation algorithm over a defined parse of coordination, interrogatives, imperatives, clause boundaries, and shared arguments. Specify whether a compound question with one requested explanation is one UTTER or multiple UTTERs.
  - `canonical-form`: Define replacement-message detection mechanically. Specify the lexical/symbolic conditions that make a later supplied message REVISES rather than REPLY_TO, and define how the lineage tip is determined when a message addresses a revised request while also asking a new follow-up.
  - `operation-vocabulary`: Do not define custom_general_<predicate> by source spelling alone. Either enumerate a controlled custom-action category with glossary meanings, or add a glossary-defined mapping from a normalized predicate token to an action semantic class; a decoder must recover more than an opaque English-derived label.
  - `Action`: Add governed search/retrieval criteria needed by the sampled closed tasks: review type/user reviews, travel deal type, date/month, guest count, pet-friendly, road-trip, last-minute, and booking. Add a containment/contents relation or state mechanism so 'box with card' and 'pan with scoop' can identify the required composite entity rather than relying only on an incidental prior sequence.
  - `Task`: Clarify TASK naming for a first step with no HNR result base, such as GENERATE with target art_structured_report, and make the stated PascalCase transformation precise for acronym/numeric/underscored vocabulary symbols.
- **Logic issues:** Canonical Form says AOR writes target first, but its own Pillow worked example writes `pick_up(quantity=2, source=sofa, target=pillow)`.; HNR says scalar Action results use `<target>_ref`, while rinse/heat/chill/slice/pour must return the same REF identity. Binding that result either shadows the input REF, creates a new identity contrary to the semantics, or violates HNR if omitted.; The revised Action semantics says transforms require and return REF identity, but grammar makes Action results optional and supplies no canonical expansion for transforming an unbound descriptor.; The phrase 'independently scoped speech acts' is not a syntactic or semantic decision procedure; two translators can split the same compound question differently.; Custom actions have an opaque source-derived predicate label and no action-specific signature/decomposition, so their decoded effect is not glossary-recoverable.; Canonical Form rejects coordinated unmatched predicates, making requests such as 'find ... and book' translation errors although the prior custom fallback could encode an atomic domain action.; The Action revision names custom operations as legal operation-vocabulary members but does not establish their signature compatibility with Action's governed-attribute validation.; The claimed complete supplied-input transcript policy is underspecified for assistant messages, feedback, greetings, corrections that add information, and whether a later request is a new REPLY_TO or a replacement.
- **Fundamental notes:**
  - N2: violated — The proposal adds separately look-upable capacity, cuisine, reservation, format, and search-criterion vocabulary, improving the descriptive lexicon. The glossary still is not the complete lexicon because free identifiers, STRING contents, and source-derived custom predicate labels remain.
  - N3: violated — New attributes have governed NUMBER or vocabulary-category values, and capacity/cuisine/reservation values are defined. Many existing attributes, notably content and code-related fields, still accept STRING during the build phase.
  - N7: violated — Fixed ordering intentions, handle conventions, deterministic turn names, explicit multiplicity, and the effort to constrain custom operations are meaningful progress over the current spec. N7 remains violated because AOR contradicts its example, transform handles conflict with REF identity and no-shadowing, speech-act segmentation is not algorithmic, and custom predicate/semantic decisions are still translator-dependent.
  - N1: violated — The language remains in the vocabulary build phase with IDENT and STRING channels, including generated task/variable names and transitional content.
  - N4: violated — Transitional STRING content remains explicitly legal in UTTER and GENERATE. The proposal does not add prose to the language beyond pre-existing channels, but it does not close them.
  - N5: violated — The source-derived custom_general_<predicate> rule still relies on an English predicate from the request, and free STRING channels remain.
  - N6: satisfied — The proposed syntax generally remains defined over vocabulary categories, and simulations use assumed members of existing categories rather than reshaping grammar around absent individual entries.
  - N8: partial — Fine-grained RAM, cuisine, reservation, ranking, multiplicity, and multi-question distinctions preserve more meaning. The restrictive custom rejection and opaque custom semantics unnecessarily sacrifice richness in unsupported domains.
- **Rebuttal rulings:**
  - `canonical-form OSR totality and legal custom symbols`: upheld — The Shaper made the predicate lexical rule ASCII-safe and rejects several invalid forms, satisfying the previous token-legality concern. The remaining semantic-domain requirement is still unresolved and is retained as a new required change.
  - `operation-vocabulary OSR alignment`: upheld — Canonical Form and operation-vocabulary now both use the `[a-z]+` predicate restriction for custom_general_<predicate>.
  - `canonical physical multiplicity`: upheld — The LIST[REF] plus FOR EACH rule and quantity-dependent pick_up result type now preserve multiple acquired entities and prevent repeated indistinguishable scalar acquisition.
  - `coordinated independent speech acts`: overruled — The proposal correctly adds multiple UTTERs, but 'independently scoped speech-act clause' lacks an operational boundary rule, so it does not yet make encoding deterministic.
  - `closed-task criteria for RAM, cuisine, reservation availability, ranking/filtering`: upheld — The proposal adds governed RAM, cuisine, reservation, rank, sort, and extract representations, though further criteria remain needed for travel and review tasks.
  - `Task report format/example repair`: upheld — format_structured_report is added and the revised Task example uses it. The remaining task-name edge cases are a separate issue.
  - `Conversation two-question worked example`: upheld — The revised Conversation example now represents both questions as ordered UTTERs.
- **Decision:** accepted
- **Documenter summary:** Steering sprint on N7 (encoding determinism in decoding): the Shaper proposed a large 'Canonical Form' rulebook (document/operation/attribute ordering, handles, multiplicity, speech-act segmentation, restricted custom-operation fallback) plus new search-criteria vocabulary (RAM/capacity units, cuisine, reservation status) and revised Action/Task/Utterance/Conversation/format-value entries, complying with the note. The Critic judged the core canonical-form rules self-contradictory and left for rework after 3 attempts (e.g. AOR's target-first rule conflicts with its own Pillow worked example, and transform operations' REF-identity preservation clashes with HNR/no-shadowing), overruling the Shaper's speech-act segmentation rebuttal as still non-operational; it forced-accepted only the four standalone, self-contained vocabulary additions (capacity-unit-value, cuisine-value, reservation-status-value, format-value) as sound incremental N7/N2 progress, deferring the rest of the required changes to a future sprint.
- **Cost this sprint:** $0.3985
